# Open questions

Decisions and gaps that are not settled yet. Each row names what is unresolved, why it matters and what closes it. Remove a row (and note the outcome in the ADR or issue that settles it) once it is resolved. GitHub issues stay the system of record for work ([ADR-033](adr/ADR-033-github-as-system-of-record.md)); this file is for questions that are not work items yet.

Last updated: 2026-10-19, after M0-01 to M0-08, M0-10, M0-11, M0-14, M0-15, M0-16, M0-17, M0-73, M0-76, M0-77, M0-35 and M0-36 landed on main.

## Release and CI

| ID    | Question                                                                                                                                                                                         | Context                                                                                                                                                                                              | Closes when                                                                                                                       |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-01 | Enable "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests", or give release-please a dedicated token?                                                                                     | `release-please.yml` fails on every push to main with "GitHub Actions is not permitted to create or approve pull requests". A release PR opened with the default `GITHUB_TOKEN` also does not trigger CI. | Maintainer picks the setting or a PAT/app token (see H-02, #63); the workflow then runs green.                                     |
| OQ-02 | Delete the stray branch `release-please--branches--main--components--source` left on the remote by the first failed run?                                                                          | The failed run created the branch and pushed a commit to it before the PR call failed.                                                                                                               | Maintainer confirms; branch deleted (or reused by the next successful run).                                                       |
| OQ-03 | Is the release-please dry-run still valid after M0-02 changed the lockfile?                                                                                                                       | The dry-run that proposed `0.1.0-alpha.1` ran before the M0-02 lockfile updates. The real merge flow (release PR, tag, GitHub prerelease) has never run.                                            | A dry-run or real run after OQ-01 is fixed.                                                                                       |
| OQ-04 | Is `initial-version: 0.1.0-alpha.1` the right start?                                                                                                                                              | release-please ignores the manifest's `0.0.0` until a tag exists and would otherwise release 1.0.0. Later alphas increment the suffix.                                                              | Maintainer confirms or picks another start before the first release.                                                              |
| OQ-06 | When does the docs site get deployed?                                                                                                                                                             | The execution plan has the release workflow deploy docs to GitHub Pages, but nothing is published until the name is cleared (ADR-029, A-22). The site is built locally, `noindex`.                  | ADR-029 accepted; then add the deploy step and drop `noindex`.                                                                   |

## Custom lint rules (M0-04)

| ID    | Question                                                                 | Context                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Closes when                                                                                  |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-30 | Which i18n keys are on the honest-wording allow-lists?                    | No locale files exist yet. The defaults are guesses: all terms are allowed under `streamBrowse`, `stream.browse`, `nodeAgent`; "read-only" is allowed under `lock`, `permission(s)`, `managedBy`, `mcp.status`. Message contexts are `messages`, `peek`, `dlq`, `mcp.tools`. | M0-51 (Transloco) fixes the key layout; update the defaults in `tools/eslint-rules/src/rules/honest-wording.ts`. |
| OQ-31 | Is path scoping of the custom rules right?                                | Guessed from the blueprint: version compares allowed only in `libs/shared/semantics` and `libs/engine/svc-capabilities`; mode branching allowed in `apps/engine/src/{entry,modes,modules}`; raw buffers allowed in `libs/engine/kernel/src/bounded`; sockets allowed in `libs/ui/platform`. | The paths exist (M0-10, M0-16, M0-51) and the `projectRules` table in `eslint.config.mjs` is checked against them. |
| OQ-32 | Are the name-based heuristics acceptable?                                 | `bounded-buffers-only` matches names ending in buffer, backlog, outbox, inbox, ring, fifo or deque (not `queue`, which is a broker entity). `no-broker-version-compare` also flags AMQP protocol versions such as `'0.9.1'`. `no-mode-branching` matches names containing `mode`. | Maintainer confirms, or the first false positives in real code tune the patterns.           |
| OQ-33 | How do we apply honest-wording to docs and to other locales?              | The critique asks for docs content and every locale. The rule lints i18n JSON and UI string literals with English terms only. Docs (Markdown/MDX) are not linted, and translated claims are not detected.                                                | A Markdown lint pass for `docs/` and `apps/docs`, and a per-locale term list.                |
| OQ-35 | A-9 (nominal settlement proofs) needs an architecture test, not a lint rule | The M0-04 issue lists A-9, but forged-literal detection and the type test need the type system or ts-morph. No rule was written for it.                                                                                                                  | The architecture test lands in `tools/arch-tests` (with the ports work).                     |
| OQ-17 | Lint `apps/docs`?                                                            | No `lint` target. Linting by hand reports errors in generated `apps/docs/.astro/*.d.ts`.                                                                                                  | Add `**/.astro/**` to the ESLint ignores and a `lint` target.                |

## Agent toolchain (M0-07)

OQ-19 (who approves release-age and trust exclusions) is settled by CODEOWNERS: `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `tools/supply-chain/` and `docs/adr/ADR-027-log.md` are owned by @RafaelJCamara, and the agent hooks block agent edits to the supply-chain keys, the allow-list and the log. The approver column keeps "maintainer".

| ID    | Question                                                                                   | Context                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Closes when                                                                                                         |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-50 | How should CODEOWNERS map owners while there is one maintainer?                             | Every path maps to `@RafaelJCamara` (amendment A-21: named people, not the `@org/...` teams of the plan). The grouping (security, desktop, semantics, lab evidence, release, maintainers) is kept as comments. GitHub does not let an author approve their own PR, so code-owner review cannot be satisfied on the maintainer's own PRs. There is no catch-all entry, so only trust-critical paths request review. | A second maintainer or an org with teams exists: split the owners. Until then decide in OQ-42 how branch protection treats the sole owner. |
| OQ-51 | Keep the guards that go beyond the plan's list?                                             | `guard-paths` also protects `.claude/settings.json`, the hook scripts, `.github/CODEOWNERS` and `docs/CODEOWNERS`, so an agent cannot disable its own guard-rails. `guard-bash` also blocks `npm`/`yarn`/`bun` package adds, bulk `pnpm update`, `git commit -n` and `git push --all`/`--mirror`. A follow-up hook change then needs a maintainer.                              | Maintainer confirms the list in `tools/agent-hooks/protected-paths.mjs`, or trims it.                                |
| OQ-52 | Is `docs/specs/<WP-id>.md` required when the issue already carries the spec?                | The plan wants one spec file per WP (`docs/specs/WP-*.md` in the layout, `<WP>.md` in the workflow). Issues already hold Goal, Scope, Given/When/Then and Verification (ADR-033). AGENTS.md and `/wp-spec` use `docs/specs/<WP-id>.md` (for example `M0-14.md`) and require it only when the issue lacks acceptance criteria or verification commands. | Maintainer picks: always, only when needed, or never (issue only). Update AGENTS.md section 5 and `/wp-spec`.         |
| OQ-53 | Branch names: `wi/<ID>-<slug>` or `wp/<WP>`?                                                | CONTRIBUTING.md says `wi/<ID>-<slug>`; the execution plan and its CLAUDE.md sketch say `wp/<WP>`. AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md follow CONTRIBUTING.md.                                                                                                                                                         | Maintainer confirms `wi/`, or amends CONTRIBUTING.md.                                                               |
| OQ-54 | Do the hook commands work from every shell Claude Code can use?                             | `.claude/settings.json` runs `node "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/tools/agent-hooks/<hook>.mjs"`, so a subfolder working directory does not break the path. The plan's form is the relative `node tools/agent-hooks/<hook>.mjs`. Hook commands run in Git Bash on Windows by default; a PowerShell hook shell was not tested. A hook that cannot start exits 1, which Claude Code treats as non-blocking, so a broken path would disable a guard silently. | Verified in real sessions on Windows, macOS and Linux (including a subfolder cwd); adjust the command form if not.    |
| OQ-55 | Should `post-edit` check module boundaries too?                                              | Outside `nx`, `@nx/enforce-module-boundaries` has no project graph and skips itself (the hook hides that warning). It only runs in the hook if `.nx/workspace-data` already holds a graph from an earlier `nx` command. `nx lint` and the Stop hook always check it.                                          | Measure the cost of building the graph per edit against the benefit; or accept the Stop hook as the check.          |
| OQ-56 | Reconcile the skills with the generators once they exist                                     | The skills `/new-action`, `/new-adapter`, `/new-ui-feature`, `/new-eslint-rule`, `/new-lint-rule`, `/new-decoder` and `/new-semantics-cell` quote the command forms of the execution plan (section 2.5). M0-08 (and M0-35 for `action`) may choose different flags or names.                                  | M0-08 and M0-35 land: update each SKILL.md and the table in AGENTS.md section 6 from the generators' `--help`.       |
| OQ-57 | Create the `security-lane` issue template?                                                   | Blueprint 14.3 says the triage issue uses a `security-lane` template; `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE` has none. `/security-lane` tells the agent to open a private advisory or a `type:security` issue, as the runbook says.                                                                                          | A template is added (and the skill and runbook point at it), or the blueprint wording is dropped.                    |

## CI (M0-05)

| ID    | Question                                                                                          | Context                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Closes when                                                                                                     |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-40 | Which fix for the OSV findings in the lockfile (form-data 4.0.4, postcss-selector-parser 6.1.4; undici and fast-uri were the 2026-10-25 findings)? | Build or docs tooling reached through nx and starlight. undici and fast-uri are now pinned by overrides (undici 7.30.1 and 8.12.2, fast-uri 3.1.9 and 4.2.3) and their ignores are gone. `osv-scanner.toml` still ignores form-data and postcss-selector-parser until 2026-11-14 so the scan is green. form-data has a patch fix (4.0.6); postcss-selector-parser needs a major bump (7.1.6) under `postcss-nested`. | Security lane (ADR-027): an override for form-data, an exact exclusion for postcss where needed, ADR log lines, then delete the two ignores. |
| OQ-41 | Enable GitHub Advanced Security, or wait until the repository is public?                           | CodeQL, dependency-review and the Scorecard SARIF upload need it on a private repository. Those jobs are skipped until the repository is public or the repository variable `GHAS_ENABLED` is `true`. A skipped required check counts as passing, so CodeQL protects nothing until then. | Maintainer enables GHAS and sets `GHAS_ENABLED=true`, or the repository goes public (ADR-029).                  |
| OQ-42 | Which checks become required, and under which names?                                              | Interfaces name `ci/gates`, `ci/affected`, `codeql`, `pr-title`, `dco`. Matrix jobs are named `gates (<os>)` and `toolchain (<os>)`; `affected`, `codeql`, `pr-title`, `dco` are single jobs. The maintainer pushes to main without PRs, so no branch protection exists yet.                  | Maintainer sets branch protection or a ruleset (consider an aggregate job if matrix names are awkward).         |
| OQ-43 | Install the Renovate GitHub App, or self-host Renovate?                                           | `renovate.json` is in place but nothing runs it until the app is installed on the repository. The config mirrors the release-age gate (3 days, strict) and ignores the supply-chain fixtures. Not validated by a live Renovate run.                                                           | Maintainer installs the app (or adds a self-hosted workflow); first dependency dashboard issue appears.          |
| OQ-44 | Is macOS only on push to main enough?                                                             | macOS runs the gates and the toolchain proof on pushes to main and manual runs; pull requests run Linux and Windows. This keeps macOS minutes down but a macOS-only break shows up after merge.                                                                                               | Maintainer reviews the cost against the risk, or adds a `ci:macos` label trigger.                               |
| OQ-45 | Adopt `uses: $/...` for local actions?                                                            | zizmor 1.31 recommends GitHub's new self-repository syntax (July 2026), but actionlint does not parse it yet, so the `self-repository` audit is disabled in `.github/zizmor.yml` and workflows use `./.github/actions/setup`.                                                                 | actionlint supports `$/`; then switch and re-enable the audit.                                                   |
| OQ-46 | Should the DCO check also cover squash-merge messages?                                            | `.github/scripts/dco-check.sh` checks every non-merge commit in the pushed or pull request range and exempts GitHub bot authors. A squash commit built from a PR keeps a trailer only if one is in the PR body.                                                                              | Decide when pull requests become routine; add the trailer to the PR template's squash body guidance.             |

| OQ-60 | What completes the `ui-feature` generator (gallery entry, Playwright spec with axe, Transloco scope)? | The execution plan lists them, but the dev gallery (web shell, M0-51), `apps/e2e-web` and Transloco do not exist yet. The generator makes the lazy route, a standalone component and an `i18n/en.json` scope, and its spec checks the route only. A component test needs Angular's unit-test builder or a JIT setup that no library has yet. | M0-51 and the e2e-web work item land; extend the generator and its snapshot, and add a component spec.                                       |
| OQ-61 | Who adopts the libraries that generators create on first use, and their stand-in types?   | `lint-rule`, `decoder`, `semantics-cell`, `conformance-cell` and `migration` create `libs/shared/lint-rules`, `libs/engine/decoders`, `libs/sdk/header-registry`, `libs/shared/semantics`, `apps/conformance` and `libs/engine/persistence` when missing, with local copies of the types from `core-interfaces.md` (`LintRule`, `DecoderPlugin`, `SemanticsCell`, `Migration`). `service-module` and `adapter` also use stand-ins for `EngineModule` and the port (the adapter switches to `@app/ports` by itself once the alias exists). | The owning work packages (lint-rules, decoders, semantics registry, persistence, kernel, ports) replace the stand-ins with `@app/contracts` types and update the generator templates and snapshots. |
| OQ-62 | The Nx CLI parses `--era=4.3` and `--since=4.3` as numbers. Is the workaround enough?     | The schemas accept string or number, so `4.3` works, but `4.10` arrives as `4.1`. Docs tell users to write `4.10.0`. Declaring the option as `string` does not help: Nx validates after the parse.                                                                                                                                  | Nx keeps option strings as typed, or the broker versions in use all survive the number round trip.                                           |
| OQ-63 | Is a 1 to 5 minute end-to-end proof inside `nx test generators` acceptable on three OSes?    | `generators:e2e` copies the policy files into a temporary workspace and runs `nx g`, `lint`, `typecheck` and `test` for every generator. About 80 seconds locally on Windows, about 4.5 minutes on the Windows CI runner. It reruns whenever its inputs (lint config, rules, generators, lockfile) change, in the CI `affected` job and in the three-OS toolchain job. | The CI time is reviewed; if it hurts, move `generators:e2e` out of `test` into a nightly or a Linux-only job.                                |
| OQ-64 | Should the existing hand-written vitest configs adopt `resolve.tsconfigPaths`?           | Generated configs set it so `@app/*` aliases resolve in tests (proven in the e2e with a service importing a pure library). `libs/shared/contracts` and `apps/engine` do not, which is fine until they import another workspace library.                                                                                              | The first cross-library import lands in a project created before M0-08; add the line there or generate the config from one template.        |
| OQ-65 | Should `--trust-critical` also add the project path to CODEOWNERS?                         | The flag only adds the `trust:critical` tag. CODEOWNERS (M0-07) lists trust-critical paths by hand, so a new `trust:critical` project is not protected until someone adds its path.                                                                                                                                                  | CODEOWNERS has a stable format; a generator (or `tools/arch-tests`) checks that every `trust:critical` project is covered.                  |

## Contracts (M0-10)

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- |
| OQ-70 | When does the oasdiff compatibility step run, and with which digest? | Blueprint 3.8 and ADR-003 want the digest-pinned oasdiff Go binary to compare the specs with the last release tag. No release tag exists yet (OQ-03, OQ-04), so there is nothing to compare with, and the digest must be chosen and verified by a maintainer. M0-10 ships the regenerate-and-diff gate only (`contracts:api-docs-check`, run in CI). | The first release tag exists; a maintainer picks the oasdiff release and its digest, then a CI step runs `oasdiff breaking` against the tag's `docs/api/openapi.v1.json`. |
| OQ-71 | Are the first registry entries right? | The registry holds one action, `meta.get` (`GET /meta`, permission `meta.read`), and no topics. It exists so the emitters, the drift gate and the docs API reference have real content. Everything else arrives with the action and topic generators (M0-35) and the service modules. | M0-35 and the first service module land; confirm or rename `meta.get`, and add the first topic. |
| OQ-72 | What else belongs in `config/*`? | The WP scope lists `config/*`, but no consumer exists yet. M0-10 ships the shared vocabularies only (`EnvClass`, `Risk`, `EngineMode`, `Qos`). Settings, runtime-config (`/runtime-config.json`) and connection schemas belong with the work items that read them. | The settings and connection work items define their schemas in `libs/shared/contracts/src/config/`. |
| OQ-73 | Which spec features are deliberately missing? | The emitters reject schemas that need `$ref` or `$defs` (recursive types) and types JSON Schema cannot express (`bigint`, `Date`); every operation has one `200` and a `default` Problem response; there is no security scheme, no per-status error mapping, and `info.version` is a fixed `1.0.0`. Frames carry `unknown` items, validated per topic. | The first action needs a recursive type, an auth scheme (team mode, ADR-019) or a per-status error table. |
| OQ-74 | Are the stricter kit rules wanted? | Beyond blueprint 3.1, `checkRegistry` also treats `peek` and `job` as mutating (audit `attempt+outcome`, as ADR-008 writes an attempt record), requires `key()` on `snapshot-delta` topics (delta frames remove by key), bounds `ring` and `maxHz`, and checks id formats. | Maintainer confirms, or names the rules to relax. |
| OQ-75 | Is a resolve hook the right way to run the emitter on TypeScript sources? | `tools/api-docs/register.mjs` maps `@app/contracts` for `node --import ...` (Node type stripping, no bundler). It needs `module.registerHooks` (Node 22.15 or later; the pin is 24.22) and the `.ts` import suffixes in the contracts sources. | The alternative (bundle with esbuild, or a `workspace:` package link) is judged better, for example when more tools need to import workspace libraries. |
| OQ-76 | Do the emitters belong in the browser bundle? | `@app/contracts` exports the emitters next to the schemas, so a deep import is the only way to keep `z.toJSONSchema` out of a UI bundle if tree shaking does not remove it (ADR-003 consequences: deep imports and a bundle budget). | The web shell (M0-51) imports `@app/contracts`; measure the bundle, then move the emitters behind a subpath export if needed. Measured in slice B of M0-51 (see OQ-690): `@app/contracts` itself is 28 kB raw in the initial bundle and the emitters are not in it; the weight is `zod`, which `@app/ui-platform` imports directly. |

## Domain model and semantics (M0-11)

`libs/shared/semantics` is now adopted by M0-11, so the stand-in types of the `semantics-cell` generator are replaced there (OQ-61 still applies to the other generator-created libraries). The rows below are judgement calls in the registry core.

| ID     | Question                                                                                               | Context                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Closes when                                                                                                          |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-100 | Where does `EraInputs.lastVerified` come from?                                                         | `resolveEra` takes the list of broker versions with a green conformance run as an input and the registry ships none: "verified" is a lab claim (I-14), so an agent cannot assert it. Until a source exists, a caller that passes an empty list gets `unverified-minor` for every version.                                                                                                                                                                                      | The evidence work (M0-15, M0-18) defines how the list is derived, for example from the recorded evidence artefacts.   |
| OQ-101 | Is the "last public patch" table in `src/era.ts` the right place for 4.2.10 to become an unknown build? | AC-1 wants 4.2.10 (no public tag) to resolve to `unknown-build`, but `EraInputs` only has a boolean-like `build`. `resolveEra` therefore knows the last open-source patch of each retired series (3.13.7, 4.0.9, 4.1.8, 4.2.9; fact sheet section a) and treats later patches as unknown builds whatever the probe says. The table is code, not data; it changes when a series loses community support.                                                                    | The profile builder sets `build` from a public-tag list, or the table moves to data. |
| OQ-102 | A commercial or unknown build below 4.3: keep the version-implied era or force 4.0-4.2?                | Blueprint 5.2 lists "unknown or commercial build gives 4.0-4.2" after "any node below 4.0 gives 3.13", and the code reads the list as ordered precedence: a commercial 3.13 build keeps 3.13 (conservative=true, unknown-build); on 4.3 and later the same evidence demotes to 4.0-4.2. Forcing 3.13 to 4.0-4.2 would apply rules that are likely wrong for it.                                                                                                              | Maintainer confirms or asks for the literal reading.                                                                  |
| OQ-103 | How is 4.4 resolved without the A-23 inputs?                                                           | `EraInputs` has no `rabbitmq_4.4.0` flag or Machine Version 9. `resolveEra` returns 4.4 when every node is 4.4 or later (a pre-release such as `4.4.0-alpha.x` counts) and the 4.3 evidence holds, and the build is never `verified`. `isDoomed` uses the 4.3 rule for 4.4 (ADR-028 says "4.3+").                                                                                                                                                                              | The 4.4 era work package (amendment A-23) adds the inputs and the 4.4 cells.                                          |
| OQ-104 | Are the gap rules wider than the interface?                                                            | `SemanticsGapError.key` is a cell key or `{ table, id }` (warning, gate, peek-caps), not only a cell key; `cell()` also treats a null effect (generator stubs) and a `lab-contradicted` status as a gap; `gate()` throws for a feature with no rule rather than returning a disabled decision; `isDoomed` treats an unknown limit and an unreadable counter as doomed and an absent counter header as 0.                                                                       | Maintainer confirms, or names the lookups that should return a value instead.                                        |
| OQ-107 | CODEOWNERS for the new paths.                                                                          | `libs/shared/semantics/` is already listed. `libs/shared/domain-model/` (carries no `trust:critical` tag; it holds the version parser the era resolver relies on) and `tools/semantics-audit/` are not. `CODEOWNERS` is protected, so the lines are proposed, not applied: `/libs/shared/domain-model/ @RafaelJCamara` and `/tools/semantics-audit/ @RafaelJCamara`.                                                                                                         | Maintainer applies the lines or declines.                                                                             |
| OQ-108 | Is `.ts` import suffixes plus a resolve hook still the right way to run `audit-cells`?                 | Same approach as OQ-75: the libraries import with `.ts` suffixes, and `tools/semantics-audit/register.mjs` maps every `@app/*` alias of `tsconfig.base.json` for `node --import`. The runner lives in `tools/` because the libraries are isomorphic and may not read the file system.                                                                                                                                                                                         | Decided together with OQ-75.                                                                                          |

## Semantics data II (M0-13)

Judgement calls in the hazard, capability, warning, peek-cap, tap-rule and provider-preset data of `libs/shared/semantics/src/data/`. Every value is read from the fact sheet and cited; none is lab-verified. OQ-105 and OQ-106 (the cap reading and the rule shapes) are settled by this data: the caps follow the reading, and the registry gained a `capabilities` table and a `providerPresets` table where a predicate did not fit.

| ID     | Question                                                                    | Context                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Closes when                                                                                                                  |
| ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-360 | What is the byte cap of a peek on a quorum queue before 4.3?                | The fact sheet gives 16 MiB for classic queues and for quorum queues on 4.3 and later, and no byte cap for the 3.13 and 4.0-4.2 quorum rows (20 / 50, administrators 200 on 4.0-4.2). `PeekCapRowSchema` needs a number, so those rows carry 16 MiB too. With 50 or 200 messages a 16 MiB cap binds only for large messages, and an unverified build gets the same cap through the conservative row.                                                                                                                                            | The maintainer names the figure (a smaller cap is the cautious choice) or confirms 16 MiB.                                  |
| OQ-361 | Should the non-cell tables carry a status and lab-evidence links?           | Only settle-matrix cells have `status`, `conformance[]` and `evidence[]` (I-14, three-way rule). Hazards, capabilities, peek caps, tap rules and presets have `citations[]` (hazards, capabilities, presets) or none (caps, tap rules) and no link to a `CF-*` lab cell, so no lab run can ever mark them verified, and the audit cannot require evidence for them. They are labelled `code-derived` by being in a data file that says so, not by a field.                                                                                          | A work package adds `conformance[]` and a status to these tables (and the lab cells they point at), or the maintainer decides the cell matrix is the only evidence the registry carries. |
| OQ-362 | Are capabilities a table of their own, and what are the gates for?          | The interface names "capability predicates (for example `channelsAcceptDisableStats`)". `gate(feature, profile)` is keyed by `FeatureId` and returns what the UI renders; a request-shape predicate for the management client is not a feature, so capabilities are their own table: `capability(id, profile)` answers true only when every node is proven inside the range (an unreadable version, `unknown` or a non-RabbitMQ product answers false) and throws for an unknown id. `gates` stays empty: no work package owns a feature gate yet. The ten capabilities are the three DC-32 names plus seven from fact sheet sections b.5, f and 0.2; adding one is a data change. | The gate owner (the profile builder or the first feature that needs a gate) fills `gates`; the maintainer confirms the capability list. |
| OQ-363 | Are the warning severities and the text placeholders right?                 | The fact sheet gives ids and, in prose, what the planner does for some (W-BUDGET and W-313 always confirm, W-OVERLIMIT blocks, W-EXCLUSIVE refuses, W-CRASH and W-STREAM are info). The rest are judgement: W-DELAY, W-DROPHEAD and W-SAC-QQ are `consent` ("confirm" in the matrix, and a peek that becomes the active consumer), W-TAIL, W-HOLD, W-TTL, W-SAC-CQ, W-UPGRADED and W-MIXED are `warn`, W-REDELIVERED is `info`. Some texts take a caller-built fragment (`{{disposition}}`, `{{consequence}}`, `{{action}}`, `{{fate}}`, `{{counterNote}}`) as in the semantics matrix wording; that is awkward to translate. Keys are `warnings.<id>.v<n>`, so a changed text is a new key and a receipt keeps the words it showed. | The peek planner (M1-12) fixes the placeholder set and the severities, or the texts become one key per variant.            |
| OQ-364 | Do the hazard ranges say more than the fact sheet?                          | Two rules go beyond its words, both towards reporting: `http-log-oauth-client-id` uses `<4.3.3` because the 4.2 backport was not merged, so every 4.2 build (4.2.10 and later too) is listed; `topic-empty-binding-key-leak-flag-off` (4.3.1 up to 4.4, feature flag known `disabled`) encodes "fixed in 4.3.1 plus feature flag". `stats-off-policy-fields-missing` starts at 3.13.0 because the table says "3.13 to 4.2.4". The 4.3 denials (transient non-exclusive declare, global QoS, CQv1 arguments) are behaviour with a permit switch, not rows of the table, so they are not hazards here. | The maintainer confirms the two rules, or asks for the denials as hazards (they need a configuration input the profile does not carry). |
| OQ-365 | Are the tap rules for the types the fact sheet does not list right?         | `direct`, `topic`, `fanout` and `headers` copy to every matching destination, so they do not divert and allow e2e (fact sheet c.1 lists only single-destination types). `x-recent-history` takes nothing but replays its cache into a new tap, so its e2e stays denied (a replay into an exchange would reach that exchange's consumers: a reading of the source, not a lab result). `x-consistent-hash` gets e2e from 4.3.6 (blueprint 5.7) but is still refused by default; modulus-hash, random and local-random never get e2e (fact sheet c.2, "refuse on hash/random types"). `x-delayed-message`, `x-jms-topic` and any other type are refused as unknown. | The maintainer confirms, or lab cells show a different answer for e2e on these types.                                       |
| OQ-366 | Are the provider presets' levels and limits right?                          | Where the matrix has two answers (Amazon MQ 3.13 against 4.2 and 4.3; "S/P", "B/U") the base is the weaker one and two Amazon MQ overrides raise it by version (tap below 4.0, Prometheus from 4.2). The Amazon MQ limits (10 consumers per channel, 512 KiB messages) are documented for 4.x clusters and applied to every version; connection limits are left out because they depend on the plan (100 to 500 per node). CloudAMQP shared takes the lowest plan (20 connections, 200 channels) and a tool budget of 2 (amendment A-10). Both CloudAMQP plans list the same host patterns. ApsaraMQ and `unknown` assume nothing (`probe`), ApsaraMQ's peek is `blocked` because its `basic.cancel` requeues delivered messages. | The probe (fact sheet j.2) replaces the guesses with measured limits; the maintainer confirms the levels.                    |
| OQ-367 | What do hazards and capabilities do with a build that is not `verified`?    | They read only the node versions and the stats mode, not `profile.verification`. A commercial 4.2.10 that carries a backported fix is still reported against the 4.2 rules (over-reporting) and does not earn a capability that starts at 4.3.6 (under-granting); the cap rule for unverified builds is in `peekCaps`. That keeps both lookups pure and in the safe direction, but a hazard the unknown build really fixed stays on the list. | The profile builder decides whether an unknown build should suppress hazards; until then the list errs towards showing them. |

## Semantics data I (M0-12)

Judgement calls in the settle matrix, broker-side counter events, dead-letter family and delayed-retry cells of `libs/shared/semantics/src/data/`. Every value is read from the fact sheet (b.1, b.2, b.4, b.5) and cited; none is lab-verified, and the lab (M0-38 to M0-40) may change any of it. The 137 cells are all `code-derived` with empty `evidence`.

| ID     | Question                                                                                       | Context                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Closes when                                                                                                                  |
| ------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-390 | How are the counter-visibility (b.1) and DLX carry-over (b.4) facts represented?               | A `SemanticsCell` can only say what a settle action does to AC, DC, the limit count and the position. It has no field for which header shows which counter per era (b.1), for header stamping, or for "the dead-letter target seeds its delivery count from the source" (b.4). The cells carry the counter deltas, the CF ids (CF-STAMP-01, CF-CARRY-01..03) and the citations; the carry-over rule itself lives in `isDoomed` and the future peek planner. | The maintainer asks for a typed `counters` / `carryover` table with its own conformance links, or confirms cells plus `isDoomed` are enough. |
| OQ-391 | What do `no_requeue`, `rejected` and `modified(df=false)` do to the counters on 3.13?          | The fact sheet gives "DLX `rejected` or drop" for 3.13 without counter changes (4.0-4.2 and 4.3 say AC+1, DC+1). The three 3.13 cells in `dlx-carryover.data.ts` use deltaAC 0 and deltaDC 0 ("nothing recorded"); the message leaves the queue, so the value only matters to a reader of the receipt.                                                                                                                     | CF-CARRY-01, CF-CARRY-02 and CF-SETTLE-06 are recorded on 3.13.7, or the maintainer names the values.                      |
| OQ-392 | Are the 4.3 broker events (close, TCP loss, partition, detach, consumer timeout) delayed under delayed retry? | The fact sheet does not say. The 4.3 cells of `counters.data.ts` exist for `delayedRetry: 'disabled'` only, so a lookup with `all`, `failed` or `returned` is a `SemanticsGapError` (the planner then applies the conservative rules). An AMQP 1.0 `modified` that carries `x-opt-delivery-time` is delayed in every mode; the cells describe a settlement without it.                                                      | CF-DOWN-* and CF-DR-* are recorded with delayed retry on 4.3.6, and the missing cells are added.                            |
| OQ-393 | Which cell values go beyond the words of the fact sheet?                                       | (1) The position of close, TCP loss and partition returns follows the requeue return path (head with a finite limit; tail on 4.0-4.2 and 3.13 without one; dropped on 3.13 with L=-1). (2) A `modified(df=true)` on 4.0-4.2 is read as the nack operation (AC+1, DC 0, head even for L=-1: "same requeue op"). (3) `modified(df=false)` is a non-failed return for delayed retry (`all`/`returned`). (4) A 4.3 consumer timeout, partition and detach use `if-dc-gt-limit` (every 4.3 return checks DC > L, b.4) where b.2 prints "no". (5) The 3.13 close family follows the 3.13 nack row. | The lab cells CF-DOWN-*, CF-SETTLE-04, CF-SETTLE-06 and CF-DR-01 record the real behaviour; a negative result changes the data. |
| OQ-394 | Which keys are intentionally gaps?                                                             | Only quorum queues and the eras 3.13, 4.0-4.2 and 4.3 have cells: classic queues (b.3, no limit, original position) and streams have none, nor has era 4.4 (no release, OQ-103). `limit: 'unset'` exists on 3.13 only (from 4.0 the broker reports an integer or "unlimited", read here as `minus1`); an in-place upgraded 3.13-era queue on 4.0-4.2 that stays unlimited is therefore a gap. 3.13 has no AMQP 1.0 detach row ("n/a"). A 4.3 cell always carries `delayedRetry`; earlier eras never do. | The classic-queue cells land (with b.3), or the maintainer decides which of these must answer instead of failing closed.   |
| OQ-395 | `SettleAction` has no value for several b.2 rows.                                              | Missing: `modified(uh=true)`, the HTTP `reject_requeue_true`, `reject_requeue_false` and `ack_requeue_false` outcomes (the first two are `basic.reject` through the management API, the last is an auto-ack: destructive removal). The enum is shared with the generators and `docs/architecture/core-interfaces.md`, so adding values is a coordinated change. The rows b.2 prints as one (for example `rejected`, `modified(uh=true)`, `nack(requeue=false)`) are covered by `amqp10.rejected` and `amqp091.no_requeue`. | The peek planner or the replay planner needs one of them; then the enum, the generators and the interface doc change together. |
| OQ-396 | Is one conformance id per action (18 ids) the right granularity?                               | Ids: CF-SETTLE-01..06 (one per requeue action), CF-CARRY-01..03, CF-DOWN-01..06, CF-DR-01..02, CF-STAMP-01. Evidence is per version (`testing/lab-evidence/<version>/<CF-id>.json`), so one id spans the eras. M0-38 to M0-40 may split or rename them; the registry audit and `apps/conformance/src/cells/registry-links.spec.ts` check that every cell id has a stub. The stubs follow the generator's naming (`cf-*.cell.spec.ts`), not the `cells/settle/*.cell.ts` paths of the work-package text. | M0-38 to M0-40 settle the ids and the file layout.                                                                          |
| OQ-397 | Are the source citations exact?                                                                | The fact sheet gives file basenames and line numbers per tag; the cells cite `RS@<tag>:deps/rabbit/src/<file>` (and `deps/rabbitmq_amqp1_0/src/` for the 3.13 plugin, `deps/rabbit/test/` for the suite). The directories come from the repository layout, not from the fact sheet, and `maybe_set_msg_delivery_count` is assumed to be in `rabbit_fifo.erl` at v4.3.6. Lines were not re-read against the tags.                  | A reviewer with the tags checked out confirms the paths and lines, or the lab evidence supersedes them.                    |
| OQ-398 | Should the new cells bump `SEMANTICS_VERSION`?                                                 | Receipts stamp `sem-2026.10.1`. M0-12 and M0-13 both changed the data without a bump, since nothing has shipped. After the first release a data change should change the version.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | The release process (or the first receipt consumer) defines when the version moves.                                          |
| OQ-399 | Who turns a broker profile and a preflight into a `CellKey`?                                   | Lookups are exact: the caller must pass `limit` (`finite` or `minus1`; `unset` only on 3.13) and, on 4.3, `delayedRetry`. That mapping (a `delivery_limit` of `unlimited` is `minus1`) belongs to the peek planner (M1-12); the registry deliberately has no fallback key.                                                                                                                                                  | The peek planner lands with a key builder and a test that every key it can build is a cell or an expected gap.              |
## Broker lab (M0-14)

| ID    | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ----- | -------- | ------- | ----------- |
| OQ-80 | Keep driving the lab containers through the `docker` CLI, or adopt the `testcontainers` npm package (12.2.1, blueprint 14.3)? | `libs/testkit` runs `docker build/create/cp/start/port/rm` and `docker compose` through `node:child_process`, behind the `Docker` function type in `src/lab/docker.ts`. Adopting `testcontainers` needs changes only a maintainer may make: an entry in `tools/supply-chain/deps-allowlist.json`, a catalog line, possibly `allowBuilds` for build scripts of its transitive `ssh2`/`cpu-features`, an OSV and licence check, and a confinement row `{ names: ['testcontainers', '@testcontainers/*'], only: ['libs/testkit'] }` in `eslint.config.mjs`. 12.2.1 was published 2026-10-13, so it clears the 3-day release-age gate from 2026-10-16 without an exclusion. What the CLI lacks: Testcontainers' Ryuk reaper (containers carry the label `io.broker-lab.run=<id>`, so a crashed run can be cleaned with `docker rm -f $(docker ps -aq --filter label=io.broker-lab.run)`) and its wait strategies (the helper polls `/api/overview` instead). The fact sheet notes the RabbitMQ module exposes neither 5552 nor 15692, so the generic container API would be used either way. | Maintainer decides; if adopted, only `docker.ts` and `broker-lab.ts` change. |
| OQ-81 | Where do the alpha images (4.4, 5.0, 4.3.7) come from? | The M0-14 goal mentions alphas; its scope and acceptance criteria list only the five released versions, which are on Docker Hub as `rabbitmq:<v>-management`. The alphas are built by `rabbitmq/server-packages` and are not official Docker Hub tags; they move nightly, so a digest pin goes stale. | The nightly workflow (M0-42) or the new-broker-release runbook picks a source and a pinning rule; `versions.json` gets an `alpha` entry type. |
| OQ-82 | Is a static `broker-lab:build` plus a separate `broker-lab:images` the right split? | `nx run-many -t build` runs on Windows and macOS runners and on machines without Docker, so `build` only validates `versions.json` and writes `dist/testing/broker-lab/images.json`. `images` runs `docker build`; `verify` builds first. | Maintainer confirms, or asks for `build` to build images when a daemon is reachable. |
| OQ-84 | Does the lab need AMQP 1.0 on 3.13.7? | One `enabled_plugins` file serves every version. On 3.13 AMQP 1.0 is the plugin `rabbitmq_amqp1_0`; from 4.0 it is core and the plugin no longer exists, so listing it would break 4.x. Nothing in M0-14 needs 3.13 AMQP 1.0. | A conformance cell needs AMQP 1.0 on 3.13; then add a per-version plugin file or a `--build-arg` for it. |
| OQ-85 | When does the OAuth variant get a Keycloak? | The variant starts the broker with the oauth2 backend, a per-run RSA public key and the internal backend as fallback; tests mint tokens with `signLabToken`. A lab-signed token is accepted on all five versions (basic login with the token as the password on `/api/whoami`). The management UI's own OAuth login (client id, provider URL) is not configured. Keycloak 26 is part of the team-server E2E (M3). | The team-server E2E work item decides whether the lab shares one Keycloak container. |
| OQ-86 | Add `testing/broker-lab/` and `libs/testkit/` to CODEOWNERS? | M0-14 carries `trust:critical` and `lane:evidence`; the lab images feed the evidence recorded under A-20. `CODEOWNERS` is a protected path, so the lines are proposed, not applied: `/testing/broker-lab/ @RafaelJCamara` and `/libs/testkit/ @RafaelJCamara`. The `broker-lab` project carries the `trust:critical` tag. | Maintainer adds the lines (see also OQ-65). |
| OQ-87 | Update the fact sheet with two 4.3.6 differences the lab found? | (1) `GET /api/extensions` on 4.3.6 returns `javascript` as a list for some entries (`["federation_management-ejs.js","federation.js"]`) and `[]` entries; up to 4.2.9 it is a single string. Code that matches `shovel.js` as a string misses the extension on 4.3. (2) 4.3.6 rejects `cluster_formation.randomized_startup_delay_range.min/max` ("no setting with that name"); 3.13.7 to 4.2.9 accept them. `rabbitmq.cluster.conf` therefore leaves them out. Both were observed on GitHub Actions runs of `broker-lab:verify`, not read from source. | A lab-verified cell or fact-sheet row records them (agents do not write evidence, I-14). |
| OQ-88 | Is a stream `PeerProperties` answer enough for "5552 responds", and what about the TLS stream port? | `verify` sends a `PeerProperties` frame and expects the OK response, which proves the stream listener speaks the protocol. It does not follow stream metadata to the advertised address, and the TLS variant's 5551 has no advertised TLS port configured (`streamTls` is returned without the advertised-port guarantee that `stream` has). | The first stream client test (M1) needs the metadata path; then verify it and set the TLS advertised port. |

## Plan graph (M0-77)

`pnpm nx run work-graph:validate` checks the feature catalogue and the work-item graph. Each row below is a finding it reports that is a judgement call, so it is listed in `tools/work-graph/known-exceptions.json` instead of being silently rewritten. Fixing the plan makes the exception stale and the run fails until the entry is removed.

| ID    | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ----- | -------- | ------- | ----------- |
| OQ-90 | PLAT-16 (Free web utilities, M4) depends on DIA-01 (Lint engine, M6). Which side moves? | The web-first re-tag left a dependency on a later milestone. The M4-F05 epic says the definitions linter and upgrade checker "follow in M4", but DIA-01 and the upgrade advisor (DIA-02) are M6 epics (M6-01, M6-02). The execution plan section 0 reconciliation (M2 ships the pure utilities, the linter and checker follow) was written before the re-tag. | PLAT-16 is split so only the x-death decoder, routing tester, policy tester and URI builder stay in M4, or PLAT-16 moves to M6, or the DIA-01 dependency is dropped. Then update `features.md` and M4-F05, and remove the exception. |
| OQ-91 | Five features are cited by no work item: PLAT-01, PLAT-02, PLAT-03, PLAT-06 (all M0) and PLAT-07 (M2 to M4). Add the citations or confirm they are umbrella features? | The validator maps a feature to a work item when its id appears in a title or body. The M0 foundation features are delivered by named WPs (the execution plan traceability table maps blueprint WP-01 to M0-01..05 and M0-08, WP-04 to M0-14, M0-15 and M0-37..42, WP-13 to M0-32..34) and PLAT-07 by the M2-P desktop WPs, but none of them names the feature id. | Each of the five is cited by at least one work item (title, body or issue) or an explicit mapping is agreed. Then remove the exceptions. |
| OQ-92 | M0-17 (Live protocol state machines, issue #16) has no `area:*` label. Which area? | Every seeded issue is meant to carry at least one area (plan section 7, `--verify`). M0-17 is a pure library under `libs/shared/live-protocol` used by the engine live hub (M0-33) and the UI client (M0-49). `area:engine` is the likely choice, but it is not obvious. | The area is chosen, added to `areas` and `labels` for M0-17 in `work-items.json` and to issue #16 on GitHub. Then remove the exception. |
| OQ-93 | U-11 and U-12 (issues #76 and #77) have the same title, "Lab/spike: Amazon MQ and CloudAMQP specifics". Rename them? | The seed script built both titles from the shared "Scheduled as" text in `docs/research/uncertainties.md`. U-11 is Amazon MQ and U-12 is CloudAMQP. Their bodies repeat the same uncertainty line too. | Both issues get distinct titles and bodies on GitHub, and `work-items.json` and `work-items.md` are updated to match. Then remove the exception. |

## Kernel (M0-16)

| ID     | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ------ | -------- | ------- | ----------- |
| OQ-110 | Are the Problem `type` URI and the statuses for the unlisted codes right? | Blueprint 3.6 lists statuses for most codes and says "as appropriate" for `TOKEN_EXPIRED`, `TOKEN_TOO_LARGE`, `EGRESS_DENIED`, `TLS_UNTRUSTED` and `INTERNAL`; `PLAN_EXPIRED`, `CONFIRMATION_MISMATCH` and `SEMANTICS_GAP` are in the contract but not in the table. `PROBLEM_STATUS` (libs/engine/kernel/src/problem) chooses 401, 413, 403, 502, 500, 409, 422 and 422 in that order. `type` is the URN `urn:problem:<code-in-kebab-case>` because the product domain is a placeholder (ADR-029); the proposals suggest `https://<product-domain>/problems/<code>`. `retryable` defaults to true only for `RATE_LIMITED`, `BROKER_RESOURCE_LOCKED`, `BROKER_UNREACHABLE` and `ENGINE_SHUTTING_DOWN`. | Maintainer confirms or edits the table; the domain is chosen (OQ-07) and `type` becomes a URL. |
| OQ-111 | Is the environment naming of `loadConfig` the wanted convention? | `PREFIX_SECTION__KEY` with UPPER_SNAKE words for a camelCase key (`APP_SERVER__PUBLIC_URL` sets `server.publicUrl`), and `..._FILE` to read a value from a file. The default prefix `APP` is a placeholder until the codename exists (OQ-07). Every variable that starts with `PREFIX_` is configuration: one that matches no key is an error (so typos fail loudly), which means a prefix must not be shared with other tools. If both `X` and `X_FILE` are set, `X` wins (the issue says env > `*_FILE`); some container conventions treat that as an error instead. Text is converted to numbers, booleans, arrays and objects (JSON) only where the schema asks for that type, and only for the environment and `*_FILE`; keys cannot contain digits-first words or consecutive capitals. | The codename is chosen; the team-server config (M3) and the Helm chart show whether this naming is enough. |
| OQ-112 | Is redacting the finished log line, and over-redacting by key name, acceptable? | `createLogger` parses each line pino wrote, masks values of keys that contain `password`, `secret`, `token`, `apikey`, `credential`, `authorization`, `cookie`, `sessionid` (and `x-` headers with `auth`, `key`, `jwt` or `session`), masks `body` and `payload` (bodies are never logged), and scrubs credentialed URIs, secret query parameters, JWTs and Bearer values in every string. pino 10.4.0 `redact` paths were not used because they cannot match by value or at any depth. Costs: one parse and one stringify per emitted line (to be measured by the I-5 gate), and false positives such as a field named `tokenCount`. Rotating file sinks (10 MB x 5) and the canary-secret E2E scan are not part of this work item. | The performance gate (I-5) measures log cost; the E2E canary scan runs; the desktop file sink is built. |
| OQ-113 | Are the bounded-buffer semantics right? | `BoundedRing.add` evicts the oldest items (drop-head) and `BoundedQueue.offer` refuses the new item and returns false; both count every drop in `dropped`/`droppedBytes` and report it to `onDrop`. An item larger than `maxBytes` is dropped itself and the contents stay. `clear()` counts the discarded items as dropped. Item size defaults to the UTF-8 length of strings or the byte length of binary data; other items need `sizeOf`. The methods are `add` and `offer`, not `push`, because `@app/bounded-buffers-only` rejects `.push` on anything named like a buffer. Live-protocol outboxes (M0-17) are a separate library and do not use these. | The live hub (M0-33) and the tap pipeline use the classes, or ask for a different overflow policy (for example "coalesce latest"). |
| OQ-114 | Who owns `yaml` and `pino` in the dependency policy? | `libs/engine/kernel` imports `yaml` (config file) and `pino` (logger). `pino` is new: catalog line, `deps-allowlist.json` entry and a confinement row (`pino`, `pino-*` only in `libs/engine/kernel`, I-11). 10.4.0 was published 2026-10-02, so it clears the release-age gate with no exclusion. `yaml` was already allowed for the supply-chain gates; its allow-list reason still says only that, and it has no confinement row (several tools import it). | A maintainer reviews the `pino` entry (see OQ-151) and decides whether `yaml` needs a reason update and a confinement row listing kernel and the tools. |
| OQ-115 | How is the 90 % line coverage of kernel measured? | AGENTS.md section 11 asks for at least 90 % lines on kernel. No coverage provider (`@vitest/coverage-v8`) is in the catalog, so the kernel tests (model and property tests for the buffers, ids, redaction and config precedence) could not be measured. | A coverage provider is added through the dependency policy and CI reports coverage per project. |
| OQ-116 | Should `AppError` and the status table live in `@app/contracts`? | M0-16 puts `AppError`, `toProblem` and `PROBLEM_STATUS` in the kernel (the interface says `toProblem(err)`). The HTTP client in the UI (the `problem` interceptor) and adapters (port layer) also need to build Problems and cannot import the kernel. Only the code list and the Problem schema are in contracts today. | M0-24 (management client) or the UI problem interceptor needs to throw or read a Problem; then move the pure parts to contracts. |
## Platform upgrade lane (M0-76)

| ID     | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ------ | -------- | ------- | ----------- |
| OQ-130 | One lane issue per platform and newest major, checked weekly: is that the right grain? | `platform-upgrade-lane.yml` runs on Mondays and opens one issue for the highest major that is at least 3 days old, so a skipped major (Angular 23 when 24 is out) gets no issue of its own. An issue counts as tracking whether it is open or closed, so closing one means "decided" and the lane never refiles it. The weekly cadence is far inside the runbook windows (4 weeks, one month). | Maintainer confirms the grain and cadence, or asks for one issue per major or a daily schedule. |
| OQ-131 | The first scheduled run will open a "Node 26" issue. Is that wanted now? | Node 24.22.0 is pinned, Node 26.12.0 is out and the runbook says to move "well before Node 24 reaches EOL (2028-04-30)". The issue lists the engines checks (all compatible today) and the checklist. Closing it without acting tells the lane the question is decided. TypeScript 7.0.2 is also out; its issue is deferred because the latest `@angular/compiler-cli` accepts only `>=6.0 <6.1`. | Maintainer lets the issue open and schedules it, or closes it as deferred. |
| OQ-132 | Renovate rules are configured but not exercised. | `renovate.json` now groups the platform packages, labels them `lane:platform` and puts their majors on the dependency dashboard for approval. It was validated with `renovate-config-validator --strict` (renovate 44.160.1, run from a scratch directory), not by a live run: the GitHub App is not installed (OQ-43). Whether the `nvm` manager reads `.nvmrc` and the `npm` manager reads `packageManager` as assumed is unconfirmed. | The app is installed and the first dashboard shows the platform groups, the `nvm` and `packageManager` updates and the major approvals. |
| OQ-133 | Which compatibility checks should block an issue? | Only one does: TypeScript waits until the latest `@angular/compiler-cli` accepts it (the runbook rule). Every other failed check, such as `@nx/angular` not yet accepting Angular 23, is listed in the issue and does not hold it back, because the lane exists to start the work. The checks read published `engines` and `peerDependencies`; they are a first filter, not the conformance or egress runs. | Maintainer names further blocking checks (for example Nx support before an Angular issue opens), or agrees. |
| OQ-134 | Electron and fastify are tracked from planned pins. | Neither is in the catalog yet, so `tools/platform-lane/platforms.json` carries `fallback` pins (Electron 44.8.0 from AGENTS.md, fastify 5.12.7 from ADR-004). The catalog wins as soon as the package is added. Until then an Electron 45 or fastify 6 release opens an issue even though no code uses them. | Electron (M2) and fastify are added to the catalog; delete the two `fallback` entries. |
| OQ-135 | Lane issues are outside the seeded work-item graph. | The issues carry `lane:platform` and `type:chore` but no `M0-nn` id, so `docs/plan/work-items.json` and `work-graph:validate` do not know them. ADR-033 (no work without an issue) is met by the issue itself. The workflow creates them with the repository `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so they are authored by `github-actions[bot]` and trigger no other workflow. | Maintainer decides whether lane issues need a milestone or wave label, or keeps them outside the graph. |

| ID     | Question | Context | Closes when |

M0-14 reported the allow-list and `allowBuilds` as hook-blocked and so avoided a new dependency. The two agents saw different behaviour. ## Live protocol (M0-17)

Decisions the blueprint leaves open, taken in `libs/shared/live-protocol` so the state machines could be built and model-tested. Each is easy to change there; the hub (M0-33) and the UI client (M0-49) must agree with them.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- |
| OQ-120 | What is the per-subscription credit ceiling, and what happens to a grant above it? | `MAX_CREDITS` is 1,048,576 per subscription. A `credit` that would exceed it is accepted up to the ceiling and the surplus is counted (`Credits.stats.refused`), not an error. The blueprint says credits are granted in rAF batches but gives no bound. | The UI client's grant policy (M0-49) is known and the ceiling is confirmed or replaced. |
| OQ-122 | What does `TopicDef.ring` bound, and how long do delivered lossless items stay for resume? | The library reads `ring {items, bytes}` as the bound of one subscription's outbox. A lossless outbox keeps delivered items inside the same bound (oldest evicted first, never a gap) so a resumed client can continue from `lastSeq`; if they were evicted the client gets `gap{resume-unavailable}` plus a snapshot. The 8 MiB session limit covers all outboxes together, delivered tails included. A separate, larger replay window per topic is possible. | A real lossless topic (`connections.status`, `alerts.events`) is sized and resume behaviour is measured. |
| OQ-123 | What does `seq` count? | Every item the producer offers to a subscription gets the next seq (1, 2, ...). A `data`, `delta` or `snapshot` frame carries the seq of the newest item it covers, a `gap` covers `fromSeq..toSeq` of offered items. For `latest` and `sampled` topics seqs therefore skip (coalesced or skipped items). The contract only says `seq` is a non-negative integer. | The UI client's gap detection (M0-49) is written against it. |
| OQ-124 | Which Problem codes and frames answer the protocol limits? | The 65th subscription and an oversized snapshot end the subscription with `end{reason: error}` and `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` (429 and 413); a subscription during `goaway` gets `ENGINE_SHUTTING_DOWN` (503). A second `hello`, a duplicate `sid` or a frame before `hello` is reported as a `violation` for the hub to close the transport. `gap{engine-restart}` is not produced here: a `hello{resume}` for an unknown or expired session gets a normal `welcome` with a new session id and the client subscribes again. | The error catalogue (blueprint 3.6) gets live-protocol codes, or the choices are confirmed. |
| OQ-125 | What if a snapshot does not fit in one frame? | A snapshot larger than `maxFrameBytes` (1 MiB) cannot be split, because `snapshot` has no continuation flag. The session ends that subscription with a Problem. `explorer.window` is windowed (I-7) so it should fit, but this is not proven. | The first snapshot-delta topic is built (M1) and its worst-case snapshot is measured, or the frame gains chunking. |
| OQ-126 | Is a gap needed for a `snapshot-delta` overflow, and is `sampleEvery` adaptive? | When the pending changes of a `snapshot-delta` topic overflow, the outbox sends a gap and then a snapshot, as for lossless topics, although the snapshot alone would heal the client. This keeps one rule for I-13. The first snapshot of a subscription has no gap. For `sampled` topics `sampleEvery` is fixed per subscription; deciding n from the tap rate is the hub's job and is not modelled. | The tap topics (M1) show whether the gap is noise and whether n must change at run time. |

## UI platform and data (M0-49)

Choices taken in `libs/ui/platform` and `libs/ui/data` where the blueprint is silent or the engine side does not exist yet. Each is easy to change in the library; the engine work packages named in the last column must agree.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- |
| OQ-264 | The `library` generator ties a project's directory to its name, and its Angular tsconfig cannot compile the shared libraries from source. | The issue and `eslint.config.mjs` need `libs/ui/platform` and `libs/ui/data` with the project names `ui-platform` and `ui-data`. The generator makes `libs/ui/ui-platform`, so the directories were moved by hand. Its Angular `tsconfig.json` also fails on `@app/contracts` and `@app/live-protocol` (`.ts` import extensions, `noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature`), so both libraries set `allowImportingTsExtensions: true` and `noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature: false`. `apps/web` and later UI libraries that import them need the same two options. | The generator takes a directory name apart from the project name and writes a tsconfig that builds against the shared libraries, and `apps/web` (M0-51) is checked. |
| OQ-191 | Where is the contract of a plan and of an execute result? | `EngineApi.plan` and `execute` call `POST /api/v1/actions/{actionId}/plans` and `POST /api/v1/plans/{id}/execute` (blueprint 3.2). `@app/contracts` has neither route nor schema, so ui-data defines `EnginePlan`, `PlanGate` and `ExecuteResult` by hand from the `Plan` interface in core-interfaces, and does not parse them. | The executor work package adds the plan and execute contracts, and ui-data uses their types and schemas. |
| OQ-192 | What does `/bootstrap.json` contain, and how does the launch token reach the page? | The boot reads `{engineVersion, apiVersion: 'v1', mode}` (the same facts as `meta.get`) and takes the local launch token from the URL fragment `#token=<16 to 512 chars of A-Za-z0-9_->`, keeps it in `sessionStorage` under `app.engine-token` and cleans the URL. The team-mode CSRF token has no source yet: `EngineCredentials.csrf` is only a slot. | The local-mode engine host serves the file and prints the URL, and the team login work package says where the CSRF token comes from. |
| OQ-194 | How does the desktop preload hand the engine port to the page? | `bootstrapPlatform` reads `window.__APP_PLATFORM__` as the `PlatformBridge` itself, calls `requestEnginePort()`, and accepts one `message` event from the same window and origin with data `{t: 'engine-port'}` and exactly one port. The origin check uses `location.origin`, which for the privileged `app://main` scheme may be `null` unless the scheme is registered as standard. The desktop reconnect after an engine restart is not built (M2). | The desktop preload (M2) is written against it, or the shape is replaced. |
| OQ-195 | Is the credit policy right: 16 frames in flight, granted back at the next animation frame? | `LiveClient` asks for `initialCredit` (16) per subscription and returns exactly the credit of the frames it delivered, once, in the next `requestAnimationFrame` (not after paint), one `credit` frame per subscription per frame. A page that is not drawing grants nothing, so the engine's outboxes fill and QoS drops. The window never exceeds the initial credit, so `MAX_CREDITS` (OQ-120) is never reached. 16 is not measured. | The tap and explorer topics (M1) are measured at their real rates, or a different policy is chosen. |
| OQ-197 | How are errors that are not Problems shown? | An answer with an error status and no Problem becomes an `EngineProblemError` flagged `synthetic`, with `INTERNAL` (or `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `NOT_FOUND`, `RATE_LIMITED` for 401, 404, 429). A live `end{reason: 'lease_expired' \| 'error'}` without a Problem becomes a synthetic `INTERNAL`, because no `ErrorCode` names a lease. A carrier failure is a `TransportError`, not a Problem. The correlation header is `x-request-id`, and the engine is assumed to echo it as `correlationId`. | The error catalogue (blueprint 3.6) gets codes for these, and the engine names the header. |
| OQ-198 | What does the web bridge do for save targets and external links? | `WebBridge.pickSaveTarget` returns `null` (a browser has no path to give the engine), so exports must reach the user as downloads; how is not designed. `openExternal` opens only `https:` links, with `noopener,noreferrer`, and has no confirmation step. | The export work package (M1) decides the download route, and the link policy of the web build is reviewed. |
| OQ-199 | The live client does not send `ping`. | A half-open WebSocket is only noticed when the browser closes it. The protocol has `ping` and `pong`, but their interval and the rule for declaring the engine gone are not specified. | A timeout is chosen (blueprint 3.5 or an ADR) and the client sends `ping` on it. |
| OQ-200 | `PortFrameSchema` uses `z.instanceof(ArrayBuffer)`. | It rejects a buffer from another realm (an iframe, and jsdom under Vitest, where structured clones come from the host realm). `MessagePortTransport` validates in dev and test builds only, and the specs that carry binary bodies turn validation off; the transport itself recognises buffers by tag. | `@app/contracts` checks buffers in a realm-independent way. |

## Persistence (M0-21)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/persistence` so the dialects, the runner and the first repositories could be built and tested. `0001_core` holds only `settings`, `connection_profiles` and `blobs`; every other table of blueprint 6.2 arrives with the work package that owns it, as a numbered migration. This work package adopts the library that the `migration` generator creates on first use (OQ-61 for `persistence`): the generator's stand-in `Migration` type now carries `dialect` and `t`, and `@app/persistence` is in the generator tests' list of adopted aliases.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- |
| OQ-160 | Which error code answers a database that is newer than the engine, and how does an adapter hand a Problem to the kernel? | `SchemaTooNewError.problem` is a valid contract `Problem` with `type: urn:problem:schema-too-new`, `code: INTERNAL` and status 500, because `ErrorCodeSchema` has no fitting code. An adapter may not import the kernel, so it cannot throw `AppError`, and `toProblem` keeps only an `AppError`, a ZodError or a plain Problem object, so a thrown `SchemaTooNewError` would become a bare `INTERNAL` with no detail unless the entry point reads `.problem`. | The error catalogue (blueprint 3.6) gets a code such as `SCHEMA_TOO_NEW` (a contracts change with the spec drift gate), and `toProblem` or the entry point (M0-42) maps `SchemaTooNewError`. |
| OQ-161 | Adopt `testcontainers` for the Postgres integration tests, or keep the `docker` CLI helper? | The issue names Testcontainers. `src/testing/postgres-container.ts` starts `postgres:16` and `postgres:18` through the `docker` CLI instead (as `libs/testkit` does, OQ-80), because installing `testcontainers` 12.2.1 fails under `strictDepBuilds`: `ERR_PNPM_IGNORED_BUILDS` for `cpu-features@0.0.10`, `protobufjs@7.6.6` and `ssh2@1.17.0`, and `allowBuilds` is a protected key. To adopt it a maintainer would add `testcontainers: 12.2.1` to the catalog, an allow-list entry, `cpu-features`, `protobufjs` and `ssh2` under `allowBuilds` (`false` to ignore their optional builds, or `true`), and a confinement row for `testcontainers` and `@testcontainers/*`. 12.2.1 was published 2026-10-13, so it needs no release-age exclusion. The CLI helper has no reaper: a crashed run leaves a container labelled `io.persistence-it=1`, removable with `docker ps -aq --filter label=io.persistence-it=1` piped to `docker rm -f`. | A maintainer decides; if adopted only `postgres-container.ts` changes. |
| OQ-162 | Is the `better-sqlite3` fallback needed, and which `node:sqlite` options does the dialect expose? | Blueprint 6.1 names `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 as the fallback behind the same interface and a `Database ?? DatabaseSync` detection. Only `node:sqlite` is implemented: it needs no native addon, it is present in Node 24.19 (the pinned 24.22 and the Electron 44 Node must be checked, spike S-03), and FTS5 is compiled in (SQLite 3.53.3, checked with `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE ... USING fts5`). `node:sqlite` is still flagged experimental, so it prints a warning on first use. `readBigInts` is an option and off by default, because repositories read numbers. Booleans are bound as 0 and 1. | S-03 runs inside Electron, or a platform lacks `node:sqlite`; then the fallback is added behind `openSqlite`. |
| OQ-163 | Where do the repository ports live, and which JSON shapes does each table hold? | `SettingsRepository`, `ConnectionProfileRepository` and `BlobRepository` are declared in `libs/engine/persistence/src/repos/ports.ts` because the `ports` library (M0-23) is being built in parallel. A service may not import an adapter, so the interfaces must move to `@app/ports` and the factories stay here, with the services getting them from the composition root. The `config` column of `connection_profiles` (endpoints, dial route, TLS, credential references, issuer pin, managed flags) is an opaque JSON document: `svc-connections` owns its schema, and a change to a stored shape is an expand/contract migration (A-18). | M0-23 lands and the port types move. |
| OQ-164 | Does the Postgres connection go through the Dialer and the TrustStore? | I-9 says every outbound socket goes through the Dialer, and `pg` opens its own socket (and its own TLS) to the team database. The database is the engine's own store, not a broker or an identity provider, and the Dialer is built for those, but the invariant has no exception for it. `openPg` takes a connection string and passes `ssl` through unchanged. | The Dialer (M0-26) is done and a maintainer rules on whether the store connection is exempt. |
| OQ-165 | What are the rules for desktop backups? | `migrate()` writes `data.db.v<NNNN>.<epochMs>.bak` next to the database (or into `backupDir`) before applying migrations to a populated database, and nothing prunes old copies. It copies `data.db` only: `capture.db` and `blobs/` (blueprint 6.1) are not covered.
