| OQ-317 | What bounds the jobs table?                                              | Sealed input, checkpoint and result are at most 1 MiB, a concurrency key 200 characters, `maxConcurrent` is 4 and every list is a window. Nothing caps the queued jobs of one principal (plans cap active plans per principal at 100), and `purgeFinishedBefore` exists but nothing calls it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | The retention sweeper (receipts, plans and jobs) is written, with its periods.                    |
| OQ-318 | Who reconciles plans stuck in `executing` and audit attempts with no outcome? | Blueprint 1.5 lists reconciliation at engine start. M0-31 reconciles only jobs (`recover`): OQ-224 and OQ-272 name M0-31 for the other two, but the issue does not, so they stay open, and the rows now point here. M0-36 reconciles jobs before ready and lists the other records of blueprint 1.5 as named steps that report `pending` (OQ-359); nothing reconciles plans or audit attempts yet.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The composition root (M0-36) or a later work package takes the reconciliation.                    |
| OQ-319 | How is a job running on another engine paused or cancelled?              | `command` on a job that is `running` but not in this runner is refused with 409, and `recover` runs at start and on `reopen` only. That is enough for one engine (desktop, local, team `replicaCount: 1`); the HA path (blueprint 1.3) needs a command channel through the store and a periodic reaper for expired leases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The HA work package (v2).                                                                         |
## Engine composition root (M0-36)

Decisions the issue and the blueprint leave open, taken in `apps/engine` (trust-critical). They wire pieces that landed earlier; the questions those pieces left (OQ-300, OQ-301, OQ-316, OQ-318) are updated above, not decided again.

| ID     | Question                                                                       | Context                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Closes when                                                                                                                       |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OQ-350 | Is a lock file the right data-directory lock?                                  | `engine.lock` holds `{pid, nonce}` and is created whole (a private temporary file hard-linked into place, which fails if the lock exists), so no engine reads a half-written lock. A lock whose process is gone is taken over; a live one, an unreadable one and a file system without hard links are refusals. Honest limits: two engines that find the same stale lock in the same instant can race on the takeover; a process id the system reused makes a dead engine's lock look alive until it is removed by hand. The data directory is created with mode 0700 when it is absent; the permissions of one that already exists are not checked. An exclusive lock held on a SQLite file would have neither limit but would put `node:sqlite` (confined to `persistence`, I-11) behind a lock API. | A field report of a stuck or shared lock, or a decision to hold the lock in `persistence`.                                         |
| OQ-351 | What unlocks the vault in local mode?                                          | Local mode lists `[file KEK (--kek-file), keyring, passphrase]` (OQ-300) and the keyring backend reports `none` (OQ-301), so without a KEK file the vault is `locked` (needs a passphrase) and nothing can type one yet: the engine is ready, `meta.get` works, and anything that must seal a plan or a job fails with `VAULT_LOCKED` (fail closed). The ready line says `vault: locked`. Desktop without a delivered key is the same.                                                                                                                                | An unlock action and its UI (the passphrase prompt), or the keyring binding (OQ-301).                                             |
| OQ-352 | What are `contract` and `schemaHash` in `ready`?                               | The issue gives the names only. `contract` is the sha256 of the OpenAPI document the registry emits (what a UI was built against); `schemaHash` is the sha256 of this engine's ordered migration ids (the schema version it migrated to), not of the DDL. Both are 64 hex characters, in the `ctl:ready` frame and the ready log line.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | The desktop main process or the web shell compares them (M2) and wants a different definition.                                    |
| OQ-353 | Are the command line and its exit codes right?                                 | Flags are `--mode` (required), `--port` (local; 0 = system pick), `--data-dir` (local; required), `--kek-file`, `--ui-dir`, `--log-level`, written `--name=value`; an unknown, repeated or malformed flag is a usage error that names the flag and never a value. No environment variables yet (blueprint 9 has `APP_*` for the container) and no `--token-file`, `--no-open`, `--exit-when-idle` or `--public-origin`. Exit codes follow sysexits: 64 usage, 75 data directory in use, 78 refused (team mode, newer schema, vault), 1 other. A failed start is one JSON line on stderr.       | The CLI (`apps/cli serve`, M1) fixes the user-facing flags, and container mode needs its environment layer.                       |
| OQ-354 | Why does the bundle contain its third-party packages, and where is it?         | The issue asks that the metafile list every bundled package, so `thirdParty` is on (it was off for the hello bundle): the bundle holds Fastify, ws, Kysely, pg, pino, zod and their dependencies (68 packages, all passing the shipped licence gate, no allow-list change). The optional native add-ons `ws` and `pg` probe for (`bufferutil`, `utf-8-validate`, `pg-native`) are aliased to a stub that throws `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, so the bundle has no external import and the JavaScript fallbacks run. A `createRequire` banner serves the CommonJS packages. The output is `dist/apps/engine/main.mjs` and `meta.json` (where the supply-chain gate already reads it), not `dist/engine/` as the blueprint says. | The release packaging (M2) decides the shipped layout, or a native add-on is wanted (it then needs a catalog entry, M0-02 rules). |
| OQ-355 | Where do `/healthz` and `/readyz` live, and what do they say?                  | In `compose-core.ts`, not in the transport kit (which is at its line cap, ADR-004): two public routes, `GET /healthz` answers `{status: "ok"}` while the process serves and `GET /readyz` answers 200 `{status: "ready"}` or 503 `{status: "starting" or "draining"}`. They sit behind the Host guard like the static UI (a rebinding page cannot read them) and say nothing else. Container mode (`0.0.0.0:8765`, blueprint 1.2) is not built: `composeEngine` refuses any listener outside loopback because authentication and TLS cannot be configured yet (DC-36).    | Container mode and the team listener (M3), or the transport kit takes the routes.                                                  |
| OQ-356 | What of desktop mode works before the MessagePort tunnel (M0-34)?              | The engine boots from `ctl:init` (data directory, delivered key), migrates, reconciles, posts `ready` and stops on `ctl:shutdown`; it has no listener. The carrier secret that will authenticate the tunnel is generated here and not handed to anyone, so until M0-34 no request authenticates (fail closed; `/healthz` answers in-process). `ctl:attach`, `suspend`, `resume`, `notify` and `save` are ignored, `init.settings` and `paths.logs` are unused, and a log sink other than standard output is not set up. | M0-34 (tunnel and carrier), then the supervisor work of M2.                                                                        |
| OQ-357 | What do actions get as write ports until the adapters land?                    | `apps/engine/src/unwired-writes.ts` refuses every management write and every connection lease with `CAPABILITY_MISSING` ("no broker adapter is wired"). The executor still audits the attempt. It is the place the management and AMQP adapters are plugged in.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | The management adapter and the connection manager land.                                                                            |
| OQ-358 | How does the engine know its version?                                          | A constant in `apps/engine/src/entry/version.ts` carrying the release-please marker, listed under `extra-files` of `release-please-config.json`; `entry.spec.ts` fails when it differs from the workspace version. Importing the root `package.json` was refused by the module-boundary lint. The first release shows whether the generic updater finds the marker.                                                                                                                                                                                                          | The first release-please PR (OQ-01 first) bumps both files.                                                                        |
| OQ-359 | Which reconciliation steps are stubs, and when does the job runner start?      | Real: jobs (`recover` before ready: running jobs become `paused-interrupted`). Stubs that report `pending`, and are named in the ready line: `plans-executing` and `audit-attempts` (OQ-318), `hold-journal`, `replay-journal`, `firehose-leases`, `tap-sweep` (the services that own them). The runner starts claiming after `ready`, so a resumed job (team) never runs before the engine reports ready; `recover` runs again inside `start` and finds nothing.                                                                                                              | Each owning work package replaces its stub; OQ-318 gets an owner.                                                                  |
## Action, topic and job generators (M0-35)

Decisions the issue leaves open, taken in `tools/generators/src/{action,topic,job}`, `libs/testkit/src/{create-test-engine,fakes}.ts` and `docs/golden-path/`. The generators are trust-critical: what they write is the first file of every action, so a gap in a template becomes a gap in every endpoint. Each is easy to change in the templates; the jobs library (M0-31), the live hub (M0-33) and the first real service modules must agree with them.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-320 | Where does the HTTP spec of a generated action live? | In `libs/testkit/src/http/<action>.http.spec.ts`. The issue asks for the spec "through `createTestEngine().inject`", but `createTestEngine` is in the `type:test` testkit and a `type:service` project may not import it (the boundary table lists no `type:test` for services), so a spec beside the action would fail lint. The cost: the testkit depends on every service (an affected service reruns its HTTP spec, which is wanted, and the testkit's own tests), and the spec reaches the action through the service's index (`create<Id>Action`, `create<X>FakeDeps`). The unit spec stays beside the action and uses the helpers of `@app/kernel`, so they stay public there; the testkit does not take them over. | A maintainer prefers a dedicated engine-spec project (`apps/e2e-engine`), or lets service specs import the testkit. |
| OQ-322 | Can a permission have a qualifier? | The generators accept `area.verb` only, because `createRoleAuthorizer` matches exactly two segments and refuses any other shape: a three-part permission could never be granted to a role (it fails closed). The catalogue of blueprint 7.3 lists `messages.peek.budgeted`. | The catalogue and the authorizer agree: flatten the names (`messages.peek-budgeted`), or teach `grantMatches` a third segment. |
| OQ-323 | What is the UI command stub, and does it need a feature? | Data only: `{ id, titleKey, action, planned }` in `libs/ui/feature-<name>/src/commands/`, with its title in the feature's `en.json` (the summary) and an export from the feature index. It registers nowhere, because the command registry is M1-41, and it needs the feature to exist (`--feature=none` skips it) because a generator that created a UI library as a side effect would hide a decision about routes. The title is the action's summary; the generator refuses summaries that call a read of messages non-destructive or read-only. | M1-41 defines the registry; the stub becomes a registration. |
| OQ-324 | Do generated services export fakes? | Every service exports `create<X>FakeDeps` from its index (the module's `src/testing.ts`), so the architecture manifest and the HTTP specs can build an action over the module's fakes. It is test support in a production index; an esbuild bundle drops it when nothing imports it, and the module's `src/deps.ts` and `src/testing.ts` are the two files a new port touches. | The composition root (M0-36) shows whether the export belongs in a `testing` entry point instead. |
| OQ-325 | Are the generator's refusals the right ones? | A mutation must state `--risk` and `--planning` (nothing is defaulted that could make it quieter); `high` and `critical` must be planned (the environment policy only raises planning); `--mcp=read` only for a query; `GET` only for a query; a path may use only the router's alphabet; an id, an endpoint and a file name must be free; a summary may not say "non-destructive" or "read-only". The default summary is the id in words, which reaches the API document until someone writes a real one. | The first real actions show which refusals get in the way, or whether `--summary` should be required. |
| OQ-326 | What keeps the generated samples honest? | The fixture's `sampleInput` and the manifest entry are made from the path parameters only. A contract with a required body field needs both edited by hand: the unit spec's first test parses the sample against the contract, and the plan-purity test of the architecture suite runs `plan()` with the manifest input, so a stale sample fails, but with a schema error rather than a pointer to the fix. | A generator pass that regenerates samples from the schema, or the manifest reads `sampleInput` from the fixture. |
| OQ-327 | Which generator and toolchain gaps did this work close? | The `library` generator now writes `noEmit` and `allowImportingTsExtensions` into every tsconfig (and turns `noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature` off for Angular libraries, as `ui-data` does), so a service can compile the kernel and the contracts from source (this closes the generator half of OQ-264, and the gap the live hub work (M0-33) hand-fixed in `libs/engine/live`). The testkit's tsconfig dropped `module: nodenext` so that it compiles the kernel, and the kernel's `Slots` lost a constructor parameter property so that the testkit's `erasableSyntaxOnly` holds (the compile half of OQ-305). | - |
| OQ-328 | What does the golden-path proof cost? | `generators:e2e` gained a second suite that copies the whole product into a temporary workspace (the router, the testkit, the architecture tests and the API emitter are real), runs the generators, shows the generated tests fail, applies the golden-path pages and runs lint, typecheck and test on every touched project. It adds several minutes to `nx test generators`. | CI time matters: cache the copy, or run the suite on changes under `tools/generators`, `libs/shared/contracts`, `libs/engine/kernel`, `libs/engine/transport` and `libs/testkit` only. |
| OQ-329 | What does a `job` action do, now that the jobs library exists? | The action generator makes the contract and the stub (`kind: 'job'` answers `202 {jobId}`; the stub's `execute` fails like any other). Starting a job means calling `JobRunner.submit` with the plan's digest and the idempotency key, as a port of the module; no golden path shows that pair yet. The job generator uses `defineJob` of `@app/jobs` and a spec context whose write ports throw. | A fourth golden path (`queues.purge-many`) shows the pair: the job and the action that starts it. |

## Design system (M0-50)

Decisions the issue and the blueprint leave open, taken in `libs/ui/design`, `apps/web` (gallery route, build `define`, global styles) and `tools/web-gallery-check`. The library is trust-critical.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-340 | Which M0-50 scope items wait for packages that are not in the catalog? | The issue scopes Spartan 1.7.0 helm copies with the MIT notice, `aria/` wrappers over `@angular/aria`, a Tailwind preset, CDK and Lucide. None of `@spartan-ng/brain`, `@angular/aria`, `@angular/cdk`, `tailwindcss`, `@lucide/angular` (nor Spartan's peers `clsx`, `luxon`, `tw-animate-css`) is in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` or `tools/supply-chain/deps-allowlist.json`, and adding them is a maintainer step (allow-list, 3-day release-age gate, CODEOWNERS). Without them nothing can be compiled or verified, so this work package ships the tokens, the three components, the ThemeService and the gallery, and `tailwind-preset.css` as an unverified file whose consistency with `tokens.css` is tested. The helm copies, the `aria/` wrappers and a compiled Tailwind build are not delivered. | A maintainer adds the packages (the report lists the exact catalog and allow-list entries); then the helm copies, the aria wrappers and Tailwind wiring land as their own work item, and `tailwind-preset.css` is compiled once. |
| OQ-341 | Why does `ui-design` run its tests in two runners, and which tsconfig options did it need? | Components use signal inputs, which Vitest's JIT cannot see (`NG0950`), so `test-components` runs them through `@angular/build:unit-test` (AOT, with `buildTarget: web:build:development` because a library has no build). The token specs read `tokens.css` as text (`?raw`, which the Angular bundler does not return as a string and `node:fs` is banned in browser code), so they run in plain Vitest. `nx test ui-design` runs both. The AOT build emits, so `allowImportingTsExtensions` needs `rewriteRelativeImportExtensions` next to `noEmit`; `apps/web` also gained `allowImportingTsExtensions` and `noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature: false` because it now compiles `@app/contracts` from source (as OQ-264 did for `ui-platform`). | The `ui-feature` and `library` generators emit the Angular unit-test target and the extra option, or the workspace picks one runner for components. |
| OQ-342 | Is the `@gallery` Playwright and axe check missing on purpose? | `pnpm nx e2e e2e-web --grep=@gallery` cannot run: `apps/e2e-web` is M0-60 and Playwright and axe-core are not in the catalog. The second acceptance criterion is proved by `<app-gated>` component tests (focusability, tab order, `aria-describedby`, live region, link attributes, source filtering), `gallery.spec.ts`, and `pnpm nx run web-gallery-check:gallery-absent`, which builds `web` for production and for development and scans both. There is no automated axe run or visual check yet. | M0-60 lands and adds the `@gallery` spec with axe on the route (and the high-contrast and compact variants). |
| OQ-343 | Are the palette, the single high-contrast theme and the compact target size right? | Chosen here: colours reach 4.5:1 (light and dark) and 7:1 (high contrast), borders and the focus ring 3:1 (7:1 in high contrast); one high-contrast theme, on a black ground; comfortable targets 28 px and rows 32 px; compact rows 24 px (the blueprint says 22 to 24), type 12 px and targets 24 px, so compact still meets WCAG 2.2 2.5.8. Without JavaScript or before `ThemeService` runs the light theme shows (there is no `prefers-color-scheme` CSS fallback, to keep one copy of each palette), so a dark system flashes light for a frame; `index.html` could set `data-theme` early. | A design review picks the palette and the type scale, or the blueprint is amended; a `prefers-contrast: more` light high-contrast variant is wanted. |
| OQ-344 | Who translates the words of the safety components? | `<app-gated>`, `<app-env-chrome>` and `<app-verification-banner>` take their words as required inputs (`labels`, `detail`), because `ui-design` may not import Transloco (not installed; M0-51) and a default English string in TypeScript would bypass the i18n review and the honest-wording lint. Every caller therefore passes translated labels; a missing environment or verification label falls back to the class or state id, never to blank, and a gate without a reason shows `labels.noReason`. Gate reasons and sources come from the engine in English. | M0-51 adds the Transloco keys and a thin wrapper (or a `provideDesignLabels()` token) so features stop passing labels by hand; the engine localises gate reasons or sends keys. |
| OQ-345 | What is `<app-gated>` not guarding? | Only clicks on the button are stopped while a gate is closed; a caller that listens to `keydown`, `pointerup` or another event on `<app-gated>` itself is not guarded, and `(activated)` is the supported hook. The `fix` action of a `GateDecision` is not rendered (the feature owns the remedy through `EngineApi`). The reason is always visible inline, with no tooltip mode, so a dense toolbar pays for it in width. The source link opens with `target="_blank"`: the web shell lets the browser open it, and the desktop shell must send it through `setWindowOpenHandler` to the https allow-list with confirmation (AGENTS.md 12), which does not exist yet. | The first feature that gates a toolbar (M0-52) shows whether a compact mode is needed; the desktop main process (M2) handles `window.open`; a `fix` affordance is specified. |
| OQ-346 | Should a missing profile show the strongest verification banner? | `normalizeVerification(undefined)` is `unknown-build`, so a page that renders `<app-verification-banner>` before the broker profile has loaded shows a red "unknown build" banner for a moment. That is the fail-closed reading (the component never hides because data is absent), at the price of a flash; the alternative is to render nothing until the profile exists, which makes the component fail open. Callers should not mount it before the profile loads. | M0-51 or M0-52 mounts the banner from the profile store and shows a neutral loading state instead. |
| OQ-347 | Why is `inlineCritical` off in the production build of `web`? | The first global CSS made the Angular production build inline critical CSS: an un-nonced `<style>` in `index.html`, the stylesheet switched to `media="print"`, and an inline `<script>` that flips it back. Under the shell's CSP (`style-src 'self' 'nonce-N'`, `script-src 'self'`, S-04 step 6) the script is blocked, so the page would stay unstyled. `apps/web/project.json` therefore sets `optimization.styles.inlineCritical: false` for production. `ngCspNonce` on the root element (S-04 step 6) is still not set: component styles would be blocked by the same CSP. | M0-51 (shell) or the transport work adds `ngCspNonce` and an end-to-end check that the served UI renders under the real CSP. |
| OQ-348 | Where does the theme choice live? | `ThemeService` remembers the theme and density in `localStorage` per browser origin (`app.theme`, `app.density`), unreadable by the engine and not shared between devices or desktop and web. Blueprint 10 persists layout per profile through `svc-workspace`; theme and density are user settings, not profile ones, but no settings contract exists yet. A throwing or missing `localStorage` is tolerated (the choice then lasts for the session). | `svc-workspace` settings (M0-47) define a user-settings document and `THEME_STORAGE` is backed by it. |
| OQ-349 | Does the production gallery check cover enough? | `web-gallery-check` looks for two strings (the gallery selector and title) and for file names containing "gallery" in the production output, with the development output as a control. A gallery component renamed without updating `MARKERS` fails the control; a second dev-only route added later is not covered unless its markers are added. The route is also behind `__DEV_GALLERY__`, which is false when a build has neither configuration or no `define`. | A second dev-only surface appears, or the build gets an assertion that dev-only chunks carry a marker file. |

## ConnectionManager (M0-43)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/svc-connections/src/internal/connection-manager` (role pools and budgets, A-10) and in `libs/engine/ports` (the lease API and the connector seam). Each is easy to change there.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-370 | Is `consumers_per_channel` the right cap for concurrent `observe` leases? | The issue says that with `consumersPerChannelMax=10` the 11th observe request queues. The adapter gives each observe lease its own connection, so the real limit that matters is the tool's connection cap; the manager nevertheless holds the number of taps to what one channel may carry, because a tap is one consumer and a later adapter may share channels. Without a stated limit, observe defaults to 8 per vhost. | The tap work (M1) shows whether taps share channels; then keep the rule or key it on channels. |
| OQ-371 | A-10 says "lazy role connections with idle close". What does idle close mean while a lease closes its own connection? | The adapter closes the connection when a lease is released, and a lease is single use, so no connection is ever idle and kept warm: connections are lazy (opened on the call) and are gone at release. There is no idle timer and no warm pool. Reusing a connection for the next job would need a lease type that survives `release`, which is an adapter and port change (trust-critical). | A measured cost of reconnecting (handshake time on the provider presets) justifies a reusable connection with an idle timer, or the maintainer accepts "close at release" as A-10. |
| OQ-372 | Are the changes to the `ConnectionManager` port right? | The critique asks for the vhost in the lease APIs and budget keys, so `peek`, `source`, `publish`, `observe` and `budget` take a `vhost` after the connection id. `RoleBudget` gains `vhost` and `etaMs` (the ETA the issue asks `budget()` to report); `usage(connectionId)` is new, for "the tool uses X of Y connections". `libs/engine/ports` is trust-critical, so the port change needs the CODEOWNERS approval. | A maintainer approves the port change; `core-interfaces.md` is updated to match. |
| OQ-373 | Are the default caps right? | Not in the blueprint, so chosen: the tool takes a quarter of the broker's `connection_max` (at least 2, at most 12, 2 at 10 and 3 at 12), 12 when the limit is unknown, and never more than `connection_max`; per vhost, peek 2, source 2, publish 2, observe 8. A preset overrides all of them (`BrokerBudgetInput`). The provider presets (M2) will carry the real numbers. | The presets land and the capability card (M0-45, M0-46) shows the figures in use. |
| OQ-374 | Are the queue bounds and the ETA model right? | A broker queues at most 64 waiting calls and a call waits at most 120 s, then fails with `RATE_LIMITED` (429) and the estimate in its text (`Problem` has no retry-after field). The ETA is the time until the soonest holder reaches the average hold time of its role (a smoothed average of past holds, 30 s until there is one), counting only holders of the role's own budget when that is what is spent, otherwise every holder. | Real peek and replay holds are measured (M1). |
| OQ-375 | Is "fewest connections in use first, then first come" the fairness the team pools need? | Blueprint 5.3 says "fair queueing" without a discipline. The manager keys the count by `principal.id` across the whole broker; connections themselves are not shared between principals (a lease is one connection of one holder), so the pool key (user, broker, vhost, protocol, role) of the blueprint is a budget key here, not a sharing key. Weights per role or per team are not modelled. | Team mode (M5) shows starvation or a need for weights. |
| OQ-376 | Is exempting `internal/connection-manager/` from the mutation funnel right? | The manager names `ConnectionManager` and the four lease types, so `checkMutationFunnel` (I-2) would fail it, and it is no action or job. It is plumbing like `jobs/src/runner.ts`: it hands leases to the executor's context and to nothing else. `FUNNEL_EXEMPT_PREFIXES` gains that directory; the rest of `svc-connections` is checked like any service. | A reviewer accepts the exemption, or the manager moves under `libs/engine/kernel` (it would then need the `Amqp091Connector` port only). |
| OQ-377 | Where do the limits come from, and why is there no `control` budget? | `BudgetSource` (in `svc-connections`) is filled by the composition root from `BrokerProfile.limits` and the provider preset; `refreshBudget` reads it before the first lease, and every lease call reads it again. The `Amqp091Connector` port (in `@app/ports`) hides the endpoint, credentials, trust and route, so a service never imports the adapter. The `control` role is the HTTP pool and has no lease in this API, so the budgets report the four lease roles. | M0-44 (profiles) and M0-45 (probe) delivered the data; wiring both at the composition root is OQ-472. |
| OQ-378 | Do AMQP connections go through the BrokerScheduler (I-8)? | No: the scheduler is the only path to `MgmtHttpPort` and `PromScrapePort` (invariant I-8), and a connection is budgeted by count here. A peek that is waiting for a connection is not "background load" in the scheduler's sense. | A load model shows AMQP work competing with management reads for the same broker. |
| OQ-379 | What stops a lease that is never released from holding its slot? | Nothing but the connection ending: the slot is freed when a release succeeds or the connection reports `closed`, never on an abort after the grant (a peek that holds deliveries must end in nack + barrier, I-4). A holder that leaks a lease keeps one slot until the connection dies (heartbeat 30 s). The jobs runner (M0-31) releases in `finally` and on drain. | A watchdog on hold time is wanted, or the job runner proves it releases on every path. |
## Workspace (M0-47)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/svc-workspace`, the `WorkspaceRepository` port (`@app/ports`), `libs/engine/persistence` (migration `0006_workspace`, `repos/workspace.ts`) and `libs/shared/contracts/src/registry/workspace.ts`. The service stores what the UI remembers for a person; none of it is a broker fact, so none of it is trust-critical.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-380 | Does the prod lock apply to a command that has no connection? | The router sends no environment class, so every call is `prod` (OQ-283) and the guardrail policy locks a mutating action until the person arms the connection in its target. `views.create`, `views.delete`, `settings.put` and `keybindings.put` change only the caller's own UI state and name no connection, so there is nothing to arm: through the real router they answer 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` (`prod-lock`, a gate with no fix) in every environment, and only the executor with `env: 'dev'` runs them. `layouts.put` works because its target is the profile (`engine.arm('c1')`). The HTTP specs of the four pin the 422 and say so; the unit specs prove the behaviour with `env: 'dev'`. This work package does not touch the guardrails (kernel, trust-critical). Options: exempt a mutating action whose risk is `none` and whose target has no connection; or add a declared `scope: 'local'` to the contract and have `checkRegistry` insist it has no broker port; or resolve the class per target (OQ-283) and treat "no target" as `dev`. Update (OQ-783): the maintainer chose the declared-scope option. A contract may declare `scope: 'local'`; a mutating action with that declaration and no target is no longer locked. `connections.create`, `bundles.import`, `views.create`, `views.delete`, `settings.put` and `keybindings.put` declare it. A targetless action without it stays 422 `GATE_BLOCKED`. | A maintainer rules on the guardrail rule (with OQ-283); the four HTTP specs then expect the 200 of the golden path. |
| OQ-381 | What is "profile" in "layouts are stored per profile", and who owns a row? | Read as the connection profile (`cid`, the key of `connection_profiles`): the IDE layout (open tabs, panel sizes) differs per connection, and saved views are the part that is not bound to one. Every row also carries the calling principal's id (`owner_id`, and the scope `workspace.<kind>:<owner>` for documents), so in the team server one person's workspace is not another's (blueprint 12: views and captures are per user). A person who uses the UI, the CLI with an API token and an MCP client therefore has three workspaces, because each is a different principal; a delegate (a job for a person) does not read the person's. `layouts.put` does not check that the profile exists (the service may not import `svc-connections`), so a layout can name a deleted profile; the per-person cap keeps that bounded. | `svc-connections` (M0-44) can delete a profile's layouts, or a person-level identity (one id across UI, CLI and API token) exists. |
| OQ-382 | Which permissions guard the workspace? | The catalogue of blueprint 7.3 has none for a person's own UI state, and `settings.admin` is governance. The actions use `workspace.read` and `workspace.write` (the two-segment `area.verb` shape the role authorizer matches). Every role should hold both, so the role table of `svc-access` (M3) needs `workspace.*` for viewer, developer, operator, admin and auditor. | The catalogue is amended (an ADR or blueprint 7.3 edit) and the role table grants it. |
| OQ-383 | Is an audit attempt and outcome for every layout save too much? | The kit invariant `mutating-audit` makes every non-query action audit `attempt+outcome`. A UI that saves the layout on every resize or tab move would write two chained audit rows (and a receipt) each time. The service cannot lessen it: calling `layouts.put` a query would be the escape the executor refuses (`isMutatingAction`). The UI must debounce (save on idle or on close), and the audit retention for `layouts.put` and `settings.put` should be short. | The UI feature that saves layouts (M0-5x) measures it, or the audit gets a class of "local state" actions that are recorded in the outcome row only. |
| OQ-384 | Which settings keys exist, and which caps bound the workspace? | Starting set, chosen here: `locale` (BCP 47 shape), `timeDisplay` (`local` or `utc`) and `defaultPageSize` (10 to 200). The theme and the density are not settings (OQ-348 stands: they stay in `localStorage`). A write names the keys to change and the others keep their value (`PUT` with merge, one document per key); there is no way to reset a key to its default except writing the default. Caps: 500 views, 200 layouts and 500 keybinding overrides per person; a layout is at most 65,536 and a view definition at most 16,384 characters of JSON (UTF-16 code units, because the contracts library has no byte counter); `views.list` returns at most 200 and defaults to 100. Past a cap the answer is 429 `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`. A stored value that no longer fits its schema, or a key the engine no longer knows, is ignored on read (defaults apply), never returned. | Features need other keys (add them to `WorkspaceSettingsSchema`), or the caps prove wrong in use. |
| OQ-385 | Who can see a saved view, and may two views share a name? | Views are private to their owner: the blueprint says sharing is explicit and no sharing action exists yet, so the team case ("saved views can be private or shared") is not served. Names are not unique: an id (a ULID from `newId`) is the identity, so two views may share a name and kind. `definition` is opaque JSON the grid owns (filters, columns, sort); a view saved by a UI version that a later one cannot read is the UI's to migrate. | Shared views are specified (a `visibility` column and a share action), or the explorer UI (M0-54) wants unique names. |
| OQ-386 | Why do the layout and view documents say `{}` in the API document? | Their values are `z.unknown()`, with a size check: the spec emitter refuses recursive schemas (`z.json()` is one), and the engine never reads these documents, so a JSON shape would only add a promise. The OpenAPI document therefore describes them as free-form objects. Layouts, settings and keybindings are stored in the existing `settings` table (scope per person and kind) rather than in tables of their own, as its comment in `repos/ports.ts` intended; only `views` needed a table. | The emitter supports recursive schemas, or the UI fixes a layout shape worth publishing. |

## Connection profiles and bundles (M0-44)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/svc-connections` (profiles, URI parsing, separate HTTP and AMQP tests, bundles), the ports in `libs/engine/ports/src/connections.ts`, `libs/engine/persistence` (migration `0007_credentials`, `repos/connections.ts`) and `libs/shared/contracts/src/registry/connections*.ts`. Not trust-critical, but it handles credentials: secrets are write-only and sealed with `@app/secrets` (I-10), and the service imports no adapter and opens no socket (I-9).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-440 | Whose credentials does a pasted URI carry, and what do a missing port, a missing path and a missing TLS flag mean? | The user and password of an `amqp(s)://` or `http(s)://` URI are stored as the AMQP credential and, unless `http.auth` is sent, as the HTTP credential too (basic): a pasted string is one login, and a management user is usually the same account. They are two stored secrets afterwards, changed separately. From an `amqp(s)` URI the management port is derived (15672 plain, 15671 TLS) and the AMQP port is the URI's or 5672 / 5671; from an `http(s)` URL the management port is the URL's or 80 / 443 (a managed broker behind a proxy), the AMQP port 5672 / 5671 and AMQP TLS follows the scheme. No path, `/` and `/%2F` are the default vhost `/` (the AMQP URI text would make `amqp://host/` the empty vhost, which no client uses); a path with a second unescaped `/` is refused. With no URI and no `tls` flag an endpoint is plain. The management port is the one thing a URI cannot say when the broker maps it elsewhere: `http.port` overrides it, and `connections.test-http` reports the AMQP ports of the broker's `/api/overview` listeners (`internal/listeners.ts`) for the AMQP form. | A maintainer confirms the reading, or asks for HTTP credentials to be entered always. |
| OQ-441 | Which work package wires svc-connections into the composition root, and where does the real `ConnectionProber` live? | The module takes four ports (`ConnectionStore`, `FieldSealer`, `ConnectionProber`, `newId`). `repos.connections` (persistence) and `vault.fields` (secrets) satisfy the first two as they are. The prober over the management HTTP adapter and the AMQP control connection is `libs/testkit/src/it/connection-prober.ts`, used by `pnpm nx run svc-connections:it`: a service may not import an adapter, and wiring a new module into `apps/engine` (with the `MgmtTargetResolver` of OQ-415 and the diagnostics `profiles` hook, which still returns `[]`) is composition-root work outside this package's scope, so `connections.*` and `bundles.*` are not registered in the running engine yet. Two adapter findings from the integration run: amqplib reports a refused vhost (530 on `connection.open`) only as `Expected ConnectionOpenOk; got <ConnectionClose>` in the cause, so the adapter's `Amqp091Error` is `broker-error` with no `broker.code`, and the prober reads the cause text; and the management client reports an AMQP port answering an HTTP request as `network`. | A work package registers the module in `coreModules`, moves the prober to `apps/engine`, wires the resolver (OQ-415), and the adapter maps the 530 close. Update: issue #278 registered the module, moved the prober to `apps/engine/src/broker` and wired the resolver (OQ-764 to OQ-771); the adapter still does not map the 530 close, and the prober still reads the cause text. |
| OQ-442 | Which permissions guard connections and bundles? | Blueprint 7.3 lists `connection.view` (read) and `catalog.admin` (curating the team catalog). Used: `connection.view` for `connections.list` and `connections.get`; `connection.manage` for create, update, delete, both tests and `bundles.import`; `connection.export` for `bundles.export`. Testing a profile dials out with its credential and import creates profiles, so neither is a read. Every role that may use a connection holds `connection.view`; `catalog.admin` (team catalog) is not used yet because sharing is OQ-449. | The RBAC role table (M3) assigns them, or the names are changed. |
| OQ-443 | How do the connection commands meet the prod lock (OQ-380)? | `connections.create` and `bundles.import` make profiles, so they name no connection to arm: through the real router they answer 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` in every environment, and the HTTP specs pin it (`GP-1 (OQ-380)`) while the unit specs run them with `env: 'dev'`. `connections.update` and `connections.delete` target their profile, so a person arms it first (`HTTP-2` shows the 422 with the `ops.arm` fix, then the 200). `connections.test-http`, `connections.test-amqp`, `bundles.export` and the reads are queries and are never locked. The kernel guardrails are untouched. | OQ-380 is ruled on; the two specs then expect the 200 of the golden path. |
| OQ-444 | Why does changing the address of an endpoint need its secret again? | A stored password is sent to whatever host the profile names. An update that moves an endpoint (host, port, TLS, path prefix; for AMQP not the vhost) while keeping the stored secret would let anyone who may edit a profile point it at a host they run and read the password from the next test or connection. So the update is `VALIDATION_FAILED` ("needs its secret to be entered again") unless the request carries the secret. A rename, a colour, a vhost or a visibility change keeps the secret. | A maintainer accepts the rule, or asks for the stored secret to be cleared instead of the update refused. |
| OQ-445 | Which features does a failed AMQP test close, and where is the answer kept? | The issue says the AMQP gates are disabled with the reason. The feature catalogue and `GateDecision` computation are M0-46's; here `connections.test-amqp` returns a `gates` map with the stand-in ids `amqp.peek`, `amqp.tap`, `amqp.publish` and `amqp.replay`, all closed with the failure's message and `source: 'connections.test-amqp'` (all open on success). The result is not stored: a test is a query (OQ-448), so nothing remembers it, and `connections.get` shows no gates. | Settled in part by M0-46: the ids are the profile's (the same four) and the probe's own handshake fills `BrokerProfile.gates`. Still open: `connections.test-amqp` keeps its stand-in map until a follow-up issue in `svc-connections` reads the profile's gates instead. |
| OQ-446 | Why can a bundle not carry secrets, even encrypted? | CON-04 says "excluded or passphrase-encrypted". Only excluded is built: the contract's `secretsIncluded` is the literal `false`. A passphrase-encrypted export needs a KDF and an AEAD over a passphrase, which belong in `@app/secrets` (trust-critical, with its own review), not in a service. `bundles.export` runs a scanner over its own output (`findSecretsInBundle`: the kernel's redaction rules, so a field named for a secret, a URI with user and password, a JWT or a bearer value fails the export with `INTERNAL`). The contract field is `secretsIncluded` (a flag), not `secrets`, because a field named for a secret is exactly what the scanner looks for. | An issue adds `PassphraseSeal` to `@app/secrets`, then bundles gain `secrets: 'passphrase'`. |
| OQ-447 | What does a bundle contain, and what does an import do with a name that exists? | Addresses, TLS flags, vhost, path prefix, colour, environment class, and the user names of each endpoint: a user name is an address of a login, not a secret, and an import is useless without it (a maintainer may prefer to drop it). Import is by name among the caller's own profiles: `skip` (default) or `rename` (`Name (2)`, `(3)`, the first free). The new profiles are private, owned by the importer, with their credentials unset (`secret.isSet: false`); a test says "No secret is stored" until they are entered. Everything is decided before anything is written, and the budget (200 per person) is checked first, but the saves are one transaction each: a failure in the middle leaves the earlier profiles, reported by the error. | A maintainer confirms, or asks for one transaction for the whole import. |
| OQ-448 | Why are the two tests queries, and what bounds them? | A test changes nothing the engine or the broker keeps, so it is a `query` (never locked, audit `outcome` because it dials out with a credential, `POST` because a draft carries secrets and a secret never travels in a URL). A failure is an answer (`ok: false` with a specific code and message: eight HTTP causes, six AMQP ones, plus `SECRET_UNAVAILABLE` when the stored secret is unset or cannot be opened), not an error; a prober that throws is `unexpected` and says nothing more. One call dials one endpoint, runs for at most 15 s, and the broker's own text is not shown (it can carry anything). The AMQP test opens a connection and closes it: no channel, no declaration, no message. `cid` or `draft`, exactly one. | A maintainer wants the last result kept per profile (a mutation, so it would meet OQ-443). |
| OQ-449 | How are profiles stored, who owns one, and what is bounded? | `connection_profiles` holds the endpoints as `config` JSON (`v: 1`, validated on every read; a row that does not fit is `INTERNAL`). `credentials` (migration `0007_credentials`) holds one ciphertext per profile and protocol, written with the profile in one transaction; the authenticated data is `credentials:ciphertext:<id>:<protocol>`, so a copy to another profile or protocol does not open, and there is no `dek_id` column because the envelope names the key. A new profile gets a ULID (`newId`), not a name. The owner is the creating principal; `private` (default) is theirs alone, `team` is listed for everyone, and only the owner changes or deletes: someone else gets NOT_FOUND for a private profile and `FORBIDDEN_RBAC` for a shared one. At most 200 profiles are visible per person and a list page is at most 100 (a page reads the credential status of each profile: up to 100 small reads). Two concurrent updates of one profile: the last wins. | Team mode (M3) defines sharing and who may edit a shared profile. |

## Capability probe (M0-45)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/svc-capabilities` (the twelve probe steps, the profile builder, `probe.run` and `profile.get`), the ports in `libs/engine/ports/src/probe.ts`, `libs/engine/persistence` (migration `0008_broker_profiles`, `repos/broker-profiles.ts`) and `libs/testkit/src/it/wire-probe.ts`. The probe reads; its output (the profile) drives gates and version-dependent behaviour, so every unknown is read the cautious way: more hazards, fewer capabilities.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-470 | Why is `probe.run` a query, and who may run it? | It reads the broker and refreshes a cache of derived data (`broker_profiles`); it changes nothing the broker keeps and nothing a person entered. As a command it would be locked by the prod lock until the connection is armed (OQ-380, OQ-443), so a new production connection could not be probed at all, which is the moment the UI needs the profile. As a query it is never locked (the HTTP spec `GP-1` shows the 200 with no arming), is audited (`outcome`: it dials with a stored credential) and needs `connection.manage`; `profile.get` needs `connection.view` and makes no broker request. A private connection of someone else is `NOT_FOUND` for both. Writing the cache from a query is the one place this service writes. | OQ-380 is ruled on; then decide whether the probe becomes a command exempt from arming, or stays a query. |
| OQ-471 | Where is step 13 (write-shaped probes on demand)? | The fact sheet lists a replica-operation probe against a queue that does not exist and a tap dry-run, only on demand. They need a write port, and the probe is built never to hold one. `restrictions.qqReplicaOps` is `unknown` until an on-demand action exists. The 12 steps are read-only: management GETs, a TLS handshake and an AMQP handshake. | An on-demand probe action (M0-46 or M1) is specified, as an `implementAction` with its own consent. |
| OQ-472 | Who registers `svc-capabilities` and wires its ports? | Like `svc-connections` (OQ-441) the module is not in `coreModules` yet. It needs: `repos.brokerProfiles` and `repos.connections` (persistence); the management read port behind the BrokerScheduler (`scheduleMgmtRead`); a `BrokerWireProbe` (the testkit's `createRealWireProbe` is the pattern: the Dialer and TrustStore for the TLS step, `connectControl` for the handshake, with the endpoint and credentials taken from the connection profile). The management client needs a profile for every request (`MgmtTarget.profile`), including the probe's own: the resolver (OQ-415) gives it an unprobed profile (`product: 'unknown'`, `commercial-or-unknown`) until a profile is cached, then the cached one. Still unwired because of this: the executor's `ProfileResolver` hook (fingerprint and gates for step 4 and 5 of the pipeline), the `BudgetSource` of the ConnectionManager (OQ-377: `profile.limits` and the provider preset), a re-probe on endpoint change and on resume (blueprint 5.10), and the diagnostics `profiles` hook. `libs/engine/ports` is trust-critical, so `probe.ts` needs CODEOWNERS approval (as OQ-372 did). `core-interfaces.md` does not list the new ports yet. | A work package registers the module (with svc-connections, OQ-441) and wires the above. Update: issue #278 registered the module and wired `mgmt`, `wire`, the stores and the executor's authorizer; the `ProfileResolver`, the `BudgetSource`, the re-probe and the diagnostics hook are OQ-764, OQ-765 and OQ-771. |
| OQ-473 | Is the OAuth discovery right? | Recorded on a 4.3.6 lab broker with OAuth off: `bootstrap.js` is served and says `set_oauth_settings({"oauth_enabled":false});`, and `/api/auth` answers 406. The enabled case is not recorded, so the issuer is read with a loose pattern (`oauth_enabled: true` and the first `oauth_provider_url`); a multi-resource-server page may name the issuer elsewhere. LavinMQ's `/oauth/enabled` is not read (it gives a flag, no issuer). A page that cannot be read gives no OAuth, never a guessed one. `pinned` is always false: pinning is svc-identity's. | The OAuth lab variant records what an enabled broker serves; the pattern and a fixture follow. |
| OQ-474 | Where does a non-RabbitMQ product's own version go? | `BrokerProfile` has no field for it, and `versions.perNode` holds RabbitMQ versions that the registry compares with ranges (hazards, capabilities, provider corrections). LavinMQ `2.10.2` there would be read as a 2.x RabbitMQ. So for LavinMQ, ApsaraMQ and `unknown` every node version is `null`, the era is the conservative 4.0-4.2 with the reason in `era.reasons`, and the verification is `unknown-build`. | A `versions.product` text field (a domain-model change) lets the UI show "LavinMQ 2.10.2" without the registry reading it. |
| OQ-475 | Where does a node's version come from? | Not from the management API: on all five lab releases `/api/nodes` carries no version, not even with `columns=applications` (the integration test would fail on it), and the single-node record has none. So only the node that answered `/api/overview` has a version, and a cluster behind one address reads its other nodes as unreadable (`null`: conservative era, no capabilities, every hazard) unless the repeated overview meets them. That is honest and also harsh for a healthy cluster. | The Prometheus adapter (M1) reads `rabbitmq_build_info` per node (each node's own listener), or another source is found; the nodes step then fills the versions. |
| OQ-476 | Are the provider and build rules right? | The provider is named only by evidence: a host name matching a preset's patterns, or a provider-owned object, and the two must agree; none or a disagreement is `unknown`, whose preset assumes nothing (`probe` levels). Only a loopback address is `self-hosted`, so a self-hosted broker behind a host name is `unknown` until the profile can say otherwise. A tie (both CloudAMQP plans share patterns) takes the shared plan, the weaker preset. The build is `oss` only for `rabbitmq` on a provider that is not Amazon MQ, CloudAMQP or IBM; the others run their own builds, so their era and verification are conservative. Managed providers count as load-balanced. The IBM user and `/api/queue-migration/status` of fact sheet step 9 are not probed: the preset data names no pattern for them. | The connection profile gets a provider override, or the maintainer confirms the rules; the presets gain the IBM and queue-migration markers. |
| OQ-477 | Are the defaults I chose right? | Three overview asks (enough to meet a second node cheaply). `prometheus.minIntervalSec` 15 when the preset gives none. `preAuthFrameMax` 4096, the smaller of the two documented values. `classicEnabled` is `unknown` (no source in the 12 steps). Streams are supported when the stream plugin is on (node list or `stream.js`) and the preset does not block them; the paths are `amqp091` and, when the overview lists a `stream` listener, `stream-protocol`; reachability is `unknown`. Firehose: `none` for another product or a blocked preset, `tracing-plugin` when the trace exchange exists or the plugin is on, else `runbook-only`. `definitionsExport`: `full` for an administrator on a preset that supports it, `synthetic` for a partial preset, else `none`. Operator-policy changes: the broker's own flag, else `disabled` for a blocked preset, else `unknown`. The channel limit and frame size the AMQP adapter does not report are left to the preset. The era does not use quorum-queue Machine Versions (they need a queue name); the era rests on node versions and the `rabbitmq_4.3.0` flag and says so in its reasons. | The maintainer confirms or names the figures; the probe gains the Machine Version read (fact sheet 0.13) with the on-demand steps. |
| OQ-478 | Where does `lastVerified` come from? | OQ-100 stands: `resolveEra` needs the list of broker versions with a green conformance run and the registry ships none. The module takes an optional `lastVerified()` dependency; the composition root passes none, so every RabbitMQ profile is `unverified-minor` and every other `unknown-build`. | The evidence work (M0-15, M0-18) defines how the list is derived; the composition root passes it. |
| OQ-479 | Why is the profile `unknown` in the contract, and why are `gates` empty? | `@app/domain-model` imports `@app/contracts` for its ids, so the contract cannot name `BrokerProfileSchema`; `profile.get` and `probe.run` answer `{ profile: unknown }` (the OpenAPI document shows a free-form object) and the service parses with `BrokerProfileSchema` before it answers. The UI renders the `gates` and `allowedActions` the engine sends (M0-46 fills both). The cache row keeps `fingerprint` and `verification` as columns and the profile as JSON; a cached profile that no longer fits the schema is `NOT_FOUND` ("probe again"). `ConnectionStore.delete` removes the connection's profile row in the same transaction. | The profile schema moves to the contracts library (or contracts gain a hook for it). |

## Capability gates (M0-46)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/svc-capabilities/src/{gates,era.ts}` and `profile.get`. The maintainer accepted the defaults of the spec (`docs/specs/M0-46.md`, Q-1 to Q-8). OQ-445 (the feature ids) is settled here except for the `connections.test-amqp` swap, and the `gates: {}` part of OQ-479 is closed.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-480 | Where do the gate rules live (Q-1)? | In `svc-capabilities/src/gates/features.ts`: one pure function per feature, reading the provider preset (`registry.providerPreset`) and the profile. The registry's `gates` table is still empty and `GateRuleSchema` cannot test a preset level or a profile field. `registry.gate` is asked only for a gate the profile opens: a row that closes it wins, and no row (`SemanticsGapError`) leaves the profile's own evidence-based answer. A gap never opens anything. | A maintainer wants the rules as registry data: `GateRuleSchema` gains a preset-support condition (`libs/shared/semantics` is `trust:critical`, adversarial review) and the rows move there. |
| OQ-481 | Where does the HTTP 405 remediation of Amazon MQ live (Q-2)? | In `reason` (English, like the registry's texts); `fix` is absent because no action fixes an operator-policy restriction (the editor is POL-02, M2). | `GateDecision` gains a `remediation` or `reasonKey` field (a contracts change and an OpenAPI regeneration), or the editor lands and `fix` can name its action. |
| OQ-482 | Is the catalogue right (Q-3)? | `streams.browse`, `tap.firehose`, `audit.event-exchange`, `policies.operator-edit` and the four `amqp.*`. Prometheus, definitions export, queue replica operations, shovels and OAuth2 have no gate yet. | A feature is added as one function and one test; the list is reviewed at the next profile change. |
| OQ-483 | What does "firehose disabled" mean, and do hazards close gates (Q-4, Q-5)? | The firehose gate is open only for `firehose.available === 'tracing-plugin'`; `runbook-only` and `none` are closed with a reason that says which. The 4.2.9 recording lists the trace exchange together with the tracing plugin and the builder reads the exchange as evidence; whether the exchange exists without the plugin is not established here. Hazards close nothing: `HazardRule` has no link to a feature. | A hazard needs to close or degrade a gate (a `gates: FeatureId[]` link in the registry), or a lab run shows the trace exchange without the plugin. |
| OQ-484 | Which features does an action need, and where is that written (Q-6)? | `ActionFeatures` (action id to feature ids) is injected with `featuresOf` and empty by default: no registered action needs a gate yet, so `allowedActions` equals the permitted actions. | `defineAction` gains an optional `requires: FeatureId[]` (ADR-003, a contracts change). |
| OQ-485 | Is `era.ts` an extraction (Q-7)? | Yes: `eraOf` moves the era and verification step out of the builder with no change of behaviour (the registry still resolves, I-3) and the profile-level tests are new. | Never, unless the era needs more engine-side inputs (OQ-471, Machine Versions). |
| OQ-486 | Where does `allowedActions` come from (Q-8)? | `profile.get` computes it per principal from the context's principal, mode and environment class, with the optional `authorizer` dependency; it is not cached and not in the fingerprint. Absent, the deny-all authorizer stands in and the list is `[]`. An authorizer that throws, or answers anything but `allowed: true`, leaves the action out. | The composition root passes the executor's authorizer (OQ-472). |
| OQ-487 | Should the event-exchange gate close when the plugin list is unknown? | It does (statistics off lists no plugins, OQ-431), even when `amq.rabbitmq.event` exists; the exchange is not used as evidence for this gate. | A lab run shows that the exchange is reliable evidence without the plugin list. |
## Management HTTP adapter (M0-24)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/adapter-mgmt-http`. Each is easy to change there. Nothing here is broker evidence: the status texts and product differences come from the fact sheet and the managed-brokers research, and the golden fixtures (M0-25) and the lab are what confirm them.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-410 | Why `node:http` and not `undici`? | The issue and blueprint 2.4 say undici (confined to this project already), but `undici` is not in the catalog or `tools/supply-chain/deps-allowlist.json` (protected paths), and the brief prefers built-ins. The client is a `node:http` keep-alive agent whose `createConnection` calls `Dialer.dial` (egress policy and TrustStore apply, I-9, and the profile's route is passed through, which `createUndiciConnector` hard-codes to direct). An `https` origin gets its TLS from the Dialer, so the HTTP client sees a secure socket. It does not use HTTP/2, pipelining or undici's connection pool; the agent keeps at most 64 pools of 4 sockets (I-6). | A maintainer wants undici (the dependency is added through the supply-chain process and `transport.ts` swaps its agent for `new Agent({ connect })`, which is the only file that changes), or confirms `node:http`. |
| OQ-411 | Is `/api/channels` sent at all on a stats-off broker below 4.3.6? | The hazard `channels-400-stats-off` says "do not call it", and the issue says `disable_stats` is never sent. Built: the client sends the request as the caller asked, without `disable_stats` (the caller's flag is dropped unless the capability `channelsAcceptDisableStats` holds), and turns the broker's 400 into `CAPABILITY_MISSING` with a closed gate (`source: channels-400-stats-off`) when the profile is stats-off and `channelsListWithStatsOff` does not hold. Services should consult the registry first; the adapter does not refuse before sending, so a stale profile still gets the truth from the broker. | A maintainer decides that the adapter should refuse without a round trip (a one-line change in `client.ts`), or the gate engine (M0-12) makes services skip the call. |
| OQ-412 | Are the status-text matches right? | `401` is read by text: `expired` is `TOKEN_EXPIRED`, `Not ... user` is `FORBIDDEN_BROKER` (the login works, the tag is missing: fact sheet status table), anything else is `UNAUTHENTICATED`; `405` with `Broker settings disallow` (any case) is `FORBIDDEN_BROKER` with a gate whose `source` is `provider-restriction` and whose `reason` is the broker's text (`GateDecision` has no id field, so `source` carries it); other `405` is `BROKER_ERROR`; `408/504` timeout, `409/412` precondition, `423` locked, `429` rate limited, `502/503` unreachable (all retryable where a retry can help), other `4xx` `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `5xx` `BROKER_ERROR`. Golden fixtures (M0-25) confirm the rest on RabbitMQ 3.13.7 to 4.3.6: a wrong password or an unknown user answers 401 `{"error":"not_authorized","reason":"Not_Authorized"}` (`UNAUTHENTICATED`), a user without a management tag answers 401 `{"error":"not_authorised","reason":"Not management user"}` (`FORBIDDEN_BROKER`), a missing queue answers 404. LavinMQ 2.10.2 answers all three logins with an empty 401, so a missing tag there reads as `UNAUTHENTICATED`. The `expired` match is still a guess: no fixture shows what an expired OAuth token answers, and the provider-restriction 405 is not recorded. A `503` of a failing health check is reported as `BROKER_UNREACHABLE` with the reason in `broker.text`. | The OAuth lab records what an expired token and a provider restriction answer; the map in `errors.ts` is corrected. |
| OQ-413 | Is the `/get` rule for LavinMQ (and other non-RabbitMQ products) right? | For every product that is not RabbitMQ or Tanzu RabbitMQ (so `unknown` too) a `POST .../get` body gets an explicit `ackmode` (default `ack_requeue_true`) and `requeue: true`; a caller that chose `*_requeue_false` keeps it and `requeue` is `false`, so a destructive choice is never reversed or hidden. The issue says "always `requeue:true`"; the exception is the destructive modes only. The rule rests on the fact sheet reading of LavinMQ 2.10.2 (`queues.cr`). RabbitMQ bodies are sent as written. The LavinMQ golden fixture (M0-25) shows the half that is safe: the body the adapter sends (`ackmode` `ack_requeue_true`, `requeue: true`) is accepted and returns the messages, and the queue holds the same counts (5 messages, 3 ready, 2 unacknowledged) once the statistics tick has passed. It does not show that a body without `requeue` loses the messages, which would destroy them to prove. | The provider contract run (M2-09) shows what 2.10.2 does with a body without `requeue`. |
| OQ-415 | Who implements `MgmtTargetResolver`, and who holds the decrypted credential? | The adapter does not know connection profiles or the vault. `MgmtTargetResolver.resolve(connectionId, signal)` returns host, port, TLS trust, route, path prefix, the auth (`basic` or `bearer`) and the probed profile (`product`, versions, stats) per request; the composition root implements it with `svc-connections` and `@app/secrets`. The client keeps nothing, so each request decrypts once; whether to cache the plaintext for a few seconds is the resolver's decision (I-10 allows memory, not logs). Credential changes reach the client at the next request; `invalidate(connectionId)` closes idle sockets that were opened with the old trust. | The composition root wires the resolver over ConnectionStore and FieldSealer (M0-44 delivered both, see OQ-441) and decides on caching. |
| OQ-416 | Are the limits right? | 30 s to the answer (the whole body for `get` and `send`; only the headers for `stream`, which has no idle timeout and no size cap because the consumer applies back-pressure), 16 MiB per `get` or `send` answer (a bigger one is `response-too-large`, not a partial read), 64 KiB of an error body, 4 sockets and 64 pools. `get` is for the page-sized answers of the scheduler; a definitions export is read with `stream`. | The sweep (M1) and the first big broker show the real sizes. |
| OQ-418 | Was the work package split? | The issue is labelled `needs-split`; it was delivered whole (client, paging, query, errors, product, transport and the endpoint catalogue with their schemas), because the pieces share the request-shaping rules and the tests. The diff is over the 800-line guideline (specs and the endpoint catalogue are most of it). Endpoint files are grouped by resource family (`overview`, `cluster`, `topology`, `clients`, `access`, `health`) rather than one file per endpoint. | A maintainer accepts the size, or asks for the catalogue to be split out. |

## Privacy, egress and diagnostics (M0-69)

Decisions taken in `libs/engine/svc-ops` (the diagnostics bundle), `libs/engine/net-dialer` (the purpose registry and the generated outbound-calls page), `libs/engine/kernel/src/log/canary.ts` (the canary scanner) and `apps/engine` (the log tail, the bundle sink and the egress run). The desktop half of the issue (e2e-desktop, `crashReporter`, the spellchecker) waits for the desktop shell and is OQ-408.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-400 | What does the deny-all egress run prove today? | There is no container image (M0-64) and no desktop shell, so the run is the built engine bundle started with a Node preload (`apps/engine/src/testing/egress-guard.mjs`) that refuses and records every `net` connect (so `fetch`, http(s), `tls` and undici too), `tls` and `http2` connect, name lookup, `dgram` socket and child process. It proves the JavaScript surface. It cannot see native code that opens a socket by itself, so the network-level layer (no network at all for the container, a deny-all proxy for Electron) is still to add. | M0-64 starts the image with `--network none` (or a deny-all NetworkPolicy) under the same soak, and the e2e-desktop run lands (M2-P09). |
| OQ-401 | How long is the soak in CI, and who runs the ten minutes? | The acceptance criterion says ten minutes. `engine:smoke` (three OSes in CI) runs the soak for 15 seconds; `pnpm nx run engine:egress` sets `EGRESS_SOAK_SECONDS=600` and is not in `ci.yml`. A scheduled workflow for it was not added (a workflow edit is a maintainer call). | A maintainer decides whether a nightly workflow runs `engine:egress`, and on which OS. |
| OQ-402 | Why is `diagnostics.bundle` a query? | It changes nothing the engine or a broker keeps: it writes a derived file to a target the person chose. As a command it would be locked by the prod lock with no connection to arm (OQ-380) and would write a receipt per export. As a query it is still audited (`attempt+outcome`) and needs `diagnostics.export`, and it has no receipt. Risk is `low`. | OQ-380 is decided; then the kind, the risk and the receipt are revisited. |
| OQ-403 | What is in a bundle, what is left out, and in what format? | JSON, not a zip (Node has no zip writer, and a package needs an allow-list entry). Sections: versions, the newest log lines (default 1000, at most 5000, 4 MiB), a summary of each BrokerProfile (50 at most, copied field by field: no user name, node names or URLs), the newest 200 audit rows (no actor, target or detail), and a manifest. Receipts and incidents are named in `omitted` because no listing of either exists yet (a `ReceiptRepository.list` and an incident record are not built). Every cap is reported as `truncated`. | The receipt listing and incident records exist; a maintainer decides on a zip. |
| OQ-404 | Where does a bundle go in local web mode, and how does the person get it? | The token is the one `PlatformBridge.pickSaveTarget` returns on desktop; the main process resolves it, and no desktop sink exists yet, so desktop registers no `diagnostics.bundle`. In local web mode the engine writes `<data dir>/diagnostics/bundle-<token>.json` (exclusive create, mode 0600 in a 0700 directory, at most 20 files, the token limited to `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,128}`), and the UI mints the token. There is no download route, so a person (or a container volume) reads the file from disk. | The web UI gets a save-as design (a streamed response to the person's browser), or the desktop sink lands. |
| OQ-405 | Which secret values does the bundle remove by value? | `OpsModuleDeps.diagnostics.secretValues()` returns what the engine holds right now; local mode returns the launch token. Each value is removed, with its JSON-escaped, percent-encoded, hex and base64 forms, from every string and key before the kernel's redaction runs (which would otherwise eat part of a value that contains `?token=...` and leave the rest). No connection credential is stored yet (M0-44), so none is listed. Any non-empty value counts, however short, which can mangle text for a one-character secret and is the safe side. File paths in logged stack traces (a home or data directory, so possibly a user name) are not removed: a bundle is a local file the person reads before sending it. | M0-44 stores credentials and says whether they are listed here (and in the logger). |
| OQ-406 | Is the Dialer the right place to enforce the registry, and are the planned purposes right? | `createDialer({purposes})` refuses an unregistered purpose with `EGRESS_DENIED` before resolving anything; without the option any purpose is dialled (the existing specs and the lab test use ad hoc ones). The engine builds no Dialer yet, so nothing passes the option. Six of the eight purposes (`prom-scrape`, `oidc`, `alert-webhook`, `otlp-export`, `diagnostics-upload`, `update-check`) are registered ahead of their code from the blueprint, as "registered, not built"; their destinations and defaults are for the owning work packages to confirm. | The first work package that builds the Dialer in the composition root passes `isRegisteredPurpose`, and each owner confirms or fixes its row. |
| OQ-407 | Should lint confine `dns`, `dgram`, `http2` and `https` like `net` and `tls`? | I-9 confines `net`, `tls`, `undici`, `ssh2` and `socks`. A client written with `node:https`, `node:http2`, `node:dns` or `node:dgram` is only caught at run time by the egress guard, not by lint. `node:http` cannot be confined by import (inbound servers and types use it). The change is a row in the shared `eslint.config.mjs`. | A maintainer decides; then the table gets the row and the places that use them today (net-dialer for `dns`) are listed. |
| OQ-408 | When do `crashReporter{uploadToServer: false}`, the spellchecker and the desktop egress run land? | `apps/desktop` is a version-holder stub (OQ-10): there is no `crashReporter` call, no spellchecker setting and no Playwright `_electron` project to put a deny-all proxy in front of. The registry's page already says desktop disables the spellchecker or bundles its dictionaries (A-13). | M2-P09 (desktop privacy and egress). |
| OQ-409 | What is "the secret scanner" of the acceptance criterion? | It is the kernel's canary scanner: the test plants known values, then looks for each in six encodings (raw, JSON-escaped, percent-encoded, hex, base64 at three alignments, base64url). It finds a planted secret, not an unknown one: there is no entropy or pattern scanner. The canary-secret log scan of I-10 over the whole engine in E2E is still planned; this work covers the diagnostics bundle and a running local engine. | The E2E canary scan over every log sink (M0-60 or later) reuses `createCanaryScanner`. |

## Golden fixtures (M0-25)

Decisions taken in `libs/testkit/src/golden` (the recorder), `testing/fixtures` (the answers), `libs/engine/adapter-mgmt-http/golden` (the replay) and `.github/workflows/golden-refresh.yml`. The fixtures are real answers of the lab brokers and LavinMQ 2.10.2, not lab evidence (I-14): they change no semantics cell. They settled two questions of M0-24 and removed their rows: OQ-414 (a plain `z.object` parses all 11 recordings, so no schema has to keep unknown keys, and the "(passthrough)" of the acceptance criterion means tolerant, not lossless) and OQ-417 (the answer is OQ-433). OQ-412 and OQ-413 are narrowed.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-430 | Should the nightly refresh open a pull request, open an issue, or commit? | `golden-refresh.yml` compares the five lab brokers (statistics on and off) and LavinMQ with `testing/fixtures` by shape every night and, on a difference, records again and opens a pull request, as the issue says. The maintainer uses no pull requests; creating one with the workflow token needs the repository setting of OQ-01, and a pull request made with that token does not start CI. The job holds `contents: write` and `pull-requests: write` for the whole run, recorder included (the recorder only talks to local containers). A manual run is a dry run unless asked otherwise. Not run yet: it needs the workflow on the default branch. | The maintainer picks pull request (OQ-01 setting), issue (like the platform lane, with a read-only recorder) or a direct commit; the last step of the workflow changes. |
| OQ-431 | Which stats-off behaviours does the hazard table not say yet? | Recorded with `management.disable_stats` and the metrics collector off, 3.13.7 to 4.3.6. (1) `/api/consumers` and `/api/consumers/{vhost}` answer 400 "Stats in management UI are disabled on this node" on every release; the adapter turns it into `VALIDATION_FAILED`, which blames the caller. (2) `/api/channels/{vhost}` is still 400 on 4.3.6, where `/api/channels` works (hazard `channels-400-stats-off` says fixed in 4.3.6). (3) `/api/nodes` keeps `name`, `running` and `being_drained`, and loses `enabled_plugins` and `auth_mechanisms`, which the adapter asks for. (4) A classic queue under a policy and an operator policy lists `policy` only, on every release including 4.2.9 and 4.3.6; the policy fields of 4.2.5 and 4.3.0 (capability `policyFieldsWithStatsOff`) reach quorum queues only. (5) Before 4.2.5 a quorum queue still lists `policy`; the missing ones are `operator_policy`, `effective_policy_definition` and `delivery_limit` (the hazard text also says `policy`). (6) The connection list keeps `name`, `vhost`, `user` and `node` on 3.13.7 to 4.2.9 and is empty on 4.3.6 (collector off), the opposite of capability `connectionStaticInfoWithStatsOff` (4.2.6 and later). (7) A stream has no message counts in a stats-off list on any release, with `enable_queue_totals=true`. `golden/golden.spec.ts` pins each of these. | The semantics owners amend the hazard and capability data through the registry process (an RFC issue, then lab evidence), and the pins change with them. |
| OQ-432 | Is "stats off" one mode? | The fixtures use `management.disable_stats = true` together with `management_agent.disable_metrics_collector = true`, what an operator sets to take the load off. `disable_stats` alone (collector running) answers differently, and the fact sheet lists four ways to switch statistics off. Only the first combination is recorded; the overview reports `disable_stats: true` in it, which is what the probe reads. | A third mode (`stats-ui-off`) is wanted for the probe; it is one more entry in `modes` and about 12 more minutes of recording. |
| OQ-433 | Which lists page, and should the bare-array lists be marked non-paged? | With `page` and `page_size` sent, RabbitMQ 3.13.7 to 4.3.6 answers the envelope for `queues` (list and per vhost), `exchanges` (both), `connections` (the list only), `channels` (the list only), `users`, and a bare array for `bindings` (all four), `consumers`, `policies`, `operator-policies`, `permissions`, `channels` and `connections` per vhost, and for `vhosts` (which no longer pages). `/api/vhosts?page=1&page_size=100` answers 500 on 4.0.9, 4.1.8, 4.2.9 and 4.3.6 (3.13.7 and LavinMQ take it), so `vhosts.list` is now non-paged.
