LavinMQ answers the envelope for every list the adapter pages. The other bare-array lists are still marked paged: `page` is harmless there, but `page_size` bounds nothing, and only the byte cap does. | A maintainer decides whether the bare-array lists are marked non-paged (a one-line `paged: false` each, `golden/golden.spec.ts` lists them) or the byte cap is accepted as their bound. |
| OQ-434 | Why did this work package change adapter code? | The acceptance criterion says every endpoint schema parses the recorded answers, and four did not (the fixtures found them): `/api/vhosts` refused `page` (see OQ-433, `defineList` gained `paged: false`), `/api/extensions` entries may be an empty array or carry a list of scripts, `exclusive` is `null` on a quorum or stream queue, and a binding of the default exchange has `arguments: null` on LavinMQ. Each is a few lines in `libs/engine/adapter-mgmt-http/src/endpoints` with a spec. Nothing else in the adapter changed. | A maintainer accepts the four fixes, or asks for them as a separate work item. |
| OQ-435 | Is 5.5 MB of fixtures too much to keep? | 623 files: 0.8 MB of management answers and 4.7 MB of Prometheus pages, because each RabbitMQ `/metrics` page is about 470 KB (the Erlang VM, telemetry and every `rabbitmq_*` family) and is recorded for 10 combinations. The pages are kept whole so the future Prometheus adapter parses what a node really sends; trimming them to the `rabbitmq_*` families would be an edit of the answer, so it is not done. `testing/fixtures` is outside Prettier and the nightly pull request only appears on a shape difference. | The Prometheus adapter (M1) names the families it reads, or the repository size matters. |

## Lab driver and evidence recorder (M0-15)

Decisions taken in `apps/conformance` (the drivers, the DSL, the recorder), `libs/testkit/src/it` (the launcher and the integration test) and `tools/arch-tests` (the independence rule). OQ-83 (which digest an artefact records) is settled here: the base-image digest pinned in `testing/broker-lab/versions.json`, plus the commit of the recording run in the provenance stamp.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-420 | Is `rhea` 3.0.5 acceptable as a dependency now, for the AMQP 1.0 lab driver? | The issue scope lists `driver/amqp10-raw.ts (rhea)`, and execution plan section 1 puts a raw-rhea lab driver in M0. Added: `rhea: 3.0.5` in the `pnpm-workspace.yaml` catalog and the root `package.json`, an entry in `tools/supply-chain/deps-allowlist.json` (`ref: M0-15`; Apache-2.0, published 2026-04-28, so it clears the 3-day gate without an exclusion; its only dependency, `debug`, is already in the lockfile), and `apps/conformance` in the `rhea` confinement row of `eslint.config.mjs`. The allow-list is a protected path; the edit was not blocked in this worktree, but it needs your review. | A maintainer confirms the entry, or moves `amqp10-raw.ts` and the confinement row elsewhere. |
| OQ-421 | Who commits `testing/lab-evidence/schema.json`? | The issue lists the file, but `testing/lab-evidence/**` is human-only. The schema is the zod schema in `apps/conformance/src/record/evidence.ts` (what the recorder and verifier use); `node apps/conformance/src/record/print-schema.ts > testing/lab-evidence/schema.json` prints the JSON Schema (a spec proves the output equals `evidenceJsonSchema()`). | A maintainer runs that command and commits the file (and decides whether CI should check it for drift). |
| OQ-424 | Is "created by broker-lab" proved well enough, and which variants does the recorder cover? | The recorder does not import the testkit (its broker comes as data, `LAB_BROKER`, like the contract suite's, OQ-187). `verifyLabEndpoint` asks Docker for the container (name, run label, version label, lab image, running, ports on 127.0.0.1 equal to the data, digest equal to the pin) and mints an endpoint only after that. Someone who can create containers on the same host can fake all of it; a person or script that merely points at a real broker cannot. Only the single plain node is covered: the TLS and OAuth variants and the 3-node cluster (Toxiproxy, HAProxy: no single container) have no `LabEndpoint`. | A conformance cell needs a variant; then `LAB_BROKER` and `verifyLabEndpoint` learn it (the compose project's containers and the TLS CA). |
| OQ-425 | When does the DSL learn AMQP 1.0 and the stream protocol? | `amqp10-raw.ts` is a driver, not yet a set of DSL steps: it opens receivers with no credit and no auto-accept, grants any credit (including more than one, which the adapter never does), settles with accept, release, reject and modify, and settles twice. It is exercised on a real broker by `conformance.it.ts` (via `src/it/drivers-smoke.ts`), not by a scenario. AMQP 1.0 settles are asynchronous: `settle` returns when rhea has written the disposition, and what the broker did shows only later (a released message was back in the queue within seconds on 4.3.6, not at once), so a scenario step must poll. Also, `rhea` batches dispositions per tick: two settles of one delivery in the same tick put only the last state on the wire, which is why `settle` waits. | The first AMQP 1.0 settle cell (CF-SETTLE-*, M0-?) adds the steps. |
| OQ-426 | Are `position`, `survived` and the counters defined the way the cells need them? | The issue names the fields, not their meaning. Built: an `observe` step drains the queue with acknowledgement off and records, per message the scenario published, `position` (the place in the drain order, 0 is the head), `redelivered` (the flag on that delivery), `survived` (found at all; a message held unacknowledged on another channel reads as not survived) and `x-delivery-count` / `x-acquired-count` (from the message headers, `null` when the broker sends none, as classic queues do not). Per-delivery facts (`redelivered`, tag, `messageCount`) are in each get step's result. CF-SMOKE-01 itself is defined here (two messages, `basic.get`, `basic.nack` with requeue, then the drain), since the issue only names it. The drain consumes what it reads. | A maintainer or the first three-way test (M0-?) confirms the field meanings, or asks for another shape. |
| OQ-428 | Is a CI job for the recorder in `ci.yml` right before M0-71? | `ci.yml` got a `conformance-record` job (Linux): `pnpm nx run conformance:it --versions=4.3.6`, then `pnpm nx run conformance:record --cells=CF-SMOKE-01 --versions=4.3.6 --out=dist/conformance-record`, uploading the `local` artefact. It proves the verification command on a hosted runner on every push and writes no evidence. Workflow edits are allowed but are called out for review; the recorder workflow itself (`conformance-record.yml`) is M0-71. | A maintainer accepts the job, or moves it into M0-71's workflow. |
| OQ-429 | Which CODEOWNERS lines does M0-15 add? | `/apps/conformance/` and `/testing/lab-evidence/**` are already owned. Proposed (CODEOWNERS is protected): `/libs/testkit/src/it/run-record.ts @RafaelJCamara` and `/libs/testkit/src/it/conformance.it.ts @RafaelJCamara` (the launcher hands the recorder its broker), together with OQ-86's `/testing/broker-lab/` and `/libs/testkit/`, and `/tools/arch-tests/ @RafaelJCamara` (it now enforces I-14's independence rule as well as I-2 and A-9). | A maintainer adds the lines. |

## CI lab-evidence recorder workflow (M0-71)

Decisions taken in `.github/workflows/conformance-record.yml`, `apps/conformance/src/record/bundle*.ts` (the checks the workflow runs) and `tools/supply-chain/workflow-hardening*.ts` (a self-test that pins the workflow's hardening). It settled OQ-423: the workflow is that file, and the recorder runs as `pnpm nx run conformance:record`. Nothing was run with write effect: the workflow cannot run before it is on the default branch.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-460 | Bot pull request, issue or branch only? | The issue says the workflow opens a bot pull request labelled `lab-evidence`; the maintainer uses no pull requests (the same conflict as OQ-430). Chosen: the conservative reading of the issue. A manual run is a dry run by default (the bundle is only a build artifact). With `dry_run` off, the `propose` job pushes a `lab-evidence/run-<id>-<attempt>` branch (never the default branch, never forced) and opens the pull request; nothing is merged or approved. Creating it with the workflow token needs the repository setting of OQ-01, and a pull request made with that token does not start CI, so `semantics-evidence:check` (M0-37) does not run on it until it is closed and reopened. | The maintainer picks pull request (OQ-01 setting, ideally a GitHub App token so CI runs), issue (artifact link only) or branch only; the last step of `propose` changes. |
| OQ-461 | Should a run need a second approver? | Anyone with write access can start `workflow_dispatch`. The workflow runs only on the default branch (another branch could carry another copy of the file and still be stamped `ci`), and the pull request needs a CODEOWNERS approval, but a run itself needs none. A protected environment with required reviewers on `propose` would add a gate before anything is pushed. | The maintainer creates an environment (a repository setting) and `propose` names it, or accepts the current gate. |
| OQ-462 | Is checking the bundle against the run's own variables enough? | `bundle-cli.ts check` requires every artefact to carry a `ci` stamp for this commit, run URL (with attempt) and workflow file on the default branch, the image digest of `testing/broker-lab/versions.json`, the requested cells on every requested version, and the layout `<version>/<CF-id>.json` only; the `record` job also verifies each artefact's schema and SHA-256. The variables are the ones the stamp is made from, so this catches mistakes, not a hostile `record` job (it runs the install, the brokers and the scenarios, with a read-only token). The `propose` job installs nothing and re-checks with Node built-ins only; it does not re-verify the SHA-256. No attestation (`id-token`, `attestations: write`, an ADR) is made. | The maintainer decides on a signed attestation of the digests (OQ-450); `semantics-evidence` already verifies the run URL against the GitHub API (M0-37). |
| OQ-463 | Which cells, versions and time does a real run need? | Only `CF-SMOKE-01` has a scenario; the defaults are `CF-SMOKE-01` on `4.3.6`. `timeout-minutes: 120` is a guess for building up to five lab images and recording each; no run has been measured. The lab variants (TLS, OAuth, cluster) are not recorded (OQ-424). The label `lab-evidence` is created by the workflow if it is missing (`gh label create` with `pull-requests: write`; believed to be enough, unproven). | The first manual run on the default branch; adjust the limit and defaults. |
| OQ-464 | What happens to the `conformance-record` job in `ci.yml` (OQ-428)? | It stays: it proves the recorder on a hosted runner on every push and stamps `local`, so it cannot be mistaken for evidence. The two share a name, which is confusing: this workflow is `conformance-record.yml` (workflow name `conformance-record`), the job is a job of `ci`. | A maintainer renames the job (for example `conformance-record-smoke`) or removes it. |

## Three-way harness, semantics-evidence check and coverage gate (M0-37)

Decisions taken in `apps/conformance/src/assert`, `tools/semantics-evidence`, `libs/testkit/src/it/run-conformance.ts` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. OQ-422 (is a self-digest enough) and OQ-427 (the CF-SMOKE-01 stub) are settled here: the check follows the run URL of every `ci` stamp through the GitHub API, refuses `local` stamps and verifies the digest, and `cf-smoke-01.cell.spec.ts` exists. What remains of OQ-422 is OQ-450.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-450 | Can a genuine artefact be told from one that was edited and re-sealed under the same real run? | The check proves the stamp names a real, successful run of `conformance-record.yml` on an allowed branch, for the stamped commit, with the stamp's time inside the run. Someone who changes the content, recomputes the digest and keeps the stamp still passes: the digest is only a checksum, and the run does not say what it wrote. Two ways to close it: the workflow uploads the evidence directory as a run artifact and the check compares each digest with it, or the workflow attests each digest (a signature the recorder cannot make) and the check verifies the attestation. Either needs M0-71 to publish something, so the check does neither. Until then the bot pull request and the human approval are the control for the content. | M0-71 lands and a maintainer picks the upload or the attestation; then the check compares. |
| OQ-451 | Which cell changes need evidence, and is evidence in the tree enough? | Cells are matched by key (what a lookup uses), not by id. A changed status or a changed effect needs the cell to list evidence that exists, verifies, is for one of its own conformance ids and was recorded on a lab version of its own era. The artefact need not be in the same change: a registry correction after a `lab-contradicted` result lands after the evidence did. Exception: a `null` effect of a `code-derived` cell with no evidence may become a value (deriving a stub; nothing exists to contradict it), and a new `code-derived` cell with no evidence is allowed (AGENTS.md section 10). Removing a cell is not checked: its lookup becomes a `SemanticsGapError`, which fails closed. A stricter rule (an artefact in the same change for every flip, so old evidence cannot back a new claim) is not built: the registry leg of the three-way assertion already fails a lab-status cell whose evidence does not confirm its effect, and the evidence of the same CF id cannot confirm a different claim without a projection saying so. | A maintainer confirms, or asks for the stricter rule. |
| OQ-452 | What run policy does the check enforce? | Events `workflow_dispatch` (A-20, the critique); branch `main`; github.com only (no GHES); status `completed` with conclusion `success`; stamp time within 10 minutes of the run's start and last update; stamped commit in the history of the change; repository from `--repo`, else `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`, else `origin`. All of it except the host and the slack is a flag (`--events`, `--branches`, `--workflow`). A bot pull request is opened by the workflow while the run is still `in_progress`, so the check on that pull request fails until it is run again after the workflow ends. An API failure, an unknown repository or a missing token on a private repository is a finding, not a pass. Only the artefacts a change touches (and those of the cells it changes) are asked about, so a recording run that GitHub later deletes cannot fail every later change; `--verify-all-runs` asks about all of them and suits a scheduled run, which no workflow has yet. | M0-71 lands; a maintainer confirms the events and branches or sets the flags in `ci.yml`. |
| OQ-453 | How does `conformance:test` treat an unverified cell, a failing 4.0.9 and the cache? | A cell with no recorded evidence, no live broker, no registry cell for its id or no projection is `unverified`: its test is skipped with the reasons printed and recorded in the report, never green; `--strict` fails it (the release gate). With `--versions` only the lab-cell specs run, live, one broker per version; without it every spec runs and needs no Docker. `--non-blocking` defaults to `4.0.9` (the issue: 4.0.9 results are non-blocking but visible): its failures are printed and do not fail the command. The `test` target is no longer cached, because a replayed result would stand in for a live run; `nx affected -t test` therefore re-runs the Docker-free conformance specs (about 2 s). | A maintainer confirms the defaults, or asks for the live run to be its own target. |
| OQ-454 | Which CI jobs run these? | `ci.yml` got a `semantics-evidence` job (Linux, full history, token `actions: read`; base is the pull request base, the push `before`, or `origin/main`) running `semantics-evidence:check`, `semantics:audit-cells` and `conformance:coverage`, and a `conformance:test --grep=CF-SMOKE --versions=4.3.6` step in the `conformance-record` job. Workflow edits are allowed but are called out for review. The check covers `before..after` of a push; a force push whose `before` is not in the clone falls back to `origin/main`. | A maintainer accepts the jobs, or moves them into M0-71's workflow. |
| OQ-455 | What do the registry leg and the live leg compare? | The registry leg compares a cell's effect with a per-CF `Projection` of the artefact (`assert/projections.ts`): only the fields the projection returns are compared, and a cell whose id has no projection is `unverified` (a lab status then fails). Only CF-SMOKE-01 has one (`position` head, tail, original or removed, and `destructive`); no registry cell names it. The counters (`deltaAC`, `deltaDC`) need the `x-delivery-count` and `x-acquired-count` observations (OQ-426) and are projected by the settle cells. The live leg compares everything the artefact records except the stamp: a changed scenario title, step or image digest shows as a difference and needs a new recording. | The first settle cells (M0-38) write their scenarios and projections. |
| OQ-456 | What is the coverage gate? | `conformance:coverage` evaluates the recorded legs of every cell on every lab version of its era. It always fails a cell whose conformance id has no lab-cell spec, any recorded disagreement and a lab-status cell the evidence does not confirm everywhere; `--strict` also fails any cell without confirming evidence and any id with no scenario. CI runs it without `--strict` (today no cell has evidence, so strict fails by design). | The release workflow runs it with `--strict`, or a maintainer defines the gate differently. |
| OQ-457 | Is a second reader of the evidence format acceptable? | `tools/semantics-evidence` is a library and may not import the `apps/conformance` application (Nx boundaries), and the recorder may not import a shared package (the independence rule), so the check has its own reader (`evidence-format.ts`). `assert/format-parity.spec.ts` feeds both readers about 60 artefacts and edits and requires the same outcome, so a schema change in one place fails the suite. | The evidence schema moves to a package the recorder is allowed to import, or a maintainer accepts the parity spec. |
| OQ-458 | Which CODEOWNERS lines does M0-37 add? | `/tools/semantics-evidence/` and `/apps/conformance/` are already owned. Proposed (CODEOWNERS is protected): `/libs/testkit/src/it/run-conformance.ts @RafaelJCamara` (it hands the specs their brokers and the strict flag) and `/testing/broker-lab/versions.json @RafaelJCamara` (the pins the check trusts: moving a pin to match forged evidence would otherwise be one ordinary edit). The `lab-evidence` label on the bot pull request is set by M0-71's workflow. | A maintainer adds the lines. |
| OQ-459 | When do the other lab-cell stubs call the harness? | `cf-smoke-01.cell.spec.ts` calls `describeThreeWay`. The 18 stubs made by the `conformance-cell` generator are still `it.todo` pairs, and so is the generator's template: a todo is neither green nor visibly unverified, but the coverage gate lists every id with no scenario and strict fails it. Each id gets its call when its work package writes the scenario and the projection. | M0-38 to M0-40 land; the generator's template then emits `describeThreeWay`. |

## Conformance CI workflows (M0-42)

Decisions taken in `.github/workflows/conformance-pr.yml`, `.github/actions/setup/action.yml` (the optional `node-version` input), `tools/conformance-ci` (the plan, the verdict and their CLIs) and `tools/supply-chain/conformance-workflows*.ts` (the hardening check and its self-test). Slices A (the PR gate) and B (the nightly, `.github/workflows/conformance-nightly.yml`, with the report in `tools/conformance-ci`) of M0-42 are built; the mutation nightly (slice C) adds rows below when it lands. The maintainer approved the spec and accepted the proposals; the rows say what to confirm once something has run.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-488 | Five brokers on every affected change, or the smoke from `docs/plan/plan.md`? | The issue and the execution plan say all five lab versions on every affected change; `plan.md` (CI gates) says a two-version smoke on a self-hosted runner while the repository is private. Built as the issue says, on hosted `ubuntu-24.04` runners (as the AMQP contract job of `ci.yml` does): 5 versions x 2 shards = 10 jobs. The cost is hosted minutes and ten anonymous image pulls from Docker Hub per run. | The first runs show the cost and any pull-rate limit; the maintainer keeps the matrix or cuts it to a smoke (`SHARDS`, or the version list in the plan). |
| OQ-489 | How does "block the merge" work without pull requests? | The maintainer lands on `main` directly (the same conflict as OQ-430 and OQ-460), so the workflow also runs on push to `main` and goes red there. It blocks only if a maintainer sets `conformance` as a required status check in the repository settings (not a file in the repository), which matters for outside contributors' pull requests. The job `conformance` always reports: an unaffected change passes with the reason "not affected". | A maintainer sets the required check, or decides that red on `main` is the gate. |
| OQ-490 | Are 2 shards and 45 minutes right, and does an empty shard fail? | Chosen: 2 shards (21 lab-cell spec files, most still `it.todo`) and `timeout-minutes: 45` (the value of the AMQP contract job). No run has been measured. What Vitest 5 does with a shard that selects no spec file is not confirmed (both shards select files today). | The first runs on the default branch; adjust `SHARDS` (in the workflow) and the limit. |
| OQ-491 | What blocks on a pull request? | `conformance:test` reports a cell with no recorded evidence as `unverified`: shown and skipped, never green, never a failure. No evidence is recorded yet, so today the gate can fail only on a recorded disagreement or a broken harness. `--strict` (the release gate, OQ-453) is used neither here nor nightly. 4.0.9 stays non-blocking inside `conformance:test` (its default), so a 4.0.9 failure is shown and does not fail its job. | The first recorded evidence lands; a maintainer decides whether the nightly should print the strict result as a non-blocking report. |
| OQ-492 | Is a new `tools/conformance-ci` project, and a CODEOWNERS line for it, right? | The plan and the verdict are not harness code (the recorder must not depend on the registry, `checkRecorderIndependence`), so they live in a new `trust:critical` project made with the `library` generator; the CLIs use Node built-ins only and run with `node` and no build. The alternative was `tools/supply-chain`. CODEOWNERS is protected: proposed line `/tools/conformance-ci/ @RafaelJCamara`. The size is over 800 lines, landed as the spec's three slices under #38 (`Refs #38` until the last). | A maintainer adds the CODEOWNERS line and labels #38 `oversize-approved`, or asks to move the code. |
| OQ-493 | Can a change to a workflow script weaken the required check from inside the same change? | The `conformance` job checks out the change and runs its own `verdict-cli.ts`, as any workflow of the change runs its own steps; a pull request could edit the job or the script to always pass. What stops that is review of the files, CODEOWNERS on `.github/workflows/` and `tools/conformance-ci/`, and `conformance-workflows-selftest` (run by `ci.yml`), which pins the shape of the job. A required check cannot be made stronger than the code that produces it without moving the check outside the pull request (a reusable workflow on the default branch, or a ruleset). | A maintainer decides on a ruleset that requires the workflow file from the default branch, or accepts review as the control. |
| OQ-494 | Where do non-release (alpha) builds come from, and does the nightly "feed the unverified state"? | `versions.json` has no alpha entry and alpha packages are not Docker Hub tags (OQ-81 settles a source and a pinning rule). Until then the `alpha` job takes an empty list (the plan reads `ALPHA_VERSIONS`, which the workflow does not set), is skipped, and the report lists it as `not available`. "Feed the unverified state" is read as: the report and its artifact tell maintainers which non-release builds ran and how; nothing automatic changes `profile.verification`, which is `unverified-minor` for any version without a green run by rule (blueprint 5.2). A failing alpha shows as `fail` and does not fail the run. | OQ-81 settles the source; then the plan reads the list from the file a maintainer names (`versions.json` is owned by the pins of OQ-458, or a new file). |
| OQ-495 | When does the Electron-runtime leg exist? | The test-fused Electron binary (DC-46) does not exist before M2 (`apps/desktop` is absent). The nightly has no job for it; the report always lists the leg as `not available` with that reason, never as a pass and never omitted. M2 packaging wires `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 <binary>` and replaces the fixed row with a real leg. | The M2 packaging work package lands and owns the leg. |
| OQ-496 | Does everything run on Node 26.12.0, and do hosted runners provide that exact version? | A failure on Node 26.12.0 turns the nightly red (only alpha may fail), and the `setup` action takes the exact version through its optional `node-version` input. Unconfirmed: that every install and native add-on works on Node 26.12.0 (the platform lane, OQ-131, has not run the upgrade), and that `actions/setup-node` finds 26.12.0. The first scheduled run answers both. | The first nightly runs; a Node 26 failure is fixed, or the maintainer makes the 26.12.0 leg non-blocking. |
## Landing and process follow-ups (M0-46, M0-42)

Things on `main` that only a maintainer can settle. They are not decided here. OQ-497 to OQ-499 are the three of them; the release-please permission is also OQ-01.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-497 | `dco` is red on `main` for two unsigned spec commits (`71ebb6e`, `f121cf9`). Does the maintainer accept the red check? | Both commits lack `Signed-off-by:`. Fixing them needs a history rewrite and a force push, which the repository rules forbid for agents (and `main` has already been pushed). Every later commit is signed. Status: open, nothing done. | The maintainer accepts the red check (for example by listing those two shas as an exception in `.github/scripts/dco-check.sh`, a change the maintainer decides), or rewrites the history themselves. |
| OQ-498 | The M0-46 change landed at about 1,020 changed lines against the 800 limit without `oversize-approved`. Is it accepted after the fact? | AGENTS.md section 5 allows more than 800 lines only with the `oversize-approved` label from a maintainer. The label was not set before landing. Status: open, nothing done. | The maintainer labels the M0-46 issue `oversize-approved` (or records that the size is accepted), and says whether the next oversize change must wait for the label. |
| OQ-499 | `release-please` fails on every push to `main` with "GitHub Actions is not permitted to create or approve pull requests". | Same question as OQ-01 (repository setting, or a dedicated token); repeated here only so that the M0-46 and M0-42 follow-ups are in one place. Status: open, see OQ-01. | OQ-01 is answered. |

## Spec questions: M0-39, M0-40, M0-51 and M0-53

The four specs were approved by the maintainer with their defaults (`docs/specs/M0-39.md`, `M0-40.md`, `M0-51.md`, `M0-53.md`). Each question in their "Risks / questions" sections is recorded below as a row, so the maintainer can answer here. Every row is **open - working default in use**: the default named is what the spec assumes and what an implementation session follows until the row is answered; nothing here decides a question. A row that repeats an existing question points to it, and the answer goes there.

Ids: M0-39 Q-1 to Q-11 are OQ-500 to OQ-510 (Q-n is OQ-(499+n)); M0-40 Q-1 to Q-14 are OQ-511 to OQ-524 (Q-n is OQ-(510+n)); M0-51 questions 1 to 16 are OQ-525 to OQ-540 (n is OQ-(524+n)); M0-53 Q-1 to Q-16 are OQ-541 to OQ-556 (Q-n is OQ-(540+n)). OQ-557 to OQ-559 were used by the M0-40 and M0-39 slice A work (new questions found while implementing), OQ-600 to OQ-602 by M0-40 slice B, OQ-603 to OQ-606 by M0-40 slice C, and OQ-610 to OQ-612 by M0-39 slice B. OQ-560 to OQ-573 (M0-38) and OQ-580 to OQ-599 (M0-41) are used by the section "Spec questions: M0-38, M0-41 and M0-75" below; OQ-574 to OQ-579 stay free for what the M0-38 implementation finds; OQ-620 to OQ-629 are M0-75; OQ-630 and up are review findings. (The first plan was to start M0-40, M0-51 and M0-53 at OQ-510, OQ-520 and OQ-540; their question counts do not fit those starts without overlap, so the ranges are contiguous instead.)

### M0-39: DOWN paths lab cells

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-500 | Is the A/B/C/D split approved, and who opens the issues for slices C and D (M0-39 spec Q-1)? | Default: the split of the spec's Slices table; if A plus B is over 800 changed lines when written they land as two commits, with `oversize-approved` as the alternative. ADR-033: no work without an issue, so C and D need issues before they start. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer approves the split (or asks for `oversize-approved`) and opens or names the issues for C and D. |
| OQ-501 | How is TCP loss recorded in slice C: Toxiproxy on the 3-node lab, or destroying the client socket on the single-node lab (M0-39 spec Q-2)? | The issue says Toxiproxy, which needs a `LabEndpoint` for the 3-node lab (OQ-424), a change to the recorder's guard against non-lab brokers and a workflow that starts the variant. Destroying the client socket is the "crash" half of the fact sheet row, with no proxy and no heartbeat wait. Default: not decided; slices A and B do not depend on it and slice C does not start. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks one; the choice is recorded here and in the issue for slice C. |
| OQ-502 | Which ids do connection close, `get`-held and cancel-then-close use (M0-39 spec Q-3)? | Default: they stay cases of one scenario, CF-DOWN-01, because the registry has one action (`close_while_holding`) and no `basic.cancel` action (OQ-261). OQ-396 allows splitting or renaming ids; a new id needs a stub from the `conformance-cell` generator and a registry link (a data edit). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the single scenario or asks for new ids (see also OQ-396). |
| OQ-503 | May a cell whose `deltaDC` or `countsTowardLimit` variant is not observed become `lab-verified` on the fields that are observed (M0-39 spec Q-4)? | On 4.0 to 4.2 the artefact shows AC but not DC; on 4.3 three rounds cannot tell `if-dc-gt-limit` from `yes`. OQ-451 and OQ-455 say only returned fields are compared. Default: the spec flips nothing and says so in its report; the status is a human edit anyway (I-14). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer rules on partial projections, or a better scenario is specified. |
| OQ-504 | In what order do M0-38, M0-39, M0-40 and M0-41 land, given they share `dsl.ts`, `driver/amqp091-raw.ts`, `record.ts`, `testing/lab.ts`, `scenarios/index.ts`, `projections.ts`, the README and this file (M0-39 spec Q-5)? | Default: slice A and M0-38's driver work are serialised (whoever lands first); each cell family registers with a one-line change and a projection file of its own; whoever lands first creates a shared `assert/counters.ts` and the second imports it. M0-38's AMQP 1.0 steps (OQ-425) are needed by slice D. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or sets the order. |
| OQ-505 | What tells the scenario that a DOWN was processed (M0-39 spec Q-6)? | Default: `awaitReady` reads the queue's ready count from a passive declare, stable for N polls (the broker answers at once, unlike management statistics, refreshed about every 5 s). It cannot see a returned-then-dropped message without a count change, so it may end early on a slow broker. AC-13 measures that; if it flakes, the options are a longer `stableFor` or a second signal (consumer count, `rabbitmqctl`). Slice C (consumer timeout, minutes) needs a long-wait policy for the recorder workflow's 120-minute job. Status: open - working default in use. | AC-13 runs; the maintainer picks the fix if it flakes, and the long-wait policy for slice C is set. |
| OQ-506 | Does the raw driver's 30 s heartbeat matter, and for which slice (M0-39 spec Q-7)? | Default: keep 30 s. It does not matter for graceful close; it matters for slice C (detection time after TCP loss). Status: open - working default in use. | Slice C is specified (see OQ-501). |
| OQ-507 | Is it intended that evidence is recorded for 4.0.9 although it is end-of-life and non-blocking (M0-39 spec Q-8)? | Default: yes. `conformance:test` treats 4.0.9 failures as non-blocking (OQ-453); a human may still need to correct 4.0 to 4.2 cells from the evidence. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or drops 4.0.9 from the lab-cell runs. |
| OQ-508 | `describeThreeWay` titles every test `AC-2 ...`, which collides with each WP's own AC-2. Rename it (M0-39 spec Q-9)? | Default: this spec lets AC-2 mean that test. A name that carries the WP id (or only the CF id) would remove the ambiguity; it is a request for M0-37's code, not part of M0-39. The same question is M0-40 Q-13 (OQ-523). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer asks M0-37's owner for the change, or accepts the mapping by file and CF id. |
| OQ-509 | Are OQ-500 to OQ-509 right for this WP, and are ranges reserved for M0-38, M0-40 and M0-41 (M0-39 spec Q-10)? | Answered by this change on the maintainer's instruction: M0-39 uses OQ-500 to OQ-510, M0-40, M0-51 and M0-53 use the ranges above, and OQ-560 to OQ-599 were reserved for M0-38 and M0-41 (now used, see the M0-38, M0-41 and M0-75 section). Status: answered, kept only so the spec's question has a row. | The row is removed once the ranges are used or changed. |
| OQ-510 | Is the separation between the fake host and the hand-written artefacts right (M0-39 spec Q-11)? | Default: `MemoryHost` simulates a broker for shape, bound and non-write checks only (AC-4, AC-6, AC-11, AC-12); AC-1, AC-7 and AC-9 use hand-written artefacts, so a projection test never "confirms" behaviour the fake encoded. A reviewer checks the separation. Status: open - working default in use. | The `/review-wp` of the implementation, or the maintainer, confirms it. |
| OQ-559 | What does the `closeConnection` step record, and which `awaitReady` defaults apply (found in M0-39 slice A)? | The spec's AC-4 says the close is recorded "with the broker's reply", but `connection.close-ok` carries nothing to record. Default: the step records `{ ok: true }` and the closed state is visible in the settle that follows on a channel of that connection (`channelOpen: false`, text "closed with its connection"). `awaitReady` defaults to `stableFor` 5 polls, 50 ms apart, `timeoutMs` 30 000; they are working values that AC-13 (slice B) may show to be too short (OQ-505), and only `stableFor` is recorded as an argument, never a duration. Status: open - working default in use. | Slice B's AC-13 runs on a lab broker, and the maintainer confirms the step shape or asks for another. |
| OQ-610 | Which fields of the `unset` and `finite` CF-DOWN-01 cells can the scenario confirm (M0-39 slice B)? | `countsTowardLimit` is projected for `finite` (from three hold-and-close rounds on a limit of 2) and for `minus1` (survival of the first return), but not for `unset`: the scenario runs no crossing rounds without a limit, so the 3.13 `unset` cell's `no` is left unobserved, and `if-dc-gt-limit` cannot be told from `yes` by three rounds (OQ-503). A field the artefact cannot show is left out of the projection and so is not compared. Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-503 is answered, or a later slice adds rounds for `unset`. |
| OQ-611 | What does the projection do with a message the broker dropped (M0-39 slice B)? | When `m1` is gone after one return (3.13 with a limit of -1), only `position` (`dlx-or-drop`), `destructive` (true) and `countsTowardLimit` (`yes`) are projected; the counters of a message that no longer exists are not (the registry's `deltaDC` 1 and `deltaAC` 0 for that cell are not asserted). The scenario has no dead-letter exchange, so a drop and a dead-lettering cannot be told apart. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer decides whether a drop-versus-DLX case is worth a scenario of its own. |
| OQ-612 | How does the projection treat paths that disagree, and rounds that hold another message (M0-39 slice B)? | The get, active-consumer and cancel-then-close queues of one limit (and the connection-close queue for `finite`) must show the same survival, position, queue size and counters for `m1`; if not, the projection is null (the cell stays `unverified`) and `pathDivergence` names the queue and field. In the crossing rounds, `m1` must be the message held in round 1, and in rounds 2 and 3 when it survives; if a round held `m2` (for example because `m1` went to the tail), `countsTowardLimit` is not projected and the finite cell stays `unverified`. Status: open - working default in use. | A recorded run shows which of these cases occur, and the maintainer confirms the conservative reading. |

### M0-40: DLX carry-over, delayed retry and header stamping lab cells

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-511 | Size and slicing: four slices as four commits under #36 with `oversize-approved`, or four new issues (M0-40 spec Q-1)? | About 2,000 to 2,300 changed lines against 800. Default: the four slices of the spec; slice D depends on OQ-515 (M0-40 Q-5). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks `oversize-approved` on #36 or asks for four issues. |
| OQ-512 | The issue's paths (`apps/conformance/src/cells/{carryover,delayed-retry,stamping}/*.cell.ts`) are stale (M0-40 spec Q-2). | The repository uses flat `cells/cf-*.cell.spec.ts` stubs made by the generator, and OQ-396 says M0-38 to M0-40 settle the layout. Default: keep the flat layout and put scenarios in `scenarios/`, as `cf-smoke-01` does. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the flat layout and the issue text is corrected. |
| OQ-513 | The issue's verification command is refused by the launcher because of a pipe character in `--grep` (M0-40 spec Q-3). | Default: the spec uses three runs, one prefix at a time. Changing the launcher's grep rule is M0-37-owned. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the three runs or asks M0-37's owner to relax the rule. |
| OQ-514 | What exactly does each of CF-CARRY-01..03, CF-DR-01..02 and CF-STAMP-01 measure, and should OQ-396's granularity change (M0-40 spec Q-4)? | No document defines them except through the cells that name them (`cells.data.spec.ts` oracle, `dlx-carryover.data.ts`, `delayed-retry.data.ts`) and fact sheet b.1, b.2, b.4, b.5. Blueprint 5.6 names `CF-43-QQ-COPIED-HEADER-01` and `CF-43-QQ-DLX-CARRY-01`, which match CF-STAMP-01 and CF-CARRY-01 in intent but not in id. Default: the shapes read from those cells. Related to OQ-396. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the mapping, or asks for one id per strategy and source type. |
| OQ-515 | Who widens the DSL to AMQP 1.0 (M0-40 spec Q-5)? | Same question as OQ-425 (and M0-39 Q-5, OQ-504). `LabScenario.protocol` is `'amqp091'` and `ScenarioHost` has no 1.0 methods; CF-CARRY-02 and the 1.0 legs of CF-DR-* and CF-CARRY-03 need it, as do M0-38's CF-SETTLE-03/04/06. Default: one WP (whichever starts first, a maintainer picks) lands the widening as its own small commit; slice D waits for it. The management API legs need nothing. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-425. | OQ-425 is answered. |
| OQ-516 | Do classic-queue cells come with this WP or with the b.3 work (M0-40 spec Q-6)? | Related to OQ-394. The registry has no classic-queue cells, so a classic source is recorded as evidence and compared with nothing (`unverified`). Default: no cells are added here. There is also no cell for a dead letter from an at-least-once versus at-most-once source (the cells are strategy-agnostic); the projection must hold for both. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer says where the classic cells belong. |
| OQ-517 | Carry-over has no field in the schema (M0-40 spec Q-7). | Same question as OQ-390. Default: the facts the issue cares most about (DC arrives as L+1; first return drops it; fresh budget on 4.2.9) are recorded in the artefact and projected only as the fields the schema has (`countsTowardLimit`, `position`, `destructive`, the deltas); the rest is evidence for people and the planner (M1-12), not a machine-compared cell field. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-390. | OQ-390 is answered. |
| OQ-518 | Cells that name several ids owned by several WPs: who speaks for which fields (M0-40 spec Q-8)? | The coverage gate confirms a cell only when every id it names is confirmed on every lab version of its era. CF-STAMP-01 and CF-CARRY-03 are named by `settle-matrix.data.ts` cells that M0-38 also tests, and CF-CARRY-03 by `counters.data.ts` cells that M0-39 tests. A projection that returns null for such a cell stops it from ever being lab-verified. Default: each WP's projections speak only for the effect fields its scenario measures and return null otherwise. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the division and it is agreed on the M0-38 and M0-39 issues. |
| OQ-519 | What are `messages_delayed` and `next_delayed_at`, how fresh are they, and how coarse may the time brackets be (M0-40 spec Q-9)? | (a) The fact sheet does not say whether they are in the management queue JSON or in Prometheus, nor the format (U-05 asks both); default: record the management queue JSON and defer Prometheus (the lab exposes only the AMQP and management ports). (b) Queue stats refresh on `collect_statistics_interval` (5 s by default, none set in the lab), so a read right after a return may not show the delay; default: read again after a wait, not a lab-wide interval change. (c) No clock value may be recorded (replay must equal evidence); default: fixed waits (0, 2.5 s, about 6 s) and `next_delayed_at` reduced to a class, with the issue's `min` of 5000 ms. The bracket can flake on a loaded runner. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer sets the tolerance and the `min`, and the lab-interval question. |
| OQ-520 | Are `x-delayed-retry-*` policy keys (4.3.1+) in scope (M0-40 spec Q-10)? | Default: out of scope. The issue's criteria use arguments only; the policy variant needs `PUT /api/policies` (the host's `management()` is POST-only; slice A widens it anyway) and a precedence cell that does not exist. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer asks for a follow-up issue for the policy variant, or confirms it is out. |
| OQ-521 | How are old versions (3.13.7 to 4.2.9) and refused declarations handled (M0-40 spec Q-11)? | Default: delayed retry on 3.13.7 to 4.2.9 is recorded as a negative control (the feature is absent) and shown `unverified`, as the issue's command implies. A declaration the broker refuses closes the channel and aborts the whole scenario today, so such a variant is left out unless the DSL learns to record a refusal. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or a DSL change to record refusals is specified. |
| OQ-522 | Is the era-aware fake broker acceptable for the plumbing tests (M0-40 spec Q-12)? | The fake encodes the registry's claims so the plumbing can be tested without Docker; a test that passes against it is not evidence. Only AC-13 and the recorded evidence are broker truth. Default: the fake is allowed for plumbing tests. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or requires the plumbing tests to run on the lab only. |
| OQ-523 | `describeThreeWay` test titles and the `conformance-cell` generator template (M0-40 spec Q-13). | Same title question as M0-39 Q-9 (OQ-508). Default: leave the titles and map by file and CF id; or parametrise the title in `assert/cell-spec.ts` (an M0-37 file). OQ-459 says the generator's template should emit `describeThreeWay` instead of `it.todo` once the lab-cell WPs land; whoever lands first updates it (a change in `tools/generators`). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer decides at OQ-508 and OQ-459. |
| OQ-524 | In what order do the trust-critical WPs run, given the cap of 2 (M0-40 spec Q-14)? | M0-39, M0-40 and M0-42 were open worktrees when the spec was written; M0-37 has landed. Default: check the cap before an implementation session starts. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the order. |
| OQ-557 | CF-STAMP-01 runs a quorum queue with no delivery-limit argument and one with a finite limit, so its projection speaks for the `unset` (3.13) and `finite` (3.13, 4.0-4.2, 4.3) `nack_requeue` cells and returns null for the three `minus1` cells (M0-40 slice A). Should a `-1` queue be added? | Four of the seven cells naming CF-STAMP-01 are projected; a cell is confirmed only when every id it names is, so the `minus1` cells stay `unverified` until the scenario also runs a queue with `x-delivery-limit` `-1` (the 3.13 `minus1` cell also claims `dlx-or-drop` and `destructive`, which a plain return does not exercise). The queue named `none` has no argument on every version, so on 4.x it exercises the broker default; the registry has no 4.x `unset` cell. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer asks for the `-1` variant (a small follow-up slice) or accepts the gap. |
| OQ-558 | How is `next_delayed_at` read: a number as epoch milliseconds, a string as a date, or another unit (M0-40 slice A, `mgmtQueue`)? | The fact sheet does not give the unit or format (U-05, OQ-519). The DSL records only its class (`absent`, `future`, `past`, `unparsed`), reading a number as epoch milliseconds and a string as an ISO date; a second-based value would read as `past`. Status: open - working default in use. | The first lab run of `CF-DR-01` shows the real format and the reading is corrected before the scenario is relied on. |
| OQ-600 | Which cells does CF-CARRY-03 speak for (M0-40 slice B)? | The scenario returns a message 21 times from a source with `x-delivery-limit` 20, then returns the dead letter once, so its projection speaks only for the 0-9-1 `nack_requeue` cell of a finite-limit queue without delayed retry, and only for `countsTowardLimit`: `if-dc-gt-limit` when the dead letter arrives above the limit and is gone from its queue after the return, `no` when it is still there, nothing otherwise. The other cells that name the id (partition, consumer timeout, delayed retry, AMQP 1.0, HTTP) get null, so they stay `unverified` until their own scenarios exist (same family as OQ-518). The dead-letter queue has no limit argument, so the limit it applies is the version default; the projection compares the arrival count with the source limit only. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer asks for the delayed-retry variant of the scenario (with CF-DR-01, slice C) or accepts the gap. |
| OQ-601 | What does the CF-CARRY-01 projection call a counter delta, and what happens when the quorum sources disagree (M0-40 slice B)? | A delta is the counter the dead letter shows in `dlq` less the counter of its last delivery from the source, and is projected only when the dead letter shows a counter at all: a dead letter that starts afresh (3.13, 4.0-4.2 in the era broker) leaves the deltas unspoken, because the registry cell's delta describes the source and the schema cannot express "not carried" (OQ-517). The at-most-once and at-least-once quorum sources must show the same thing or the projection returns null (OQ-516: the cell is strategy-agnostic). A real lab may show a different delta than the cell, which is a `fail` for a human to resolve (I-14). Status: open - working default in use. | The first CI recording of CF-CARRY-01 shows what each version really does; the maintainer then confirms or changes the definition. |
| OQ-602 | A surprising lab result aborts a CF-CARRY run instead of being recorded (M0-40 slice B). | The DSL has no conditional: if a source does not dead-letter at the expected return, the later `get` of the dead letter is empty and `settle` of it throws, so nothing is recorded; likewise a declaration the broker refuses (the at-least-once strategy needs `x-overflow` `reject-publish`) closes the channel (OQ-521). Dead letters arrive asynchronously, so both scenarios read after a fixed 500 ms pause and `awaitReady` with `stableFor` 10 (OQ-559, OQ-505); a slow runner can flake. Status: open - working default in use. | The first lab run (AC-13, CI) shows whether the waits and the declarations hold on every version; the maintainer then asks for a recorded-refusal step or longer waits. |
| OQ-603 | Which cells do CF-DR-01 and CF-DR-02 speak for, and what about the `minus1` ones (M0-40 slice C)? | The scenarios set `x-delivery-limit` 20 on every queue, so the projections speak for the 0-9-1 `nack_requeue`, the HTTP `ack_requeue_true` (the nack-and-requeue read) and the 0-9-1 `reject_requeue` cells of a finite limit under delayed retry `all`, `failed` and `returned` (nine cells), and only for `position`, `deltaAC`, `deltaDC` and `destructive`; `countsTowardLimit` is not projected (the scenarios never reach the limit; CF-CARRY-03 has the other legs). The `minus1` cells, the AMQP 1.0 cells and the `disabled` control get null, so they stay `unverified` (same family as OQ-557, OQ-518). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer asks for the `-1` variant (a follow-up slice) or accepts the gap; slice D adds the AMQP 1.0 legs. |
| OQ-604 | How do the delayed-retry scenarios tell a delay from an ordinary return, and how coarse is the bracket (M0-40 slice C)? | Each queue holds the message `m` and a message `t` behind it. `m` read first is `head`; `t` read first and then `m` with no empty read between is `tail`; an empty read before `m` is `delayed`. The reads are at 0, 2.5 s and about 6 s (CF-DR-01, minimum 5 s) and at 0, 1.5 s, 4.5 s and 7.5 s (CF-DR-02, minimum 3 s, maximum 6 s), so a delay is bracketed to about 1.5 s on each side and can flake on a loaded runner (OQ-519). A message that is back before the minimum has passed reads as `tail`, which is a mismatch against a `delayed` cell, never a match. The `disabled` control sets no delayed-retry argument (whether `disabled` is a valid value is unknown). The delay itself has no registry field (OQ-517): the artefact records the first read at which the grow message is visible after each of three rejects (expected 3 s, 6 s, 6 s), for people and the planner, not projected. Status: open - working default in use. | The first lab run of CF-DR-01 and CF-DR-02 shows the real delays and the stats format (OQ-558); the maintainer then confirms the minimums and the reads. |
| OQ-605 | The DSL gets one conditional: `settle(..., { optional: true })` (M0-40 slice C). | The growth rounds of CF-DR-02 cannot know at which of four reads a hidden message becomes visible, so the next reject settles every read optionally and only the read that delivered it has anything to settle; a skipped settle is recorded (`{ skipped: true }`), a plain settle of an undelivered alias still throws (OQ-602). It is one small hunk in `dsl.ts` (a shared edit point with M0-38 and M0-39). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the optional settle or asks for a general conditional step (which M0-38 and M0-39 may also need). |
| OQ-606 | Where is the AC-13 smoke (`apps/conformance/src/it/lab-cells-smoke.ts`) (M0-40 slice C)? | Slices A to C add no smoke run on a real lab broker (the specs run on the era broker only); it needs a Docker daemon, which the implementation sessions do not have, and the three-way lab run (`conformance:test --grep=CF-DR`) is the first real exercise of the scenarios. Status: open - working default in use. | CI runs `conformance:test --grep=CF-DR --versions=...` once, or the maintainer asks for the smoke as a follow-up. |

### M0-51: web shell

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-525 | Four parts as proposed, or three with `oversize-approved` (M0-51 spec question 1)? | The estimate is 2,550 to 3,000 changed lines before the split. Default: the four parts of "Proposed split", with new ids and graph edges. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer approves the split and the ids. |
| OQ-526 | Transloco: add `@jsverse/transloco` 8.4.0, is its peer range compatible with Angular 22.2.3, and is the bundled-JSON `import()` loader acceptable (M0-51 spec question 2)? | A maintainer must add the package to the catalog and the allow-list (protected paths). Default: the bundled-JSON loader, because the HTTP loader would pass through the engine transport and `no-direct-fetch` forbids a direct one. The dependency is in the catalog and the allow-list (40a7686). Slice B of M0-51 (boot, i18n, routes) uses the loader: each scope is an inline loader of `import('./en/<scope>.json')`, a lazy chunk. The peer range is verified: 8.4.0 asks for `@angular/core >=16.0.0` and installs and runs on 22.2.3. Status: open - working default in use (dependency and peer range settled). | The maintainer confirms the loader, or picks another one. |
| OQ-527 | Aria, CDK and Spartan packages are not available (M0-51 spec question 3). | Same family as OQ-340. Default: a small local splitter, tabs and tree written to the WAI-ARIA patterns in `apps/web/src/app/shell/`, swappable for the aria wrappers later; the alternative is to add `@angular/aria` and `@angular/cdk` first. Slice C of M0-51 (layout) writes the splitter (`shell/splitter`), the bottom panel tabs and the activity bar locally to the WAI-ARIA patterns, with the keyboard helpers in `shell/keyboard.ts`; slice D adds the tab strip, the editor groups and the tree the same way. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-340. | OQ-340 is answered. |
| OQ-528 | How is "no horizontal page scroll" proved in a browser (M0-51 spec question 4)? | Default: the interim proof (pure fit rules, style assertions, one recorded manual run) until M0-60 adds `@shell`; the alternative is to pull the e2e-web scaffold (Playwright, axe-core in the catalog) forward. Related to OQ-342. Slice E of M0-51 ran the built app once under the engine at 1280x720: `scrollWidth` 1280 and `clientWidth` 1280, but the component styles were blocked by the CSP (OQ-538), so this is not a proof of the layout; the pure `fitLayout` spec and the style-source assertions remain the interim proof. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the interim proof or pulls M0-60 forward. |
| OQ-529 | Layout saves on a locked connection, and the audit cost of a save per resize (M0-51 spec question 5). | Through the real router `layouts.put` answers 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` until the connection is armed, so a restart restores nothing on an unarmed prod-classed profile, which today is every profile (OQ-380, OQ-283). The options (exempt risk `none` actions with no broker port, a declared local scope, per-target classes) belong to the guardrail owner. AC-5 passes against the fake engine and the golden-path HTTP behaviour, not the real router. Audit cost: OQ-383. Default: idle delay 1,500 ms plus a flush when the page becomes hidden (best effort). Slice C of M0-51 implements it in `ShellLayoutStore` (`LAYOUT_SAVE_DELAY_MS`, a flush on `visibilitychange` and `pagehide`); a failed save, including the 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` of a locked connection, keeps the layout in memory and shows the engine's Problem as sent, with a retry. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-380, OQ-283 and OQ-383. Update (OQ-783): `layouts.put` was NOT declared local, because it names the connection (`cid`) as its target and a local contract may name none; it stays locked until the connection is armed. See OQ-784. | OQ-380 and OQ-283 are answered, and the delay is confirmed. |
| OQ-530 | What does the "lock" segment of the status bar show and read from (M0-51 spec question 6)? | No action returns the arming state and `ConnectionProfile` has no read-only flag. Default (fail-closed): `locked` unless the engine said armed. The label sits under a `lock` key that the OQ-30 defaults allow to say "read-only"; confirm it should say that at all, given DC-15. Slice E of M0-51 reads the state from the `LOCK_STATE` token (`status-source.ts`), whose default is the constant `locked`, and its labels are "Locked", "Unlocked" and "Lock state unknown" (the word "read-only" is not used). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer names the source (the `ops.arm` result held by M0-52, a new query, or the gate) and the wording. |
| OQ-531 | How does the shell show engine health (M0-51 spec question 7)? | There is no health action, `LiveClient.state` is `idle` until something subscribes, and no topic exists for the shell. Default: derive from `LiveClient.state` (`ready` is ok, `reconnecting`, `down`) and show `unknown` while `idle`. Open: does the shell open the live session itself (which topic?) or wait for the first feature? Slice E of M0-51 maps `LiveClient.state` (`ready` is ok, `reconnecting`, `down`; `idle` and `connecting` are unknown) and does not open the session itself. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer answers, or a health topic is specified. |
| OQ-532 | What is the "UI build version" for skew detection (M0-51 spec question 8, AC-14)? | Default: not decided in the spec; the options are comparing `LiveClient.engineVersion()` with the `engineVersion` seen at page load (from `meta.get` or `/bootstrap.json`, which `PlatformSetup` does not expose), or with a build-time constant (`define`) set by the release. Local dev builds would show `0.0.0` on both. Status: open - working default in use (first option is the spec's reading until answered). Slice E of M0-51 takes the first version `LiveClient.engineVersion()` reports as the version of this page and shows the banner when a later session reports another, or on a `goaway` with reason `version-mismatch` (`skew/version-skew.ts`); it does not call `meta.get` and needs the live session to be open, which the shell does not open itself (OQ-531). | The maintainer picks one. |
| OQ-533 | How is a failed chunk load detected, and what is the reload window (M0-51 spec question 9, AC-13)? | "ChunkLoadError" is the webpack name; the esbuild-based builder fails with a `TypeError` whose message differs by browser. `isChunkLoadFailure` needs checking against a real removed chunk in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. Default: one reload per 60 s, state in `sessionStorage`. Slice E of M0-51 implements `isChunkLoadFailure` for the messages of Chromium, Firefox and WebKit as documented (`Failed to fetch dynamically imported module`, `error loading dynamically imported module`, `Importing a module script failed`) plus the bundler names, and reloads at the address being opened (`location.assign`), after a best-effort flush of a pending layout save (at most one second). It never reloads when the reload cannot be remembered (blocked storage), so it cannot loop. It has not been checked against a real removed chunk in the three browsers. Status: open - working default in use. | The browser check is run and the window is confirmed. |
| OQ-534 | Budget method: kilobyte, gzip level, definition of "initial", and what the limits are (M0-51 spec question 10, AC-3)? | Defaults: 1 kB is 1,000 bytes (the stricter reading); `ui.md` says "<= 350 kB gzip (error at 450)" while the issue says "at most 350", so `web:budgets` fails above 350 and the Angular raw-size `budgets` in `project.json` stay as a coarse backstop; feature chunk limit 200 kB gzip as in `ui.md`; translation chunks of the `shell` and `system` scopes count as initial. The gzip level and the exact definition of "initial" (everything `index.html` references, including modulepreload) are not fixed. Slice A1 of M0-51 (`tools/web-budgets`) implements the defaults: gzip level 6 (the zlib default), "initial" is every script, modulepreload and stylesheet that `index.html` loads from its own origin, and the measured baseline is 60,215 bytes. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the numbers, the gzip level and the definition. |
| OQ-535 | Layout document caps and storage (M0-51 spec question 11)? | Defaults: 3 editor groups, 30 tabs per group, 60 tabs in total, 512-character URL, 120-character label, all inside the 65,536-character contract limit; shell-owned schema, `version: 1`; a newer version is read as the default and not overwritten until the person edits; stored per principal and profile so web and desktop share it; sidebar and panel sizes in pixels (clamped on load) rather than ratios. Slice C of M0-51 implements them in `apps/web/src/app/shell/layout/shell-layout.ts` (`SHELL_LAYOUT_LIMITS`, `ShellLayoutSchema`, `parseLayout`); a tab address must stay under `/c/<cid>/` after URL normalisation, and a stored document over a cap is replaced by the default with a notice, not truncated. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the numbers and the pixel choice. |
| OQ-536 | Which activity bar entries and which explorer content in M0 (M0-51 spec question 12)? | Default: only entries that have a registered route (Explorer in M0, Settings when its route exists), and Team only when `meta.get` says the mode is `team`; the explorer tree shows only the connection root node until M0-48 or M0-54 supply a source (M0-48 is not a dependency). Slice B of M0-51 shows the Explorer entry only; it links to `ExplorerHome`, a placeholder view in `apps/web` (no data, no engine call) that the explorer feature replaces by mounting its own `explorer` route. Slice C adds the sidebar and panel toggles to the activity bar; its entries are text, not icons (no icon library, OQ-340), so the bar is 72 px wide. Slice D shows the explorer tree in the sidebar, fed by `EXPLORER_TREE_SOURCE` whose default is the connection's own root node; M0-54 provides the real source. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-537 | Key layout and honest-wording allow-lists (M0-51 spec question 13). | Related to OQ-30. Default: three scopes (`shell`, `status`, `system`), English only; no change to the defaults in `tools/eslint-rules/src/rules/honest-wording.ts`. Any new allowed key is a loosening and needs approval. Slice B of M0-51 lays the files out as `apps/web/src/app/i18n/en/{shell,status,system}.json` (lower camel case keys, no key under the honest-wording ban list, no allow-list change); slices C and D add keys to them (slice C added `activity`, `sidebar`, `panel`, `notice`, `refusal` and `saveFailed` to `shell`; slice D added `tree`, `tabs` and `editor`). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or OQ-30 is answered. |
| OQ-538 | Is `ngCspNonce` a separate issue (M0-51 spec question 14)? | Same family as OQ-347. Default: a separate issue; the served UI under the real CSP (with Trusted Types) is not proven by this WP. Measured in slice E of M0-51 (the built web app served by the engine in local mode, Chromium at 1280x720): the CSP (`style-src 'self' 'nonce-...'`) blocks every component `<style>` element Angular injects, because the app sets no `ngCspNonce`, so the served UI is unstyled (the grid of the shell collapses to one column); a `TrustedScript` assignment is also refused once. This is the gap OQ-347 describes and it affects every screen, not only the shell. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-347, and it needs the separate issue now. | OQ-347 is answered or the issue is opened. |
| OQ-539 | Where is the decision recorded: an ADR-021 addendum, or a new ADR, for the layout document, the bundled-JSON loader and the budget method (M0-51 spec question 15)? | Default: an ADR-021 addendum (proposed). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-540 | Where does the theme choice live (M0-51 spec question 16)? | Same question as OQ-348. Default: stays in the browser; this WP does not move it. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-348. | OQ-348 is answered. |

### M0-53: ui-grid adapter

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-541 | Which AG Grid packages: `ag-grid-community` only, or also `ag-grid-angular` 36.2.0 (M0-53 spec Q-1)? | Same question as OQ-230 (the dependency edits are protected paths a maintainer applies; the spec lists the exact lines). Default: `ag-grid-community` only (the spike's choice; the component calls `createGrid` itself); the confinement pattern `ag-grid-*` covers both. Implementation is blocked until the package is in the catalog. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-230. | OQ-230 is answered and the lines are applied. |
| OQ-542 | The Aria menu cannot use `@angular/aria` yet (M0-53 spec Q-2). | Same family as OQ-340. Default: a hand-written menu to the WAI-ARIA menu pattern, positioned with CSSOM (`[style.--x]`, which the CSP allows; no CDK overlay), behind `GridContextMenu` so it can be swapped later; the alternative is to wait for the packages. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-340. | OQ-340 is answered. |
| OQ-543 | Lint rule against AG Grid string expressions in `ui-grid` (M0-53 spec Q-3)? | Same question as OQ-235. `GridColumn.value` is function-only (AC-10), so features cannot pass a string; the question is only the library's own code. Default: none, undecided. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-235. | OQ-235 is answered. |
| OQ-544 | The `perf` project and `perf:grid-100k`: who creates them, with which browser and which definitions (M0-53 spec Q-4)? | `apps/perf` does not exist and no perf bench has landed, so this WP creates it unless another does first. The browser comes from the machine (`PERF_BROWSER`), as in the S-04 harness; Playwright is not in the catalog, so the driver speaks the DevTools protocol itself (about 300 lines, not a dependency). To confirm: "at least 55 fps" as a mean over the scroll window or a low percentile; "no long task above 50 ms" counted on the main thread from the trace; flat memory as 1.15 times after a forced GC; the densities (comfortable and compact) and viewport (1280x720) tested; the CI runner class and whether it runs in `perf:smoke` on PRs or only `perf:nightly` (execution plan section 4). Headless software rendering on shared runners may not reach 55 fps; that would be a measured S-14 finding, not a reason to loosen the test silently. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the definitions and where the bench runs. |
| OQ-545 | Split, or `oversize-approved` (M0-53 spec Q-5)? | About 1,800 to 2,300 changed lines against the 800 limit ("Size" section of the spec). Default: the spec's own proposal. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-546 | Who defines the window shape: `EngineWindowSource` in `ui-grid`, or the engine first (M0-53 spec Q-6)? | `inventory.query {kind, filter, sort, window}` and the `explorer.window` topic (M0-48) do not exist yet and M0-53 does not depend on M0-48. Default: the interface is this spec's proposal, in `ui-grid`; M0-54 writes the adapter from `inventory.query` and `explorer.window` to it. Open: should M0-48 define the shape first (offset and limit versus cursor), and should the type live in `ui-grid` or `ui-data`? Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer answers, or M0-48 settles the shape. |
| OQ-547 | 100k rows or 50k rows for the 55 fps budget (M0-53 spec Q-7)? | The blueprint budget table says 50k; the issue and the design entry say 100k. Default: 100k (the stricter). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms and the blueprint is aligned. |
| OQ-548 | Row height: 24 px or the density token (M0-53 spec Q-8)? | `scale-first` says a fixed 24 px; the design tokens have comfortable rows at 32 px and compact at 24 px (OQ-343, open). Default: the grid follows the density token (AC-17) and chooses no number. Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-343 is answered. |
| OQ-549 | Clipboard policy: row cap, formula injection and the secure-context requirement (M0-53 spec Q-9). | Defaults: (a) cap of 5,000 copied rows (equal to the 25 x 200 resident rows); (b) not decided: a value that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` can run as a formula when pasted into a spreadsheet, and the choice is to prefix it or copy it verbatim; (c) `navigator.clipboard.writeText` needs a secure context and a user gesture, `PlatformBridge` has no clipboard method and `app://` behaviour is M2-P15. AC-11 pins a cap and an error path, not (b). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer rules on (b) and (c). |
| OQ-550 | Shape of `GridKeybinding` before the command registry exists (M0-53 spec Q-10)? | The registry (tinykeys) has not landed and tinykeys is not in the catalog. Default: the chord is a small structure of our own that the registry can feed later; the alternative is to defer `keybindings` to the registry WP. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the shape or defers it. |
| OQ-551 | Register an explicit list of Community modules instead of `AllCommunityModule` (M0-53 spec Q-11)? | To protect the feature-chunk budget (<= 200 kB gzip) and to make AC-3 a statement about names. Default: an explicit list (infinite row model, row selection, column auto size and the like, chosen when the spike runs); the S-04 harness used `AllCommunityModule`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-552 | Where does the grid's CSP nonce come from at run time (M0-53 spec Q-12)? | Related to OQ-231, OQ-233 and OQ-347. The CSP rules for the grid (`styleNonce`, function getters only, no `unsafe-eval`) come from the ADR-021 addendum, still marked proposed. Options: the `csp-nonce` meta tag read by the adapter, or a token the app provides. `ngCspNonce` is not set on the web shell yet. AC-16 runs under the harness policy, not the real shell. Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-231 and OQ-347 are answered. |
| OQ-553 | Is accessibility proved by component specs until M0-60 (M0-53 spec Q-13)? | Same family as OQ-342. Default: role, name, focus and live-region assertions in component specs, not axe; AG Grid's own ARIA grid roles are relied on and not re-tested. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-342. | OQ-342 is answered or M0-60 lands. |
| OQ-554 | How is the `ui-grid` project created at `libs/ui/grid` with the `library` generator (M0-53 spec Q-14)? | The generator creates `libs/<dir>/<name>` with the project name equal to the directory name, but the UI projects are `ui-design` at `libs/ui/design` with alias `@app/ui-design`. Default: run `pnpm nx g @app/generators:library grid --type=ui-lib --importPath=@app/ui-grid` (to be confirmed with `--help`) and rename the project in `project.json`, as `ui-design` evidently did. If the generator cannot produce this, the implementer stops and asks; the scaffold is not hand-written. Status: open - working default in use. | The generator's behaviour is confirmed, or the maintainer names the way. |
| OQ-555 | How are AG Grid's built-in English strings handled (M0-53 spec Q-15)? | Default: the adapter forwards every string it can show to `labels` through the locale-text hook; any string it cannot intercept is a gap the S-14 run must list. Honest wording applies to every label (`@app/honest-wording`). Status: open - working default in use. | The S-14 run lists the gaps. |
| OQ-556 | Where is S-14 recorded (M0-53 spec Q-16)? | Default: a new `docs/architecture/s-14-ag-grid-infinite-100k.md` plus an ADR-021 addendum; the alternative is only an addendum. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |

## Spec questions: M0-38, M0-41 and M0-75

The three specs were approved and signed off by the maintainer with their defaults (`docs/specs/M0-38.md`, `M0-41.md`, `M0-75.md`). Each question in their "Risks / questions" sections is recorded below as a row, so the maintainer can answer here. Every row is **open - working default in use**: the default named is what the spec assumes and what an implementation session follows until the row is answered; nothing here decides a question. A row that repeats an existing question points to it, and the answer goes there.

Ids: M0-38 Q-1 to Q-14 are OQ-560 to OQ-573 (Q-n is OQ-(559+n)); OQ-574 was used by M0-38 slice A, OQ-575 to OQ-578 by slice B; OQ-579 by slice C. M0-41 Q-1 to Q-20 are OQ-580 to OQ-599 (Q-n is OQ-(579+n)). M0-75 Q-1 to Q-10 are OQ-620 to OQ-629 (Q-n is OQ-(619+n)). OQ-630 and up record the review findings below.

### M0-38: settle lab cells

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-560 | Are the six slices approved, who opens the four child issues, and how does #34 close (M0-38 spec Q-1)? | Default: the spec's Slices table; #34 keeps slices A and B (`oversize-approved`, or A becomes its own issue), its title and AC-1 are narrowed to `CF-SETTLE-01` (the headline cells: AC on 4.0 to 4.2, TAIL, the 3.13 drop), `Closes #34` goes on the slice B commit, and C to F are new issues (ADR-033). A slice over 600 lines moves its named cut to a new issue. Blocks the slice it names. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer approves the slices (or asks for `oversize-approved`) and opens or names the issues for C to F. |
| OQ-561 | Is the counter reading fixed in the landed M0-40 projections by M0-38 slice A (M0-38 spec Q-2)? | Slice A edits `projections-stamp.ts`, `-carry.ts`, `-dr.ts` and `era-broker.ts` (M0-40 files) and `projections-down.ts` (M0-39). Default: yes, because the nack cells name `CF-STAMP-01` and could never be confirmed otherwise, and no evidence exists yet to invalidate. Alternative: a follow-up issue on M0-40 that lands before M0-38 slice B. If the lab shows 4.0 to 4.2 does send `x-acquired-count`, `counters.ts` is the one place to change. See also OQ-630 and OQ-631, the code-review findings this slice is proposed to fix. Status: decided - yes (maintainer, before slice A); fixed by M0-38 slice A except the `projections-down.ts` refactor (OQ-574). | Done: `counters.ts` is the one place to change if the lab shows otherwise. |
| OQ-562 | Which cells can M0-38 confirm alone (M0-38 spec Q-3)? | 30 of 40. The 7 nack cells need `CF-STAMP-01`, which has no `minus1` queue (OQ-557); 3 cells (4.3 finite released, HTTP, modified df=false) need `CF-CARRY-03` legs that do not exist (OQ-600). Default: each WP's projection speaks only for the fields its scenario measures (OQ-518); M0-38 does not edit `cf-stamp-01.ts` or `cf-carry-03.ts`; slice B's report names the cells still blocked. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer answers OQ-557 (add a `minus1` queue: about 40 lines in M0-40's scenario plus a test) and OQ-600 (the AMQP 1.0 and HTTP legs: #261). |
| OQ-563 | Shape and owner of the AMQP 1.0 DSL steps (M0-38 spec Q-4)? | `LabScenario.protocol` becomes the protocol of the action under test; the steps are `openReceiver`, `grantCredit` (exactly one), `receive`, `settleAmqp10` (names proposed); settle is followed by `awaitReady`. The DSL offers no credit count, so a scenario cannot make the adapter's forbidden grant above one by accident. Default: slice D of M0-38 owns the widening and lands it as its own commit before #259 and #261 start. Same question as OQ-425, OQ-515 and OQ-504. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-425. | OQ-425 is answered. |
| OQ-564 | How does the 3.13.7 lab broker get AMQP 1.0 (M0-38 spec Q-5)? | Seven cells (3.13 released and modified df=true at `unset`, finite and -1, and the modified df=false drop) need `rabbitmq_amqp1_0`, which 3.13 only has as a plugin and 4.x must not list (OQ-84). Options: a per-version plugin file copied by the Dockerfile, or a `--build-arg` from the testkit (`args.ts`). The recorded `imageDigest` is the pinned base digest, not the built lab image (OQ-424), so a changed plugin list does not change what the evidence says about the image: is that acceptable, or should the artefact also name the lab files? Default: slice E, a per-version file, base digests untouched; until E lands the 3.13 AMQP 1.0 cells are `unverified` (no live leg). `testing/broker-lab/` is not on the `guard-paths` list but feeds the evidence, so a maintainer reviews it as protected (OQ-86). Blocks slice E. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks the mechanism and rules on the digest question. |
| OQ-565 | How is `if-dc-gt-limit` told from `no` on 4.3 (M0-38 spec Q-6)? | On 4.3 a nack, a release, an HTTP get or a `modified` with delivery-failed false never raises the delivery count, so no number of returns tells the 4.3 finite cells' `if-dc-gt-limit` from `no`. Default: the field is left out of the projection (not compared); `CF-CARRY-03` is the scenario that decides it (OQ-600). Related: OQ-503, OQ-610. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer rules on partial projections at OQ-503. |
| OQ-566 | When has a return been processed (M0-38 spec Q-7)? | `awaitReady` waits for the ready count to hold still. A nack that drops the message (3.13 with -1) changes nothing the count shows, and an AMQP 1.0 settle is asynchronous (OQ-425, OQ-559); a slow runner could end the wait early. The 0-9-1 `settle` already waits for a round trip on its channel. Default: reuse `awaitReady` (`stableFor` 5 or more, the 1.0 steps use a larger value); AC-12 measures it. If it flakes the options are a longer `stableFor` or an additive, bounded `expect` option on `awaitReady` for the cases that do not drop. Same family as OQ-505. Status: open - working default in use. | AC-12 runs on a lab broker, and the maintainer picks the fix if it flakes. |
| OQ-567 | Are classic queues (the "CQ" of the issue title) in scope (M0-38 spec Q-8)? | The registry has no classic-queue cells (OQ-394). Default: out of scope; `CF-SMOKE-01` already records a classic nack and its position; classic cells belong to the b.3 work (OQ-516). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer says where the classic cells belong. |
| OQ-568 | Keep the `unset` queue and its 21 rounds (M0-38 spec Q-9)? | `unset` is projected on 3.13 only; on 4.x it records the default limit of 20 (message dropped on the 21st return) as evidence for people, not as a cell (OQ-394). 21 rounds cost about 45 steps per scenario. Default: keep it (the precedent is `CF-CARRY-03`'s `LIMIT + 1`), recorded on every version. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or drops the `unset` queue. |
| OQ-569 | What can `position` say in the settle scenarios (M0-38 spec Q-10)? | With `m1` first in the queue, "returned to its original place" and "returned to the head" are the same observation, and no `CF-SETTLE` cell claims `original`. Default: two messages (`m1`, `t`) are enough; if a cell ever claims `original`, a third message ahead of `m1` is a new scenario. Drop versus dead-letter is OQ-611. Status: open - working default in use. | A cell claims `original`, or the maintainer confirms. |
