| OQ-570 | What happens to a contradicted cell, given the issue's "registry-fix PR" (M0-38 spec Q-11)? | The issue says a contradicted cell is "marked lab-contradicted and a registry-fix PR is opened". Agents never flip a status (I-14) and the maintainer uses no pull requests (OQ-460). Default: the three-way report shows `fail` with the field diff; a human opens the RFC issue and makes the registry edit; no automation opens a PR or sets `lab-contradicted`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms and the issue text is corrected. |
| OQ-571 | Which HTTP variants are recorded, and where do the `ack_requeue_true` counters come from (M0-38 spec Q-12)? | Only `http.ack_requeue_true` has cells; `reject_requeue_true` (fact sheet b.2 row 2) has no `SettleActionSchema` entry. The management `/get` response carries no counter headers in the step record, so counters come from the 0-9-1 drain afterwards, like `CF-STAMP-01`. Default: record `ack_requeue_true` only; a `reject_requeue_true` run and the response's own headers are follow-ups if a cell is added. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or a cell for the other variant is added. |
| OQ-572 | In what order do the M0-38 slices land, given the shared files and the trust-critical cap (M0-38 spec Q-13)? | Suggested order: A, then B; C and D in either order (D first if #259 and #261 should be unblocked first); E before F's 3.13 leg is recorded; F last. `dsl.ts`, `record.ts`, `lab.ts`, `era-broker.ts`, `scenarios/index.ts`, `projections.ts`, the README and this file are shared with #258, #259, #261 and M0-41. The cap of two trust-critical WPs in progress (AGENTS.md section 12, OQ-524) counts each open slice. Default: one M0-38 slice at a time; whoever lands second rebases. Same family as OQ-504. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer sets the order. |
| OQ-573 | Is the separation between the fake broker and the hand-written artefacts right for the settle projections (M0-38 spec Q-14)? | `EraBroker` is made era-faithful to the registry's claims in slice A (AC-5), so it can encode the same mistake as a projection. Default: every projection AC uses a hand-written artefact written from the fact sheet, not recorded off the fake (OQ-510, OQ-522); a reviewer checks this in `/review-wp`. Status: open - working default in use. | The `/review-wp` of the implementation, or the maintainer, confirms it. |
| OQ-574 | What did M0-38 slice A leave out to stay under 600 changed lines? | Slice A landed at about 570 changed lines (spec budget 430, the M0-40 ratio of 1.6x to 2.0x held). Left out, in this order: (1) `projections-down.ts` still has its own per-era table; it reads the same headers as `counters.ts` (the AC-4 clause "it now imports the table" is open, behaviour is unchanged and tested by `projections-down.spec.ts`); (2) the AC-23 test `cells/lab-cells-m0-38.spec.ts` (no CF-SETTLE scenario is wired yet, so it is created with the first wiring in slice B); (3) `CF-CARRY-03` has no 3.13 or 4.0 to 4.2 cell and its projection reads no counter delta, so AC-4 has no per-era artefact for it. Also: a 4.0 to 4.2 artefact read with a 4.3 cell now contradicts it on the counters as well as the position (the AC-12 helper in `lab-cells-m0-40.spec.ts` checks the field on at least one failing cell for CF-DR-01 and CF-DR-02). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the cuts, or asks for them in slice B (the `projections-down.ts` refactor is about 40 lines). |
| OQ-575 | What did M0-38 slice B leave out, and what does it change in the spec (CF-SETTLE-01)? | Slice B wires `CF-SETTLE-01` only and landed at about 594 changed lines (spec budget 590; the first draft was about 1,030 before the cuts). Moved to slice C, in this order: (1) the spec's named cut, AC-12 (determinism IT: `it/settle-determinism.ts` plus a test in `conformance.it.ts`), AC-13 (a contradicting artefact fails with the field diff) and AC-14 (the coverage case for the open CF-SETTLE ids); (2) AC-11 (recording writes nothing into `testing/lab-evidence`: the recorder's own `record.spec.ts` already covers a `local` artefact refused for the evidence tree); (3) `settlePathDivergence` (the findings text of AC-9, which has no consumer yet: the projection already returns null when the get and consume paths of a limit disagree, and the AC-9 test pins that). AC-19 does not apply: slice B changes no DSL step. The `projections-down.ts` refactor of OQ-574 is still open. The conformance `README.md` has a stray "Open questions:" line inside the file table (not from this slice). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the list, or asks for any of it in slice B or C. |
| OQ-576 | `testing/era-broker.ts` does not model a delivery limit of -1: it treats -1 as a number and drops the message on the first return in every era, where the registry says 3.13 drops it, 4.0 to 4.2 returns it to the tail and 4.3 to the head. Fix it, and in which slice? | The fake is used for plumbing only (OQ-510, OQ-522), so the projection specs use hand-written artefacts and the AC-13 test contradicts the finite 4.3 cell, not the `minus1` one. Fixing it touches `era-broker.ts`, which #259 and #261 also edit. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer says whether to fix it in slice C or leave the fake as it is. |
| OQ-577 | What does the CF-SETTLE-01 projection say for a 4.3 nack that is gone after the limit plus one returns? | `yes`: the broker dropped a message that the 4.3 cell says only counts toward the limit when the delivery count is above it (`if-dc-gt-limit`). The cell then shows a field-level mismatch for a human to read, not a gap. If it survives, the field is left out (OQ-565). Alternative: leave it out in both cases. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or asks for the field to be left out (OQ-503). |
| OQ-578 | How is "no registry cell or evidence file changed" (AC-23) tested without turning red when a maintainer merges evidence and flips a status? | `cells/lab-cells-m0-38.spec.ts` compares with `origin/main` (or `CONFORMANCE_BASE`) and asserts only when the change also edits a settle scenario or projection; it skips, saying why, when git cannot name the base. Related: `lab-cells-m0-40.spec.ts` (AC-11) still asserts that `testing/lab-evidence` does not exist, and its DOWN twin in `coverage.spec.ts` asserts the cells stay `code-derived`: both turn red when evidence lands (review finding; M0-40 and M0-39 files). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens a fix for the two older tests. |
| OQ-579 | What did M0-38 slice C (CF-SETTLE-02 and -05) leave out, and what does it assume? | Slice C wires `CF-SETTLE-02` (`basic.reject`) and `CF-SETTLE-05` (management `/get`, `ack_requeue_true`, six queues on the one `http` path) and landed at about 520 changed lines (spec budget 440). Moved to a later slice, in this order: (1) AC-12, the determinism IT (`it/settle-determinism.ts` plus a test in `conformance.it.ts`; needs Docker); (2) AC-13 (a contradicting artefact fails with the field diff and nothing in the repository changes) and AC-14 (the coverage case: `CF-SETTLE-03`, `-04` and `-06` are still listed as ids with no scenario, which `conformance:coverage` already shows); (3) AC-11 (recording writes nothing into `testing/lab-evidence`, scoped to the change); (4) `settlePathDivergence`, the `projections-down.ts` refactor (OQ-574) and the `era-broker.ts` fix for a limit of -1 (OQ-576). Assumed: the management answer carries no counter headers, so the counters of the first delivery are read as none (0), like a first 0-9-1 delivery; if a 4.3 first delivery already shows `x-acquired-count`, the HTTP and nack deltas differ by that amount and `counters.ts` or the HTTP row is the place to change. A 4.3 `reject` that survives the limit plus one returns is projected `no` (a reject raises the delivery count, so survival decides), where the cell says `yes`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the cuts, or asks for any of them in a slice before D lands. |

Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or a lab run settles the idle variant. |

A version that differs is a recorded finding, and a human decides whether it becomes versioned registry data. Status: open - working default in use. | A recorded run shows the real behaviour and the maintainer decides. |

Status: open - working default in use. | The scratch run before slice F. |

Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |

Alternative: make it a finding that stays `unverified`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or picks the alternative. |

Default: the upgrade has its own timeout and launcher mode (AC-23); whether the nightly workflow runs its live leg is a cost decision. ### M0-75: RabbitMQ 4.4 readiness

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-620 | Does a 4.4 GA image exist today, and on what date does the runbook's 14-day clock start (M0-75 spec Q-1)? | The repository only shows "none" on 2026-10-16. Default: no; slices A, D and E go ahead, slice B waits, and the clock starts at the GA publication date a maintainer states in the tracking issue. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer checks for an official `rabbitmq:<4.4.x>-management` image and records the date. |
| OQ-621 | If only alpha builds exist, what is the image source, the pinning rule and the staleness rule (M0-75 spec Q-2)? | Extends OQ-81 and OQ-494. Is an alpha an image at all (the repository says only "packages from rabbitmq/server-packages")? What does an alpha's `/api/overview` report as `rabbitmq_version`? Does pulling it need a credential? Default: slice C is specified only for an OCI image reference pinned by digest, pulled anonymously, replaced by a reviewed change (never floating, never "latest nightly"); if no such image exists, slice C is not started; no secret is added to any workflow. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer answers OQ-81, and a first manual pull shows the version string. |
| OQ-622 | Does the flag `rabbitmq_4.4.0` exist, with what stability, and is the quorum-queue Machine Version 9 on the v4.4.x branch (M0-75 spec Q-3)? | The fact sheet says "9 on main"; A-23 and the runbook give both as examples. Default: slice A begins by reading `rabbit_core_ff.erl` and `rabbit_fifo.erl` (`version()`) at the v4.4.x tag or commit, recording the refs as `RS@` citations (as fact sheet 0.12 did for 4.3.6); if the flag or the number differs, stop and report; the rule is not coded from the A-23 text alone. Status: open - working default in use. | The slice A reading, and a maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-623 | When every node is 4.4 or later but the 4.4 evidence is missing, which era is returned, and how are unprobed Machine Versions treated (M0-75 spec Q-4)? | Default: return 4.3 when the 4.3 evidence holds (otherwise 4.0-4.2 by the existing rule), with a reason. The flag must be positively `enabled`; `disabled` and `unknown` demote. A reported Machine Version below 9 demotes; an empty list does not demote (as for 4.3, the profile already says it was not probed). That a flag-off 4.4 cluster behaves like 4.3 is an assumption until a rolling-upgrade lab run (U-02) shows it. Status: open - working default in use. | Slice A review and the U-02 lab run. |
| OQ-624 | What is an era-4.4 cell when the v4.4.x source matches 4.3, and how are source refs pinned while 4.4 has no tag (M0-75 spec Q-5)? | Default: derive each of the 68 keys from the v4.4.x source and cite it (`RS@v4.4.<tag>:<path> <function>`; `RS@<commit>` while only a branch or alpha exists, never a moving branch name). A key the source does not settle keeps `effect: null` (a gap). A 4.3 effect is never copied by assumption. Cells are made with the `semantics-cell` generator, one invocation per key; a throwaway loop in the scratchpad is acceptable and is not committed. Status: open - working default in use. | Slice D review. |
| OQ-625 | What does "the conformance matrix runs nightly against it" promise, and is evidence for 4.4 recorded before GA (M0-75 spec Q-6)? | Default: it means the lab starts the broker, the scenarios execute, and the report lists the leg; cells stay `unverified` (no baseline, `three-way.ts`). No evidence for an alpha (the recorder and the format accept `x.y.z` only). Evidence is recorded at GA on the exact release version. A live-versus-4.3 divergence report is not built. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or asks for the report as its own issue. |
| OQ-626 | Shape of an alpha entry in `versions.json`: a separate `alpha` array or a `channel` field (M0-75 spec Q-7)? | Default: a separate top-level `alpha` array, so every existing reader of `brokers` (testkit, the recorder, the coverage gate, the driver, the nightly release matrix) keeps seeing releases only and an alpha cannot become a blocking leg or a Tier claim by accident. `versions.json` is a pin the evidence check trusts (OQ-458). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer picks. |
| OQ-627 | How are the M0-75 slices landed, and what counts against the cap of two trust-critical WPs (M0-75 spec Q-8)? | Default: the maintainer opens one issue per slice (A, B, C, D1 to D4, E), each one commit, as an alternative to `oversize-approved` on #66; A, C and D1 to D4 are trust-critical, so they are taken at most two at a time (OQ-524). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer, as for M0-39 and M0-42. |
| OQ-628 | What is "green" for the Tier-1 promotion, what goes in `support` before it, and does 4.2 leave Tier 1 when 4.4 enters (M0-75 spec Q-9)? | Default: this WP never sets `tier-1`. Green means `conformance:coverage --strict` passes for 4.4 on approved CI-recorded evidence and the registry statuses were flipped by a human. Until then the 4.4 entry's `support` is `"unverified, not yet tier-1"` (proposed text; the field is free text today). Whether 4.2 changes tier (community support ended 2026-07-31 per the fact sheet) is not decided here. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer, at GA. |
| OQ-629 | Which follow-ups of the 4.4 readiness work are separate issues (M0-75 spec Q-10)? | Candidates: a release detector (like `tools/platform-lane`), 4.4 golden fixtures, adapter and probe behaviour on 4.4, and review of the other version-ranged tables (hazards, capabilities, warnings, tap rules, provider presets, the four existing 4.4 peek-cap rows). Default: all are separate issues, listed in the rewritten runbook as checklist items; none is done in M0-75. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer opens them. |

## Review findings: lab cells, conformance workflows and gates

Findings of the code reviews that are not yet fixed, recorded so they are not lost. Nothing here is decided and no fix is started: each row stays **open - fix work package pending maintainer go-ahead** until a maintainer says which work package fixes it. OQ-630 to OQ-632 are the three blocking defects the lab-cell review found in the landed M0-39 and M0-40 scenarios and projections; they must be fixed before any `conformance-record` dispatch, so no evidence is recorded from a projection that reads the broker wrongly. OQ-633 and OQ-634 are the blocking items of the M0-42 review that only a maintainer can apply. OQ-635 to OQ-640 are its non-blocking findings (NB-4 to NB-9), and OQ-641 and OQ-642 are non-blocking findings of the M0-46 review (NB-1, NB-2).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-630 | B1: `projections-stamp.ts:58-62` reads `x-acquired-count` and `x-delivery-count` the same way on every era. Which work package fixes it? | The fact sheet (b.1) says 4.0 to 4.2 has only `x-delivery-count`, and it means AC; 3.13 has only DC; 4.3 has both. The era broker emits the 4.3 headers for 4.0 to 4.2 as well, so the landed tests pass and miss the defect. A recording from this projection could confirm or contradict cells for the wrong reason. Proposed fix: M0-38 slice A (`assert/counters.ts`, one reading per era; M0-38 spec Q-2 = OQ-561). Status: closed - fixed by M0-38 slice A (`assert/counters.ts`, hand-written artefacts per era in `projections-lab-cells.spec.ts`). | Done. |
| OQ-631 | B2: `projections-carry.ts:55-67` computes a counter delta across two queues with an absent header read as 0. Which work package fixes it? | The delta is computed across two queues and a header that is absent is read as 0, so on 4.3 the projection reports a false `fail` of `deltaAC` -1. Related: OQ-601 (what the carry-over projection calls a delta). Proposed fix: M0-38 slice A together with B1 (OQ-561). Status: closed - fixed by M0-38 slice A (a delta across two queues is projected only for a header both sides send, with a test for 4.3). | Done. |
| OQ-632 | B3: the CF-DR-01 and CF-DR-02 artefacts include management statistics read at fixed moments under the default statistics interval (about 5 s). Can a live run and the recorded evidence differ run to run? | The live leg is compared with the recorded artefact (a replay must equal evidence), and a statistics value read at a fixed moment can fall before or after a refresh, so a live run and the evidence can differ from run to run. Related: OQ-519 (freshness of `messages_delayed` and `next_delayed_at`), OQ-558, OQ-604. Status: fixed in 1f6e9f3 and 33212b4 (M0-FIX-1 slices 1 and 1b; the default, the class of each count, failed in CI, see OQ-647; `mgmtQueue` now records the HTTP status only, and nothing in `testing/broker-lab` changed). | The maintainer names the fix (reduce the statistics to a class, wait for a refresh, or set the lab interval) and a work package carries it; two live runs of CF-DR-01 and CF-DR-02 on one broker then produce equal output (the CI check `dr-determinism.ts` is the arbiter). |
| OQ-647 | M0-FIX-1 slice 1b (33212b4): the management statistics of a queue cannot be part of a compared artefact. Does the maintainer want them back as evidence, and how? | The default taken for OQ-632 (record the class of each count, `zero`, `nonzero` or `absent`) failed its own arbiter: the live check `dr-determinism.ts` in CI run 39452047612 (4.3.6) found the first CF-DR-01 run with every count `absent` (the statistics of a new queue had not been emitted) and the second with `zero`. A class cannot fix this: a statistic read right after an action is stale, or missing before the first emission, and one read at about 6 s may still be from before the delayed message expired. So `mgmtQueue` records the HTTP status only, and CF-DR-01 keeps its stats steps (they prove the API answered at those moments). No projection or registry cell read the counts: the cells are judged on the `basic.get` reads, which the scenario brackets with fixed waits. Waiting for a refresh before each read would move the `get` reads that follow it, which the experiment depends on; lowering `collect_statistics_interval` is a `testing/broker-lab` change. Related: OQ-519, OQ-558, OQ-604, OQ-632. Status: open - default taken (status only); the recorded evidence says nothing about the counts. The live check is green on 4.3.6 with it (CI run 39455373516: CF-DR-01 and CF-DR-02 twice each, equal); other versions were not run. | The maintainer decides that the counts are not needed as evidence, or asks for a freshness-aware design (a separate, bounded wait for the first emission before the timed section, and a lab statistics interval set in `testing/broker-lab`), and a work package carries it. |
| OQ-633 | M0-42 review, blocking: CODEOWNERS lines for `/tools/conformance-ci/` and `/.github/actions/` are missing. | CODEOWNERS is a protected path, so an agent only proposes the lines. Proposed text: `/tools/conformance-ci/ @RafaelJCamara` and `/.github/actions/ @RafaelJCamara`, next to the unapplied lines of OQ-86 and OQ-458 and the control OQ-493 relies on. Status: open - fix work package pending maintainer go-ahead (a maintainer applies the lines). | The maintainer adds the lines. |
| OQ-634 | M0-42 review, blocking: is `oversize-approved` set on #38, or the size accepted after the fact? | The review asks for the label (AGENTS.md section 5 allows more than 800 changed lines only with it). Same kind of question as OQ-498. Status: open - fix work package pending maintainer go-ahead (only a maintainer sets the label). | The maintainer labels #38 `oversize-approved` or records that the size is accepted. |
| OQ-635 | M0-42 NB-4: the status-step run text is not pinned by a test. | The run text of the status step is not pinned, so it can change without a test failing (`conformance-workflows-selftest` pins the job shape, OQ-493). Non-blocking. Status: fixed in cb961fe (M0-FIX-1 slice 2a; `LEG_STATUS_RUN` pins the whole run text of the leg-status step of both legs; the selftest plants seven changes in each leg). | A selftest pins the text, or the maintainer accepts the gap. |
| OQ-636 | M0-42 NB-5: the conformance CI command-line tools are untested. | The command-line entry points of the conformance CI tools (`tools/conformance-ci/`) have no tests. Non-blocking. Status: fixed in 035fac7 (M0-FIX-1 slice 2b; `tools/conformance-ci/src/cli.spec.ts` runs the three tools as processes). | Tests run the entry points, or the maintainer accepts the gap. |
| OQ-637 | M0-42 NB-6: the exact `CONFORMANCE_PROJECTS` list is not pinned. | No test pins the exact list, so a project can drop out of it without a test failing. Non-blocking. Status: fixed in 035fac7 (M0-FIX-1 slice 2b; the exact list is pinned by a test, and every name must be a real Nx project). | A test pins the list, or the list is derived from project tags. |
| OQ-638 | M0-42 NB-7: with push concurrency, can an intermediate commit be skipped? | Push concurrency can skip an intermediate commit, which then has no `conformance` run. Non-blocking. Status: decided by default in cb961fe (M0-FIX-1 slice 2a): every commit needs its run, so a push is its own concurrency group (`conformance-pr-${{ github.event.pull_request.number || github.sha }}`) and is never cancelled; the cost is OQ-648. | The maintainer decides whether every commit needs a run, or accepts a run on the latest commit only. |
| OQ-639 | M0-42 NB-8: a file name can inject text into a workflow log. | A file name can inject text into a log (log injection). Non-blocking. Status: fixed in 035fac7 (M0-FIX-1 slice 2b; `printableName` replaces control, format and line-separator characters with `?` before a name enters a reason; the step summary and the log show only reasons). | File names are escaped or not printed, with a test. |
| OQ-640 | M0-42 NB-9: only `conformance-pr.yml` may define a job named `conformance`. How is that enforced? | Only `conformance-pr.yml` may define a job named `conformance`, because the required check is that name; nothing enforces it today. Related: OQ-493. Non-blocking. Status: fixed in cb961fe (M0-FIX-1 slice 2a; `checkSingleConformanceJob` reads every workflow file; the selftest runs it on the real files and on planted copies). | A selftest asserts that no other workflow defines the job, or the maintainer accepts review as the control. |
| OQ-641 | M0-46 NB-1: the "AMQP handshake did not run" gate is untested. | No test covers the "AMQP handshake did not run" gate. Non-blocking. Status: fixed in 52d8e5e (M0-FIX-1 slice 3; a test removes the handshake result from the facts and requires the four `amqp.*` gates closed with the reason and source; the mutant "open when the result is missing" fails it). | A test covers the gate, or the maintainer accepts the gap. |
| OQ-642 | M0-46 NB-2: a `catch` swallows errors in `gates/index.ts`. | The `catch` in `gates/index.ts` swallows errors, so a failure is not reported (errors are Problems, blueprint 3.6). Non-blocking. Status: fixed in 52d8e5e (M0-FIX-1 slice 3; the gate stays closed, as the fail-closed rule needs, and its `source` now carries the class of the error, the constructor name only and never the message (I-10); a thrown non-error reads `non-error`; there is no logger in this library, so the decision is the diagnostic). | The catch maps the error to a Problem or rethrows, with a test. |
| OQ-648 | M0-FIX-1 slice 2a: with one concurrency group per commit outside a pull request, two pushes to `main` in quick succession run their lab matrices at the same time. Is the load acceptable, or should runs on `main` queue? | `conformance-pr.yml` used the ref as the group, so a push to `main` waited behind the running one and a third push replaced the waiting one (a group keeps one running and one pending run), and the replaced commit never got its `conformance` run (OQ-638). Grouping by commit gives every commit a run, at the price of up to as many concurrent lab jobs (5 versions, 2 shards, Docker on hosted runners) as commits landed within the run time (about 20 to 45 minutes). The alternative that bounds the load keeps the old group and accepts a run on the latest commit only. Status: open - default taken (every commit runs); no cost measured yet. | The maintainer accepts the load, or asks for a queue (a shared group for `main` with the skipped commits listed in the run), and a work package carries it. |

## M0-75 slice A: the 4.4 era rule

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-643 | M0-75 AC-8 says a 4.4 pre-release is `unverified-minor` "with `lastVerified` containing `4.4.0`", but `verificationOf` gives `unverified-patch` in that case (same series, and a pre-release is never `verified`). Which is meant? | Slice A kept the existing rule (changing it would also change `4.3.6-rc.1`) and its AC-8 test asserts `unverified-minor` when `lastVerified` has no 4.4 entry and `unverified-patch` when it lists `4.4.0`; in both the pre-release is never `verified`. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the existing rule or asks for a pre-release to count as a new minor. |
| OQ-644 | Slice A coded the flag name `rabbitmq_4.4.0` and Machine Version 9 from amendment A-23 alone, against the OQ-622 default ("stop if the source is not read"). Are they right for the v4.4.x branch? | The maintainer told slice A to proceed with the spec defaults without browsing external sources. Both values are two named constants in `libs/shared/semantics/src/era.ts` (`FF_4_4_NAME`, `MIN_QQ_MACHINE_VERSION_FOR_4_4`), commented as unverified; the fact sheet says Machine Version 9 only "on main". Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer or slice D reads `rabbit_core_ff.erl` and `rabbit_fifo.erl` (`version()`) at the v4.4.x tag or commit, cites them as `RS@` sources and confirms or corrects the constants. |

## M0-41 slice A: recording broker refusals

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-645 | After the broker closes a connection on one `tryGet`, a `tryGet` on another channel of that connection is recorded as refused with the connection's reply without sending the get (M0-41 AC-3, STREAM-01's `p2`). Is that the right record? | The driver keeps the connection's code and reply name and answers every later `tryGet` on its channels with them, because the library only says "Channel ended" for the second channel. The record then says what the broker did to the connection, not that it answered a second request. The alternative is to send the get and read the library's failure, which cannot name the reply. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the record, or asks for the second channel to be recorded as a distinct outcome. |
| OQ-646 | A `tryGet` on a channel the broker already closed with a channel-level reply aborts the run ("closed before the get") instead of recording a second refusal. Is that the right fail-closed rule? | The broker never answered that request, so recording a refusal would claim an answer that was not given. A scenario that wants the second probe opens a new channel. Slice A also treats any connection close that carries a numeric reply code (including 320 `connection forced`) as the broker's reply. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms, or asks to record the second attempt as a refusal, or to exclude `connection forced`. |
| OQ-649 | M0-38 slice D: the era broker (`testing/era-broker.ts`) has no AMQP 1.0 half. Is that the right cut, and who adds it? | Slice D landed the DSL steps, the `Amqp10Host` interface, `openLabHost` (the existing `driver/amqp10-raw.ts`, `rhea` is already confined and declared here, so no lint or dependency change was needed) and `MemoryHost`; the spec names the era broker the cut when the slice runs past its budget (it did: about 590 changed lines against 500). A run of a scenario with AMQP 1.0 steps on the era broker fails with "no AMQP 1.0 connection". Default: the first slice that needs an era-broker plumbing run of a 1.0 scenario (#259 or #261 slice D legs, or M0-38 slice F) adds `amqp10` to `EraBroker` next to its first 1.0 scenario. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the cut or asks for a follow-up issue. |
| OQ-650 | M0-38 slice D: `openLabHost` addresses the receiver as `/queues/<name>` (the 4.x style, the one `it/drivers-smoke.ts` uses). How are 3.13.7 receivers addressed once slice E gives that image the AMQP 1.0 plugin? | The 3.13 plugin takes a plain queue name as the address and does not know `/queues/`; the mapping must come from the lab (OQ-564) and not from a version comparison in the host (I-3). Default: slice E owns it and passes the address style through the lab endpoint or the scenario host options; until then the 3.13 AMQP 1.0 cells stay `unverified` and no 3.13 1.0 scenario is recorded. Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-564 is answered. |

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-651 | A fact whose `appliesTo` excludes the lab version being run (`CF-OPS-UPGRADE-01` on 4.2.9): skipped or reported? | `evaluateCase` returns no result for it and `settleCase` treats no result as `unverified`, so the cell test is skipped with nothing to show and `--strict` does not fail it (nothing was asked of that version); the coverage gate does not ask about it either. The alternative is a result per version saying "not applicable", which `--strict` would have to tell apart from `unverified`. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms, or asks for an explicit "not applicable" result. |

Is that the intended order? | The check is what stops an id from counting as covered because nobody wrote the fact; the lenient gate is unaffected (it fails only a recorded contradiction), and `--strict` already fails the same ids with "no scenario can record it" (OQ-598). A fact whose `appliesTo` names no lab version also fails `--strict`. Status: open - working default in use. | The slices that add the facts land, or a maintainer wants the check lenient. |

## Spec questions: M0-48

The spec `docs/specs/M0-48.md` (svc-explorer: inventory windows, overview, `explorer.window` topic, S-13) has 23 questions in its "Risks / questions" section. Each is recorded below as a row, so the maintainer can answer here. Q-n of the spec is OQ-(652+n). Every row is **open - working default in use**: the default named is what the spec assumes and what an implementation session follows until the row is answered; nothing here decides a question. A row that repeats an existing question points to it, and the answer goes there.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-653 | Is `svc-explorer` trust-critical, and is `/libs/engine/svc-explorer/` added to CODEOWNERS (M0-48 spec Q-1)? | The issue (#44) carries `trust:critical`, but blueprint 2.1 and `CODEOWNERS` do not name this module. Default: follow the label (the module is generated with `--trustCritical`, the review includes the adversarial pass, a maintainer approves) and propose the line `/libs/engine/svc-explorer/ @RafaelJCamara`. CODEOWNERS is a protected path, so an agent only proposes the line. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer applies the line, or removes the label from #44. |
| OQ-654 | Split M0-48 into eight child issues, or approve `oversize-approved` (M0-48 spec Q-2)? | About 3,200 changed lines by estimate against the 800 limit of AGENTS.md section 5; the slices A to H of the spec have hard budgets of at most 600 and named cuts. Default: eight child issues (A to H), proposed in the spec and not opened. Same family as OQ-545 and OQ-634. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens the child issues, or labels #44 `oversize-approved`. |
| OQ-655 | Which window shape does `inventory.query` use, and where does the type live (M0-48 spec Q-3)? | Same question as OQ-546. Default: `offset` and `limit` (default 200, at most 500) plus an optional pinned index `version`, defined in `@app/contracts`; the grid adapter (M0-53) adapts to it. A cursor is the alternative. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-546. | OQ-546 is answered. |
| OQ-656 | Are windows served from the resident index, and what does the first query get before the first sweep ends (M0-48 spec Q-4)? | Default: from the index; the first query starts the sweep and is answered at once from what is loaded, labelled `loading` and `complete: false`, with filters and sorts applying to the loaded rows only. Alternative: an interactive-lane passthrough page until the first sweep is complete (one broker request per window; each page re-sorts the full list on the broker). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-657 | How does the UI tell `explorer.window` which keys are visible (M0-48 spec Q-5)? | A subscription's `params` are fixed when `sub` is sent and no frame changes them. Default: `params.keys` (at most 500) and a resubscribe (`unsub` then `sub`), debounced at 100 ms by the client; no protocol change. Alternatives: a new client frame that replaces the params, or an action that sets them (a change of `live-frames.ts` and the hub). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-658 | How does the topic tell the grid that membership changed (M0-48 spec Q-6)? | Default: a reserved item with the key `~version` and no row, published when a sweep added or removed rows; the grid refreshes its cache at most every 5 s (scale-first 10). Alternatives: use `status` frames, or a second topic. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-659 | What are the "overview totals from `enable_queue_totals`" when statistics are off (M0-48 spec Q-7)? | The 4.2.9 stats-off recording of `/api/overview` has `object_totals` (queues, exchanges, connections) and no `queue_totals` or `message_stats`, and the adapter drops `enable_queue_totals` on that path; queue lists with the flag do carry the three message counts. Default: message totals are the sum over the queue-list sweep, labelled `source: 'queue-sweep'` with `asOf` and `complete`; channel and consumer counts are absent. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or names another source. |
| OQ-660 | Where does an alarm come from, and what does it look like (M0-48 spec Q-8)? | No recording holds an alarm; stats-off `/api/nodes` drops resource information; the only failure body in the repository is a hand-written test string; the Prometheus recordings carry `rabbitmq_alarms_*` gauges but no reader exists. Default: the per-node flags of `/api/nodes` (`mem_alarm`, `disk_free_alarm`, from the broker's HTTP API, not from this repository) and then `/api/health/checks/alarms`; what cannot be attributed to a node and a resource is `unknown`, never none. The AC-13 shape is assumed. Status: open - working default in use. | A recording of a raised alarm exists (a lab change and a `record-golden` run by a maintainer or CI), or the Prometheus reader lands. |
| OQ-661 | What does the overview show for rates (M0-48 spec Q-9)? | Default: statistics on, rates from the broker overview (`available`, `source: 'broker-overview'`); statistics off, `unavailable` with `reason: 'statistics-off'`; a broker that reports none, `unavailable` with `reason: 'not-reported'`; per-queue rates never. Prometheus-derived rates belong to the metrics work packages. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-662 | Sweep strategy and what S-13 decides (M0-48 spec Q-10). | Default: per vhost, paged at 500, with `disable_stats` and `enable_queue_totals` (scale-first 8.1). S-13 measures per vhost against global, page sizes 100, 200 and 500, and an unpaged request sent by the lab script outside the engine. The adapter cannot send an unpaged list (DC-48), so adopting that is a decision for the adapter. Status: open - working default in use. | The S-13 numbers are recorded and the maintainer confirms or changes the default. |
| OQ-663 | Sweep cadence, demand and warm start (M0-48 spec Q-11). | Default: `clamp(30 s, 15 min, 50 x last sweep server time)` (blueprint 8), the first sweep on first demand, an idle stop after 10 minutes without demand (the "hot" definition of scale-first 9), no persisted snapshot (`inventory_snapshot`, a migration, is a later work package). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the numbers. |
| OQ-664 | Resident limits and topic bounds (M0-48 spec Q-12). | Defaults: 250,000 rows and 128 MiB per connection (the blueprint's 1M rows is the team total), 5,000 vhosts per sweep, topic ring 1,000 items and 1 MiB, `maxHz` 2, window 200 by default and 500 at most, search 50 results. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-665 | In-process index or an index worker, and the I-5 gate (M0-48 spec Q-13). | The blueprint puts the index in a worker; the issue names `internal/index.ts` and no worker. Default: in-process, sort permutations cached per (version, kind, column, direction) and few kept; AC-24 asserts event-loop delay p99 at most 20 ms at 20,000 queues and only prints 100,000. A sort of 100,000 rows may exceed the budget; then a worker or a chunked sort becomes a new issue. Status: open - working default in use. | The 100,000-queue measurement is recorded, and the maintainer decides. |
| OQ-666 | Is there a regex filter in the engine (M0-48 spec Q-14)? | Default: none. The filter is a substring of the name plus exact facets (vhost, type, state). A regex over 100,000 names on the event loop is a denial-of-service risk, and a linear-time matcher is not in the catalog (a dependency decision, AGENTS.md section 4). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer decides whether a regex filter is wanted, and with which matcher. |
| OQ-667 | Which inventory kinds does M0-48 serve (M0-48 spec Q-15)? | Default: `queues`, `exchanges`, `connections` (slices A to C) and `bindings` as slice H (a vhost is required, loaded on demand, resident cap 500,000 rows per scale-first); no channels, consumers, policies, users or definitions. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the list. |
| OQ-668 | Action ids, routes, permission and exposure (M0-48 spec Q-16). | Default: `inventory.query` (`POST /connections/{cid}/inventory/queries`), `overview.get` (`GET /connections/{cid}/overview`), `search.query` (`POST /connections/{cid}/search`), topic `explorer.window`, permission `broker.read` for all, no MCP and no CLI exposure (as `profile.get` and `probe.run`). Generated file names (`inventory-query.ts`) replace the issue's `inventory.query.ts`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the ids and the exposure. |
| OQ-669 | Is the module wired into the composition root in M0-48 (M0-48 spec Q-17)? | Default: not wired, as M0-46. `ExplorerModule` returns `topicSources` and `stop()`; `apps/engine`'s `EngineModule` has no topics and `coreModules` holds no service yet. Same family as OQ-472. Status: answered for the registration, the topic sources and the drain phase by issue #278 (docs/specs/M0-ENGINE-WIRING.md, OQ-764 to OQ-771). | Closed by #278. |
| OQ-670 | Row-level access on the shared inventory in team mode (M0-48 spec Q-18). | Default: none in M0-48; every principal that may see the connection and holds `broker.read` sees every row of its index. The team server must not expose this module to principals with partial visibility until `svc-access` filters rows on read (blueprint 7.3). Status: open - working default in use. | `svc-access` lands, or the maintainer names another control. |
| OQ-671 | The `perf` project and the thresholds of `perf:explorer-20k` (M0-48 spec Q-19). | Same family as OQ-544. Default: slice F creates `apps/perf` with the `library` generator if no perf work package has landed; the harness is node-only; p95 of engine time per window at most 100 ms and event-loop delay p99 at most 20 ms asserted at 20,000, 100,000 measured and printed; where it runs in CI is not decided. Status: open - working default in use; answer at OQ-544. | OQ-544 is answered. |
| OQ-672 | Scope and record of S-13 (M0-48 spec Q-20). | Default: the five lab versions at 20,000 queues and 100,000 on 4.2.9 and 4.3.6 only, recorded in an ADR-024 addendum plus `docs/architecture/s-13-mgmt-api-cost-100k.md` (the S-14 precedent, OQ-556). The run needs Docker on Linux; the numbers cannot be produced in an agent session without it. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the scope and the run happens (CI or a maintainer). |
| OQ-673 | Copy the visibility helper, or lift it to a shared place (M0-48 spec Q-21)? | Default: copy the 15-line `requireVisible` of `svc-capabilities` into `svc-explorer/src/internal/visible.ts`, because a service never imports a service. Alternative: lift it into `@app/kernel`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-674 | Policy fields that statistics-off drops below 4.2.5 (M0-48 spec Q-22). | Default: rows leave a missing field absent and `freshness.hazards` names the hazard; the effective-config resolver that would compute them locally is a later work package. Observation: the hazard text says `policy` is missing, but the 4.0.9 and 4.1.8 stats-off recordings carry `policy` and lack only `delivery_limit` and `effective_policy_definition`; the AC follows the recordings, the hazard data is not changed. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, and the hazard text is corrected by its owner if the recordings are right. |
| OQ-675 | How much search does M0-48 build (M0-48 spec Q-23)? | The issue lists `search` among the files and gives no criterion. Default: a ranked substring and prefix search over names in the index, at most 50 results, p95 at most 50 ms at 20,000 queues, as slice G (the first cut); the character-bitmask prefilter and the fzf-style scorer of scale-first 8.1 are not built. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the scope or moves search to its own issue. |
| OQ-676 | How is M0-53 split into slices, and what is slice A (M0-53 slice plan)? | The spec estimates 2,400 lines and proposes a (core, 1,300) and b (owned features, 700); a is still above the 800 limit. Default: slice A (no AG Grid, so no protected-path change) is the `ui-grid` project from the `library` generator, the `EngineWindowSource` contract, `WindowedDatasource` (AC-5 to AC-8 at datasource level) and the fake window source; slice B is columns, column state and selection (written, parked on branch `wi/M0-53-ui-grid-b-wip`); then the AG Grid component, theme bridge and `bundle-check` (needs the dependency); then the owned features (AC-2, 11, 14); then `apps/perf` and `perf:grid-100k`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the slices or adds `oversize-approved`. |
| OQ-677 | Which live changes refresh the grid cache, and what are the datasource limits (M0-53 slice A)? | AC-8 says an insert or remove refreshes at most every 5 s, but `GridRowChange` has only `upsert` and `remove`, and an upsert of an id that is not shown cannot be told from an insert. Default: an upsert of a shown id is applied in place, an upsert of an id not shown is dropped and counted (`changesDropped`), a `remove` of a shown id refreshes (first at once, then at most every 5 s); inserts are seen on the next refresh or sort. Also by default: `maxConcurrent` 2 requests in flight with a wait queue of 25 blocks, then the block fails; `GridStats` gains `staleAnswers` (late answers dropped by an aborted request). Status: open - working default in use. | The explorer WP (M0-54) or the maintainer decides whether the contract needs an `insert` change kind. |

## M0-48 slice A: svc-explorer skeleton and the inventory.query contract

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-678 | Slice A passed its 450-line budget; the `connections` kind was cut (M0-48 spec, Slices row A). | Slice A landed `queues` and `exchanges` only: `INVENTORY_KINDS` is `['queues', 'exchanges']`, there is no `ConnectionRowSchema`. The hand-written lines are about 520 against the 450 budget even after the cut, mainly the shared contract types, the scripted broker fake and the AC-1 test. `inventory.query` is a stub that answers an empty window with `freshness.state: 'unavailable'` and `complete: false`, so the generated specs pass until slice C writes the real body. The generator tests and the golden path xplorer.queues.list used the module name svc-explorer as a sample; they now use svc-catalog (with --feature=explorer) so the real module can exist. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens "M0-48 connections kind" (row schema, fake, union branch) to land after slice C, and proposes the CODEOWNERS line `/libs/engine/svc-explorer/ @RafaelJCamara` (OQ-653). |
| OQ-679 | Slice B passed its 600-line budget by about 480 lines, and the cost-based cadence was cut (M0-48 spec, Slices row B). | Slice B landed the bounded index (rows and bytes caps, counted drops, versions, filter, sort, window, change feed), the sweeper (per vhost, 500 per page, stats-off flags, background lane, single-flight, last good version kept and marked stale) and the row mapping, with the tests AC-2 to AC-5. The hand-written lines are about 1,080 against 600, mainly the index (about 310), the sweeper (about 280), the two specs (about 255) and the scripted broker (per-vhost paths, failure hook, about 135). The named cut was applied: a fixed 60 s wait between sweeps and a manual `stop()`, with no idle stop and no `clamp(30 s, 15 min, 50 x server time)`; the AC-5 test proves demand, one sweep in flight, the fixed wait and the stop. The sweep is not run through a real BrokerScheduler yet (the scripted port stands in; AC-15 and slice F use the real one), so the backed-off interval of AC-4 is the fixed wait. Page installs are chunked by the page requests; `install` itself is O(rows) and its event-loop cost is measured by AC-24 in slice F. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens "M0-48 sweep cadence" (the formula and the idle stop, about 45 lines with tests) and decides whether the overrun needs a re-split of slice C onward (OQ-545). |
| OQ-680 | Slice C passed its 500-line budget (M0-48 spec, Slices row C); the `exchanges` cut was not applied. | Slice C landed `inventory.query` over the index for `queues` and `exchanges` with the tests AC-6 to AC-10: about 490 lines of code and fixtures and about 515 lines of tests against 500. The maintainer said not to split, so the cut was not applied. Decisions the slice made, to confirm: (1) slice B's index retained only the previous version, but a sweep installs two kinds, so a pinned scroll could not survive one sweep (AC-7); the index now has `installAll` and a sweep over a warm index installs every kind as one new version. (2) `freshness.expected` is the broker's own `total_count` summed over the first page of each vhost, kept by the sweeper (`Sweeper.status`); a first read installs a kind only when it is whole, so while the first queue list runs the answer has `loaded: 0`, an empty window and the count to come, and `complete` is index-wide, so queues are not called complete while the exchanges are still unread (spec Q-4: possibly nothing). (3) AC-9 says the totals are present on both recordings: they are present on every queue except the stream queue, which the stats-off recordings carry with no `messages` fields; the row has none rather than a zero. (4) The hazard ids that concern queue rows (`stats-off-policy-fields-missing`) are a small table in `internal/freshness.ts`; the profile decides whether one applies (I-3). (5) `createExplorerFakeDeps` now seeds a profile by default and carries a sweeper on manual time; the generated testkit HTTP spec uses `ownerId: 'local-token'` as `profile.get` does. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms (1) and (2), and decides whether the stream-queue totals are worth a follow-up with the lab recording (OQ-674 family). |
| OQ-681 | Slice D passed its 450-line budget, was delivered whole with alarms (the cut was not applied, at the maintainer's instruction), and made four calls the spec leaves open (M0-48 spec, Slices row D; Q-7, Q-8, Q-9). | `overview.get` landed with `internal/overview.ts`, `queueTotals()` on the index and the tests AC-11 to AC-16. The hand-written lines are about 1,000 against 450 (tests about 385, the overview steps about 320, the contract about 85, the action and fixtures about 125, the scripted broker routes and the index sum about 80). Calls made: (1) an alarm check that answers `ok` is `none` (the broker says no alarm is in effect), while a failure, an unparsable body or a body without `status` is `unknown` with the fixed reason; the spec's "no alarm fields at all (stats off)" is read as the check's body having no `status`, so the real 4.2.9 stats-off recording (check `ok`) reads `none`; (2) the node flags `mem_alarm` and `disk_free_alarm` are requested through `columns` and are assumed (OQ-660), and the resource `other` is never produced; (3) the overview asks the sweeper for demand (`touch`) so the message sums fill in, through the module's one sweeper (`deps.sweeper`, as in `inventory.query`); (4) a failed overview or nodes read fails the call with a fixed-text Problem, and `freshness.hazards` is always empty because the overview does not read the profile. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms (1) to (4). |

## M0-48 slice E: the explorer.window topic

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-682 | Slice E passed its 550-line budget, was delivered whole (the version marker and the hub-level tests were kept), and made decisions to confirm (M0-48 spec, Slices row E). | Slice E landed `explorer.window` (contract, `TopicSource`, module `topicSources`) with the tests AC-17 to AC-21: about 240 lines of code (the source 165, the contract 62, the index, module and topic list 15) and about 655 lines of tests (the topic spec 477 beyond the 92 generated lines, the output scan 173, the module spec 4), about 895 in all against 550; the named cut was not applied. Decisions: (1) the item is `{ key, version, row }` where `row` is the `inventory.query` row WITHOUT its `key`, because the object key of a 255-character name and vhost is 682 characters and the spec shape (key twice) put 500 such rows at 1.14 MB, over the frame (OQ-684); the grid adapter (M0-53, OQ-677) re-adds the key. (2) The hub asks the source synchronously but visibility is an async store read, so a subscription is admitted first: until then its snapshot is empty and nothing is followed; once admitted the source publishes the current rows of the visible keys, which the outbox absorbs when the first snapshot has not been taken and sends as a delta when it has (never redundant). An invisible and an unknown connection end the subscription with the same `NOT_FOUND` (after `subok`, as an `end` frame; the hub cannot refuse in `sub`), and create no index, sweep or listener. (3) Visibility is checked once, at subscription; a connection made private or deleted later is not re-checked until the client resubscribes. (4) A topic subscription counts as demand (`Sweeper.touch`), like a query. (5) `InventoryIndex.current()` was added (the version the index is at). (6) Not wired into the hub or `coreModules` (OQ-669); the `stop()` of the module ends every subscription with `ENGINE_SHUTTING_DOWN`. (7) The overflow path is reachable only through the per-session outbox budget (8 MiB over up to 64 subscriptions): one subscription holds at most 500 keys and the marker, which is under its own ring of 1,000 items and 1 MiB. AC-19 drives it with a smaller session budget. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms (1) to (3), and decides whether visibility must be re-checked while a subscription lives (a recheck per install is one store read per subscription per sweep). |
| OQ-683 | A `sub` naming 500 long keys is bigger than the hub's default client frame (OQ-657 family, M0-48 spec Q-5). | `params.keys` holds up to 500 keys of up to 1,024 characters (the contract), and a real key for a 255-character name and vhost is 682 characters, so a valid `sub` can be about 340 KB. The hub reads a client frame of at most `maxClientFrameBytes`, default 64 KiB, and answers a longer one by closing the whole connection (code 1009), which drops every other subscription of the session. 500 keys of ordinary names (about 80 characters each) are about 45 KB and fit. AC-19 raises the limit to 1 MiB for the worst case. Alternatives: the carriers (`@app/transport`, the MessagePort tunnel) raise `maxClientFrameBytes` to about 640 KB when they create the hub; the UI sends fewer keys when the total is large; the keys travel as a hash or an index into the last `inventory.query` version; or the key length in the params is capped to what fits. `live`, `live-protocol` and the carriers are not touched by this slice. Status: open - working default in use (the topic works with the hub's limit raised). | The maintainer picks, and the carrier or the contract changes in its own issue. |
| OQ-684 | What did the worst-case snapshot of `explorer.window` measure (closes the measurement OQ-125 waits for, for this topic)? | AC-19 builds 500 queues with 255-character names and vhosts, a 48-character node and policy fields, and the other columns filled: the snapshot (500 items and the marker) is 793,812 bytes, 76 % of `maxFrameBytes` (1 MiB). With every optional string also at 255 characters it is 1,104,312 bytes, 5 % over the frame: the hub then ends the subscription with `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` (413), which AC-19 also proves (loud, not silent, I-13). The row schemas do not bound their strings, so "the largest row the schemas allow" has no upper limit; the broker's own limit (255 bytes for a name, a vhost and a policy name) is what the numbers assume. The ring is 1 MiB, equal to the frame, so a subscription that fits one snapshot fits its ring. Alternatives if the 5 % matters: bound the row strings in the contract (255 for names, 64 for types and states), name at most 400 keys, or let the snapshot carry only `{ key, version }` and leave the rows to `inventory.query`. OQ-125 stays open until a maintainer accepts the numbers. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the numbers or picks an alternative. |

## M0-48 slice G: search.query

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-685 | Slice G was delivered whole, and made calls the spec leaves open (M0-48 spec, Slices row G; Q-23, OQ-675). | `search.query` landed with `internal/search.ts`, `rows(kind)` on the index and the test AC-25. Calls made: (1) `rank` is a word (`exact`, `prefix`, `substring`) rather than a number, so a client needs no table; the order is also the order of `results`. (2) The order is rank, then the shorter name, then the key, then the kind, so a search is deterministic. (3) `matched` is the exact count of names that matched in the searched kinds, `truncated` says more matched than the 50 shown; the answer never holds a list of all matches (one pass keeps a sorted shortlist of 50). (4) `kinds` is its own list (`SEARCH_KINDS`: queues and exchanges), not `INVENTORY_KINDS`, so a kind joins search only when its names are searchable. (5) `freshness` covers the kinds searched: the worst state wins (a search over a loaded kind and a loading kind is `loading`), `complete` needs every kind, `loaded` and `expected` are summed and `expected` is left out when any kind does not know it. (6) The text is a plain text of 1 to 200 characters, not trimmed (a space matches names with a space); an empty text is `VALIDATION_FAILED`. (7) Case folding is `toLowerCase` of the text and the name, which does not fold every script alike; a locale-aware fold is not built. Measured at 20,012 names through the executor: p95 about 3.6 ms and maximum about 4.5 ms over 500 searches on a developer machine, against the 50 ms target; 100,000 names is not measured here (slice F). | The maintainer confirms the calls, and slice F measures 100,000 names. |

## M0-48 slice H: the bindings kind

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-686 | Slice H made the calls the spec leaves open for the `bindings` kind and passed its 450-line budget (M0-48 spec, Slices row H; Q-15). | `inventory.query` serves `bindings` through `internal/bindings.ts`, with the tests AC-26 (and two more). Decisions to confirm: (1) the first query of a vhost awaits the whole read (paged at 500, `interactive` lane) before it answers, so it can take many requests for a large vhost; the alternative is the sweep's answer-at-once `loading` window. (2) At the cap the read stops early when the broker's `total_count` says what is left, and `dropped` is that count minus the kept rows; a broker that sends no count is read to the end and the overflow counted. (3) The cap is per connection across its vhosts (default 500,000 rows, `ExplorerLimits.maxBindingsResident`), by rows only (no byte estimate); a new vhost is read first and then the least recently used other vhosts are evicted until the rows fit, so the transient is at most twice the cap; evictions and evicted rows are counted (`BindingsStore.stats`), not yet shown in an answer. (4) A resident vhost older than `bindingsMaxAgeMs` (60 s, the sweep wait) is read again by the next query for it; a failed read serves the resident rows as `stale` and is not retried until the age passes again. (5) `explorer.window` follows only the swept kinds (`SWEPT_KINDS`: queues, exchanges); its `kind` no longer accepts `bindings`, because no sweep installs them. (6) The row has `source`, `destination`, `destinationType`, `routingKey`, `propertiesKey` (the broker's unbind identity) and `argumentsKey`, the arguments in canonical JSON cut at 512 characters with `argumentsTruncated`; the arguments are not carried whole. The key is the object key of the vhost and a name made of source, destination type, destination and `propertiesKey` (routing key when absent). (7) The text filter looks at source, destination and routing key; `types` is the destination type; `states`, `hasConsumers` and `minMessages` match nothing, as for exchanges. (8) `/api/bindings/{vhost}` paging is assumed from the broker's HTTP API; no recording holds a bindings list yet. Slice C of M0-48l (#275) now reads the vhost as a bounded stream: (1) still holds, for a read of up to about 100 MB, and the answer-at-once `loading` window stays the alternative; the number of resident bytes (3) is not bounded by it (spec `docs/specs/M0-48l.md`, Q-12). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms (1) to (7), and a lab recording of `/api/bindings/{vhost}` (a `record-golden` run by a maintainer or CI) confirms (8). |
| OQ-688 | Slice H assumed the paged envelope for `/api/bindings/{vhost}`; the golden recordings show otherwise (correction of OQ-686 (2) and (8); #269). | The recordings (`testing/fixtures/mgmt`, manifest entry `bindings.inVhost`, `envelope: false`) show that RabbitMQ 3.13.7, 4.0.9, 4.1.8, 4.2.9 and 4.3.6, statistics on and off, ignore `page` and `page_size` on this endpoint and answer a bare array with every binding of the vhost (OQ-433 already said so); only LavinMQ 2.10.2 answers the envelope. Slice H parsed the answer with an object schema (`items`, `page`, `page_count`), so on every RabbitMQ version the first query of a vhost failed in the adapter as `unexpected-body` and the user saw the fixed `BROKER_ERROR` "could not be read"; the cap, `dropped` and `expected` were never reached. AC-26 passed only because the scripted broker always answered the envelope. Fixed here: the schema accepts both shapes. A bare array is one answer (one request, `interactive` lane): the cap is applied to that answer, rows past it are counted in `dropped`, `truncated` is true, `expected` is the array length (known), and the freshness `reason` is the fixed text "The broker sent every binding of the vhost in one answer, without paging." (state stays `live`; a failed re-read still says `stale`). A paged answer keeps the early stop at the cap (OQ-686 (2)) and carries no such reason. Tests replay the recorded 4.2.9 bare array, all ten RabbitMQ recordings and the LavinMQ envelope through the scripted port. Limit that remains: with a bare array the broker has already sent, and the adapter has already read and parsed, the whole vhost before the cap can apply, so the transient memory is the answer, not the cap. The only guard is the adapter client's body limit (`maxBodyBytes`, default 16 MiB, one value per client, error `response-too-large` shown as the same fixed `BROKER_ERROR`): the recorded shape is about 215 bytes a binding, so a vhost of more than about 75,000 bindings cannot be read at all until that limit is raised for every request of the client. Proposed change for a maintainer (adapter and port are out of scope here): add an optional `maxBodyBytes` to `MgmtGetRequest` (`libs/engine/ports/src/mgmt.ts`), honoured by `MgmtHttpClient` (`readText` limit is the smaller of the request value and a hard ceiling of 128 MiB, the default stays 16 MiB), keep `response-too-large` as the error, and let `svc-explorer` pass an `ExplorerLimits.maxBindingsBodyBytes` (suggested 128 MiB) for the bindings read; a vhost past it then fails with a clear fixed text instead of an unbounded read. A cap-bounded read (a streaming array reader that stops after `maxBindingsResident` items) would bound memory by the cap, but needs an incremental JSON parser; that is a new dependency or a hand-written parser, so it is a separate work item. Not built: per-queue or per-exchange bindings (`bindings.ofQueue`, `bindings.fromExchange`, also bare on RabbitMQ) as a way to read big vhosts in pieces. Slice C of M0-48l (#275) schedules and builds that streaming reader: the bindings are read through `readBoundedList` (`@app/json-stream`), so `maxBodyBytes` on `MgmtGetRequest` is not needed for bindings, and a cut read now says the number left out is not known (`dropped` is a minimum, a second fixed reason; OQ-710 to OQ-722). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer applies or declines the proposed `maxBodyBytes` change (or schedules the streaming reader), and confirms that a bare-array answer is labelled with the freshness reason rather than a new freshness field. |

## M0-48 sweep cadence and idle stop

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-687 | The sweeper now follows the cost-based cadence and the idle stop (M0-48 spec, behaviour rule "Cadence"; the cut of OQ-679 is closed), and made calls to confirm. | The next sweep starts `clamp(sweepMinIntervalMs, sweepMaxIntervalMs, sweepCostFactor x server time)` after the last one ended, so 0.4 s, 4 s and 20 s of server time wait 30 s, 200 s and 900 s. Calls: (1) a failed or partial sweep waits by the same formula on the server time it spent, so it is never sooner than the 30 s minimum; the backed-off request pace of a failing broker belongs to the BrokerScheduler (I-8), which the sweeper does not see, so a fast failure is retried after the minimum and a slow one after more. (2) The idle stop is checked when the wait ends, not by its own timer: a loop with no `touch` for `idleStopMs` (10 minutes) ends at the first wait that finishes after that, without a sweep and with no timer left; a wait longer than the idle time (above 600 s of cost) therefore ends the loop at the end of that wait unless a `touch` came in. (3) Demand is a `touch` (a query, an overview, a topic subscription); `sweepNow` alone is not demand. A later `touch` starts the loop again with a sweep at once. (4) The time is the injected Clock's monotonic timeline. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms (1) and (2). |

## M0-51 slice B: boot, Transloco, routes

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-689 | `ConnectionIdSchema` accepts any non-empty string, so what makes `/c/not%20an%20id/explorer` "fail `ConnectionIdSchema`" (M0-51 spec AC-4)? | `ConnectionIdSchema` is `z.string().min(1)` with a brand (`libs/shared/contracts/src/brands.ts`); `not an id` passes it, so the spec's third AC-4 case cannot fail on the schema alone. Default (slice B): the UI also requires a plain route token, `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._~-]{0,127}$` (`parseConnectionParam` in `apps/web/src/app/shell/active-connection.ts`), and treats anything else as no connection, which is the fail-closed reading. The engine's own rule for the id of a new connection (ULID, UUID or alias, per the persistence helpers) is not stated in the contract. Status: open - working default in use. | The contracts owner gives `ConnectionIdSchema` a shape (the UI rule then moves there), or confirms the UI rule. |
| OQ-690 | The initial bundle went from about 60 kB to about 194 kB gzip when the shell started to import the platform, data and contracts libraries; who trims it (M0-51 slice B, OQ-76, AC-3)? | Measured with `pnpm nx run web:budgets` (limit 350,000): 193,681 bytes gzip, 749 kB raw. Of the raw size, `zod` is about 516 kB, including every locale file (`zod/v4/locales/*`) and the JSON Schema converters, which `@app/ui-platform` (`BootstrapSchema`, `z.strictObject`) and `@app/contracts` pull in through `import { z } from 'zod'`; Angular core and router are about 230 kB, `@app/contracts` 28 kB, Transloco 19 kB. The budget passes with room, but the Angular raw-size warning at 500 kB (`project.json`) now fires on every production build. Not changed: the other libraries are not this work package's, and the warning limit is the coarse backstop the spec keeps. Default: leave it. Status: open - working default in use. | A decision on how the browser reaches `zod` without its locales (a mini build, or an import that tree-shakes), or on raising the raw warning; then the measurement is repeated. |

## M0-48 slice I: the connections kind

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-691 | Slice I joined `connections` to the swept kinds and made the calls the spec leaves open (M0-48 spec, Slices row I; Q-15; closes the cut of OQ-678). | `inventory.query` and `explorer.window` serve `connections` from the index, with the test AC-27 (and `connections` added to the reference test of AC-6 and to the topic and output scans). Decisions to confirm: (1) the sweep reads one global `/api/connections` list (paged at 500, `disable_stats`, no `enable_queue_totals`, `background` lane) after the queues and exchanges, so every sweep makes at least one request more; a vhost skipped past `maxVhostsPerSweep` does not make the connections partial, because the list spans every vhost. (2) A failed connection list fails the sweep like any other kind, so the index is marked stale as a whole (the queues and exchanges too), and a broker that refuses `/api/connections` to the sweep's account would keep the whole index stale; a per-kind stale flag is not built. (3) The registry has the capability `connectionStaticInfoWithStatsOff` (from 4.2.6) but no hazard id for the static information that statistics-off drops below it, so `freshness.hazards` is empty for this kind and no hazard is invented; when the registry gains one, the row-hazard table in `internal/freshness.ts` gets one line. (4) The sweep passes no `columns`: the adapter has no column list for `connections.list`, so the broker computes every field it keeps with statistics off; a list of the nine columns the row reads would be an adapter change, outside this slice. (5) The row is `key`, `name` and what the broker sent of `vhost`, `user`, `node`, `state`, `protocol`, `channels`, `peerHost` and `ssl`; a connection with no vhost is keyed under the empty vhost and no `vhosts` filter matches it. The `types` facet is the protocol and `states` the state; `hasConsumers` and `minMessages` match nothing. The user name and the connection name (which holds the peer address) are shown as the broker sends them; neither is a secret. (6) The index byte estimate counts the name and vhost only, so user, node and peer strings of a connection are not in the 128 MiB figure. (7) `search.query` does not search connections (`SEARCH_KINDS` is unchanged). (8) Observation: the capability `connectionStaticInfoWithStatsOff` says 4.2.6 and later keep the peer, TLS and authentication information with statistics off, but the 4.2.9 statistics-off recording of `connections.list` carries the same four fields as 4.0.9 (name, vhost, user, node), and the 4.3.6 statistics-off recording holds no connection. Either the capability text is off or the flag the recorder sends with statistics off (`disable_stats=true`, which this sweep sends too) drops the information by itself (then a statistics-on broker asked with the flag sends the sparse row too, and the full 4.3.6 row of AC-27 is what a statistics-on broker sends without it); the rows follow the recordings, as for OQ-674, and the registry is not changed here. AC-27 uses 4.0.9 and 4.2.9 statistics off for the sparse row and 4.3.6 statistics on for the full one. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms (1) to (7), and the owner of the capability or of the recording settles (8) (a lab recording from 4.2.6 or later with a connection open, taken with and without `disable_stats`). |

## M0-53 slice C: the AG Grid component, the theme bridge and the bundle check

Ids: M0-53 slices C to E use OQ-700 to OQ-709, so that parallel work packages that add questions at the end of this file do not take the same ids.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-700 | Where does the `ui-grid` bundle check live (M0-53 spec, Files: `libs/ui/grid/bundle-check/`)? | The spec puts the script in the library. The lint rules forbid Node built-ins in `runtime:browser` code, and the script needs `node:fs`, `node:child_process` and `node:zlib`. Default: a tooling project `tools/grid-bundle-check` (tags `type:tooling`, `runtime:node`, like `web-gallery-check`) holds `bundle-check.mjs` and `bundle-check.test.mjs`; the target `ui-grid:bundle-check` runs it, and AC-3 is the test "AC-3 the ui-grid bundle holds no AG Grid Enterprise module" there (Node `--test`, `.mjs`, not `bundle-check.spec.ts`). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the place or names another. |
| OQ-701 | The adapter does not fit the 200 kB gzip budget of a feature chunk: what is done (M0-53 slice C, S-14 finding)? | `ui-grid:bundle-check` builds the public entry with esbuild (minified) and prints the size of the adapter with AG Grid Community but without Angular, RxJS and tslib: 208,903 bytes gzip with the explicit list of nine Community modules (OQ-551) after slice C and 214,025 with the menu, clipboard, chooser and status bar of slices D and E, against 200 kB (blueprint 10, execution plan budgets). AG Grid's core is most of it, so a shorter module list would not bring it under. The number is esbuild's, not the Angular production build's (M0-54 is the first app to import the grid and measures that one). Default: nothing is loosened; the check prints the number and does not fail on it. Options: a budget exception for the explorer chunk in `apps/web`, a shared lazy grid chunk, or a different grid. Status: open - working default in use. | M0-54 measures the real chunk, and the maintainer picks an option or raises the budget. |
| OQ-702 | How the selection is shown, and what jsdom could not prove (M0-53 slice C; the S-14 uncertainties). | The adapter owns the selection set (row ids); AG Grid only displays it: the component turns click selection off and mirrors the set into the loaded rows with `setSelected(..., 'api')` (`aria-selected`, selected-row class), again on every model update. It does not depend on whether AG Grid keeps a selection across block eviction (the first S-14 uncertainty); that stays unmeasured. The component specs run in jsdom with virtualisation off (`provideGridRenderAll`): jsdom does not inject AG Grid's `<style>` elements, so the style nonce is proved only as the grid option (`styleNonce`), and the run under the enforced S-04 policy (AC-16) needs the browser harness. The nonce comes from the token `GRID_STYLE_NONCE`, else Angular's `CSP_NONCE`, else the `csp-nonce` meta tag (the working default of OQ-552). Status: open - working default in use. | The `perf:grid-100k` harness runs in a browser (AC-1, AC-4, AC-16) and records the selection and nonce behaviour in the S-14 record. |
| OQ-703 | Which AG Grid strings the adapter cannot translate (M0-53 slice C; OQ-555). | AG Grid reads `localeText` once, at creation, so a change of `labels` after that updates only the accessible name of the grid. The component forwards `loadingOoo` and `noRowsToShow` to `labels`. The ARIA strings AG Grid writes itself (for example the row selection and sort hints, the header and cell descriptions) stay in English; the full list is a gap the S-14 run in a browser must write down. Honest wording applies to every label (`@app/honest-wording`) and to this gap. Status: open - working default in use. | The S-14 run lists the strings, or `labels` grows the keys that matter. |
| OQ-704 | Choices of the context menu and the key chords that the spec leaves open (M0-53 slice D; OQ-542, OQ-550). | (1) The `keybindings` input replaces the defaults instead of adding to them, so the command registry can own every chord; a feature that wants the defaults plus its own spreads `DEFAULT_GRID_KEYBINDINGS`. (2) Space toggles the selection of the focused row and is not a binding. (3) The menu has no type-ahead and no sub-menus. (4) Tab closes the menu and returns focus to the cell before the page moves on. (5) A click on an item that is closed leaves the menu open, so its reason can be read. (6) The menu is placed with the style bindings `left` and `top` (CSSOM, allowed by the CSP; no CDK overlay) and kept inside the window after it renders; it does not follow the grid when the page scrolls, it closes when focus leaves it. (7) The copy chord (Ctrl or Cmd+C) joins the defaults with the clipboard in slice E. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or the command registry lands. |
| OQ-705 | Choices of the clipboard that the spec leaves open (M0-53 slice E; OQ-549). | (1) The copy has no header row. (2) A value is copied verbatim: one that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` is not prefixed, so it can run as a formula when pasted into a spreadsheet (OQ-549 b stays open); a cell with a tab, a line break or a quote is quoted with the quotes doubled, so a value with a quote is not byte-equal to the cell. (3) The cap is 5,000 rows, the same number as the selection cap, so a selection set by the feature (not by clicks) is the only way past it. (4) Selected rows that are not loaded (their block was evicted) cannot be copied; they are counted as not copied, never skipped silently. (5) With nothing selected, Ctrl or Cmd+C does nothing and the browser's own copy is left alone. (6) The default `GRID_CLIPBOARD` is `navigator.clipboard.writeText`, which needs a secure context and a user gesture (OQ-549 c); the desktop bridge (M2-P15) replaces it. (7) `copyTruncated` counts copies that left rows out, not the rows. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer rules on (1), (2) and (6). |
| OQ-706 | Choices of the column chooser, the status bar and the labels (M0-53 slice E). | (1) The chooser is a disclosure (a button with `aria-expanded` and a panel), not a dialog; it resizes a column with a number field and reorders with Up and Down buttons, because drag and drop has no keyboard way. (2) Reset puts order, visibility and widths back and keeps the sort. (3) The chooser and the status bar are always shown; there is no input to hide them. (4) `labels` values mark numbers with `{count}`, `{omitted}` and `{name}`, replaced by the grid; Transloco (M0-51) interpolates with `{{count}}`, so the feature maps its keys to these strings. (5) AG Grid shows a placeholder row for a block that failed; the status bar, not the row, says why. (6) The loaded count walks the cached nodes (at most 5,000) on every model update. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or M0-54 shows a need. |

## Spec questions: M0-48l

The spec `docs/specs/M0-48l.md` (a bounded streaming reader for bare-array management lists, bindings first; issue #272) has 13 questions in its "Risks / questions" section. Each is recorded below as a row, so the maintainer can answer here. Q-n of the spec is OQ-(709+n); OQ-700 to OQ-709 are reserved for M0-53. Every row is **open - working default in use**: the default named is what the spec assumes and what an implementation session follows until the row is answered; nothing here decides a question.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-710 | Where does the incremental JSON array reader live (M0-48l spec Q-1)? | Default: a new `type:pure`, `runtime:isomorphic` project `json-stream` (`libs/shared/json-stream`, `@app/json-stream`), so a service, the adapter's specs and a later service can import it. Alternatives: `internal/` of `svc-explorer` (no reuse, the adapter spec could not import it), `@app/domain-model` (wrong subject), `@app/kernel` (a kernel project is not allowed in the adapter). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the project and its name, or names another place. |
| OQ-711 | Hand-written reader or a dependency (M0-48l spec Q-2)? | Default: hand-written (a byte scanner that finds the item boundaries, then `JSON.parse` of each item; about 300 lines with tests, no dependency). Dependency proposal if preferred: `@streamparser/json` 0.0.26 (MIT, no dependencies, published 2026-08-21, older than the 3-day gate, pre-1.0, source and advisories not reviewed): an entry in `tools/supply-chain/deps-allowlist.json` (reason, ref M0-48l), the catalog line `'@streamparser/json': 0.0.26`, a confinement entry in `eslint.config.mjs` limited to `libs/shared/json-stream`, no release-age exclusion, CODEOWNERS approval. `stream-json` 3.7.0 is not proposed (depends on `stream-chain` and Node streams). These are protected paths: an agent only proposes them. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer accepts the hand-written reader, or applies the dependency change. |
| OQ-712 | Does the stream need a port or adapter change (M0-48l spec Q-3)? | Default: none. The adapter shapes a stream request exactly as a get request (`open()` and `shapeRequest`), so the wire request is the same. `stream` has no byte ceiling and no body deadline (`deadlineCoversBody: false`); the reader and the glue enforce both.
