Optional hardening for a maintainer: add `maxBytes?` and `deadlineMs?` to `MgmtStreamRequest` (`libs/engine/ports/src/mgmt.ts`, trust-critical), count bytes in the adapter's `chunks` generator and throw `response-too-large` past `maxBytes`, start the body deadline when the headers arrive; the default stays unlimited. It makes the proposal of OQ-688 (`maxBodyBytes` on `MgmtGetRequest`) unnecessary for bindings. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer declines the change or schedules it (the label of the WP is then decided). |
| OQ-713 | The scheduler's `holdMs` watchdog frees a streamed slot without aborting the body (M0-48l spec Q-4). | `BrokerScheduler.holdSlot` releases the slot after `holdMs` (default 120 s) and counts `holdTimeouts`, but the response keeps flowing with no slot counted. Default: not changed here; the bindings reader's deadline (`bindingsReadDeadlineMs`, 90 s) is below `holdMs` and AC-11 proves it, and the only other stream user reads at most 64 KiB. A kernel change (abort the task signal when the watchdog fires) would cover every stream user. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer asks for the kernel change as its own issue, or accepts the reader-side deadline. |
| OQ-714 | What does the answer say when the read stopped at the cap and the total is not known (M0-48l spec Q-5)? | Default: stop at the cap and do not read the rest; `truncated: true`, `dropped` is a minimum (1, the reader saw that one more item follows), `expected` absent, and a fixed `reason` says the number left out is not known (a reason text, as OQ-688). Alternatives: (a) a new optional `FreshnessSchema` field such as `droppedIsMinimum` (changes `@app/contracts` and the API documents); (b) keep scanning without keeping items up to a byte budget (for example 32 MiB, about 150,000 more bindings) so the count is exact when the body ends inside it, at the cost of that much more transfer and a longer slot hold. The issue says "counting the rest"; the default does not. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the reason text, or chooses (a) or (b). |
| OQ-715 | What happens when the byte ceiling is reached before the item cap (M0-48l spec Q-6)? | Default: the read fails closed (`too-large`) like `response-too-large` does today, and the last good rows are served `stale` if there are any. Defaults: `maxBindingsBodyBytes` 256 MiB (500,000 bindings of the recorded shape are about 107 MB), `maxBindingItemBytes` 256 KiB, `maxBindingsEnvelopeBytes` 8 MiB (one page of at most 500), `bindingsReadDeadlineMs` 90 s. Alternative: keep what was read and mark it `truncated` (needs a rule for a cut inside an item). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the numbers and the behaviour. |
| OQ-716 | How is the paged envelope (LavinMQ) told from a bare array (M0-48l spec Q-7)? | Default: from the first significant byte of the body (`[` or `{`), so one code path reads both and no product or version is named (I-3). Alternatives: key on `profile.product` (broker behaviour in code), or a registry capability for "bindings list pages" (data, but a change in `libs/shared/semantics` and a lab recording to back it). A body of any other shape fails closed. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the sniffing, or asks for the registry capability. |
| OQ-717 | Do the other bare-array lists use the reader now (M0-48l spec Q-8; OQ-433)? | Default: the reader is shared and generic, but only the bindings store adopts it in this WP. `/api/vhosts` in the sweeper stays a `get` (limited by the 16 MiB body limit and `maxVhostsPerSweep`); consumers, policies, permissions and per-queue bindings have no engine reader yet, and the first one added uses the same glue (`readBoundedList`, which starts in `svc-explorer/src/internal` and moves to `@app/kernel` when a second service needs it). Alternative: put the glue in the kernel now. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the scope, or asks for the kernel placement or for the vhost list to adopt it. |
| OQ-718 | Is `json-stream` trust-critical, and does it get a CODEOWNERS line (M0-48l spec Q-9)? | Default: no tag. The issue says the WP is not trust-critical unless it touches the ports or the adapter; this spec adds only a spec file to the adapter, and `svc-explorer` keeps its CODEOWNERS line. The reader parses untrusted broker bytes, so a maintainer may prefer `--trustCritical` and the line `/libs/shared/json-stream/ @RafaelJCamara`; CODEOWNERS is a protected path, so an agent only proposes it. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer applies the line and the tag, or confirms there is none. |
| OQ-719 | Which lane and how many slots does a bindings stream use (M0-48l spec Q-10)? | Default: the `interactive` lane (a person waits for the first window), at most 2 streams open per connection (`maxBindingReadsPerConnection`), each holding one of the 4 `interactive` slots until the body is read, cut or timed out. Alternatives: the `background` lane (one at a time, slower, queued behind sweeps), a limit of 1, or no limit beyond the scheduler's. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the lane and the limit. |
| OQ-720 | Is the event-loop assertion a gate or a measurement (M0-48l spec Q-11; I-5)? | Default: AC-15 asserts a delay p99 of at most 20 ms on the CI Linux runner and prints the maximum; AC-7 (yield counts at 256 KiB and 5,000 items) is the deterministic gate. If AC-15 is flaky on shared runners it becomes a printed measurement in the M0-48 perf harness. The yield sizes are starting values until measured. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms, or the first CI runs show the assertion is stable or flaky. |
| OQ-721 | What does the streaming reader close (M0-48l spec Q-12; OQ-688, OQ-686)? | Default: it makes the `maxBodyBytes`-on-`MgmtGetRequest` proposal of OQ-688 unnecessary for bindings and schedules the streaming reader that OQ-688 names; slice C adds a pointer to the OQ-688 and OQ-686 rows, and a maintainer closes OQ-688 when it lands. OQ-686 (1) (the first query awaits the whole read) now covers a read of up to about 100 MB; the answer-at-once `loading` window stays its alternative. The resident rows still have no byte bound (OQ-686 (3)). Status: open - working default in use. | Slice C lands and a maintainer closes OQ-688, or declines the plan. |
| OQ-722 | Split M0-48l into four child issues, or approve `oversize-approved` (M0-48l spec Q-13)? | About 1,100 changed lines by estimate (about 1,740 to 2,180 at this repository's usual 1.6 to 2.0 times) against the 800 limit of AGENTS.md section 5. Default: four child issues, A (scanner), B (shapes, abort, yield), C (bindings store), D (wire proof), proposed in the spec and not opened, with hard budgets of 650, 500, 700 and 450 and a named cut each. Same family as OQ-654. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens the child issues, or labels #272 `oversize-approved`. |
| OQ-730 | Which calls did slice C of M0-48l (#275) make where the spec leaves the detail open? | (1) A read that passes `bindingsReadDeadlineMs` fails as `BROKER_UNREACHABLE` (retryable) with the fixed text "could not be read in time"; the spec names the text and not the code. (2) Each binding of a bare array is checked with the schema after the stream ended well, not while it streams, so a bad binding fails the read after the body was read to its end (the stream is settled and nothing is installed); checking while streaming would stop the body earlier at the cost of keeping the schema inside the reader. (3) The gate of `maxBindingReadsPerConnection` wraps the whole read of a vhost (all pages of an envelope), not each page, and at most 64 reads wait per connection, the next is refused with `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` (retryable) so the waiting list is bounded (I-6). (4) The cut case of the existing test AC-26b asserted `expected` and an exact `dropped` for a bare answer cut at the cap; under AC-9 of the spec a cut read has `dropped` 1 as a minimum and no `expected`, so those assertions changed, the test title did not. (5) A second page of an envelope that arrives as a bare array, or a first answer that is neither, fails closed with the "not a complete list" text. (6) The scripted broker answers a `get` of `/api/bindings/{vhost}` with 404, so a reader that still used `get` fails its tests. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms (1) to (5), or names other codes and rules. |
| OQ-731 | Which calls did slice D of M0-48l (#276, the wire proof) make where the spec leaves the detail open? | (1) AC-15 is asserted on the delay the reader adds, not on the raw p99: the p99 with the reader less the larger of the idle floor of the platform (about 16 ms on Windows, 15.6 ms timer tick) and the p99 of the same transfer read without the reader, measured in the same test (OQ-724 precedent, extended by the control run). Measured on a Windows host with 32 logical cores: quiet 0.0 to 1.7 ms added (raw p99 14 to 17 ms, which is the timer tick); with 32 busy processes 0.0 ms added (raw 6 to 7 ms); with 64 busy processes (twice the cores) the OS descheduling of a CPU-bound loop adds 31 to 46 ms and the assertion fails, which is a machine that cannot tell a held loop from a preempted one. AC-15 stays an assertion, as the spec says; per OQ-720 it becomes a printed measurement in the M0-48 perf harness if CI runners show it flaky (AC-7 stays the deterministic gate). It was not cut for size. (2) AC-13 says the server saw one request; `get` and `stream` each send one, both `GET /api/bindings/v?page=1&page_size=500`, so the test asserts two requests with that URL. (3) The spec file runs its own server (a generator written with backpressure) because `startMgmtServer` answers with a whole string; `test-support.ts` is not changed. (4) AC-14 measured: the socket closed 1 ms after the item limit and the server had written 0.47 to 0.54 MiB of about 31 MiB (the 8 MiB limit of the spec is kept). (5) The heap growth of AC-15 is sampled every 20 ms and printed, not asserted (8 to 46 MiB over runs, mostly garbage not yet collected; not a peak measurement). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms (1), or asks for AC-15 as a printed measurement now. |

## M0-48 slice F: golden replay, lab run, perf harness and S-13

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-723 | Slice F was delivered whole, about 2.8 times its 550-line budget, and made calls to confirm (M0-48 spec, Slices row F; the maintainer's rule was not to cut). | About 1,360 hand-written lines of tests, tooling and harness (the golden replay 282, the lab run 408, the seeder 136, the alternatives 223, the perf harness 308) and about 180 of documents, against 550: the lab run grew because the S-13 figures needed a recording proxy, the alternatives in two rounds and the 100,000 case, and the harness because of the loop-delay and search measurements. Calls made: (1) the seeder imports definitions in batches of 2,500 (20,000 queues in 7 to 10 s, 100,000 in 68 to 72 s) rather than declaring queues one by one; (2) `createSweeper` and its types are exported from the package index so the lab run can use the real clock and read the sweep outcome (the module still owns its own sweeper); (3) `perf` is `apps/perf` from the `library` generator (type:test, runtime:node) with a target per harness and its own Vitest config, kept out of `nx test`; (4) the lab and perf runs print their figures with `process.stdout.write`, because Vitest 5 hides the console output of a passing test; (5) the 100,000-queue lab run measures three variants once, because the others take hours; (6) no workflow was added. | The maintainer accepts the size and the calls. |
| OQ-724 | Is the event-loop budget of AC-24 asserted on the delay above the platform's idle floor, and what follows from the 100,000-queue figure (M0-48 spec Q-13, OQ-665, OQ-671; related OQ-720)? | `monitorEventLoopDelay` reports the whole tick interval, so an idle loop reads about 16 ms on Windows (a 15.6 ms timer tick), where a raw 20 ms limit left 4 ms and failed on 2 of 3 runs; the harness measures the idle floor first and asserts the p99 above it (on Linux the floor is expected to be about 1 ms, not measured, and the assertion is then the limit as written). The scripted broker answers in the same tick, which held the loop for a whole sweep (a raw p99 of 139 ms in the first version, an artefact), so each request now gives the loop one turn. At 20,000 queues (synthetic): window p95 6.9 to 11.2 ms, loop p99 above the floor 0 ms in four final runs. At 100,000: window p95 44 to 63 ms and loop p99 47 to 55 ms above the floor, which exceeds the 20 ms budget; in isolation one 100,000-queue sweep cost about 240 ms of engine time (loop delay at most 6.5 ms) and a cold sort by `messages` 33 ms, so the cause is the mix and was not taken apart. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the floor-adjusted assertion, and opens the issue for a worker or a chunked build and sort at 100,000 queues (nothing was opened here). |
| OQ-725 | What do the S-13 readings change in the defaults (M0-48 spec Q-10, Q-11, Q-20; OQ-662, OQ-663, OQ-672)? | Measured on a Windows host with Docker Desktop, idle queues in four vhosts, not on Linux CI (docs/architecture/s-13-mgmt-api-cost-100k.md). Page size 500 and one list per vhost are the cheapest paged shapes; total broker time follows the number of requests, and the per-page cost grows with the queues in the vhost, so a broker with all queues in one vhost is expected to be worse (not measured). On idle queues a per-vhost sweep with statistics on and no flags took about half the broker time of the sweep with `disable_stats` and `enable_queue_totals` (51 s against 144 to 152 s at 100,000); the two flags were not separated and nothing was measured under traffic. An unpaged list cost an eighth (20,000) to a twenty-fourth (100,000) of the paged sweep but the engine cannot send it and 25 MiB exceeds the adapter's 16 MiB body limit. With the shipped cadence the broker spends about 14 % of its time answering sweeps at 100,000 queues, under 1 % at 20,000. Nothing was changed. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer decides on the flags, the cadence cap and paging against unpaged, and asks for the measurements listed under "Not measured" (one vhost, busy broker, each flag alone, Linux). |
| OQ-726 | Where do the golden replay, the lab run and the perf harness run in CI (OQ-544, OQ-671, OQ-672)? | AC-22 is a testkit spec and runs with `nx test testkit`. AC-23 needs a Docker daemon (Linux in CI) and 20,000 queues take about 6 minutes per version including the seed; `IT_QUEUES=100000` takes about 8 minutes per version more and the alternatives at that size were cut to three. AC-24 takes about 20 seconds with the 100,000 run and asserts a time budget, so it should not share a machine with other work. No workflow was added; the PR gate, a nightly run and the runner class are the maintainer's call. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer names the workflow and the runner. |
| OQ-727 | What does the shell do with the layout details the spec left open (M0-51 slice C, AC-5 to AC-9)? | (1) `LayoutNotice` has a sixth member, `load-failed`, for a `layouts.get` that fails: the default layout is shown, the engine's Problem is displayed, nothing is saved until the person changes the layout. (2) A change made while the layout is loading is ignored (the regions are not usable before the answer). (3) `fitLayout` runs on open and on a window resize; the fitted sizes are saved with the next change by the person, never by themselves. (4) A stored document over a cap becomes the default with the `over-limit` notice instead of being truncated (no silent eviction). (5) The activity bar shows text, not icons, so it is 72 px wide. (6) The panel is hidden by default and the sidebar visible. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the six readings, or names the ones to change. |
| OQ-728 | How do tabs, editor groups and the explorer tree behave where the spec leaves it open (M0-51 slice D, AC-10)? | (1) Tabs use manual activation: the arrows move focus, Enter, Space or a click shows the tab and its address (WAI-ARIA allows both; automatic activation would navigate on every arrow). (2) Delete closes the focused tab; closing the active tab shows its next neighbour (else the previous one) and moves focus there, and with no tab left focus goes to the group. (3) The close buttons are for the pointer and are not tab stops. (4) The routed view lives in the active group only; the other groups say that selecting one of their tabs shows it there. (5) A group is closed only when it has no tab, so closing a group never loses a tab; tabs do not move between groups yet. (6) A tab id is a plain token (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, at most 64) because it becomes part of DOM ids. (7) Group splitters work in pixels computed from the layout model and the window, not from measured elements. (8) The tree draws at most 500 rows and says so; a source returns windows (I-7). (9) Activating a tree node opens a tab for its address; if the tab is refused for a cap the address is not shown. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the nine readings, or names the ones to change. |
| OQ-729 | How does the status bar and the skew handling behave where the spec leaves it open (M0-51 slice E, AC-11 to AC-14)? | (1) The name and the environment class come from `connections.get`, the broker facts from `profile.get`; both are read when the connection changes and a failure of either is an unknown, not an error message. (2) The profile is parsed with the whole `BrokerProfileSchema`, which is strict: a field the UI does not know makes the parse fail and the bar reads the profile as unknown (fail closed) until the UI is updated. (3) While either call is on its way the bar shows a loading text, no environment strip and no verification banner (OQ-346); afterwards the verification banner is mounted for every state, and an absent profile is an unknown build. (4) `<app-env-chrome>` sits in the status bar as its first segment; `<app-verification-banner>` and the skew banner sit at the top of the main column, not in the bar. (5) The bar shows the connection id where the profile name is unknown. (6) The reload after a failed chunk goes to the address being opened, not to the current one. (7) The initial bundle grew by about 10 kB gzip (the domain model's BrokerProfileSchema and the skew code). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the seven readings, or names the ones to change. |

## Spec questions: M0-54

The spec `docs/specs/M0-54.md` (feature-explorer: grids, saved views and cluster overview, EXP-02/03 UI; issue #50) has 29 questions in its "Risks / questions" section. Each is recorded below as a row, so the maintainer can answer here. Q-n of the spec is OQ-(731+n). Every row is **open - working default in use**: the default named is what the spec assumes and what an implementation session follows until the row is answered; nothing here decides a question.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-732 | How is M0-54 sized and sliced (M0-54 spec Q-1)? | About 2,290 changed lines by estimate (about 4,580 at the usual 2.0 times, 6,400 at 2.8) against the 800 limit. Default: ten child issues A to J with the estimates, expected landings, hard budgets and named cuts of the spec's "Slices"; proposed and not opened; `oversize-approved` is not asked for. When opened, the work graph needs the ids and edges (M0-60 and M1-42 depend on slice I at least). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens the child issues or adds `oversize-approved`. |
| OQ-733 | Who wires `svc-explorer`, `svc-workspace`, `svc-capabilities` and `svc-connections` into the engine (M0-54 spec Q-2; same family as OQ-441, OQ-472, OQ-669)? | `coreModules` registers `meta`, `ops` and `svc-ops` only and `EngineModule` has no topics, so a served engine answers none of `inventory.query`, `overview.get`, `profile.get`, `connections.get`, `views.*` or `layouts.*`, and the hub serves no `explorer.window`. No work package owns the wiring. Default: M0-54 (like M0-51 and M0-52) is proved against fakes and says so in every commit body. Status: open - working default in use. Update (2026-10-22): issue #278 registered `svc-connections`, `svc-capabilities`, `svc-workspace` and `svc-explorer` in `coreModules` (`apps/engine/src/modules/index.ts`): the executor serves their actions, the `explorer.window` source is registered with the hub's topic registry, and `explorer.stop()` runs as a `taps-firehose` drain phase for quit, SIGTERM and emergency (OQ-669, OQ-764 to OQ-771). The wiring question is answered; M0-54 is still proved against fakes and a stand-in at the HTTP boundary (OQ-787, OQ-788), and the real-engine proof of M0-60 is still owed. | A work package or issue registers the modules, the topic sources and the drain phase, before the real-engine proof of M0-60. |
| OQ-734 | How do the browser proofs of M0-54 depend on issue #277 (M0-54 spec Q-3)? | The served app is unstyled under the real CSP and refuses one `TrustedScript` assignment (#277, OQ-347, OQ-552). Every browser proof of M0-54 waits for it, except a harness page served under its own S-04 policy and nonce (the way `perf:grid-100k` is planned), which does not exercise the real shell. Default: no browser claim is made; AC-27 and the manual run at 1280x720 are planned. Status: open - working default in use. Update (2026-10-22): #277 is settled and `web-csp-check` proves the production build renders styled under the real CSP with zero reports; slice K (OQ-787) therefore runs AC-27 against the real production build served by the real engine bundle, in a real Chromium, with no harness page. The "no browser claim" default no longer holds for AC-27. | #277 is settled (the nonce source, which also feeds `GRID_STYLE_NONCE`). |
| OQ-735 | Which `ui-grid` contract deltas does the explorer need (M0-54 spec Q-4; follows OQ-677)? | The grid cannot be told that membership changed (only a `remove` of a shown id reloads the cache), its source is not told when the cache reloaded (a version pin needs it), and its multi-sort exceeds the one-column sort of `inventory.query`. Default, slice A: an optional `{kind: 'refresh'}` change (reloads at most every 5 s, never dropped), an optional `refreshed()` on the source, and an input `sortMode` (`multi` default, `single`); no existing behaviour changes. Alternative for the first: send a `remove` of one shown id, which counts a drop for a removal that did not happen. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts the three additions, or names another way. |
| OQ-736 | When does the explorer resubscribe to `explorer.window`, and does it follow a margin (M0-54 spec Q-5; OQ-657)? | Default: follow exactly the ids the grid reports (displayed rows only) after the grid's own 100 ms debounce; send nothing when the set equals the last reported set or is a subset of the followed one; no margin of extra rows. Not decided: a longer settle (each `sub` costs an access check and a profile read in the engine) or a margin above and below the screen; slice J measures before either is added. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or slice J's measurement shows a need. |
| OQ-737 | What is the UI rule for the key bytes of one `sub` (M0-54 spec Q-6; OQ-683)? | A valid `sub` of 500 long keys is about 340 KB and the hub closes the whole connection over 64 KiB. Default UI rule: the keys of one `sub` total at most 48 KiB (length plus 3 each) and at most 500; the ids left out are counted and shown ("live updates paused for N rows"). Interim cut of slice C: at most 60 ids (60 keys of 686 characters are about 41 KB). Engine-side options stay open under OQ-683: raise `maxClientFrameBytes` in the carriers, cap the key length in the contract, send an index instead of keys. Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-683 is answered; the rule is kept, relaxed or removed. |
| OQ-738 | How are the version marker and the version pin used (M0-54 spec Q-7; OQ-658)? | The snapshot of `explorer.window` always ends with the marker at the index's current version, whether or not membership changed, so after any new sweep the first subscribe or resubscribe can cause one extra cache reload (at most every 5 s). Default: accept it; the pin of `inventory.query` is per sort and per reload epoch; a scroll that spans a reload may show a row twice or miss one at the seam until the next block. Alternative (engine side): a membership version in the marker. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts, or the marker carries a membership version. |
| OQ-739 | Is there a regex filter in the explorer UI (M0-54 spec Q-8; OQ-666)? | The issue scope and EXP-03 say "regex filter"; the engine has none. Default: substring and facets only, and no client-side regex over loaded rows (it would filter only the blocks that happen to be loaded and look complete). Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-666 is answered; if a regex is wanted, the engine gets a matcher first. |
| OQ-740 | Where do the facet values come from (M0-54 spec Q-9)? | No action lists vhosts, states or protocols. Default: the vhost facet is typed values (chips); queue types offered from `QueueTypeSchema` (classic, quorum, stream), exchange types from `profile.exchangeTypes` when the profile loaded, protocols and states typed; values are exact and case-sensitive as the engine matches them. Engine gap: a bounded `inventory.facets` action (distinct values with counts); it would also give the tree a vhost level. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer accepts, or an issue adds the action. |
| OQ-741 | One sort column or several (M0-54 spec Q-10)? | `inventory.query` takes one sort column and AG Grid allows several. Default: `sortMode: 'single'` (slice A) so the header cannot make a second sort; if that option is cut, the feature keeps the first entry, publishes the trimmed column state and says so. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-742 | Where do the sortable column lists live (M0-54 spec Q-11)? | They are a constant inside the `svc-explorer` action. Default: the UI keeps its own lists in `@app/explorer-model` and a testkit spec proves them against the real action; `svc-explorer` (trust-critical) is not changed. Alternative: export the lists from `@app/contracts`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-743 | What is the shape of a saved view and how is one replaced (M0-54 spec Q-12)? | Default: definition `{version: 1, kind, filter, columns}` where `columns` is the grid's `GridColumnState`; `kind` is the grid kind (`queues`, `exchanges`, `connections`, `bindings`); at most 25 vhost chips so a definition fits 16,384 characters; names are not unique (the engine allows it); replacing is create then delete because there is no `views.update`; the first 200 views are listed and the page says when there are more. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the shape and the `kind` names, or asks for a `views.update`. |
| OQ-744 | What does saving a view do while the guardrails class every call as prod (M0-54 spec Q-13; OQ-380, OQ-283)? | `views.create` and `views.delete` answer 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` in every environment through the real router, so a save fails on a real engine until the guardrail ruling. Default: show the engine's Problem as sent and keep the filters and columns applied; Save and Delete are `<app-gated>` with a gate built from `allowedActions` (closed with a stated reason when the action is missing or the profile is unknown), which is deny-all until the authorizer is wired (OQ-472). Alternative: do not gate, let the engine refuse. Status: open - working default in use. Update (2026-10-22): `views.create` and `views.delete` declare `scope: 'local'` (OQ-783, answering OQ-380) and name no connection, so the router no longer answers 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` for them: `libs/testkit/src/explorer-ui/explorer-calls.spec.ts` expects 200 from both through the real router. #278 wired the executor's authorizer (OQ-472); the UI gate is still built from `allowedActions` as described, and the maintainer still confirms or drops it. | OQ-380 is ruled on, and the maintainer confirms or drops the `allowedActions` gate. |
| OQ-745 | How does a saved view apply on another connection or kind (M0-54 spec Q-14)? | Default: facets that do not apply to the kind are dropped; unknown column ids are ignored by the column state; vhost chips that the connection does not have are kept because the UI cannot tell (no vhost list), and a result of no rows lists the chips that could be the cause; a bindings view whose vhost is missing shows the engine's Problem; a newer `version` applies nothing; every drop is listed on the page. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-746 | What does a restored tab remember (M0-54 spec Q-15)? | A tab keeps its address (kind, vhost, `?view=`) and nothing else; filter edits, column changes and the selection are lost on restore. Default: accept; the person saves a view. Alternatives: the shell stores per-tab state in the layout document, or the last column state per kind goes to `localStorage` as a per-viewer convenience. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or picks an alternative. |
| OQ-747 | Which routes does the explorer use, and how does the generated route meet the shell (M0-54 spec Q-16)? | The `ui-feature` generator emits `explorerRoute` at the full path `c/:cid/explorer`; the shell mounts children under `c/:cid`. Default: the shell mounts the feature's `explorerRoutes` at the child path `explorer` with `EXPLORER_CONNECTION` provided there; lists at `explorer/queues`, `explorer/exchanges`, `explorer/connections`, `explorer/bindings?vhost=`; M1-42's detail route `/c/:cid/queues/:qkey/:tab` can coexist. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the shape or asks for `/c/:cid/queues` and so on. |
| OQ-748 | Where is the `explorer` Transloco scope registered and what shape is its JSON (M0-54 spec Q-17)? | The generator writes `src/i18n/en.json` as `{"explorer": {...}}` and registers no scope; the shell's files are scope roots registered in `apps/web` (`SCOPE_LOADERS`). Default: the feature's route providers register the scope `explorer` with an `import()` loader of its own JSON, the file is the scope root, the feature imports `@jsverse/transloco` (not confined by lint; slice A confirms), and the route lists only `explorer` because a child route's scopes replace its parent's. Alternative: register it in `apps/web` with a relative import into the library. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-749 | What does the explorer tree show (M0-54 spec Q-18; OQ-536)? | Default: a static source in `apps/web` (the overview and four kinds, translated labels, no counts, no vhost level, no engine call), so opening the shell costs no broker request and the initial bundle gains nothing from the feature. A vhost level needs a vhost list (OQ-740); counts need `overview.get` or `inventory.query` per kind. The placeholder `ExplorerHome` and its two keys are removed. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-750 | How does the overview refresh (M0-54 spec Q-19)? | There is no overview topic and each `overview.get` makes up to three interactive-lane broker requests (I-8). Default: load on open and on a Refresh press with a 10 s floor; no polling. Alternative (engine side): an overview topic. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the floor or asks for the topic. |
| OQ-751 | What of the EXP-02 text does the overview deliver (M0-54 spec Q-20)? | The feature text lists top backlogs, DLQ totals, lint highlights and rates "from Prometheus"; `overview.get` carries none of them and no Prometheus reader exists. Default: not delivered and no placeholder card; stats-off rates read "unavailable" with the engine's reason; "from Prometheus" is never shown. Status: open - working default in use. | The features that own them land, or the maintainer narrows EXP-02. |
| OQ-752 | What is the capability card made of (M0-54 spec Q-21)? | `<app-gated>` always draws a button and no feature has a destination yet. Default: display rows built in the feature with the same fail-closed rules (`enabled === true`, `parseSourceLink`), reading `profile.get` itself (the status bar reads it too); `feature-connections` (M0-52) also plans a capability card and a feature cannot import a feature, so sharing is a later issue (a component in a lib). Alternative: ask `ui-design` (trust-critical) for a display-only variant. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks. |
| OQ-753 | Where do the hazard and freshness texts come from (M0-54 spec Q-22)? | Default: the page has its own sentences by freshness state and a key table for hazard ids (`explorer.hazard.<id>`, one id today: `stats-off-policy-fields-missing`), shows an unknown id as the id, and always adds the engine's fixed English `reason`. Alternative: the engine sends hazard text (a registry change). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-754 | How do cells, selection and the menu behave in the explorer grids (M0-54 spec Q-23)? | Default: a missing field reads "not reported" and is copied as that text (OQ-705); selection mode `multiple` for copying only; no context menu; no row activation (M1-42). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-755 | How are the explorer's interaction time and the perf harness defined (M0-54 spec Q-24; OQ-544, OQ-671)? | "Interaction" is the call (scroll jump, applied filter, facet, sort) to the rows of the first block; the filter's 200 ms pause is excluded and fixed; p95 over 1,000 interactions; thresholds 300 ms and a block limit of 200 (contract 500). The Node run measures engine plus adapter and says so; AC-26 is asserted at 20,000 and printed at 100,000; if it is flaky on shared runners it becomes a printed measurement (the OQ-720 precedent). The browser time (AC-27) waits for the DevTools driver; whether M0-53 or M0-54 builds it is open, as is where `perf:*` runs in CI. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the definitions, names who builds the browser driver, and places the target in CI. |
| OQ-756 | What stands in for `pnpm nx e2e e2e-web --grep=@explorer` (M0-54 spec Q-25; OQ-342)? | `apps/e2e-web` is M0-60 and Playwright and axe are not in the catalog. Default: the jsdom specs (roles, names, focus, live regions, requests), one recorded manual run at 1280x720 once Q-2 and #277 are settled (or "not run" with the reason in the commit body), and a follow-up issue for the `@explorer` spec with axe so the gap is tracked. Status: open - working default in use. | M0-60 lands and adds the `@explorer` spec. |
| OQ-757 | What is done about the grid chunk over the 200 kB budget (M0-54 spec Q-26; OQ-701)? | The grid with AG Grid is about 214 kB gzip without Angular (esbuild), over the 200 kB lazy limit, and `web:budgets` compares each lazy chunk. Default: the grid page is its own lazy boundary so the per-chunk number is read; slice I measures the real Angular chunk and records it in an ADR-021 addendum; nothing is loosened. If the chunk is over 200 kB the maintainer picks before slice I lands with `web:budgets` green: a limit for the grid chunk in `tools/web-budgets`, a shared lazy grid chunk, or another grid. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks, after slice I's measurement. |
| OQ-758 | Which ARIA patterns does the explorer write locally (M0-54 spec Q-27; OQ-340)? | `@angular/aria` and `@angular/cdk` are not in the catalog. Default: the chips, the facet pickers and the views control are written to the WAI-ARIA patterns (disclosure panels, as the column chooser), behind small components that can swap to the aria wrappers later. Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-340 is answered. |
| OQ-759 | How does the explorer word what it reads (M0-54 spec Q-28; DC-15)? | The plan's goal line calls it "the read-only explorer". Default: UI strings and the spec's own prose do not use "read-only", "non-destructive", "without side effects" or "safe peek" for anything the explorer does; it reads the inventory and reads no message, which the strings say plainly. The honest-wording rule applies to the `explorer` JSON. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-760 | Where does the plain logic of the explorer UI live (M0-54 spec Q-29)? | `apps/perf` and `libs/testkit` are `runtime:node` and may not import a `runtime:browser` project, but AC-12 and AC-26 need the real window source in Node. Default: a second new project, `explorer-model` (`type:pure`, `runtime:isomorphic`, `libs/shared/explorer-model`, made with the `library` generator like `json-stream`), with no Angular, DOM or `rxjs`; `feature-explorer` wraps its state in signals. Alternative: everything in `feature-explorer`, tested in jsdom only, giving up the real adapter in AC-12 and AC-26. The planned `grid-100k` browser harness of M0-53 meets the same rule. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-761 | How does the served page give Angular and the libraries the CSP nonce (#277; OQ-231, OQ-347, OQ-538, OQ-552)? | The engine already minted one nonce per response (CSP header and `__CSP_NONCE__` in `index.html`), but the built `apps/web/src/index.html` had no placeholder, so Angular's component styles were blocked and the shell collapsed. Default: `apps/web/src/index.html` carries the placeholder in `<meta name="csp-nonce">` and in `<app-root ngCspNonce>`; Angular reads the attribute (its default `CSP_NONCE` factory), the grid falls back to the meta tag (`GRID_STYLE_NONCE`, OQ-552), and no `CSP_NONCE` provider is added. The policy is unchanged (no `unsafe-inline` or `unsafe-eval` for scripts or style elements, `connect-src 'self'`, Trusted Types enforced). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the placeholder approach for the desktop `app://` protocol handler too (M2-P15 re-runs S-04 inside Electron), or picks another carrier. |
| OQ-762 | How is zod's `new Function('')` probe kept from violating Trusted Types in the web app (#277)? | zod 4 probes `new Function('')` whenever it builds an object schema; the browser refuses it under `require-trusted-types-for 'script'` and reports it (the one TrustedScript refusal of #277), although zod catches the error. Default: `@app/contracts` calls `z.config({ jitless: true })` as its first import when the realm has the `trustedTypes` API, and `apps/web/src/main.ts` imports `@app/contracts` first; the engine (Node) keeps zod's compiled fast path. No Trusted Types policy is added (a `TrustedScript` policy would let `new Function` run, which the S-04 findings rule out). Cost: object schemas in the browser take zod's interpreted parser; no measurement yet. Status: open - working default in use. | A web performance run (M0-60 or the perf smoke) shows whether the interpreted parser matters for the explorer windows; if it does, the alternative is a fixed zod version that skips the probe under a flag. |
| OQ-763 | Where does the browser proof of the served page's CSP run, and what happens without a browser (#277)? | `pnpm nx run web-csp-check:csp-check` needs a Chromium-family binary (`CSP_CHECK_BROWSER`, `PERF_BROWSER`, `CSP_SPIKE_BROWSER`, then Chrome or Edge in their default places). Default: it runs in the `affected` job of `ci.yml` with `CSP_CHECK_REQUIRE_BROWSER=1` (a missing browser fails); locally a missing browser prints `SKIPPED ... Nothing was proved` and exits 0. Playwright is not used (it is not wired in the catalog for this); the driver is plain Node on the DevTools protocol, like the S-04 harness. `apps/e2e-web` (M0-60) may absorb it. Status: open - working default in use. | M0-60 lands `apps/e2e-web` and either takes over this check or keeps it as the fast gate. |

## Engine wiring (issue #278, docs/specs/M0-ENGINE-WIRING.md)

The engine now registers `svc-connections`, `svc-capabilities`, `svc-workspace` and `svc-explorer`, registers `explorer.window` with the hub and ends the explorer on drain. OQ-441, OQ-472 and OQ-669 are answered for the registration, the topic sources and the drain phase; what they listed beyond that is the rows below. Every row has a working default in use.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-764 | Is the executor's `ProfileResolver` (fingerprint and gates on a plan, blueprint 5.1 steps 4 and 5) wired (OQ-472)? | Default: no. `composeEngine` passes none, so the executor uses `noProfiles` (no fingerprint, no gates). Every served action is a query or a command with no broker effect, so none needs it today. `svc-capabilities` exports the pieces (`computeGates`, `computeAllowedActions`) but no resolver. Status: open - working default in use. | The first served action that mutates a broker lands (M1), with a resolver over `repos.brokerProfiles`. |
| OQ-765 | What does the diagnostics bundle say about brokers (OQ-441)? | Default: nothing. `BrokerProfileStore` has `get`, `put` and `delete` and no listing, and a bundle needs a window of profiles (I-7), so `profiles` still answers `[]`. Status: open - working default in use. | `BrokerProfileStore` gains a bounded listing (a `trust:critical` port change) and the hook reads it. |
| OQ-766 | In which drain phases do the new parts stop? | Default: the explorer's `stop()` (final: it ends every subscription and sweep and refuses new ones) runs in `taps-firehose` for quit, SIGTERM and emergency; the BrokerScheduler closes (queued requests are refused, running ones aborted) and the management client drops its sockets in `flush`, for the same reasons. A suspend leaves all of it alone, because the engine comes back; the sweeps then idle out by themselves (`idleStopMs`). Status: open - working default in use. | The drain phases are revisited with the peek and tap services (M1), or a resume hook (blueprint 5.10) wants the sweeps stopped on suspend. |
| OQ-767 | What does the management target resolver assume about a profile? | Default: the route is direct (no tunnel yet); the TLS trust is the process's (bundled, system and organisation CAs) because a profile stores only `tls: boolean`, no CA and no `insecure` override; an `auth.kind` of `none` is sent as an empty basic login (the broker answers 401, which says so); the BrokerProfile is read at every request, so a re-probe is used at once; a login that cannot be opened (a locked vault, a credential sealed for another row) fails closed with `GATE_BLOCKED` before any request is made. `connections.test-http` and `connections.test-amqp` dial a draft with no connection id, so they are not scheduled (two reads on one client closed afterwards). Status: open - working default in use. | A profile gets CA and insecure fields (CON-01), tunnel routes land (M3-09), or the drafts are scheduled under a throwaway id. |
| OQ-768 | Is a broker 401 an engine 401? | `MgmtHttpError` codes map one to one to Problem codes, so a broker that refuses the stored login makes `probe.run` answer 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED` (with the broker's reason), the same status the router uses for a bad launch token. A UI that signs out on any 401 would do so here. Likewise a login that cannot be opened (`GATE_BLOCKED` from the resolver) is reported by `probe.run` as `BROKER_UNREACHABLE`, because its `PASS_THROUGH` list leaves `GATE_BLOCKED` out. Default: unchanged; the UI must tell the two by `Problem.broker`. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer chooses a distinct code (for example `FORBIDDEN_BROKER` or a new `BROKER_AUTH_REFUSED`) in `@app/contracts` and the adapter, and `probe.run` passes `GATE_BLOCKED` on. |
| OQ-769 | What does `pnpm nx run engine:serve` assume? | Default: port 4310 (`ENGINE_PORT`), data in `tmp/engine-data` (`ENGINE_DATA_DIR`), and a random 32-byte KEK file made beside it on the first start (`tmp/engine-data.kek`, mode 0600) because no OS keyring binding is bundled and nothing can type a passphrase (OQ-300, OQ-301, OQ-351); it builds the engine and the web app first. The KEK next to the database is a development convenience: it protects nothing against a person who can read the directory. Status: open - working default in use. | The keyring binding or a passphrase prompt lands, then `serve` stops making a key file. |
| OQ-770 | Can a connection be added to a running engine? (extends OQ-380, OQ-283, OQ-529) | No, today. `connections.create` and `bundles.import` are commands with `target: () => ({})`, so through the real router (no environment class, every call `prod`) the prod lock answers 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` and there is no connection to arm, the same as `views.*`, `settings.put` and `keybindings.put`. `compose-services.spec.ts` (AC-5) pins the refusal. The guardrails are not touched by this work. Options, all in the kernel or the contracts (trust-critical): (1) exempt a mutating action of risk `none` or `low` whose target has no connection (a profile row and a view are the caller's own state); (2) a declared `scope: 'local'` on the contract that `checkRegistry` insists has no broker port, exempt from the lock; (3) resolve the environment class per target (OQ-283) and read "no target" as `dev`; (4) arm a reserved id such as `workspace` for the actions with no target. The runbook (`docs/runbooks/run-locally.md`) says what a person can and cannot do meanwhile. Status: open - working default in use. Update (OQ-783): option 2 was taken. A connection can be added to a running engine through `connections.create` (and `bundles.import`) with nothing armed; `docs/runbooks/run-locally.md` shows how. The page still has no form for it (M0-52). | A maintainer rules on OQ-380 and OQ-283; the four HTTP specs and AC-5 then expect 200. |
| OQ-771 | What stays unwired in the engine after this work? | The write ports of the executor (`createUnwiredWrites`: every broker write, peek, source, publish and observe refuses with `CAPABILITY_MISSING`), the ConnectionManager and its `BudgetSource` (OQ-377), a re-probe on endpoint change and on resume (blueprint 5.10), `lastVerified` (OQ-478), the Prometheus read port, and any route other than direct. Default: unchanged. Status: open - working default in use. | The peek, publish and tap services (M1) wire the write side, and the probe scheduler of blueprint 5.10 lands. |
| OQ-772 | What did M0-54 slice A decide where the spec left a choice (Q-4, Q-10, Q-16, Q-17; OQ-677)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) `ui-grid` gained, additively, a `{kind: 'refresh'}` row change (reloads at most every 5 s, never dropped as not shown), an optional `refreshed()` on the source (called before every cache reload, refresh or retry) and `sortMode: 'multi' \| 'single'` (default `multi`): `single` sets AG Grid's `suppressMultiSort`, sends only the first sort to the source and, when a second sort is applied through the API or a stored state, takes it back out, keeps the first and emits `sortTrimmed`. This answers the OQ-677 question about change kinds for the explorer; a separate `insert` kind is not needed because the topic's marker becomes `refresh`. (2) The generated `explorerRoute` (full path `c/:cid/explorer`) and its spec were removed, because the shell mounts the feature's `explorerRoutes` at the child path `explorer` (Q-16). (3) `src/i18n/en.json` is the scope root and the feature's parent route registers the scope `explorer` itself with an `import()` loader (Q-17). (4) Component specs run through `test-components`: files named `*.component.spec.ts` plus an explicit list of page specs (`inventory-page`, `filter-bar`, `freshness-banner`, `views-control`, `overview`, `capability-card`, `explorer-requests`, `explorer-i18n`); the plain Vitest run excludes the same files. (5) The generator tests (`tools/generators`) generate a UI feature named `explorer` of their own, which collides with the real `@app/feature-explorer` alias, so the alias is in the adopted-alias list of `first-use.ts` and the end-to-end workspace (which copies no `libs/ui`) drops it too. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the grid deltas and the route shape. |
| OQ-773 | What did M0-54 slice B decide where the spec left a choice (R1, R2, Q-5, Q-7)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) `explorer-model` derives its contract types with `InputOf`/`OutputOf`/`ParamsOf`/`ItemOf` from `@app/contracts`, so it imports no `zod`. (2) The port's live events gain `end` (the engine ended the topic without a Problem) next to `error`. (3) The source drops, before sending, every facet that does not apply to the kind and every empty value (`sanitizeFilter`), so a saved or forced facet can never empty a grid; the page lists what was not applied. (4) After an answer that says the pinned version is gone the pin is cleared, one `refresh` change is asked (once per epoch), and the next block of the same scroll may pin the newer version until the grid reloads and calls `refreshed()`; `state().versionChanged` is the last answer's flag, so the page latches the notice. (5) A window that fails or is aborted never changes the pin; an answer of an older epoch updates freshness and the matched count but never the pin or a reload. (6) `createFakeExplorerData` keeps two index versions like the engine's index (a pin of an older version is gone), matches text on the name (bindings: source, destination and routing key too) and is not the engine. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms; the engine proofs of M0-48 stay the authority for the real filter and sort. |
| OQ-774 | What did M0-54 slice C decide where the spec left a choice (R3, Q-5, Q-6, Q-7)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) `watch` replaces the followed set with exactly the reported ids (no union, no margin of extra rows); an empty report sends nothing; the grid's own 100 ms debounce is the only settle. (2) `fitKeys` keeps the earliest ids that fit 48 KiB of key text (length + 3 each) and 500 keys and stops at the first that does not (a screen is read top down); a key over the contract's 1,024 characters is skipped and counted as left out. (3) A snapshot is reconciled against the followed keys only; a marker newer than the pin asks one `refresh` per version, and with no pin yet nothing is compared, so a first subscribe can ask one extra reload after any new sweep (Q-7, accepted). (4) A `reset` clears the pin, asks one reload and goes back to `connecting`; the live client resends the `sub`. (5) An `error` or `end` ends following for good for this source (no resubscribe); the grid keeps working from windows and the person reloads; `liveProblem` holds the Problem as received. (6) `status` is shown as the topic's health string; the model does not interpret it. (7) The counters gain `removals` (and `windowFailures`, `versionChanges`) beyond the spec's list, and the state gains `liveStatus`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms; measure the resubscribe rate in slice J before adding a settle or a margin. |
| OQ-775 | What does AC-15 of M0-48l (#276) prove after the macOS CI failure of commit 261630e, and what does it leave unproven (OQ-720, OQ-724, OQ-731)? | Cause: on 	oolchain (macos-15) the old assertion read 26.5 ms added (p99 39.2 ms with the reader, 12.7 ms for the transfer alone, 4.8 ms idle, max 50.5 ms; the read took 1,219 ms against 231 ms without the reader, about 4x slower than a development machine). monitorEventLoopDelay reports the whole interval between two ticks, so a 3-vCPU shared runner that preempts the process, runs garbage collection or stalls the sampler thread cannot be told from a held loop; it is the case OQ-731 recorded under 64 busy processes. A second finding: the old test could not have caught a reader that holds the loop. With the yield removed from the reader (a temporary mutant, reverted) the read still paced itself on the socket chunks, the loop turned between them, and the p99 stayed at about 3 ms. Default now: AC-15 keeps its title and its two jobs, and asserts counts the OS cannot stretch. The body of the real read is first taken whole through the real client (about 31 MiB held in the test only), so the reader is handed a source that is always ready and nothing but its own yield can give the loop a turn; a setImmediate chain counts the turns of the loop and books to each turn the bytes the reader pulled and the items it delivered. Asserted: (1) more turns than ytes / (256 KiB + largest chunk); (2) at most 256 KiB plus the largest chunk pulled per turn; (3) at most 5,000 items per turn (the defaults of openJson, repeated in the test); (4) the cheapest turn that did at least 90% of the byte share took at most 20 ms (preemption only lengthens a turn, so a minimum is stable; measured 1.4 to 1.6 ms on a quiet host, 2.0 to 2.9 ms with 4 busy loops and the test pinned to the same 2 cores, 1.8 to 2.2 ms with 64 busy processes pinned to 2 cores); plus every item and byte arriving. Mutants, all failing on (1): no yield (1 turn of 32,250,001 bytes and 150,000 items, 181 ms), a yield every 1 MiB (31 turns), a yield every 64 MiB and 20,000 items (8 turns, 4.3 MB and 29.6 ms at worst). The loop-delay p99 and maximum are printed, not asserted (the OQ-720 fallback, for the measurement only; AC-7 of json-stream stays the deterministic unit gate and AC-15 is its wire counterpart). Not proven: that no turn of the reader exceeds 20 ms on a given machine (the worst turn is printed: 3 to 5 ms quiet, up to 29 ms under 64 busy processes pinned to 2 cores, which is preemption), the delay seen with a socket-paced reader, the engine's loop under sweep plus tap load (I-5 stays planned for the perf harness), and the heap (the printed heap peak was dropped: the test now holds the body). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the counts-plus-best-case form, or asks for the p99 back as a perf-harness measurement (M0-48 perf) with a dedicated runner. |
| OQ-776 | What did M0-54 slice D decide where the spec left a choice (R4, R7, Q-22, Q-23, Q-27)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) Pages translate through the `*transloco` directive and hand its `t` to pure helpers (`inventoryColumns`, `gridLabels`, `freshnessLines`), because AG Grid reads its locale text once and the labels must exist when the grid is built; the bundle is memoised per `t`. (2) A bindings "at least N" is chosen when the engine's reason contains "is not known" (its fixed text for a count that is a minimum); if the engine words it differently the page says N without "at least", never less. (3) The banner's lines are plain text; a separate visually hidden polite status region announces the state without counts, so a count that moves while loading is not read again. (4) Booleans read `true` and `false` and counts use `Intl.NumberFormat` (R7: read as they are); a missing field reads "not reported". (5) The page shows its own "{matched} of {total} rows" line, because the grid status bar knows only the matched count. (6) The grid's empty text is neutral ("No rows to show"), since AG Grid cannot change it after creation; the banner carries loading and incomplete. (7) Inventory pages are routed through `InventoryRoute` (kind from route data, `?vhost=` for bindings), one lazy chunk for all four kinds. (8) The jsdom page spec scrolls with `ensureIndexVisible` jumps in a renderAll grid (8 jumps; eviction beyond 25 blocks is the grid's own M0-53 proof) and a new `@app/ui-grid/testing/components` entry exports the warm-up and wait helpers of the grid specs. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms; the real-browser run of AC-27 proves scrolling. |
| OQ-777 | What did M0-54 slice E decide where the spec left a choice (R5, R6, Q-8, Q-9, Q-10, Q-11)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) The facet pickers offer suggestions as checkboxes (queue types classic, quorum and stream; exchange types direct, fanout, topic and headers; the AMQP protocols; a few broker states) and any exact value may be typed; exchange types are not read from `profile.exchangeTypes` yet (the profile is read by the overview and capability card, slice H), so a plugin exchange type is typed. (2) The vhost of a bindings page is typed in a chooser and lives in `?vhost=`; it is not a removable chip, because the engine requires it. (3) The bar emits only the contract's filter with the facets that apply to the kind (a forced facet is neither shown nor sent); a text over 200 characters is cut with a reason, and the caps are 16 types, 16 states, 25 vhost chips (the contract allows 100). (4) A text change applies after 200 ms through an injectable `ExplorerTimer` (a manual clock in specs); Enter applies at once and cancels the pause. (5) `WindowSourceState` gains `windowError` (the Problem of the last failed window, cleared by the next success) and the banner shows it as "The inventory could not be read" with the Problem as sent, which is how a bindings page shows an unknown vhost. (6) A filter that matches nothing says so and lists its chips, never while the first read is running. (7) The sortable lists live in `explorer-model`; the testkit spec runs the real window source over the real router and action, both directions for every offered column, and checks that every other column is refused. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms; an `inventory.facets` action (OQ-740) would replace the suggestions. |
| OQ-778 | What did M0-54 slice F decide where the spec left a choice (R8, Q-12, Q-13, Q-14, Q-15)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) A view is `{version: 1, kind, filter, columns}`; applying one drops, and lists, what the kind cannot apply (facets that do not apply, unknown or non-sortable columns, values over a cap cut at the UI caps, a second sort column) and says "nothing changed" when nothing applies; a view of another kind or a newer version applies nothing. Applying never resets state it does not name: a view that applies nothing leaves the current filters and columns as they are. (2) A bindings view keeps its vhost in `filter.vhosts` and applying it opens that vhost (`?vhost=`). (3) The applied view lives in `?view=`; the page reads nothing until a view named there is resolved, so its first read is the view's own. (4) Save, Replace and Delete are gated from `profile.get`'s `allowedActions` read by the page (a profile that is loading, failed, does not parse or has no `allowedActions` closes every gate with a stated reason); Delete and Replace ask inline first; replace is create then delete and a failed delete leaves both views, listed. (5) The views list is read for the kind when the page opens (200 views; more are said, not paged) and again after a save or delete. (6) A bug found here and fixed in `ui-grid`: a replaced datasource answered AG Grid's late `getRows` calls with nothing, so the grid kept counting those blocks as loading and the next datasource never loaded when two were pending (the concurrency cap is 2); a datasource that was destroyed, and its blocks that were waiting, now release every block (`fail()`), without counting a failure. (7) CI showed that a spec that mounts a whole page (the real grid in jsdom, a fake engine and the bars) takes 5 to 8 s on a shared runner, over the 5 s default: those specs carry one justified timeout (`PAGE_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, 20 s, commented) and the fake data port defaults to fewer rows (400 queues), because every row the grid holds is rendered in a DOM with no layout. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-779 | What did M0-54 slice G decide where the spec left a choice (R9, Q-19, Q-20)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) The overview is read when the page opens and again only when Refresh is pressed after a floor of 10 s; the floor is a timer started at each read (an injectable timer, so a spec moves it by hand), not a wall-clock comparison, and a read that fails starts it too because it cost the broker the same. (2) Refresh stays focusable and is `aria-disabled` while closed, with its reason in text beside it and tied to it with `aria-describedby`; a press while closed does nothing. (3) The messages line says where the numbers come from ("Reported by the broker" or "Summed from the last queue read") and the time they describe, and "Partial" when the sum is not complete; the inventory freshness block is the grid banner (kind `queues` for its wording), so a partial or loading index is worded exactly as on the grids. (4) A rate the broker did not send reads "not reported" next to one it did; "from Prometheus" appears nowhere. (5) A failed read keeps the last good answer and marks it as from an earlier read, with the engine's Problem as sent in an alert. (6) The placeholder landing page and the root `title` key were removed with this page. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-780 | What did M0-54 slice H decide where the spec left a choice (R10, Q-21)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) The card reads each gate of the raw profile body on its own, not through the strict `BrokerProfileSchema`: a gate that is `{}`, a string or a non-object is a closed row, so one malformed gate does not turn the whole card into "unknown"; a profile with no gates object, an empty one, a failed call or an unprobed connection is unknown. The strict parse stays in the profile store for `allowedActions` (a profile that does not parse closes the views gates). (2) The state words are "Available", "Available with limits" and "Not available"; an open gate shows no reason, a closed or degraded one shows the engine's reason as sent (or "No reason given") and its source, which is a link only when it is an absolute https URL without credentials, with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. (3) The eight known ids have names (`amqp.peek` reads "Side-effect-aware peek"); any other id is shown as the id. (4) The card is a region of the overview page, shares the page's profile read with nothing else, and is not shared with `feature-connections` (a feature never imports a feature). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms; a shared card moves to a lib when M0-52 lands its own. |
| OQ-781 | What did M0-54 slice I decide where the spec left a choice (R11, R12, Q-18, Q-24, Q-25, Q-26)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) The shell mounts the explorer with `loadChildren: () => import('@app/feature-explorer').then((m) => m.explorerRoutesFor(() => inject(ActiveConnection).cid))`, because the workspace rule that a lazy library is never imported statically by an app forbids the plain `explorerRoutes` constant; the feature still takes its connection from `EXPLORER_CONNECTION`, so it never imports the app, and an id that is not a plain token shows the shell's no-connection view and makes no call. (2) The tree source is a static list of five nodes (overview and the four kinds, no counts, no vhost level, no engine call), so opening the shell costs the broker nothing; deeper nodes wait for a later work package. The placeholder `explorer-home` view is removed. (3) The AG Grid chunk is a lazy chunk the explorer pulls in, which exceeds the 200 kB lazy-chunk limit on its own (OQ-757); the narrowest exception is one named entry (240,000 bytes for that chunk) in `LAZY_EXCEPTIONS` of `tools/web-budgets/check-budgets.mjs`, with an ADR-021 addendum line, and every other budget is unchanged. (4) AC-25 is proved in two halves: the source and the English file are read as text by a node test in `tools/web-gallery-check` (browser code may not read files, and a generated e2e workspace of the testkit has no explorer): every key literal exists, every key is asked for, no forbidden wording, no `innerHTML`, and the overview (with data and with a failed read) and a grid page are mounted with a scope that answers `[[explorer.<path>]]`, so every visible string, `aria-label`, `title` and placeholder is a key or engine data. States that need a driven interaction (open pickers, the views panel in each state) are covered by the key scan, not by the mounted scan. (5) The generator self-tests adopt the `@app/feature-explorer` alias as an existing project, so a generated feature never collides with it. (6) No browser run was made for this slice: the proof is jsdom, fakes and the real composed router, and a manual browser check is listed as not run. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms; the browser proof lands with the e2e work package. |
| OQ-782 | What did M0-54 slice J decide where the spec left a choice (Q-24, Q-25; AC-26, AC-27, AC-28)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) `perf:explorer-ui-20k` drives the real window source of `explorer-model` through a data port that runs the real `inventory.query` action over the 20,000-queue fake index while sweeps install versions; 1,000 interactions (scroll jumps, text filters, facet changes, sorts) are timed from the call to the first block; it asserts limit at most 200 on every request, no window failure and p95 at most 300 ms, and prints the 100,000-queue run (`PERF_EXPLORER_UI_LARGE=0` skips it). It has no live topic, so its resubscribe and refresh counts are the source's reaction to the rows it was told were on screen; it says in its output that it measures engine plus adapter in Node only. (2) AC-27, the browser time, stays planned: no browser driver exists and no browser figure is claimed; the follow-up is a new issue for `explorer-ui-20k.browser.perf.ts` and the Playwright spec with axe (Q-25), to be opened by the maintainer. (3) AC-28 is two specs: `explorer-requests.spec.ts` mounts the grid page (filter, sort, views apply, save, delete, live follow) and the overview (open, spaced refresh) through the real engine-backed data port, `EngineApi` and live client over a recording fake engine and checks every request and client frame against an allow-list (six actions and the `explorer.window` topic, no plan or execute, no `fetch`, no credential words); it is not a global hook over every page spec, which would not aggregate across spec files. `libs/testkit/src/explorer-ui/explorer-calls.spec.ts` sends the same calls through the real router (`createTestEngine`) to the real actions with the services' in-memory fakes and parses each answer with the contract schema. (4) What that check found: `views.create` and `views.delete` answer 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` from the router today (the prod lock has no connection to arm, OQ-380), so a view cannot be saved or deleted on a running engine, while the capability fake deps have no authorizer and so list no `allowedActions`; the UI shows the engine's Problem as sent (AC-19) and the spec pins today's answer so it fails, and must be updated, when OQ-380 is ruled on. Status: open - working default in use. Update (2026-10-22): item (4) is superseded. The maintainer ruled on OQ-380 by the declared local scope (OQ-783): `views.create` and `views.delete` are no longer locked and `explorer-calls.spec.ts` now expects 200 for both through the router, so the "pins today's answer" text no longer applies. Item (2) is superseded too: slice K (#280) built the browser run (OQ-787) on the same machinery. | The maintainer rules on OQ-380 (view commands with no connection) and opens the browser-proof issue; the spec of AC-28 then asserts a 200 for the two commands. |
| OQ-783 | What exactly does `scope: 'local'` allow past the prod lock, and what stays closed? (answers OQ-380, OQ-283, OQ-529, OQ-770 by option 2) | Agile mode, the maintainer chose option 2; working defaults in use. `defineAction` takes an optional `scope: 'local'`: the action changes only the engine's own state (a saved profile, a view, a setting, a keybinding) and never a broker. The guardrail policy (`libs/engine/kernel/src/guardrails/policy.ts`) lets a mutating action past the lock only when its contract declares it AND the call names no target (no connection, vhost, kind, name or count); it comes after the AI-client refusal, so an AI client still has no mutations in prod, and the permission check, the plan rules, the audit chain and the receipts are unchanged. Enforcement is in four places: (1) `checkRegistry` rejects `scope` on a peek or a job, on a value other than `'local'`, on an input or path that carries `cid`, `connectionId`, `vhost`, `qkey`, `queue` or `exchange`, and on an action an AI client may call to mutate; (2) the executor refuses a call whose `target()` names anything (`INTERNAL`, nothing runs, nothing is audited); (3) the policy applies the same test, so a different executor cannot loosen it; (4) the architecture test (`checkLocalScopeModules`) fails the module of a local action that names a write-capable broker symbol (`*WritePort`, a role lease, `ConnectionManager`, `ctx.writes`). The type system cannot say which ports a module uses, so (4) is a syntactic check and (1) is by field name: an input field that addresses a broker object under another name would pass (1), which is why a human reviews each new `scope: 'local'` (CODEOWNERS covers the kernel and the contracts). Declared today: `connections.create`, `bundles.import`, `views.create`, `views.delete`, `settings.put`, `keybindings.put`. Not declared: `layouts.put` (OQ-784), `connections.update` and `connections.delete` (OQ-785), `connections.test-http` and `connections.test-amqp` (they dial a broker; they are queries and are never locked anyway). No allow-list by name, no environment-class bypass, no reserved id. | A CODEOWNERS review of the kernel change; then the field-name list of (1) is revisited if a local action ever needs one of those names. |
| OQ-784 | Should `layouts.put` be `scope: 'local'`? (extends OQ-529) | No, today. It saves a document of the engine's own store, but it names the connection profile (`cid`) as its target, and a local contract may name none. Declaring it local needs `target: () => ({})`, which drops the connection id from its audit rows and from the authorizer's request (a team-server role could then no longer scope `workspace.write` by connection, M3). Until it is ruled on, a layout save on a connection that is not armed answers 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` and a restart restores nothing there; the shell keeps the layout in memory and shows the Problem. Options: (a) drop the target and keep `cid` in the input; (b) let a local contract name a connection in the profile store but never a broker (needs a second kind of target); (c) leave it armed-only. | The maintainer rules; (a) is a one-line change plus a test. |
| OQ-785 | Do `connections.update` and `connections.delete` need arming on a prod-classed connection? | Yes, today, unchanged: they name the connection (`cid`), the router sends no environment class (OQ-283), so every connection counts as production and the person arms it (`ops.arm`, 15 minutes) before editing or deleting a profile. That is the lock doing what it was built for (a profile edit can repoint a prod connection), but with no `dev` class resolved per target it also asks for arming on a lab broker. `connections.create` is not affected: a new profile names no connection. | OQ-283 (class per target) is ruled on; then a `dev` or `local` connection stops asking. |
| OQ-786 | Can an AI client call a `scope: 'local'` action in prod? | No. The AI-client refusal of the prod lock runs before the local exemption, so an MCP principal, however far down the chain, still has no mutations in prod, local or not (blueprint 5.9, row AI), and `checkRegistry` rejects `scope: 'local'` on an action that exposes a mutating MCP tool. None of the six has an `mcp` entry today. | The MCP surface (M2) decides whether profile and view management may ever be offered to an AI client. |
| OQ-787 | What did M0-54 slice K (#280, AC-27) decide where the spec left a choice (Q-3, Q-24, Q-25)? | Agile mode, working defaults in use. (1) No harness page: the page under test is the real production web build served by the real engine bundle under the real Content-Security-Policy (issue #277 is settled; `web-csp-check` proves the page renders styled), opened at `/c/perf/explorer/queues` at 1280x720 in a real Chromium found the way `web-csp-check` finds one. The driver is `apps/perf/src/explorer-ui-20k/cdp.ts`, a TypeScript form of `tools/web-csp-check/cdp.mjs` kept in step by hand (a test project does not import a tool script); no dependency was added. (2) The fake data port stands at the HTTP boundary: the engine bundle registers no explorer module (OQ-441), so `POST /connections/perf/inventory/queries` is answered by the harness from a 20,000-row `createFakeExplorerData` through the DevTools `Fetch` domain; `EngineApi`, `HttpClient`, the window source, AG Grid and the filter bar are the real ones. (3) The live socket is replaced inside the page by a stand-in that answers hello, ping and sub (subok, then a snapshot that holds only the version marker): the real engine ends the `explorer.window` subscription with "no such connection", which puts the page in its "live updates ended" state and makes each query run twice. (4) An interaction is timed from `event.timeStamp` of the click or Enter, or `performance.now()` just before a `scrollTop` jump, to the end of the first animation frame whose DOM holds the rows an oracle (a second fake) says belong there (names of the 8 rows at the top of the viewport, the matched count, the sort mark, the scroll position); the end is a message posted from that frame's animation-frame callback, which runs after the main-thread work of the frame (so it is not light on a screen, and a figure is a multiple of the 16.7 ms frame). The filter's 200 ms pause is excluded by construction: the text and the Enter key go in one task, and Enter applies the filter and cancels the pause. Sorts and facet checkboxes are real mouse clicks (a plain `click()` does not sort in AG Grid); scroll jumps are `scrollTop` assignments, the scrollbar's jump. (5) 500 interactions are timed after 12 warm-up ones (a fixed sequence; Q-24 said 1,000 for the Node run, and the browser run takes about 2.5 minutes for 500, `PERF_EXPLORER_UI_BROWSER_INTERACTIONS` raises it). (6) Flake policy: the two timing budgets (p95, long tasks) repeat the whole run up to 3 attempts (`PERF_EXPLORER_UI_BROWSER_ATTEMPTS`) and pass when one attempt meets them, all attempts printed; a CSP or Trusted Types report, a request over a block of 200, rows that never appear and fewer timed interactions than planned fail at once. The page proves its two detectors alive before anything is timed (a 90 ms busy loop must be a long task, an inline script a CSP report), and `PERF_EXPLORER_UI_BROWSER_ANSWER_DELAY_MS=400` slows the data port to show the gate fails (p95 455 ms). (7) CI: a step in the `affected` job of `ci.yml` after `web-csp-check`, on Linux with `PERF_REQUIRE_BROWSER=1`; without a browser the test is skipped loudly everywhere else. (8) First measured run on a development machine (Windows 11, Intel Core i9-14900HX 32 logical CPUs, 31.7 GB, Chrome 155.0.8059.85, headless, software compositing): 500 interactions, p50 32.7 ms, p95 50.1 ms, max 68.3 ms (scroll 53.6 / text filter 39.1 / facet 50.1 / sort 38.4 ms at p95); three runs gave a p95 of 45.6 to 50.3 ms; 573 requests, the largest limit 200, the data port's own answer p95 3 ms, no long task above 50 ms, no CSP or Trusted Types report. A browser time exists only for that machine; no other is claimed. Status: open - working default in use. Update (2026-10-22): in (2), the engine bundle has registered the explorer module since issue #278 (`apps/engine/src/modules/index.ts`); the `Fetch` stand-in stays for a different reason: the served engine has no broker and no connection `perf`, so the module would answer "no such connection" and `inventory.query` would be 404. Item (3) is the same: the real topic exists but would end the subscription for the same reason. | The maintainer confirms the design and the CI step, and says whether the CI step stays blocking once a hosted-runner figure exists (the OQ-720 precedent would make a timing miss a printed measurement). |
| OQ-788 | What does the AC-27 browser run not prove, and what must be run again when the engine and the topic exist? | Not exercised: a real broker; the engine's `svc-explorer` and the router in the path (the Node run `perf:explorer-ui-20k` has the action, not the page); transport (the answer comes over the DevTools protocol, so a network, TLS and an HTTP server cost nothing here); the live `explorer.window` topic (a stand-in socket sends only the version marker, so the page never receives a row change, and the rate of `unsub` plus `sub` while scrolling is not measured; the topic's engine cost is `perf:explorer-20k`); a GPU, a physical display and a device scale factor other than 1; any browser but Chromium; data other than 20,000 queues of one shape (no argument-heavy rows, no stream queues' extra fields). The viewport leaves the grid 312 px, so 8 rows are checked per step; a taller grid shows more rows and does more work per frame. The figure is for the machine and browser it printed. Default: AC-27 stays a headless, single-machine, fake-engine proof and says so in its output, README and commit body. Re-run and extend it when the engine bundle registers `svc-explorer` and the topic (Q-2, OQ-441): drop the `Fetch` stand-in and the socket stand-in, point the page at a real connection to a lab broker with 20,000 queues, and compare. Status: open - working default in use. Update (2026-10-22): the condition "when the engine bundle registers `svc-explorer` and the topic" is met on the module side (#278, OQ-733); what is still owed is the connection: a real connection to a lab broker with 20,000 queues (a Docker daemon; `libs/testkit/src/it/seed-queues.ts`), after which the `Fetch` and socket stand-ins can be dropped and the figure compared. | The work package that registers the explorer module and its topic source (Q-2) replaces the stand-ins; the maintainer decides whether a hosted-runner or a dedicated-runner figure is recorded for S-13. |
| OQ-789 | Should a new filter send the explorer grid back to its first row? | Found by the AC-27 browser run. A text or facet filter applied while the grid is scrolled far down keeps AG Grid's scroll position, clamped to the new length: from row 1,668 of a long list, a filter that left 127 rows put the viewport at the end of the new list (rows 107 to 126), not at the top. A sort goes back to the top. A person who filters expects to see the best match first, and the page does not say that the view stayed where it was. Default: unchanged (this slice changes no UI); the browser run starts each filter step from the top, by an untimed return, so the rows it checks are known, and says so in `apps/perf/README.md` and the `feature-explorer` README. Option: `ui-grid` (or the page) scrolls to the first row when the datasource is replaced by a filter; one `ensureIndexVisible(0)` after `setGridOption('datasource')`, with a spec in `ui-grid` and a step of AC-27 that filters from deep in the list. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer decides whether a filter resets the scroll, and (if yes) which library owns it. |

## Spec questions: M0-52

Working defaults used by `docs/specs/M0-52.md` (agile mode: the approval stop was waived). Q-n is OQ-(789+n).
