| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-790 | Where does the connections page live (M0-52 spec Q-1)? | The shell host is `c/:cid` and needs a connection. Default: a lazy `/connections` outside it (list, `new`, `:id`, `:id/edit`), reached from the switcher's "Manage connections" link and from the no-connection view. Alternative: inside the shell with a sidebar entry, which needs the shell to render without a connection. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or asks for the sidebar entry. |
| OQ-791 | Which environment class does the create form preselect (M0-52 spec Q-2)? | The engine reads an unset class as `prod`. Default: `dev`, because the first use is a lab broker; the class is a required, visible choice and the env chrome follows it live. Alternative: no preselection. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-792 | One login or two for the two endpoints (M0-52 spec Q-3; DC-37)? | Default: the create form has "use the same user name and password for AMQP" on (it copies the management login into the AMQP fields at submit; the edit form has it off and never copies a stored secret). Two separate logins are one switch away. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-793 | Where does the UI learn whether a connection is armed (M0-52 spec Q-4; extends OQ-530)? | No action returns the arming state. Default: `ArmingStore` remembers what its own `ops.arm` answered (`expiresAt`, clock-driven); after a reload it reads "locked" (fail closed) and a second unlock is harmless. The status bar's `LOCK_STATE` comes from it. Alternative: an engine query for the state (a contract change, kernel owner). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer names the source, or a query lands. |
| OQ-794 | Two capability cards (M0-52 spec Q-5; follows OQ-780)? | A feature never imports a feature, so `feature-connections` has its own pure card model and component beside the explorer's. Default: duplicate now, extract one into a shared lib in a follow-up issue. Status: open - working default in use. | The extraction issue lands. |
| OQ-795 | Does the UI pre-compute the prod lock for a control (M0-52 spec Q-6; AGENTS.md section 9)? | The issue's AC-1 says a PROD profile's mutating controls are disabled with reason `prod-lock` until the unlock is confirmed. Default: the gate of a control is built from two engine facts (the profile's `envClass`, unset or unknown reading `prod`, and the answer of `ops.arm`) and shown through `<app-gated>`; the engine stays the authority and its 422 `GATE_BLOCKED` is shown as sent, with its own fix offered (the unlock dialog), including for a `dev` profile, which the engine still locks (OQ-283, OQ-785). Alternative: enable every control and rely on the refusal path, which does not meet the AC. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or the engine sends a gate per control. |
| OQ-796 | What does the connection switcher do with an address that is not a page (M0-52 spec Q-7)? | Object pages do not exist (M1-42). Default: page-level explorer addresses carry over; anything else is asked of `ADDRESS_EXISTS` (default: no), and a "no" opens the explorer root of the target with a dismissible notice naming the address. AC-2 of the issue is proved against a fake that says yes or no. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-42 provides the check. |
| OQ-797 | No human-only action for M0-52 (M0-52 spec Q-8). | No dependency, protected path or budget limit change was needed at spec time. If a chunk or the initial bundle exceeds a limit, the number is reported and the exception recorded in `tools/web-budgets` as M0-54 did, never silently. Status: open - recorded for the report. | The WP lands. |
| OQ-798 | Should the whole web unit-test suite run one file at a time? | The ci run 39732699215 (first attempt) ended `explorer-tree.spec.ts` at vitest's 5 s default (5.7 s) on a loaded hosted runner; a re-run passed. Cause: the specs mount the real shell, and some tabs mount the real grid in jsdom; twenty files at once on a three or four core runner (with other Nx tasks beside them) starve each other. Measured on a 32-thread development machine with 96 busy processes added: the first tree test took 2.8 to 3.4 s with files in parallel and 0.9 to 1.4 s one file at a time; the suite takes about 11 s either way (without extra load 10.3 to 12.5 s parallel, 11.0 s serial). Default: `apps/web/vitest.config.mts` sets `fileParallelism: false` for the `web` test target (the `feature-explorer` component-spec precedent, commit 1bfedd8), and the one spec that opens the real inventory page by a click carries a commented 20 s timeout like `explorer-mount.spec.ts`. Option: leave the suite parallel and split only the heavy specs into their own target. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer says whether serial files stay once the hosted runners' figures show the cost of the web target. |
| OQ-810 | Where does the local web mode live while `apps/cli` is only a version holder? | The issue (#51) scopes `apps/cli/src/commands/serve.ts` and verifies with `pnpm nx test cli` and `pnpm nx e2e e2e-web --grep=@local-security`. `apps/cli` has no `project.json` and no source, there is no `cli` test target, and `apps/e2e-web` does not exist (M0-60). The re-sequencing note says `npx serve` stays internal until M2-P17. So M0-59 puts the whole behaviour in the engine bundle (`--host`, `--public-origin`, `--token-file`, `--open`, the `APP_*` layer), proved by `nx test engine` and `nx run engine:smoke`; the later `npx <product> serve` wrapper only forwards these flags. Status: open - working default in use. | The CLI work package builds the wrapper; M0-60 adds the `@local-security` browser cases on top of the same engine. |
| OQ-811 | Are the flags of the web mode right? | `--host` (loopback values only in native), `--public-origin` (comma separated), `--token-file` (read only; the file must exist, nothing is written), `--open` and `--no-open` (the only bare flags; default is no browser, as the task says, although the issue lists `--no-open` as if the default opened one), `--port` (native default 0, container 8765), `--data-dir`. A repeated flag or both switches is a usage error. The issue's blueprint line says "optional `--token-file` 0600", which could mean "write the token there"; it is read here because a fixed token from a secret file is the container need. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the wrapper to open the browser by default, or `--token-file` to create the file when it is absent. |
| OQ-812 | Which environment variables does the container layer read, and under what prefix? | `APP_` (the placeholder prefix of `loadConfig`, ADR-029): `APP_RUNTIME` (`native` or `container`; the issue's `<PRODUCT>_RUNTIME`), `APP_HOST`, `APP_PORT`, `APP_DATA_DIR`, `APP_KEK_PATH` (a path; `APP_KEK_FILE` would mean "the key is in this file" to `loadConfig`), `APP_UI_DIR`, `APP_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` (the issue's `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`), `APP_TOKEN`, `APP_TOKEN_FILE` (the file form of the token), `APP_LOG_LEVEL`. `loadConfig` rejects an unknown `APP_*` key, so a misspelt variable stops the start (exit 64) instead of being ignored; the cost is that a future variable in an image must be added here first. The variables are read for every mode but only `--mode=local` uses them. Status: open - working default in use. | ADR-029 names the product (the prefix changes in one constant), and the image work package (M0-64) confirms the list. |
| OQ-813 | May the launch token come from an environment variable? | I-10 says secrets are never in env. The task asks for "token provided by env or file", so `APP_TOKEN` works, the banner says `docker inspect` can show it, and `APP_TOKEN_FILE` (a file secret) is the documented form. The token is an engine token, not a broker credential. It is still checked (32 to 512 characters of `A-Za-z0-9_-`), never printed in an error and never logged (the log says only where it came from: `generated`, `env` or `file`). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer forbids `APP_TOKEN` (remove the variable; the file form stays). |
| OQ-814 | Is a loopback-only `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` right when the issue says the container "accepts PUBLIC_ORIGIN"? | Amendment A-12 (binding) refuses a non-loopback origin unless real authentication and https are configured, and neither exists in this build, so every origin that is not `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]` is refused at start (exit 78) with the SSH-tunnel guidance. A loopback-form origin is accepted, goes to the transport's exact Host policy, and gives the banner and the URL their host port. The transport already accepts any listed origin; the refusal is in the mode file, so lifting it is one condition when M3 lands real auth. Status: open - working default in use. | M3 (OIDC or hashed passwords plus TLS) turns the refusal into a check of that configuration. |
| OQ-815 | A made token in container mode is printed in `docker logs`. Is that acceptable? | Without `APP_TOKEN` or `APP_TOKEN_FILE` the engine makes a token and prints the launch URL (with the token in its fragment) to standard output, like a notebook server. Whoever can read `docker logs` can read it; they can already `docker exec`. The banner says so and names `APP_TOKEN_FILE` for a fixed token. It is never written to a log line (the logger redacts and the diagnostics bundle removes it). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the container to refuse to start without a provided token. |
| OQ-816 | Is the redirect-file opener right for `--open`, and was it run on every platform? | Passing the launch URL to `xdg-open`, `open` or `start` would put the token in the process list (I-10). The engine writes `open.html` (a meta refresh to the URL, mode 0600 on POSIX) in a fresh `mkdtemp` directory, hands the opener that path, and deletes the directory after 30 s or at once when the spawn fails. Unit tests prove the argv, the file and the cleanup with a fake spawn; it was run once by hand on Windows (`rundll32 url.dll,FileProtocolHandler`). macOS `open` and Linux `xdg-open` are not run. On Windows the file mode is not enforced; the temp directory is per user. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer runs `--open` on macOS and a Linux desktop once; or the opener serves the redirect from a one-shot loopback listener instead of a file (a second listener, so not preferred). |
| OQ-817 | Is the healthcheck a script next to the bundle, and does it use `/healthz`? | `apps/engine/scripts/healthcheck.mjs` (Node only, `127.0.0.1`, `APP_PORT` or `--port`, 3 s timeout, exit 0 only for `{"status":"ok"}`). The blueprint says `HEALTHCHECK node /app/healthcheck.mjs` against `/healthz`; `/readyz` is stricter (503 while starting or draining) but a draining container would then be marked unhealthy and restarted by an orchestrator. M0-64 copies the file into the image. A `--healthcheck` flag of the bundle was not chosen: it would put an HTTP client in the engine bundle. Status: open - working default in use. | M0-64 confirms the file name and path in the image. |
| OQ-818 | What may `--host` be in container mode, and do the tests bind the wildcard? | `0.0.0.0` (default), `::`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1` and `localhost`; a specific interface address is refused because the namespace decides exposure and a typo would silently change it. The in-process and bundle tests bind `0.0.0.0` on a system-picked port and reach it through `127.0.0.1`; on a developer machine the operating system may show a firewall prompt once for Node. `meta.get` still answers `mode: "local"` in container mode: `EngineMode` is not extended (contracts are out of scope). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants a specific container bind address allowed, or a `container` value in the `EngineMode` contract. |
| OQ-819 | Who sends the idempotency key of a command (found by the M0-52 browser run)? | The engine refuses every command, peek and job without an `Idempotency-Key` header (`VALIDATION_FAILED`, "idempotencyKey is required"), and `EngineApi.call` sent none, so no command of the UI (the connection form, the saved views, the layout save) could succeed against a real engine; the specs ran over fakes that do not check it. Default (agile mode): `EngineApi.call` makes a key per call for every action whose kind is not `query` (a UUID, 36 characters, inside the engine's alphabet), once per call so a repeat of the same Observable repeats the key; `execute` already did. Proved in `engine-api.spec.ts` and through the real router in `connections-calls.spec.ts`. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms, or the key moves to the transport. |
| OQ-820 | Playwright pointer clicks were intermittently swallowed in the M0-52 browser run. | In the session's Playwright browser a real pointer click on a link or button of the served app sometimes did nothing (no console error, no request) while the same element clicked by script worked, on pages that had been up for a while and straight after a navigation. Not reproduced in jsdom, not diagnosed, not shown to be the app. The recorded run therefore drove the pages by script clicks after the first form interactions. Status: open - note only. | M0-60 puts a real Playwright suite on the pages and either reproduces or clears it. |
| OQ-821 | How does the first run get a BrokerProfile, so a never-probed connection does not answer 404 in the explorer? | A new connection has no cached profile until `probe.run` stores one, and `profile.get` and `inventory.query` answer 404 NOT_FOUND until then (`overview.get` does not need it: the management target resolver reads the unprobed profile). Chosen (UI only, agile mode): (1) the connection form probes once right after a successful `connections.create` (best effort: a failing probe never undoes the save, and the connection page still offers Check capabilities); (2) the explorer's `ProfileStore` knows the case (`needsProbe`: `profile.get` says NOT_FOUND, or sends a profile this version cannot read) and the inventory pages then read nothing and show a "has not been checked yet" panel with one button, "Check capabilities and load", that runs `probe.run` and goes on with the profile the probe answered; the overview's capability card offers the same button. Not chosen: `svc-explorer` probing on demand (it would need a probe port and a wiring in `compose-core.ts`, a service cannot import the capabilities service, and the first window would then wait for a probe). The explorer therefore now calls one action that is not a pure read, `probe.run` (`audit: outcome`, no broker change), and only on the person's press. Proved with fakes: `first-run.component.spec.ts`, `connection-form.component.spec.ts` (AC-15), `libs/testkit/src/explorer-ui/first-run.spec.ts` (real router, in-memory services); not seen against a real engine and broker. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the engine to probe on demand, or the profile built when a connection is saved. |
| OQ-822 | What does the lab seed helper create, and what does it not? | `pnpm nx run testkit:seed-lab -- [--queues=300 --vhosts=2 --exchanges=6 --bindings=4 --version=4.3.6 --no-build --existing=URL]` starts a lab broker (or seeds one named by `--existing`), imports vhosts `shop` and `billing` (the default vhost stays empty), the login's permission on them, exchanges of four types, queues in a 7:2:1 classic, quorum and stream mix and bindings (exchange to queue, exchange to exchange) by `POST /api/definitions`, prints the host, ports and login for the form, and removes the container on Ctrl+C. It creates no message, no connection and no consumer, so the Connections list stays empty until a client connects. The plan (counts, order of imports) is unit-tested without Docker (`seed-plan.spec.ts`); that a real broker accepts the definitions, and the Ctrl+C teardown on Windows, are not proved. Status: open - working default in use. | The first real run shows a definition the broker refuses, or the maintainer wants connections and messages seeded too. |
| OQ-823 | How are the edge states of a first session worded, and what do they not say? | Unreachable broker, refused login and "inventory not being read" keep the engine's Problem as sent and add one sentence of help by code (`BROKER_UNREACHABLE`, `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `FORBIDDEN_BROKER`, `TLS_UNTRUSTED`; `explorer.hint.*`). A broker 401 reaches the page as `UNAUTHENTICATED`, the same code as a refused launch token (OQ-768), so that hint names both causes. When the first sweep fails the inventory says only "not being read" (the sweep's Problem is not in `inventory.query`'s answer), so that line points at the overview, which asks the broker directly. An empty broker says "This broker has no queues" only for a live, complete answer with no filter; a list still loading never says it. The pages' views control stays visible while the probe is needed (it closes its buttons with "permissions not known"). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the sweep's failure carried in the inventory answer (a contract change) or other wording. |

## Spec questions: M1-41

Working defaults used by `docs/specs/M1-41.md` (agile mode: the approval stop was waived). Q-n is OQ-(823+n).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-824 | Is a small own chord parser acceptable instead of `tinykeys` 4.0.1 (M1-41 spec Q-1)? | The blueprint names `tinykeys` but it is not in the catalog, and a new package needs a maintainer-applied allow-list entry, a 3-day release-age check and CODEOWNERS approval. Default: about 120 lines of own code (`chord.ts`: parse, format, match per platform, desktop accelerator), no sequences (`Mod+K Mod+S`), no dependency. Option: the maintainer adds `tinykeys` and `chord.ts` becomes a thin wrapper. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the own parser or adds the dependency. |
| OQ-825 | Where does the registry live and what are its tags (spec Q-2)? | `libs/ui/commands`, project `ui-commands`, alias `@app/ui-commands`, tags `type:ui-lib` and `runtime:browser`, made with the `library` generator (the `ui-feature` generator makes a routed feature, which this is not). A feature may import a `type:ui-lib`, so features register their own commands without importing each other. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the name and layer. |
| OQ-826 | Does the palette search entities (spec Q-3)? | No. The blueprint says entity search uses the engine's `search`, but no UI consumer of `search.query` exists and navigation to an entity page needs M1-42. The palette ranks registered commands only (a local subsequence ranker with highlights); the issue's example `abau` -> `account.balance.update` is a command-id match. Entity results are a later slice that adds a second source to the same list. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-42 lands entity pages, or the maintainer asks for entity search first. |
| OQ-827 | May a dynamic command be bound to a key (spec Q-4)? | No. `switch to connection <cid>` is generated from the directory; a connection id may hold `_` and `~`, which the `keybindings` command-id schema rejects, and a binding to a connection the person later deletes would dangle. Dynamic commands are `bindable: false` and are not in the menu model. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants a "switch to connection 1..9" family with fixed ids. |
| OQ-828 | Where do the gates and `allowedActions` of the palette come from, and what is "open" (spec Q-5; extends OQ-795)? | From a `COMMAND_CAPABILITIES` token that `apps/web` fills from `StatusSource` (it already reads and parses `profile.get`; one read-only signal is added, no second call). The UI looks a name up in what the engine sent: an action command is closed unless the snapshot is `ready` and lists the action; a gate that is not `enabled === true` is closed with its reason and source as sent; navigation commands declare no requirement and are always open. The UI computes no authorisation and does not know the prod lock (OQ-795). Status: open - working default in use. | The engine sends a per-principal command list that is not tied to a connection profile. |
| OQ-829 | Which Transloco scopes do the palette and its commands use (spec Q-6)? | `ui-commands` owns the scope `palette` (its UI words, bundled JSON, `provideCommandsScope()`); `apps/web` owns `commands` (the titles of the app commands, outside `SCOPE_NAMES` because the shell key-coverage spec requires every key of those files to appear in the shell DOM); a feature's commands are keys of its own scope (`explorer.commands.*`) translated by the feature with an explicit scope definition. A command title is text at registration, not a key, so the palette needs no scope of any feature. Status: open - working default in use. | A second language lands (OQ-537), or the maintainer wants one commands scope. |
| OQ-830 | What does this WP do about the native menus (spec Q-7)? | The issue note moves native menus to M2-P14. Shipped: the typed `MenuModel`, `buildMenuModel(commands, platform)` and `syncNativeMenu(bridge, model)`, which calls `PlatformBridge.setMenu` only for a `desktop` bridge and routes `onMenuCommand` to the registry. `WebBridge.setMenu` stays a no-op. No Electron code, no OS accelerator registration. `PlatformBridge.setMenu(menu: unknown)` keeps `unknown`; typing it in the contracts is the desktop WP's change. Status: open - working default in use. | M2-P14 starts and decides the Electron mapping. |
| OQ-831 | What happens when two commands share a chord (spec Q-8)? | The chord is bound to nothing and the host announces the conflict and lists it in the cheat sheet; the shortcut never picks a winner silently. Conflicts are counted only among commands whose `when` is true at that moment, so two pages that reuse a key do not conflict. `assign` refuses a conflicting chord unless the person confirms the replacement, which unbinds the other command in the same saved document. A reserved or unparseable chord in the stored document is listed and not bound. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer prefers "last registered wins" or a priority order. |
| OQ-832 | Which chords are reserved and which are the defaults (spec Q-9, Q-11)? | Reserved: what a browser keeps (tabs, windows, private window, reload, print, save, find, address bar, history, developer tools, quit/hide) plus, by policy, the grid's cell chords (Ctrl+C, Shift+F10, ContextMenu). Defaults: `Mod+K` opens the palette, `/` focuses the filter of an inventory page (bare key, ignored while typing), only the palette and the filter have a default chord; every other command has none until the person assigns one (a browser has few chords left, and an unbound command is still reachable from the palette). `Mod+Shift+P` is not used because a browser takes it for a private window. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants more defaults, or a different filter chord. |
| OQ-833 | What small additions does this WP make outside its own library (spec Q-10)? | Additive only: `DataGrid.openColumnChooser()` and a `show()` on the chooser (`ui-grid`); `ViewsControl.openSave()`, `FilterBar.focus()` and a reload counter on the inventory page (`feature-explorer`); `StatusSource.profile` (`apps/web`); one registration call per page. No existing flow changes. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer asks for the commands to be wired elsewhere. |
| OQ-834 | Where does the keyboard dispatcher listen, and what does it ignore (spec R8)? | One `keydown` listener on the document in the bubble phase, after the widgets under it (AG Grid's cell handling, the palette's own field) have handled the key. It ignores `defaultPrevented` events, a key with no Ctrl, Meta or Alt (other than a function key or Escape) typed in an input, a textarea, a select or an editable element, and every chord while another `aria-modal` dialog is open; A key the grid handled is not handled twice because it is `defaultPrevented`. Auto-repeat never runs a command. Status: open - working default in use. | A browser run finds a case the rule misses. |
| OQ-835 | What is proved only against fakes in M1-41, and what must be run by hand? | Everything. The specs run the real registry, host, palette and cheat sheet in jsdom with a `FakeEngine` for `keybindings.*` and `profile.get`. Not run: a real browser (focus return, the real Ctrl/Cmd+K against browser chords, screen readers), a real engine that serves `keybindings.get` and `keybindings.put`, a bundle served under the real CSP. `pnpm nx e2e e2e-web --grep=@palette` does not exist (M0-60). Status: open - working default in use. | `apps/e2e-web` lands, or the maintainer's first manual test. |

## Spec questions: M1-42

Working defaults used by `docs/specs/M1-42.md` (agile mode: the approval stop was waived). Q-n is OQ-(849+n); the block starts at 850 to leave room for rows other work packages append at the same time.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-850 | What is the route schema of an entity page (M1-42 spec Q-1)? | The issue's interface is `/c/:cid/queues/:qkey/:tab`. Default: `/c/:cid/{queues\|exchanges\|connections\|bindings}/:key[/:tab]` beside, not under, `/c/:cid/explorer/<kind>` (the grids), one lazy mount per kind in the shell. `:key` is the object key (base64url of vhost, NUL, name), so `/` vhosts and unicode names survive proxies (ADR-003). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms or asks for the entity pages under `explorer/`. |
| OQ-851 | How does the UI read one object, when the engine has no `queues.get` (spec Q-2)? | The engine answers `explorer.window` for a list of keys (a snapshot of the rows that exist, then deltas) and `inventory.query` for windows. Default: an entity page follows exactly one key and reads a one-row window of the kind for the freshness; a binding is found by key over at most five 200-row windows of its vhost. A real `queues.get`, `exchanges.get` or `connections.get` (with the fields the inventory does not carry, OQ-860) would replace this. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens the engine follow-up, or accepts the default. |
| OQ-852 | When is "not found" certain (spec Q-3)? | Absence from the index only means the object is not on the broker when the last sweep was live and complete and dropped nothing. Default: certain exactly then (state `live`, `complete`, not `truncated`, `dropped` 0); in every other case the page says "not found in what has been read so far" with the freshness lines, and the connection switcher treats it as unknown. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-853 | Can a name in a grid be a real link (spec Q-4)? | `ui-grid` cells are text only (ADR-021, no markup, no cell renderer). Default: Enter, a double click and the right-click or Shift+F10 menu ("Open details", "Copy link") open the entity; names inside the entity pages are real `<a href>`. A link cell renderer in `ui-grid` (a component-free anchor, keyboard and middle-click aware) is a follow-up. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer opens the `ui-grid` follow-up or accepts activation. |
| OQ-854 | What does a workspace tab remember of an entity (spec Q-5)? | A tab stores `{url, label}` and the shell dedupes by url. Default: the tab's url is the entity's address without a sub-tab (Overview); choosing Routing or Config changes the address in the browser (deep link, history) but not the stored tab, so re-activating the tab opens Overview. Updating a stored tab's url needs a layout operation in the shell. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer asks for the sub-tab to be kept. |
| OQ-855 | Which tabs does a stream have (spec Q-6)? | EXP-06 lists Browse, Consumers and Config for a stream. Default: Overview and Routing are kept as well (the identity, state and bindings are the same questions for a stream), and Messages is replaced by Browse; no get-based action is reachable on a stream. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-856 | Are tabs whose content does not exist yet listed (spec Q-7)? | Messages, Browse, Publish, Tap, Charts, Forecast and Lint belong to later work packages. Default: they are listed in the tab set, disabled, with a reason: the engine's `reason` and `source` as sent when the profile's gate is closed, otherwise "arrives in a later version". This shows the framework and satisfies "a gated tab shows its why-disabled reason" without building any message read. Status: open - working default in use. | The work package that builds a tab flips its `built` flag in `entity-tabs.ts`. |
| OQ-857 | What does the connection switcher do when it cannot know whether the object exists on the other connection (spec Q-8)? | The check is `ADDRESS_EXISTS`, a boolean. Default: false only when the other connection's index is live and complete and does not hold the object; loading, partial, an error, a never-probed connection and a deadline (5 s) answer true, and the page there says what it knows (probe prompt, loading, not found so far). Saying "does not exist" for a connection that has not been read yet would be a false statement. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-858 | What is delivered of the OS deep link (spec Q-9)? | `PlatformBridge.onDeepLink` is an optional method with no implementation, and the OS scheme is M2 (critique: a coined scheme, never `app`). Default: this work package adds the navigation-only allow-list parser (`/c/:cid/explorer[/<kind>]` and entity addresses; no action, no unknown query, no `..`, no scheme) and a handler over the optional bridge method, proved with a fake bridge. Registering the scheme, focusing the window and `e2e-desktop --grep=@deeplink` are not done. Status: open - working default in use. | M2 desktop shell. |
| OQ-859 | What is a "binding group" (spec Q-10)? | The task names it next to queue, exchange and connection. Default: one binding has its own page (source, destination, routing key, arguments key, vhost, links to both ends), and every queue and exchange page lists its bindings in (and an exchange its bindings out) with links. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer says what a group should be. |
| OQ-860 | Which fields of the plan's detail tabs does the inventory not carry? | Queue and exchange `arguments` (x-message-ttl, x-max-length, dead-letter settings), the consumer list (tag, channel, prefetch, ack mode), the channel list of a connection, queue message ages and rates (per-queue rates never, OQ-661), the effective policy definition. The detail pages show the three counts, the consumers count, the policy name and the flags the rows carry, and say "not reported by the inventory" for the rest. Each needs a new read action (a `*.get`) in `svc-explorer` or a new service; none is made here (`libs/shared/contracts` and `svc-explorer` are out of scope). Status: open - follow-up issue to open. | The maintainer opens the engine issue. |
| OQ-861 | What of M1-42 is proved only against fakes? | Everything: the entity pages over the model's fake data port and a fake engine (`FakeEngine`, `MemoryTransport`); the shell mounts over the real router and in-memory services; the switcher's check over fake ports per connection; the OS link over a fake bridge. No browser run against a broker, no Docker, no real `explorer.window` subscription for one key (whether the real engine answers a one-key snapshot and a delta for it is what the first manual run shows), no desktop. `e2e-web --grep=@navigation` and `e2e-desktop --grep=@deeplink` do not exist. Status: open - recorded. | The first real run, M0-60 and the desktop shell. |
| OQ-862 | What permission do the metrics actions and the metrics topic need (M1-26 spec)? | Default: `broker.read`, the explorer's, for `metrics.query`, `metrics.forecast` and `metrics.live`. No catalogue entry `metrics.view` exists yet (blueprint 7.3 lists only `broker.read` for reads; the scale-first proposal names `metrics.view`). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer adds `metrics.view` to the RBAC catalogue, and the three contracts switch to it. |
| OQ-863 | Is the Prometheus body parsed in a worker thread above 1 MiB (blueprint 1.4, M1-26 scope)? | Default: no worker. The parser reads on the main loop and gives it a turn every 64 KiB and every 2,000 lines (a cooperative yield, `setImmediate`), so a 450 KB recorded page is about eight turns and a 400,000-series body (29 MiB) about 470 turns; `perf:metrics-100k` counts them and prints the loop delay (p99 about 2 ms on the development machine, with the Windows timer floor included). A worker needs a second entry in the engine bundle and a structured clone of every sample, which costs more than the parse for a body this size. Status: open - working default in use. | A team-scale run shows the I-5 loop delay above 20 ms under sweep, tap and scrape load; then the parser moves behind a worker with the same interface. |
| OQ-864 | Which endpoints get the connection's login when scraped (M1-26 spec, I-10)? | Default: only endpoints whose source is `provider` (Amazon MQ: 16001-16003 need the broker credentials); a `listener`, `default` or `user` endpoint is scraped with no `Authorization` header, because the management login is not the Prometheus plugin's and is not sent to a second port that did not ask for it. A provider endpoint that answers 401 is a failed scrape and counts toward the fallback. Status: open - working default in use. | A broker with an authenticated listener (a reverse proxy) needs a per-endpoint login setting on the connection. |
| OQ-865 | Which series does the metrics engine keep, and with what caps (M1-26 spec)? | Default: cluster and node series from the aggregated page (messages ready, unacked and total, connections, channels, consumers, queues, publish, deliver, ack and redeliver counters, resident memory and its limit, disk free and its limit) always; queue series (ready, unacked, total, consumers) only for pinned or demanded queues, at most 2,000 queue series per broker (20,000 is the team figure of blueprint section 8, not wired: there is no team mode here), the highest backlogs kept when more arrive; each series is a ring of at most 720 points and 16 KiB (1 h at 5 s), at most 64 brokers. Drops are counted and returned. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer sets other caps, or the persisted store (M4-04) replaces the hot window. |
| OQ-866 | When does the collector fall back from Prometheus to management sampling, and when does it come back (M1-26 spec)? | Default: no endpoint, or a blocked provider, is management from the start; a plan whose every target failed 3 scrapes in a row switches to management for 5 minutes, then Prometheus is tried once; a success returns to Prometheus. The management fallback reads `/api/overview` and `/api/nodes` only (and single queues only where `perObjectAllowed`), so Amazon MQ never sees a per-object read; rates from it are labelled approximate. Status: open - working default in use. | A run against a real broker with a stopped plugin tunes the counts and the cool-down. |
| OQ-867 | Does `PromScrapeRequest` carry the endpoint (M1-26 spec)? | Default: yes, an optional `endpoint` URL, because Amazon MQ lists three listeners under one node name. The resolver in `apps/engine` looks the URL up in the cached BrokerProfile's `prometheus.endpoints` and refuses one that is not there, so a request can never name an address the engine did not probe (no SSRF through the port). The field is additive; the scheduler passes it through. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer prefers a unique target id per endpoint in the profile. |
| OQ-868 | Which node's value wins when several nodes report one queue (quorum and stream replicas) (M1-26 spec, blueprint section 8, "deduplicated by leader")? | Default: the node whose `detailed_queue_info` sample says `membership="leader"` in the same scrape cycle, else the lowest node name in sort order; the choice is per cycle and per queue and the series carries no node label. A classic queue has one owner and is unaffected. Status: open - working default in use. | A cluster run shows replicas disagreeing for long enough to matter. |
| OQ-869 | What cadence does the planner choose (M1-26 spec, DC-33)? | Default: the scrape interval is the largest of the profile's `minIntervalSec` (60 s for Amazon MQ), 15 s, and 10 times the last scrape duration of the target, at most 5 minutes; the BrokerScheduler's own background spacing and AIMD back-off apply on top. A scrape that takes longer than the interval is not started again while it runs. Status: open - working default in use. | A perf run on a loaded broker shows 15 s is too eager. |
| OQ-870 | What does `metrics.live` carry and what does `metrics.query` accept (M1-26 spec)? | Default: `metrics.live` (`latest`, `snapshot-delta`) is keyed by series and carries the newest point of every cluster and node series of the connection plus the named queues (at most 50) and one `~status` item (source, partial, stale, fallback). `metrics.query` takes up to 50 series ids, a time range and `maxPoints` (at most 2,000), returns the points oldest first (thinned by last-in-bucket), and returns counters as rates when asked. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-54 (charts) needs other shapes. |
| OQ-871 | What are the forecast numbers (M1-26 spec, MON-05)? | Default: R2 at least 0.95 on the longest recent window of at least 8 points and 60 s (windows shrink by a quarter), a 95 % t band on the slope, no ETA beyond 30 days, `no-reliable-forecast` below the gate. The depth series of a queue is the hot ring (up to 1 h). "Consumers needed", memory and disk alarm times and anomaly scores are later MON work. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer changes a number, or lab data shows the gate too strict for bursty queues. |
| OQ-880 | Is the `scope: 'local'` boundary the contract field-name list, the arch test, or the executor? | A review found that the three checks are tripwires except one. The field-name list in `checkRegistry` (`BROKER_ADDRESS_FIELDS`) misses a broker address under another name; the architecture test (`checkLocalScopeModules`) reads one module's syntax and misses a computed key, `Reflect.get(ctx, 'writes')`, an alias and a helper in another file (it now also reads `ctx['writes']`). Added (agile mode, additive to OQ-783, which is not edited): the executor hands an action whose contract declares `scope: 'local'` a `writes` bundle in which every access throws an `INTERNAL` Problem (`libs/engine/kernel/src/actions/local-trap.ts`), and fails the call, audited as an error, even when the action caught that error. Actions without the declaration get the real ports. The list and the arch test are documented as tripwires. Not done: a type-level split of `ExecuteContext` into a local and a broker form, which would need the action contract to carry the scope in its type. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the split in the types, or the list and the arch test removed in favour of the executor's check. |
| OQ-881 | What does `--open` leave on disk, and what does the opener inherit? | The redirect file (`#t=<token>`) is removed after 30 s by an unref'd timer, and now also synchronously on the process `exit` event (`apps/engine/src/entry/open-browser.ts`), so an engine that ends inside the delay leaves nothing. A hard kill (SIGKILL, power loss) still leaves the directory until the system sweeps its temporary folder; the file is mode 0600 in a private directory. No extra drain hook was added: the engine's drain ends in a process exit. The opener starts with a copy of the environment without any `APP_*` name or name containing `TOKEN`, `SECRET`, `PASSWORD` or `KEK` as a word, and on Windows is `%SystemRoot%\System32\rundll32.exe` (`C:\Windows` when `SystemRoot` is unset). The word list is a working default. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants another name list, or the opener spawned with an empty environment plus an allow-list. |
| OQ-882 | Which URLs does the UI send the launch token to? | `isEngineUrl` (`libs/ui/platform/src/credentials.ts`) now parses the URL against the page origin and accepts only a same-origin path below `/api/`; a backslash or a control character is refused first (the browser reads `/\host` as `//host`). Static requests such as `/assets/i18n/*.json` no longer carry the Bearer header; the engine does not need it there (the static UI is served before a token exists). Status: open - working default in use. | A route outside `/api/` must carry the token, or the prefix must be configurable. |
| OQ-883 | What happens when a management list repeats a key (review of svc-explorer, finding B1)? | The management paging is not a snapshot: a queue created or deleted between two pages of one sweep moves rows across the page edge, so one key can arrive on two pages while another is missed. Before, the index held both rows as a complete, live version (a duplicate row id for the grid, `total` and `matched` off by one). Default: the install keeps the FIRST row of a key and counts the repeats (`IndexVersion.duplicates`, `stats.duplicates`, `SweepOutcome.duplicates`); a kind that saw a repeat is never `complete`, so the unchanged `freshness` already says `stale` with the reason "Part of the inventory could not be read." until a later sweep reads it clean, and the sweep ends `partial`. No contract field was added (the repeat count is not in the answer; a UI that wants the number needs a contract change). The sweep outcome is not retried within the same sweep. The index dedupes after the sweeper has counted rows against the row cap, so a repeat can take a slot of the staging cap (it is then not counted as dropped, only as a repeat); a paged envelope of the bindings (LavinMQ) is not deduped here, and neither is the connection list across vhosts. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the count in the answer (a contract change), a retry of the page that repeated, or a snapshot-consistent listing (an adapter question, OQ-433 family). |
| OQ-884 | What does `maxBytesPerConnection` bound, and what is counted beyond it (review of svc-explorer, finding N1)? | It bounds the estimate of the rows of the CURRENT version (plus the folded names a search built), checked at install: `stats.bytes`. The previous version kept for pinned windows, the by-key lookups (an estimated 48 bytes a row once built) and the sorted copies (8 bytes a row each) were not counted at all and the sort caches were never purged. Now an install retires every sorted copy of a version that is neither current nor previous, and `stats.retainedBytes` and `stats.sortCacheEntries` report what is held beyond the cap, as estimates. They are counted, not capped: a connection can hold about twice its row estimate plus up to `sortCacheEntriesPerKind` (8) copies per kind. Fixed constants, not measured heap. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the retained bytes inside the cap (the cap then covers both versions, halving the rows a connection can hold) or measured heap instead of estimates. |
| OQ-885 | How is the engine's own work over a long vhost kept off the event loop (review of svc-explorer, finding N2)? | The bindings of a vhost (up to 500,000 per connection) were converted in one loop and sorted with one synchronous sort (about 500 ms for 200,000 bindings). Default: both run in chunks of `ExplorerLimits.yieldEveryItems` (2,000) with a turn of the event loop between them (`internal/cooperative.ts`: runs sorted whole, then a stable pairwise merge, the same order a plain sort gives); the sorted copy is cached as a promise so a second window waits for the first sort. The specs count turns and comparisons, never wall-clock. NOT done: the sort of the swept kinds in `index.ts` (queues, exchanges, connections, up to 250,000 rows) is still one synchronous sort per version and order, as Q-13 of M0-48 accepted; `window` there is synchronous and used by `search`, `overview` and the topic source, so making it cooperative changes their signatures. Status: open - working default in use. | S-13 shows the queue sort exceeds the loop budget at 100,000 rows, and the maintainer opens the issue that makes the swept sort cooperative (or moves it to a worker). |
| OQ-886 | Should the install diff of the index touch only the watched keys (review of svc-explorer, finding N3)? | The install builds a map of the old rows and compares every row of the kind (`sameRow`) when at least one listener exists, once per install, whatever the subscribers watch. Diffing only the watched keys would need the index to know the union of the subscriptions' keys and a second way to tell that membership changed (the `~version` marker needs to know that a key was added or removed anywhere, which is a whole-kind comparison of key sets). That is a redesign of the listener contract, not a local fix, so it is left. The cost is O(rows of the kind) per install, which the sweep interval (30 s or more) and `perf:explorer-20k` bound today. Status: open - working default in use. | A measurement shows the diff matters at 100,000 rows, and the maintainer asks for per-subscription key sets or a membership counter in the index. |
| OQ-887 | Does `overview.get` start a sweep for a connection nobody probed, and what is a 200 alarm check that is not ok (review of svc-explorer, findings N8 and N5)? | `overview.get` calls `sweeper.touch` without reading the cached profile, unlike `inventory.query`, so opening the overview of an unprobed connection starts the inventory sweep. Kept: the overview needs no profile (OQ-821: the management target resolver reads the unprobed profile), and the sweep is what gives it the message totals when statistics are off; the sweep is the same background work the explorer would start a click later, bounded by the scheduler. A 200 answer of `/api/health/checks/alarms` whose `status` is not `ok` is `unknown`, never `none` (AC-14 fail closed): it says something is wrong without naming a node or a resource, and the case is now tested (it was not, which let a mutant that accepts any status survive). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the overview not to start a sweep before the first probe, or a non-ok alarm check to map to an active alarm of kind `other`. |
| OQ-888 | How are test titles tied to a work package so that "one AC, one named test" can be checked (review of svc-explorer, finding N6)? | AC ids repeat across work packages and files. Titles that start with an AC id now start with the owner work package (`M0-48 AC-3 ...`, `M0-48l AC-8 ...`, `M0-53 AC-5 ...`, `M0-54 AC-13 ...`); `--testNamePattern=AC-22` still selects by substring. Generated template tests of an action or a topic are not acceptance criteria and carry `KIT-n` (overview.get) and `KIT-Tn` (explorer.window) ids. A criterion proved by several tests gets a letter (`AC-3b`); `M0-54 AC-29` stays one id over several tests as its spec says, and the 16 unit tests of the browser harness are `M0-54 harness (AC-27)`. Not done: the entity-detail specs of `explorer-model` (M1-42) and the tests other changes added to `ui-grid` and `feature-explorer` while this was reviewed, the generator templates in `tools/generators` that still emit the old template titles, and the sentence in AGENTS.md section 5 ("test names quote the AC id") that should say the work package id comes first. A mechanical check (a lint or arch test that finds the same `WP AC-n` prefix in two tests) is not written. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer approves the convention, and a work package updates the generators, AGENTS.md section 5 and the M1-42 specs and adds the check. |

## M1-41 as landed (issue #126, docs/specs/M1-41.md)

Working defaults taken while building the three slices; agile mode, none needs an answer before the first manual test.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-836 | Where is the command host mounted, and why not in `App`'s template? | `provideCommands()` (in `appConfig`) runs an environment initializer that registers the app's commands and creates `CommandHost` next to the root with `createComponent` and `ApplicationRef.attachView`. `App` and the three specs that mount it (`app.spec`, `boot.spec`, `chunk-reload.spec`) therefore need nothing of the command system, and the host exists on every route, including `/connections`, which is outside the shell. The cost is that the host is not in the router's component tree; it needs only the root injector. `provide-commands.spec.ts` runs this mounting on a TestBed injector. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer prefers `<app-command-host />` in `App`'s template (the three specs then provide the engine data layer). |
| OQ-837 | Why do the explorer's command ids start with `inventory.` and `overview.`, and its context keys read `page` and `page.kind`? | `tools/web-gallery-check/explorer-i18n-source.test.mjs` reads every string literal that starts with `explorer.` in the feature's source as a translation key, so a command id or a context key spelled `explorer.reload` fails it. The ids are `inventory.focus-filter`, `inventory.reload`, `inventory.save-view`, `inventory.columns`, `inventory.copy` and `overview.refresh`; the keys are `page` (`inventory` or `overview`) and `page.kind`. A person's saved keybinding overrides are keyed by these ids, so renaming them later orphans the overrides. Status: open - working default in use. | The ids are settled before a release that people bind keys in. |
| OQ-838 | What do two inventory pages alive at once do (a split editor)? | The registry keeps one definition per id (the latest registration wins and the one below returns when it is disposed), and `CommandContext.provide` stacks the providers of one key. With two grids side by side the commands act on the page that registered last, not on the page with focus; there is no notion of an active page yet. Status: open - working default in use. | The shell tracks the focused editor group and the registry takes an active-page context. |
| OQ-839 | What does the `switch to <connection>` command do when the open page does not exist on the other connection? | It uses the connection switcher's own `resolveSwitchTarget` (so the same carry-over and the same `ADDRESS_EXISTS` question), opens the other connection's explorer root, and says so in the polite live region (`Announcer`, key `text`, from the `commands` scope: "<name> does not have <address>. The explorer of <name> is open instead."). The switcher's own dismissible notice is a component and is not shown for this path. Status: open - working default in use. | The notice moves to a service that both the switcher and the command use. |
| OQ-840 | What does the palette do on a connection nobody has probed? | `StatusSource.profile` is `unknown` when `profile.get` answers 404, so every command that names an action (Save the view) is closed with "The capabilities of this connection are not known." until the probe has run; navigation commands stay open. The explorer's own first-run prompt is the place that offers the probe. Status: open - working default in use. | A command to probe the connection (`probe.run`) is added to the palette. |
| OQ-841 | What happens at every start when the engine does not serve `keybindings.get`? | The host loads the keymap once at start. A failure keeps the defaults, sets `loadStatus: 'failed'` with the Problem as received, says so in the live region ("Your keyboard shortcuts could not be loaded. The defaults are in use.") and shows it, with a Try again button, in the shortcuts dialog. It is not retried by itself. Whether a given engine build serves it is the engine wiring's question (OQ-441, OQ-472, OQ-669), and was not checked against a real engine in M1-41. Status: open - working default in use. | The engine serves `keybindings.*`, or the maintainer wants the failure silent when the engine is down. |
| OQ-842 | What are the palette's small numbers? | At most 50 rows (`PALETTE_LIMIT`), the result count is announced 300 ms after the last key, Page Down and Page Up move five options (Home and End keep moving the caret in the text field), the keymap is saved 400 ms after the last change, a bare-key shortcut is ignored in text fields, and the palette matches the title first and the command id second with a small bonus for the title. The ranking is a subsequence match scored on word starts and consecutive characters; the issue's `abau` example (an id-initials match) ranks `account.balance.update` first with its highlights in the id. Status: open - working default in use. | A browser run shows a number that feels wrong. |

## M1-26 as landed (issue #111, docs/specs/M1-26.md)

What of the metrics engine is proved, and what is not built. The working defaults taken while building are rows OQ-862 to OQ-871 above; agile mode, none needs an answer before the first test.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-889 | What of M1-26 (the metrics engine) is proved only against fakes, and what is not built? | Proved against real code but not a live broker: the parser and the catalogue against the recorded pages of five RabbitMQ versions and LavinMQ; the scrape client against a loopback server through the Dialer; the whole chain (client, Dialer, BrokerScheduler, parser, collector, router) against the recorded pages served over loopback; the composed engine against a loopback Prometheus listener and management API. Proved only against fakes and manual time: the cadence, the fallback and its cool-down, the stale rule, the live topic's following of rounds. Not proved at all: `queue=` on a broker that supports it (the loopback server ignores the filter), a 3-node cluster (the lowest-node and leader rules), Amazon MQ with its credentials on ports 16001-16003, an LB-fronted cluster, a real 100,000-queue page (`perf:metrics-100k` is synthetic), and the I-5 loop delay under sweep, tap and scrape load together. Not built: the persisted store and rollups (M4-04), alerts (M4-05), charts (M1-54), consumers-needed and alarm-time forecasts, an automatic top-K of queues. Samples of queues nobody asked for are skipped by the parser (`skipped`), not dropped: `dropped.samples` counts only what was wanted and lost (a truncated scrape, a full round, a value that is not finite). Status: open - working default in use. | A lab run and a provider account record the missing pages and the cluster behaviour; the maintainer decides where `perf:metrics-100k` runs in CI (OQ-544, OQ-671). |
| OQ-890 | Does the store keep queue series beyond the ones somebody demanded (a top-K by backlog or growth, blueprint section 8)? | Default: no. The collector admits only the queues a view, a pin or a forecast named (at most 50), because reading every queue of a large broker is the per-object cost DC-33 forbids on some providers and costs a 100,000-queue page per round elsewhere. The store itself implements the rule (queue series capped per broker, pinned queues first, then the highest backlog), proved by `store-hot.spec.ts` and the 400,000-series run, so an automatic top-K is a change in what the collector admits, not in the store. Status: open - working default in use. | A consumer (fleet overview, alerts) needs the busiest queues without naming them. |
| OQ-900 | Why does the shell import `@app/explorer-model/entity` and not `@app/explorer-model` (M1-42 slice E)? | The barrel pulled the window source, the entity watch and the view definition into the main bundle (about 20 kB raw, 7 kB gzip). `libs/shared/explorer-model/src/entity.ts` re-exports only the address helpers and `EXPLORER_KINDS`, with an alias in `tsconfig.base.json` as `/testing` has. Measured: initial 228,966 of 350,000 bytes gzip (192.3 to 195.4 kB by Angular's estimate; before the shell took the entity addresses 192.3). No budget exception is needed. A specification import of `@app/explorer-model/testing` in the web specs must stay static (a dynamic import makes the project "lazy" for the boundaries rule). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the extra alias. |
| OQ-901 | When does a deep link get a workspace tab (M1-42)? | `shell/tabs/entity-tab-keeper.ts`, once the layout of the connection is loaded: an entity address with no tab for that entity (any sub-tab counts) gets one stored with the address as visited (so a reload at `/routing` restores it) and the entity's name as label; nothing is navigated. A refused tab (address over 512 characters, too many tabs) is shown by the shell's own notice and the page still opens. Closing the tab of the page being shown leaves the page there and does not add the tab again until the next navigation. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms. |
| OQ-902 | What did M1-42 not do that the plan names? | The Messages, Browse, Publish, Tap, Charts, Forecast and Lint tabs have no content (listed disabled with a reason); `e2e-web --grep=@navigation` and `e2e-desktop --grep=@deeplink` do not exist; the OS scheme, the desktop window focus and registration are M2; names inside a grid cell are not links (OQ-853); the engine has no `*.get` for arguments, consumers or channels (OQ-860); no browser run was made against a real broker or a real `explorer.window` subscription for one key (OQ-861). Status: open - recorded. | The follow-up issues are opened. |
## Spec questions: M1-54

Working defaults taken while writing `docs/specs/M1-54.md` (live charts and the forecast overlay, issue #139). Agile mode: none needs an answer before the first test.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-920 | Which chart renderer, given the issue names uPlot 1.6.32 and no charting package is in the catalog (M1-54)? | Default: SVG written locally in `libs/ui/charts` (a `type:ui-lib`), with the draw maths as pure functions (nice ticks, time ticks, M4 downsample to the pixel width, gap-breaking paths) and one component per chart kind (line and area, sparkline, data table). A new dependency is a protected-path change (the allow-list, the release-age gate, CODEOWNERS), and the maintainer's instruction for this work package forbids one. The confinement table already reserves `libs/ui/charts` for `uplot`, `echarts` and `ngx-echarts`, so a library can replace the renderer later without touching the pages. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer approves uPlot (or another library) through the dependency policy, and the renderer is swapped behind the same inputs. |
| OQ-921 | Is there a `libs/ui/feature-metrics` (the issue's scope names it)? | Default: no. The Charts and Forecast tabs live on the entity page and the Trends section on the overview, both owned by `feature-explorer`, and a feature may not import another feature (issue #281 is the precedent for what duplication costs). The shared parts are `libs/ui/charts` (presentational, no engine) and `libs/shared/metrics-model` (pure: ring, session, explanations, forecast view); the panels and the engine-backed data port are in `feature-explorer`. Status: open - working default in use. | A standalone metrics page or a fleet view needs the panels outside the explorer; then they move to a `type:ui-lib` that may use `ui-data`. |
| OQ-922 | Do counters stay raw in the client ring, and who derives rates? | Default: raw. `metrics.live` carries the raw counter value, so the session asks history with `rate: false`, keeps raw values and derives rates with the reset rule of `counterRate` (a reset interval is skipped, never a negative rate). History and live points then join without a seam. Status: open - working default in use. | The live topic carries rates for counters (an engine change), or a server-side rate window is wanted for long ranges. |
| OQ-923 | What are the client ring's numbers, and what ranges are offered (M1-54, I-6)? | Default: one ring per series, at most 1,000 points and 16,000 bytes (two `float64` per point), at most 50 rings (the engine's query limit), drops counted (`evicted`, `stale`) and shown. The range selector (5 min, 15 min default, 30 min, 1 h) filters the ring and asks nothing; the hot window holds one hour. Longer ranges are listed disabled with "arrives with the persisted store" (M4-04). Status: open - working default in use. | M4-04 lands, or the maintainer sets other numbers. |
| OQ-924 | Which charts does the cluster page show and which does the queue page show? | Default: cluster: backlog (ready, unacknowledged, total), message rates (published, delivered, acknowledged, redelivered per second), consumers (consumers, channels, connections), memory per node (used and its limit) and disk free per node (free and its limit); queue: depth (ready, unacknowledged, total, with the forecast overlay) and consumers. Memory and disk charts need the node names, which come from the overview answer; no nodes, no chart, and the section says so. Per-queue publish and deliver rates are not in the metrics catalogue and are not charted. Status: open - working default in use. | The catalogue gains per-queue rates, or the maintainer wants other groups. |
| OQ-925 | Do streams get Charts and Forecast tabs (M1-54, EXP-06)? | Default: no. The stream tab set of M1-42 is unchanged (Overview, Browse, Consumers, Routing, Config). The depth series of the catalogue are queue metrics, and a stream's backlog is not its message count; showing a chart that means something else would mislead. The queue tab set flips `charts` and `forecast` to built. Status: open - working default in use. | A stream-specific series (offset lag, committed offset) is added to the catalogue. |
| OQ-926 | How often does the forecast refresh, and where is it computed (M1-54, MON-05)? | Default: by the engine only (`metrics.forecast`); the UI never computes an ETA. The Forecast tab reads when it opens, on Refresh, and every 30 s while open and not paused (never two reads at once); a failure keeps the last answer marked as earlier. The overlay on the depth chart and the Forecast tab share the answer of the page. Status: open - working default in use. | A cheaper push of the forecast through the live topic is wanted, or 30 s is too slow or too eager. |
| OQ-927 | How is the chart palette made colour-blind-safe (M1-54, `ui-design` tokens)? | Default: six `--ds-color-chart-1..6` tokens in every theme (plain hex, at least 3:1 on every surface and 7:1 in high contrast, pairwise distinguishable under protan, deutan and tritan simulation in `tokens.spec.ts`), and colour is never the only signal: each series also has its own dash pattern and marker shape. The tokens are mapped in the Tailwind preset like every colour token. `libs/ui/design` carries `trust:critical`: the change is for review, not a protected path. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer chooses another palette; M1-57 (themes pushed to every rendering library) revisits it. |
| OQ-928 | How are the live-region announcements kept from flooding a screen reader? | Default: the readout line (time and values at the cursor) is a polite live region updated by keyboard moves only; a pointer hover shows the same text silently. A summary line (newest values and state) is a polite live region updated at most once per 15 s of data time and on state changes. No animation or transition exists in the chart CSS, so `prefers-reduced-motion` is honoured by construction, and Pause freezes the view. Status: open - working default in use. | A screen-reader session finds the cadence wrong; the number moves. |
| OQ-929 | What is the Trends section of the overview, and does it change existing flows (M1-54)? | Default: an additive section at the end of the overview page, loaded as its own lazy chunk after the overview (`@defer`), for every connection (a connection with no statistics shows its explanations: that is the point). It adds the three read contracts to the overview's request record; the existing allow-lists of `explorer-requests.spec.ts` name them. Nothing the overview already shows or reads changes. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants the section collapsed by default or behind a setting. |
| OQ-930 | What does M1-54 prove only against fakes, and what is not done (M1-54)? | Proved: the draw maths as pure functions with property tests, the ring, the session, the explanations and the forecast view in node, the components and the pages over the real `EngineApi` and `LiveClient` on a fake engine serving the real live protocol, the token contrast and colour-vision checks, the source scans and the production budgets. Not proved: the pixel result in a real browser (jsdom has no layout), the renderer CPU of 50 charts at 1 Hz (the issue's first criterion; `perf:charts-50` counts the draw work only), a real collector round and real cadence, screen reader speech, appearance on a real display, and `e2e-web --grep=@charts` (no e2e project, M0-60). Status: open - working default in use. | A browser profile of 50 charts and a hand test on a lab broker with statistics on and off. |

## M0-53 perf harness as landed (issue #49, docs/specs/M0-53.md)

Why `perf:grid-100k` (AC-1, AC-4, AC-16) asserts what it asserts, and what it leaves open. Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-940 | What is "flat memory" in AC-4: at most 1.15 times the heap after the first five windows, or something else? | The spec proposed 1.15 times the JS heap (after a forced collection) at the end of 300 jumps over the heap after the first five windows (Q-4c). Measured in a real Chromium on the reference machine (Windows 11, i9-14900HX, Chrome 155, headless): 5.33 MB after five windows, 6.82 MB at the middle of the run, 6.93 MB at the end, so 1.30 times the five-window heap and 1.02 times the mid-run heap, repeatably (1.296 to 1.303 over five runs). The 1.30 is the cache filling, not a leak: after five windows the cache holds a third of its 25 allowed blocks (about 330 bytes a row, 5,000 rows). Default: the test asserts the end heap at most 1.15 times the **mid-run heap**, taken where the cache holds a comparable number of blocks (at least 15 at both points, asserted), plus the 25-block bound as a count; the literal ratio is printed on every run and not asserted. A leak is caught (a run that keeps every answer measures 1.27 and fails). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer confirms the mid-run comparison, or names a number for the literal ratio (for example 1.5 against five windows) and the test asserts that too. |
| OQ-941 | Is a page-side frame and long-task record enough for AC-1, which says "the CDP trace shows"? | The spec asked for a CDP trace. The harness records one `requestAnimationFrame` timestamp per frame and the Long Tasks API entries in the page (installed before the page's first script), and sends the scroll as the browser's own wheel gesture (`Input.synthesizeScrollGesture`, 40,000 px/s for 6 s). Frames per second are the `n - 1` intervals over the window; a task above 50 ms is any long-task entry (the API reports nothing below 50 ms, so the longest shorter task is not known). A trace (`Tracing`, `RunTask`, `DrawFrame`) would add compositor frames and perturb the run; it was not built. Measured: 60.0 fps (32 px rows) and 59.7 fps (24 px rows), no task above 50 ms; a 60 ms task in every answer gives 38 to 40 fps and 37 long tasks, so the gate bites. Default: page-side records, both densities. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants compositor frames (a trace analysis), or accepts the page-side records. |
| OQ-942 | Why is the harness page a project of its own, and why does the PR gate run `perf:grid-100k`? | The spec put the page under `apps/perf/src/grid-100k/`. `perf` is `runtime:node` and `eslint.config.mjs` forbids it to import the browser-only `ui-grid` and `ui-design`, so the page is `apps/perf-grid-page` (`type:test`, `runtime:browser`) and the two sides share no import: the page's counts are checked by the driver at run time (the row count, the library's own block sizes). `perf:grid-100k` depends on `perf-grid-page:build` (about 6 s). The spec's "Out of scope" excluded a CI workflow; the step is added to the `affected` job of `ci.yml` (three hosted OSes, `PERF_REQUIRE_BROWSER`), as for `perf:explorer-ui-20k-browser`, because an acceptance criterion with no gate is the defect this change fixes. `perf:smoke` and `perf:nightly` placement (Q-4) is untouched. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer prefers the page inside `perf` (with a `runtime` tag change) or a nightly placement. |
| OQ-943 | Does the explorer's browser harness (`explorer-ui-20k.browser.perf.ts`, M0-54 slice K) count the CSP reports of the page's boot? | Found while writing `grid-100k`: `ExplorerPage.detectorsAreAlive()` clears `violations` (Node side) and `clearReports()` clears the page's own list **after** the page has booted, so a report made while booting is dropped before the final count, although the README says "over the whole life of the page, boot included". The grid harness keeps what was reported before its self-check and removes only what the self-check caused. Not changed here (out of scope of M0-53). Status: open - recorded. | A follow-up issue fixes `detectorsAreAlive` in slice K the same way. |
| OQ-944 | What does `perf:grid-100k` not prove? | A real engine (the window is a function in the page: no network, no JSON, 15 ms an answer), a GPU or a display (headless, software compositing), a user's hardware, a viewport other than 1280x720, high contrast, the real `explorer.window` live feed under the grid, the nonce from the real shell (OQ-347; the harness server uses the engine's placeholder and policy), a selection surviving block eviction in the browser (AC-15 is a jsdom spec). AC-16 runs under the engine's policy as it is and under it without `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` (both pass); the spec's wording lists only `style-src 'self' 'nonce-N'`, which is the second. Status: open - recorded. | A lab run with the real engine and a person's hardware records the figures; the maintainer decides whether the stricter policy becomes the binding one (OQ-231). |
| OQ-931 | Why are the pure draw maths their own library, `libs/shared/chart-math`, and not part of `ui-charts` (M1-54)? | `apps/perf` is `runtime:node` and may not import a browser library, and the draw work of 50 charts is exactly what `perf:charts-50` counts. The maths (ticks, scales, M4 downsample, paths, picking, overlay geometry, formatting) have no DOM, so they are `type:pure` and `runtime:isomorphic`; `ui-charts` keeps the components and imports them. Status: open - working default in use. | A second renderer (a charting library, OQ-920) wants the same maths, or the maintainer prefers them back in `ui-charts` with a browser perf run. |
| OQ-932 | What did M1-54 land, and what is not proved (M1-54)? | Landed: the Charts and Forecast tabs of a queue, the overview Trends, the session and its bounded rings, every gap and forecast state in words, the chart palette tokens, `perf:charts-50`. Not proved: the issue's first criterion (renderer CPU of 50 charts under 20 %, needs a browser profile), `e2e-web --grep=@charts` (no e2e project, M0-60), a real collector round, pixels, screen-reader speech, colour on a real display. The issue is left open for those. Status: open - working default in use. | A browser profile and a hand test on a lab broker with statistics on and off. |
| OQ-960 | Where do the k.3 tables live and what shape do they have (M1-01)? | A new `effectiveConfig` table in `RegistryData` (`merges`, `resolutions`, `warnings`) in `libs/shared/semantics`, rows in `src/data/effective-config.data.ts`, looked up through `registry.effectiveConfig`; the resolver is the pure library `libs/shared/effective-config` and takes the lookup as an argument (default: the shipped registry). Like the other M0-13 tables the rows carry citations and CF ids but no `status` or `evidence` field (OQ-361), so nobody can mistake them for lab-verified; no cell is added to `cells`. | A maintainer wants the rows as `SemanticsCell`s (a contracts change to the cell key, which is built for settle actions) or the resolver inside `semantics`. |
| OQ-961 | How far do the rows reach beyond the broker source that was read (M1-01)? | The k.3 table was read at v4.3.6; the acceptance examples add 4.0.8 (min) and 3.13.7 (`expires`, policy wins). Rows for classic, quorum and stream cover 3.13 to 4.3 with `basis: 'source-read'` only where a cited source was read at a tag in that range, and `basis: 'extrapolated'` otherwise; every answer carries the basis, so the UI can say "inferred from 4.3.6". 4.4 has no row. | A lab recording for the era (OQ-968) turns a row into `source-read` plus evidence; a source read of 3.13 or 4.0-4.2 does the same without the lab. |
| OQ-962 | What does the resolver say for `delayed-retry-min` and `delayed-retry-max` (M1-01)? | The k.3 table (fact sheet) groups both under `min(policy, argument)`; fact sheet b.5 says `max()` applies to min and `min()` to max, and that policy keys exist from 4.3.1. The sources disagree, so both rows are `undetermined` on 4.3.1+ (status `unknown`, warning `W-EC-UNDETERMINED`, both readings in the text) and `unsupported` below 4.3.0; `delayed-retry-type` is policy-wins (both sources agree) and the operator value wins its merge (b.5). | CF-EFFCFG-07 recorded on 4.3.x, or a source read of `rabbit_quorum_queue.erl` delayed retry config at 4.3.6. |
| OQ-963 | Is broker 4.4 covered by the effective-config rows (M1-01)? | No: no source has been read for 4.4, so a 4.4 broker gets `unknown` (`W-EC-NO-ROW`), never the 4.3 answer. | The 4.4 source read in the new-broker-release runbook (OQ-622, OQ-644). |
| OQ-964 | What happens when both policies define a key that no merge rule names (M1-01)? | The policies guide says operator policies override user policies, but the code only lists some keys (min, delivery-limit, overflow, queue-version, booleans). For a key in both layers without a merge rule (for example `max-age`, `consumer-disconnected-timeout`) the merged value is `unknown` with `W-EC-NO-MERGE`; a key in one layer only passes through unchanged. | A source read of `rabbit_policy:merge_operator_definitions` for the default clause; if it is "operator wins", add the rule and drop the warning. |
| OQ-965 | What does the library answer when two policies tie on priority or a pattern cannot be evaluated like the broker (M1-01)? | The broker sorts matching policies by priority and takes the first; the broker API documentation says equal priorities mean a random choice (research sweep, rabbitmq-management-surface.md and rabbitmq-4x-semantics.md), so no order is assumed. The library resolves every candidate and keeps a key only where all candidates agree; the other keys are `unknown` with `W-EC-TIE`. The same rule covers a policy whose pattern is not evaluated (`W-EC-PATTERN`) and whose priority could change the winner. | A source read of the policy sort for ties (the broker may be deterministic by name). |
| OQ-966 | Which regular expressions are evaluated and which are treated as uncertain (M1-01)? | The broker uses Erlang `re` (PCRE, bytes); the library uses `RegExp`. It evaluates a conservative subset: ASCII names and patterns, length at most 255 for names and 512 for patterns, escapes only `\d \D \w \W \s \S \b \B` and escaped punctuation, groups `(`, `(?:`, lookarounds, no inline flags, no backreferences, and no quantified group that itself contains a quantifier (the classic catastrophic shape). Anything else is `uncertain` and never run. The subset is a heuristic, not a proof against every pathological pattern: the security review should read `hypothetical.ts` first. | A linear-time matcher (a dependency, protected path) or the review asking for a smaller subset. |
| OQ-967 | What is the broker default of `delivery-limit` in the effective-config view (M1-01)? | The semantics matrix says a missing quorum `delivery-limit` is 20 on 4.0+ and unlimited on 3.13. The row has a `brokerDefault` of 20 (4.0 to 4.3, `extrapolated` for 4.0-4.2) and `null` (unlimited) on 3.13; the entry status is `default`, never `effective`, and the warning says the `.delivery_limit` stat outranks the computed value (W-UPGRADED when they differ is the planner's job, not this library's). | A lab recording of CF-EFFCFG-03 on 3.13 and 4.0. |
| OQ-968 | Which lab recordings does a maintainer need to make the M1-01 rows lab-verified (M1-01)? | CF-EFFCFG-01 (classic and quorum min keys: `max-length`, `max-length-bytes`, `message-ttl`, `expires` with argument and policy on 4.0.9, 4.2.9, 4.3.6), CF-EFFCFG-02 (3.13.7 quorum `expires`: policy wins), CF-EFFCFG-03 (`delivery-limit` operator and user merge and argument, including -1, read back from the `delivery_limit` stat), CF-EFFCFG-04 (operator merge of `overflow`, `queue-version`, a boolean), CF-EFFCFG-05 (stream policy-wins keys), CF-EFFCFG-06 (classic `x-overflow` argument against an operator-policy `overflow`: source-read, untested), CF-EFFCFG-07 (quorum `delayed-retry-min/max`, 4.3.1+). The stubs exist under `apps/conformance/src/cells`; they record nothing. | Whenever the conformance recorder gains a policy-aware scenario DSL. |
| OQ-969 | Which protected change does the new library need (M1-01)? | `CODEOWNERS` should gain `/libs/shared/effective-config/ @RafaelJCamara` next to the semantics line (the project is `trust:critical`). It is a protected path: a maintainer applies it. | The maintainer adds it, or decides the library inherits the semantics owner. |
| OQ-970 | Does M1-01 need `oversize-approved` (M1-01)? | Yes. Two libraries, 33 data rows, seven lab stubs and the tests that pin them are about 5,700 changed lines (about 2,400 source and data, about 3,400 specs and property tests, formatted at 80 columns), against the 800-line guide; the four slices are far above their 600-line budgets for the same reason (the spec estimated 2,000). Splitting the issue would have separated the table from its only consumer and the tests from the rules they pin. | A maintainer adds the label or asks to split the WP. |
| OQ-971 | How does the caller pick the `version` for a mixed-version cluster (M1-01)? | The caller passes the lowest node version it knows (`lowestSemVer` of the `BrokerProfile` nodes); `null` (unreadable) gives `unknown` for every key, never a guess. The library does not read the profile, so it holds no version logic (I-3). | M1-20 decides to resolve per node and show the disagreement. |
| OQ-980 | Why does the capture store run in a worker thread with its own connection, and what is built but not started (M1-09)? | Blueprint 1.4 lists a capture-ingest/search worker: `node:sqlite` is synchronous, so a transaction blocks the thread it runs on. On SQLite the writes and searches therefore run in a worker thread that opens a second connection to the same file (WAL, `busy_timeout`); sealing stays on the main thread with the vault, so no key ever crosses into the worker and the worker sees only ciphertext and the masked text. Postgres uses the pool in-process (no worker).
