The worker is a second entry point of the engine bundle (`apps/engine/src/workers/capture.ts`, built to `dist/apps/engine/workers/capture.mjs`; the Nx esbuild executor refuses `outputFileName` next to `additionalEntryPoints`, so `main.mjs` is now named by its entry, which gives the same file) and `capture-host.ts` starts it; nothing in the engine start-up calls the host yet, because no action uses the store (`apps/engine/src/entry` is not touched). Status: open - working default in use. | The first action that saves a capture (M1-33 and later) wires `startCaptureWorker` into the composition root, or the I-5 gate shows the in-process path is enough. |
| OQ-981 | Is the masked text of a saved message plaintext at rest? | Yes. Blueprint 6.2 lists "masked text" and an FTS table, and an index over text needs the words. What is sealed (AES-GCM) are the body, the headers and the properties; what is plain is the routing facts, the size, the hash and a redacted projection of at most 8,192 characters, kept for the preview and the index. A `prod` or unset environment class never indexes the body (the projection holds metadata only). A person who wants no plaintext words keeps the capture `metadata only` (`indexBodies: false`). The database file itself is protected only by the operating system, as `data.db` is. Status: open - working default in use. | A security review decides whether the projection should also be sealed (search then needs a blind index) or the whole `capture.db` file be encrypted. |
| OQ-982 | Which redaction rules build the projection, and what is never indexed? | The kernel's redaction (`isSensitiveKey`, `redactValue`, `redactText`: credential keys at any depth, credentialed URIs, secret query parameters, JWTs, bearer values) is the only policy, plus the exact secret values the caller names (`secretValues`, for example the connection's own login) in every encoding the canary scanner knows. A string over 2,048 characters is never given to the kernel's expressions (they are quadratic on one long unbroken run, I-5); it is replaced by a marker. A body that is not valid UTF-8, or is over 64 KiB, or a JSON body nested deeper than 12 levels, is not indexed (the message is still stored and found by metadata); the row says `body_indexed = 0`. Header values are indexed only outside `prod`, names always. Status: open - working default in use. | A review of the rules, or a rule set per environment class. |
| OQ-983 | Is the blob address the plaintext hash? | Yes: a blob is addressed by the sha256 of its plaintext (blueprint 6.2 "sha256 content-addressed"), so the same large body saved twice is stored once. The cost: a person who can read the database can test whether a known large body is stored (a confirmation attack); `captured_messages.body_sha256` holds the same hash for every body. Mitigations: ownership is checked on every blob read (a blob is readable only through a message of the reader's capture), and the sealed chunks themselves are random-IV AES-GCM. Status: open - working default in use. | A keyed hash (HMAC under a purpose key) replaces it if the review wants no confirmation, at the price of no dedupe across key rotations. |
| OQ-984 | Why own blob tables, and what are the size numbers? | `blobs` (0001) is unsealed, unbounded and unowned. The capture store uses `capture_blobs` (ref, size, chunk count, refcount) and `capture_blob_chunks` (sealed 256 KiB chunks, AAD `capture_blob_chunks:sealed:<ref>.<n>`), so a range read opens only the chunks it needs and one seal never exceeds a few milliseconds (I-5). A body up to 64 KiB is sealed inline in its row; the blob cap is 16 MiB (the field cipher's limit, `MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES`); above it the row is `omitted` with its size and hash and a counter. Status: open - working default in use. | The retention WP (M4) merges the tables, or a larger cap is justified by a lab need. |
| OQ-985 | What are the retention and quota defaults, and who runs the purge? | The store takes `retentionMs` (default 7 days), `ownerQuotaBytes` (1 GiB, the desktop and local default of blueprint 6.3) and `captureMaxMessages` (100,000) as options; the team defaults (24 h per user, quota) are the composition root's to pass. `purgeExpired(limit)` is bounded and idempotent; nothing schedules it yet (no job in this work package: a `defineJob` module would be an action-layer decision). Quota counts the stored bytes (sealed inline bodies, sealed chunks, sealed metadata, masked text). Status: open - working default in use. | The first consumer registers the purge job; the team server's per-user quota table (blueprint 12) replaces `ownerQuotaBytes`. |
| OQ-986 | What is a volatile capture here? | An in-memory `BoundedRing` per capture (items and bytes), plaintext in memory only (blueprint 6.3: volatile captures are memory rings), never written before `save`. An evicted message counts as dropped on the capture (I-13). `save` copies what the ring holds through the ingest path into a new saved capture and reports what it could not save. The ring's size is the caller's option (default 5,000 messages and 32 MiB per capture, 64 volatile captures per store). Status: open - working default in use. | The tap WP (M1-15) decides whether a live tap writes straight to a saved capture. |
| OQ-987 | What is the key-rotation story, and how many seals can one key make? | Every sealed value names its data key in its authenticated header (`@app/secrets`), so rotating the vault's key changes only what new rows are sealed with; rows sealed under a retired key keep opening while the vault holds it. There is no `dek_id` column (it would go stale), and no re-seal job yet. IVs are 96 random bits under one key, so the NIST bound of about 2^32 seals per key applies; the 1 GiB quota and the 100,000-message limit keep a store orders of magnitude below it. Per-capture data keys (blueprint 12) are not built. Status: open - working default in use. | A re-seal job (retire a key, rewrite its rows) is specified, or per-capture keys are required. |
| OQ-988 | What happens when FTS5 is missing, and how do the two dialects differ in search? | The migration creates `captured_fts` (FTS5, contentless with `contentless_delete = 1`, tokenizer `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`) and, if the runtime refuses, skips it. `searchAvailable()` then says false, ingest and metadata queries work, and a text query fails with a `CAPABILITY_MISSING` Problem (the Problem codes are a closed set in the contracts, so no code of its own; never an empty answer). No dependency is added. Postgres uses a `tsvector` column (configuration `simple`) with a GIN index. The two tokenise differently (for example `order-123`); user text is turned into quoted terms joined by AND (a trailing `*` makes a prefix), so operators and quotes are plain text. Results are in id (time) order, not ranked, so a keyset cursor is stable. Status: open - working default in use. | A ranked result is wanted by the UI, or a platform without FTS5 is supported (the better-sqlite3 fallback of OQ-162). |
| OQ-989 | Which fields of a message are plain, sealed or searchable? | Plain: id, capture id, received time, exchange, routing key, source (queue or stream), content type, redelivered, body size, plaintext sha256, body state, blob ref. Sealed: the body, the properties and headers (one JSON document). Searchable (projection): exchange, routing key, source, content type, message id, correlation id, type and app id, header names, and outside `prod` the redacted scalar header values and the redacted body text. Routing keys and queue names can carry business ids; they stay searchable because the blueprint says the index holds metadata. Status: open - working default in use. | The review of OQ-981 or a per-field masking request. |
| OQ-990 | What is proved only on SQLite here? | The Postgres half of the capture contract (AC-4, 7, 8, 10, 15) and the `tsvector` path run in CI (`persistence:it --dialects=sqlite,pg`, Postgres 16 and 18); this machine has no Docker daemon, so only SQLite ran locally. The real worker thread is proved by `engine:smoke` on the built bundle and by `perf:capture-ingest`, both on SQLite; Postgres has no worker. The timing criteria (5 ms a transaction, 1 s a search, loop delay) are measured on the developer's or runner's hardware and are not a promise for others: `perf:capture-ingest` on a Windows laptop (32 threads, Node 24.19) read, for 50,000 messages of about 1 KiB, a transaction p50 1.3 ms, p95 4.0 ms, p99 11 ms and max 17 ms (the tail is the operating system's, not the batch), a loop delay p99 at the sampler's idle floor (15.6 ms timer tick on Windows), a search p95 under 10 ms, 4,400 messages a second. Status: open - recorded. | A Postgres run on the maintainer's machine, or the CI result is read. |
| OQ-991 | Is a deleted capture really gone from the file, and what about backups? | Retention and `deleteCapture` delete rows, chunks and index entries in one transaction; on SQLite the connection runs with `secure_delete = ON` for those deletes and the index is optimised, so freed pages are zeroed, and a test scans the file after a checkpoint for a unique token. The write-ahead log and the `.bak` copies that `migrate()` makes (OQ-165) are not rewritten: a backup taken earlier still holds deleted captures until it is removed. The blueprint's separate `capture.db` file is not used (captures live in the one database; the repository takes any handle, so a separate file is a wiring change). Status: open - working default in use. | The backup feature (blueprint 6.5) excludes captures or prunes copies, or `capture.db` is split out. |
| OQ-992 | Who may call the store, and what is an unset environment class? | The store has no authorisation: it is an internal API of `svc-messages` for later actions, which must go through the `ActionExecutor` (I-2) and name the owner from the principal. Every call carries `ownerId` and every query filters on it (a capture of another owner is "not found"). An unset or unknown environment class is `prod` (fail closed), so its bodies are not indexed. Status: open - working default in use. | The action layer lands; the captures actions declare their scope and permissions. |


## M1-04 death-history normaliser (issue #89, docs/specs/M1-04.md)

Working defaults taken while building the three slices; agile mode, none needs an answer before the first manual test.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1040 | Why does `normalizeDeaths` take a broker context and not a bare `era`? | The issue sketches `normalizeDeaths(msg, era)`. Whether a client-published `x-death` is interpreted is version-dependent broker behaviour, so it is registry data (I-3), not an `era === '3.13'` branch in a pure library. The registry answers `clientDeathHeaderInterpreted` (`<4.0.0`, a capability row, no new table) from a BrokerProfile, `deathContext()` turns the answer into `{ clientDeathInterpreted: boolean \| null }` (a gap or an unreadable node gives null and the verdict fails closed), and `normalizeDeaths(message, context)` stays free of versions. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer prefers the era signature (then `era` maps to the capability inside `@app/semantics`) or a dedicated registry table. |
| OQ-1041 | Is a WP of about 3,300 changed lines acceptable for M1-04 (size S, guide 800; about 1,500 of them tests and about 400 spec, README and OQ rows)? | The count includes 12 ACs, a table-driven malformed-input spec and fast-check properties. It was built as three slices that came out at 784, 963 and 1,524 lines, over the 600-line budget of the spec. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer adds `oversize-approved` or asks for a split into a follow-up WP (the properties, the canonical round trip). |
| OQ-1042 | Which lab recordings does the death-history normaliser still need? | No real `x-death` or `x-opt-deaths` message exists in `testing/fixtures` or the lab evidence. The shapes come from the DLX docs, release notes 4.0.1 and the broker source (`mc.erl`, `mc_amqpl.erl`, `mc_amqp.erl`; `docs/research/tap-streams-ops.md` T3). Proposed, not created: CF-DEATH-01 (0-9-1 consumer: `x-death` newest first, time in seconds, `x-first/last-death-*`), CF-DEATH-02 (1.0 consumer: `x-opt-deaths` with first-time and last-time in ms and `ttl`), CF-DEATH-03 (a client-published `x-death` on 3.13 is merged into the history, on 4.x it is delivered verbatim until the next dead-lettering), CF-DEATH-04 (a 1.0-published `x-opt-deaths` read by a 0-9-1 consumer). Status: open - nothing here is `lab-verified`. | `conformance:record` has run the four cells on the five lab versions and a maintainer added the evidence. |
| OQ-1043 | How is a copied `x-death` told from the broker's own on 4.x? | No wire marker is known: a republished copy of the broker's headers is byte-identical. The verdict is evidence-based: the caller's `clientPublished` hint, the product's replay stamps, a fixed list of framework republish markers, a missing `x-first-death-*`, or a mismatch of first/last with the entries give `client-supplied` or `inconsistent` (untrusted). A consistent, unmarked record is `broker-shaped` and trusted, and the reasons say that nothing proves it was not copied. Status: open - working default in use. | CF-DEATH-03 shows a marker (for example an annotation the broker adds), or the maintainer wants every 4.x record untrusted without a hint. |
| OQ-1044 | Which headers count as a republish marker? | Product stamps `x-redrive-count`, `x-redriven-from`, `x-redriven-at` (research: a redrive tool must write its own headers) and the Spring republisher's `x-original-exchange`, `x-original-routingKey`, `x-exception-message`, `x-exception-stacktrace` (framework research C6). Only the header names are read, never the values. Decoders (`/new-decoder`) will know more frameworks. Status: open - working default in use. | A decoder WP adds its framework's markers to the shared list. |
| OQ-1045 | Is a capability row in the trust-critical `semantics` data acceptable without an RFC issue? | `clientDeathHeaderInterpreted` (`<4.0.0`) is added to `capabilities.data.ts` with citations (release notes 4.0.1, `mc_amqpl.erl` at v3.13.7 and v4.0.0). No schema, cell, status or evidence changes; the capability evaluator already fails closed. Status: open - working default in use. | CODEOWNERS signs off, or asks for an RFC (GOVERNANCE.md). |
| OQ-1046 | Why is `time` of a 0-9-1 entry the first death while 1.0 has a last-time too? | The 0-9-1 `time` is the first death of the `{queue, reason}` pair, in seconds; 1.0 adds `last-time` in ms. The model keeps both: `firstTimeMs` and `lastTimeMs`. For 0-9-1 `lastTimeMs` is known only when `count` is 1 (then it equals the first); otherwise it is null with the entry flag `last-time-unavailable`. The array order of 0-9-1 is the broker's (most recent last-time first) and is kept, never re-sorted by `time`. Status: open - working default in use. | CF-DEATH-01/02 show a different order or unit. |
| OQ-1047 | Is the 90 % line coverage target of `type:pure` met by `deaths`, and how is it measured? | The workspace has no coverage provider (`@vitest/coverage-v8` is not in the catalog and a new package is a protected-path change), so the figure cannot be produced. Instead every AC has named tests, the malformed-input table covers each flag, and mutants of the ordering, the bounds (off by one), the trust rule and the count check were run by hand: all were killed except dropping the stable-sort tie-break, which is equivalent because `Array.prototype.sort` is stable. Status: open - working default in use. | A coverage provider is added to the catalog by a maintainer, or the target is dropped for pure libraries. |
| OQ-1048 | Is the broker order of a 0-9-1 `x-death` reliable before the `message_containers_deaths_v2` feature flag? | The flag "fixes the ordering of deaths and cycle detection" (stable in 3.13 to 4.2, required from 4.3.0). On a cluster where it is not enabled the order the broker sends may be wrong, and 0-9-1 carries no last-time to check it with. The normaliser keeps the order as sent and reports `orderBasis: broker-order`; it does not sort by `time`, which is the first death. Status: open - working default in use. | CF-DEATH-01 shows the order with the flag disabled on 3.13, or a consumer needs the order guaranteed. |

## Spec questions: M1-02 (issue #87, docs/specs/M1-02.md)

Routing simulator I. Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test. Ids start at OQ-1100 to stay clear of rows other work packages append at the same time.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1100 | Where does the simulator live and what is it called (M1-02)? | The issue and the design document say `libs/shared/routing` (`pnpm nx test routing`, `@app/routing`); the repository layout lists `routing` under `shared`; the first brief called it `routing-sim`. Default: project `routing`, `type:pure`, `runtime:isomorphic`, so the verification command in the issue works as written. | The maintainer wants the name `routing-sim`, which is a rename of one directory and one alias. |
| OQ-1101 | How are header values typed (M1-02)? | The broker compares header values with Erlang `=:=` on typed terms: an integer and a float are different (`1` and `1.0`), a timestamp is converted to milliseconds, a long and a short integer are the same number. A JSON-like `HeaderValue` (string, number, boolean, null for void, array, table) cannot say int from float or long from timestamp. Default: numbers compare by value (so `1` and `1.0` match, which the broker would not), strings by code units, tables and arrays deeply, `null` is void (presence). The input is documented that way. | Lab fixtures (M1-05) show a case where it matters; then the topology input gets an explicit typed header value (`{type, value}`) and the matcher compares type and value. |
| OQ-1102 | Why are there no registry cells for routing (M1-02)? | The cell schema keys on era, queue type, protocol and a settle action (`SettleActionSchema`) and has a settle effect; a routing rule is none of these. Reading `rabbit_exchange.erl`, `rabbit_exchange_type_{direct,fanout,topic,headers}.erl`, `rabbit_db_topic_exchange.erl` and `rabbit_basic.erl` at v3.13.7 and v4.3.6 shows the matching rules are the same across the verified range; the one difference is the topic `#` limit (a capability row, `topicBindingHashWildcardLimit`, from 4.3.5; the spec lists the evidence). The rules therefore live as code with table tests, citing the sources, until M1-05 records lab evidence. Default: no cell, one capability. | The maintainer wants a routing table in the registry (a new table kind with its own schema), or lab evidence arrives and a `routing` table is added with `CF-ROUTE-*` ids. |
| OQ-1103 | Is the 4.3.0 topic empty-key leak modelled (M1-02)? | Release notes 4.3.1 and the fact sheet: on 4.3.0 (and later until feature flag `topic_binding_projection_v5` is enabled) a topic binding with key `""` receives `""` publishes from any topic exchange. Whether the flag is on is a per-cluster fact the topology snapshot does not carry. Default: not modelled; the simulator answers as the fixed broker. A person on an affected cluster can see an extra delivery the simulator does not predict. | The flag state is available in the broker profile; then `simulate` takes it as an option and adds the leak with a note. |
| OQ-1104 | Who resolves the alternate exchange when an argument and a policy both set it (M1-02)? | The broker reads `rabbit_policy:get_arg(<<"alternate-exchange">>, <<"alternate-exchange">>, X)`. Default: `TopologyExchange.alternateExchange` is the **resolved** value, filled by the caller (the effective-config resolver of M1-01 owns the precedence). The simulator never reads policies. | M1-01 lands and an adapter from inventory rows to `RoutingTopology` states the precedence it uses. |
| OQ-1105 | Which plugin exchange types could be modelled instead of reported unknown (M1-02)? | Reported unknown with a hint: `x-consistent-hash` and `x-modulus-hash` (one destination chosen by hash, a weight share), `x-random` and `x-local-random` (one destination at random), `x-delayed-message` (held, then routed by `x-delayed-type`), `x-recent-history` (probably fanout plus a cache; the source was not read), `x-jms-topic` and any other. Default: indeterminate, with the AE listed. | Lab evidence and a decision per type; `x-recent-history` and `x-delayed-message` are the cheapest. |
| OQ-1106 | Are the default limits right (M1-02)? | Depth 64, 1,000 expanded exchanges, 200,000 binding evaluations, 10,000 queues, 8 paths per queue, 16 loops, 256 words per topic key and pattern. Chosen so a very large real topology (fanout of 5,000, chains of tens) is exact and a pathological one is cut and counted. The broker has no such limits; a cut result is a lower bound and says so. Not measured on a large vhost. | A perf run on a 100,000-binding vhost, or a report of a real topology that hits a limit. |
| OQ-1107 | Which lab fixtures does M1-05 need to prove the simulator equals the broker (M1-02)? | One topology per case, published with tracing on, `routed_queues` compared: direct (exact, missing, with CC and BCC); topic (`*`, `#` at the start, middle and end, empty words, the empty key, `a.`, literal `*`, more than one `#`); fanout; headers (all, any, all-with-x, any-with-x, absent, invalid value, void argument, valued argument, a message `x-` header); default exchange (existing and missing queue, CC); E2E chains (the message keys apply at every hop: the CC/BCC chain case of simulate.spec.ts), a diamond and a cycle; AE (empty exchange, E2E to a dead end, AE chain, AE on the default exchange); an internal exchange; mandatory unroutable. Not run in this work package (no Docker daemon in scope). | M1-05 creates the `CF-ROUTE-*` cells and records evidence on 3.13.7, 4.0.9, 4.1.8, 4.2.9 and 4.3.6. |
| OQ-1108 | CC and BCC for protocols other than AMQP 0-9-1 (M1-02)? | `CC` and `BCC` are 0-9-1 headers read by `rabbit_basic:header_routes/1` when the channel builds the message. The code was not read for AMQP 1.0, MQTT or STOMP publishers, which convert their own properties. Default: the simulator models an AMQP 0-9-1 publisher only and says so in the README. | A decision to simulate other publishers, with their own sources read. |
| OQ-1109 | What does M1-02 prove and what not (M1-02)? | Proved by tests: the matchers against tables and independent oracles, the walk against a reachability oracle on random cyclic graphs, the bounds and the outcomes. Not measured: statement coverage (no coverage provider in the catalog; adding one is a protected-path change); a 16-mutant hand sample was all killed. Not proved: equality with a real broker (OQ-1107), int/float typing (OQ-1101), plugin types (OQ-1105), the 4.3.0 leak (OQ-1103), a large topology (OQ-1106). The planned slice budgets (600 lines) were exceeded by a factor of two to three, because the proof is mostly tables and properties (about 55 % of the lines); the work was kept in commits of one concern each, every commit green, and `oversize-approved` was not asked for because the agile-mode instruction waives the stop. | M1-05 lands and the maintainer reviews. |
| OQ-1200 | Why is the tier-1 worker pool first-party over `node:worker_threads` and not `piscina` (M1-10)? | The issue and blueprint section 11 name `piscina 5.3.2`. It is not in the `pnpm-workspace.yaml` catalog, and a new dependency is human-only (allow-list, release-age check, CODEOWNERS). The pool needs little: bounded queue, bounded concurrency, a timeout with terminate, `resourceLimits`, abort, counted rejections. That is about 300 lines over `worker_threads`, and a terminate-and-replace policy that fits the "engine stays healthy" criterion better than a generic task pool whose queue is unbounded by default (I-6). Working default: `libs/engine/plugin-host` is first-party and generic (a worker module URL plus a `{id, input}` to `{id, ok, value}` convention). If a maintainer prefers `piscina`, only `pool.ts` changes; the pool contract (`run`, `stats`, `close`, `PoolOutcome`) and every spec stay. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer decides whether to adopt `piscina` (catalog line, allow-list entry with reason, ADR-027 log line if an exclusion is needed). |
| OQ-1201 | Are msgpack, cbor and xml written first-party, and where does `CodecPlugin` live until `plugin-sdk` exists (M1-10)? | The issue lists msgpack, cbor and xml. The only libraries for them are third-party parsers (`msgpackr` is already refused: `msgpackr-extract: false` in the build allow-list). Each format is small, and a first-party decoder can enforce the depth, node and byte limits itself, which a third-party parser would not (I-6). Working default: decode-only, bounded, first-party, proved against the MessagePack specification vectors and RFC 8949 appendix A; msgpack also has an encoder (for the round-trip property). XML is a tokenizer with a pretty view, not a DOM: no DOCTYPE, no custom entities, so no expansion attack exists. `CodecPlugin` is a stand-in in `libs/engine/decoders/src/codecs/types.ts` (like the `DecoderPlugin` stand-in, OQ-61) and moves to `plugin-sdk` with `apiVersion: 1` when that library lands. protobuf, avro, thrift and similar need a schema and a dependency: human-gated follow-up. Status: open - working default in use. | `plugin-sdk` lands (the stand-in moves), or a maintainer asks for third-party codecs. |
| OQ-1202 | How does the codec worker get into the engine bundle (M1-10)? | The pool starts a worker from a URL; the bundle is one esbuild file, so the worker needs a second esbuild entry (like the capture worker of M1-09), and `apps/engine` must create the pool in `compose-core`. Both are the composition root, which this work package does not touch (the entry and compose files are shared with other agents). Working default: `@app/decoders` exports `codecWorkerUrl` (the source file, for specs and `node` runs) and the README says what the bundle adds: an esbuild entry for `worker-entry.ts` emitted next to `main.mjs`, and `new URL('./codec-worker.mjs', import.meta.url)` in the composition. Until then the pool is proved on the source worker only; the `engine:smoke` bundle test is unchanged. Status: open - working default in use. | The wiring work package (messages service or the engine composition) adds the entry and a bundle smoke test that decodes one payload. |
| OQ-1203 | How does the 250 ms budget treat worker start-up (M1-10)? | Loading a worker takes tens of milliseconds, more on a cold Windows runner, and counting it would make the budget a measure of the machine. Working default: the budget starts when the job is posted to a worker that has said `ready` (the worker posts `ready` once its module is loaded); a worker that has not said `ready` within 5 s is killed, counted as `start-failed`, and the jobs it would have served are `worker-error`. A job that waits in the queue is not timed by the 250 ms budget (the queue is bounded by count, and each waiting job can be aborted by its signal). The pool is lazy: workers start with the first job and a worker killed for a timeout is replaced by the next job. Status: open - working default in use. | A real-engine perf run shows start-up to matter, or the product owner wants a total deadline. |
| OQ-1204 | Which limits does the decoder enforce, and can a caller loosen them (M1-10)? | Blueprint section 11, tier 1: 64 MB old generation, 250 ms, input at most 16 MiB, output at most 4 MiB of JSON. Added by this work package: decompressed bytes at most 16 MiB, ratio at most 1000 once the output passes 64 KiB, at most 3 encoding layers, depth at most 64, at most 200000 nodes, and the string and binary content of a msgpack, cbor or PHP document at most the view cap. Working default: `limits` can only be tightened by a caller (the effective value is the minimum of the caller's and the default), so a rule or a request cannot raise a cap. Every limit that fires is a counted truncation or a coded refusal in the result (I-6, I-13); none is silent. The numbers are working defaults chosen to keep a worst case of a few hundred milliseconds per call in the worker, not measured limits. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer wants an admin setting for the caps, or a perf run measures the worst case. |
| OQ-1205 | How are the codec and the encoding selected, and are per-queue rules regular expressions (M1-10)? | The issue says "selection by content_type, content_encoding and per-queue rules". Working default: first a matching rule (a queue pattern with at most one `*` wildcard, no regular expression: a rule list is user data and must be timeout-safe, ADR-022 tier 2), then the headers (`content-type` mime and `charset`, `content-encoding` as an ordered list), then a sniff (magic numbers for gzip and zstd, a BOM, a JSON or XML first character, the first byte of a msgpack or cbor map or array, pickle protocol 2 to 5 and PHP array, object and string openings; the codec that decodes the whole body is the judge, the sniff only orders the candidates), then the hex fallback. The result says which step decided (`detected.source`) and how sure it is (`confidence`, 0 to 1). A header that names an unknown encoding or type is not trusted over the bytes: it gives a coded note and the sniff still runs. The first matching rule wins, in list order. Status: open - working default in use. | The per-queue rule store and its UI (a later message-browser work package) show a rule shape this does not cover. |
| OQ-1206 | What does the decoder say about pickle and PHP payloads, and may it use the word "safe" (M1-10)? | Blueprint section 11: pickle and PHP objects are never deserialised, they are shown as an opcode disassembly or tokens. Working default: pickle streams (protocol 0 to 5) are read opcode by opcode into a text listing (the dangerous opcodes `GLOBAL`, `STACK_GLOBAL`, `REDUCE`, `BUILD`, `INST`, `OBJ`, `NEWOBJ` are flagged in the listing but never evaluated, and no module is named to any loader); PHP `serialize()` text is tokenised and printed with its type tags. Both views carry `deserialised: false`. The UI words (not this work package) must say "shown as tokens, not loaded" and never "safe to open". The decoder has no `eval`, `Function`, `vm` or `child_process` import (a spec scans the sources). Status: open - working default in use. | The message browser work package words the view; a security review of the disassembler. |
| OQ-1207 | How much of CloudEvents does v1 cover (M1-10)? | The issue lists a `cloudevents` codec. Structured content mode (a JSON body with `specversion`, `id`, `source`, `type`, and `data` or `data_base64`) is a pure body envelope and is covered. Binary content mode puts the attributes in message headers (`ce-` or `cloudEvents:` prefixed, depending on the binding and the client); the exact names on an AMQP 0-9-1 publisher must come from a recorded message, and none exists in `testing/fixtures`, so binary mode is not guessed. The same holds for the `header-registry` entry that `/new-decoder` would create: the generator ran with the `cloudevents` name only to create the `decoders` library, and its stub decoder, registry entry and fixture folder were removed (a stub whose `decode` throws would be a trap). Status: open - working default in use. | A real CloudEvents publisher message is recorded (headers and body) for the fixtures. |
| OQ-1208 | What does the hex fallback show, and how much (M1-10)? | AC-1: a malformed payload gives hex plus the inline error. Working default: the hex view is the first 64 KiB of the original (not decompressed) bytes, 16 per row with an ASCII gutter, and a counted `preview` truncation for the rest; the error carries the code and, when the codec knows it, the byte offset. When a compressed layer decoded fine and the inner format failed, the hex shows the inner bytes (what the user can act on) and `layers` records the layer. The result also carries `sample` (the first 256 bytes, base64) only through the hex view, so a consumer needs no second call. Status: open - working default in use. | The message browser shows the view and the preview size needs a change. |
| OQ-1209 | What is M1-10 not proved with (M1-10)? | Proved with synthetic payloads built in the specs: every codec, the bombs, the pool failure paths. Not proved: a payload recorded from a real producer of any framework (none is in `testing/fixtures`), a broker round trip (the codecs take bytes; the `content_type` and `content_encoding` the AMQP adapter delivers were not exercised against a live broker here), the worker started from the engine bundle (OQ-1202), the 250 ms and 64 MB limits as measured on a user's machine (the specs use short timeouts and a small `resourceLimits` to keep the run fast and deterministic; the defaults are asserted by value), and I-5 as a percentile (the off-loop proof is a tick count). Status: open - working default in use. | A recorded-fixture work package, the bundle wiring and the perf gate. |
| OQ-972 | What did M1-01 land, and what does it not prove (M1-01)? | Landed: the k.3 tables as registry data (`effectiveConfig`: 12 merge rules, 33 resolution rows, 13 `W-EC-*` warnings with texts, 7 planned CF-EFFCFG lab-cell stubs), the pure library `libs/shared/effective-config` (`resolveEffective`, `mergeOperator`, `matchPolicy`, `resolveHypothetical`, a pattern scanner), property tests of the merge and precedence laws, a table oracle for every row and era, and hand-made mutants (30 killed, none survived). Not proved: any row against a real broker (no lab recording: OQ-968), the 3.13 and 4.0-4.2 rows beyond the two acceptance examples (extrapolated, OQ-961), 4.4 (OQ-963), the `apply-to` list `all, queues, classic_queues, quorum_queues, streams, exchanges` (read from the research sweep, not from the broker source), that the pattern subset agrees with Erlang `re` on every pattern it accepts (no differential test against PCRE: none is available offline), and that no accepted pattern backtracks badly (a heuristic: OQ-966). The mapping of management API objects (`policy`, `operator_policy`, `arguments`) to `ResolveInput` is M1-20; for a broker that omits `effective_policy_definition` (stats off below 4.2.5) this library is the local evaluation the research asks for. CODEOWNERS review and the adversarial `security-review` pass are still required (trust-critical); neither has been done. | A maintainer runs the seven recordings; a differential test of `testPattern` against a PCRE binding becomes possible if a dependency is approved. |
| OQ-993 | Why does the capture worker's connection use `synchronous = NORMAL` and its own checkpoints (M1-09)? | A commit that syncs the log costs a disk flush (1 to 3 ms on Windows), which alone is a third of the 5 ms budget, and SQLite checkpoints inside whichever commit crosses 1,000 pages, which made that one transaction tens of milliseconds long (p99 90 ms, max 180 ms before). On the worker's connection only (the engine's main connection keeps `FULL`): `synchronous = NORMAL` syncs the log at checkpoints, `wal_autocheckpoint = 0` and a passive checkpoint after 8 MiB of writes between requests, a full one when the worker closes. An engine crash loses nothing; a power cut can lose the last committed batches of captures (copies a person can take again) and never corrupts the file. The file is then checkpointed less often, so the write-ahead log can grow to a few multiples of 8 MiB while a reader holds an old snapshot. Status: open - working default in use. | A review decides captures need `FULL`, or the first long-lived reader shows the log growing without bound. |
| OQ-994 | What is "a transaction of at most 5 ms", and how does the ingest keep within it (M1-09)? | The time is measured next to the database, in the worker, from the first statement after BEGIN to the end of COMMIT (`txnMs`); waiting for the connection is not the writer's hold on it and is not counted. The 5 ms is a budget on the 95th percentile (the perf gate asserts p95 at most 5 ms and p99 under 25 ms and prints the max), because a single transaction on a desktop operating system can take several times its median for reasons outside the batch (the disk, antivirus, a scheduler tick). The ingest halves its batch when a transaction takes more than half of the budget and grows it by four rows when one takes less than a quarter, which keeps the median near 1.3 ms and the p95 near 4 ms; the batch is at most 200 rows and 4 MiB. Status: open - working default in use. | A lab or CI machine shows a p95 over 5 ms with these settings, or the budget moves. |

## Spec questions: M1-03 (issue #88, docs/specs/M1-03.md)

Routing simulator II (dead-letter paths, DLX loops, DLX sources). Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test. Ids start at OQ-1300 to stay clear of rows other work packages append at the same time.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1300 | Where does the dead-letter input live, and how does `TopologyQueue` grow (M1-03)? | The issue names `libs/shared/routing/src/{dlx,ttl,cycles,dlx-sources,hash-random-notes}.ts`. `TopologyQueue` gains optional fields (`type`, `deadLetter`, `messageTtlMs`, `maxLength`, `maxLengthBytes`, `overflow`, `deliveryLimit`, `rejectable`, `heldDeathSources`); every M1-02 input stays valid. A second topology type was rejected: `simulate` and the sources scan would take two inputs that must agree. Working default: additive optional fields. Status: open - working default in use. | A caller (M1-12, M1-20) finds the shape awkward. |
| OQ-1301 | What does an absent `deliveryLimit` mean (M1-03)? | Unknown, never unlimited: the same reading as `isDoomed` (a limit that cannot be established is the dangerous case). `dlxSources` then adds `source-limit-unknown` and `complete` is false. Status: open - working default in use. | The U-01(4) lab result says something else about classic and unlimited sources. |
| OQ-1302 | How does the topology say that a consumer may reject (M1-03)? | The broker drops a message that completes a dead-letter cycle without a `rejected` death; a consumer's rejection is not in a topology. Working default: optional `rejectable` on a queue, absent means not known to; a cycle with no rejectable queue is a `drop-loop` (the issue's AC), one with a rejectable queue is a `retry-ring`. The caller sets `rejectable` from the consumer count. A wrong default flags a retry pattern (false alarm) or misses a silent drop. Status: open - working default in use. | The preflight (M1-13) says how it knows consumers. |
| OQ-1303 | What about per-message `expiration` (M1-03)? | A topology cannot see it. `expired` is a reason only for a queue with a message TTL; a queue without one but with a DLX and publishers that set `expiration` is under-reported. Status: open - working default in use. | A caller can supply "messages with a TTL are seen" per queue. |
| OQ-1304 | Exact or may-reach when the dead-letter routing key is open (M1-03)? | Without `x-dead-letter-routing-key` the dead letter keeps the message's original keys, unknown to the analysis. Working default: over-approximate (every binding of a key-dependent exchange is a candidate, `certainty: 'possible'`); a false positive makes W-OVERLIMIT stricter, a false negative loses the warning. Status: open - working default in use. | A caller can supply the keys that reach a source queue. |
| OQ-1305 | Which gaps does the caller pass to `dlxSources` (M1-03)? | `policy-unreadable`, `operator-policy-unreadable`, `stats-fields-missing`, `other-clients-may-declare` (critique, A-2). The library cannot know what the principal could not read; the preflight (M1-13) builds the list. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-13 lands. |
| OQ-1306 | What does `via` show, and are all paths kept (M1-03)? | The dead-letter exchange chain of the first (shortest) path, and paths bounded by `maxDlxPaths`. Status: open - working default in use. | The DLQ centre needs more. |
| OQ-1307 | Which lab fixtures does M1-05 need for dead-lettering (M1-03)? | Rejected, expired, maxlen and limit-exceeded dead letters published with tracing on and compared with `deadLetterPath`; a fully automatic cycle (dropped on return) and the same cycle with one rejection; fixed and original routing key on direct, topic and fanout DLX; a QQ source L=20 into a QQ DLQ L=20 on 4.0.9 and 4.3.6; an `x-consistent-hash` DLX with weights (distribution, not a pick). Status: open - for the lab work package. | M1-05 lists the cells. |
| OQ-1308 | Where do the claims about the broker come from (M1-03)? | Read at v3.13.7 and v4.3.6 in slice A: `rabbit_dead_letter.erl`, `mc.erl` (`is_death_cycle`), `mc_amqpl.erl`, `rabbit_amqqueue_process.erl`. Two claims of the first spec were wrong and are corrected in it: the cycle rule (no check at all when the current reason is `rejected`; otherwise the target is dropped when every death since its last one is not a rejection, the current death included) and the dead-letter routing keys without a fixed key (publish key, CC **and BCC**). Not read from source: the quorum dead-letter code (`rabbit_fifo_dlx*`) and the plugin exchanges (documentation only). Status: closed by slice A for the classic path; open for quorum and plugins. | M1-05 lab cells prove the quorum and plugin rows. |
| OQ-1310 | Is the old (v1) death-record format modelled (M1-03)? | `is_cycle_v1` looks at the order of death records, which cannot be relied on; it applies to messages that carry the old format. Working default: v2 (by recency) only. Status: open - working default in use. | A message of the old format matters to a caller. |
| OQ-1309 | Size of M1-03 (M1-03)? | Landed as three commits: A (types, reasons, share notes, spec correction) and B (path walk, loops) and C (DLX sources, docs). About 2,900 changed lines in all, about 60 % of them tests, against a planned 1,700: the property and mutation-driven specs and the path types were larger than estimated, as in M1-02. `oversize-approved` was not asked for; each commit is green on its own. Status: closed - recorded. | A maintainer objects to the size. |
| OQ-1311 | Is an absent `rejectable` a rejection or not (M1-03)? | It differs by question, on purpose. `findDlxLoops` and `deadLetterReasons` read an absent flag as NOT rejectable (OQ-1302: the issue's automatic ring is a drop loop). `dlxSources` reads it as MAY be rejected from: a source we skip is a hazard we hide (A-2), so only `rejectable: false` rules rejection out. Found while writing the sources spec: a quorum source without TTL would otherwise not have been a source at all. Status: open - working default in use. | The preflight (M1-13) always sets the flag. |
| OQ-1312 | Why can a diamond give two paths to one queue (M1-03)? | The broker's cycle rule depends on the deaths behind a message, so the walk keys its states by (queue, queues of the current run of non-rejected deaths, keys). Two routes to a queue with different runs are two states and two paths; the reached SET is the same. A long chain of diamonds multiplies states; `maxDlxSteps` (1,000) and `maxDlxPaths` (64) bound it and count what they cut. Status: open - working default in use. | A real topology hits the limit. |

## Spec questions: M1-20 (issue #105, docs/specs/M1-20.md)

svc-topology: graph, subgraph, routing simulation, dead-letter and effective-config actions. Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test. Ids start at OQ-1400 to stay clear of rows other work packages append at the same time.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1400 | Why does svc-topology read the broker itself and not use the explorer's rows (M1-20)? | The brief suggested building the input from the explorer rows and the management client. A service may not import another service or an adapter (blueprint 2.2), and the explorer's rows carry neither queue and exchange `arguments` nor the policy lists, which the dead-letter exchange, the limits and the alternate exchange are made of. Working default: the module is given the scheduler's `MgmtReadPort` (lane `interactive`) and has its own schemas, as the explorer has. A cross-service port to the explorer's resident index stays possible later as an optimisation. Status: open - working default in use. | A measured need to share the explorer's index (read amplification on a large vhost). |
| OQ-1401 | Six actions and not four (M1-20)? | The issue names `topology.graph`, `topology.subgraph`, `routing.simulate`, `effective-config.get`. The brief asks that `deadLetterPath`, `findDlxLoops` and `dlxSources` get an engine face with the A-2 fields unflattened, so `routing.dead-letter` (path plus the loops that include the queue) and `routing.dlx-sources` are added. They are reads like the others and share the snapshot. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer wants them in a separate work package. |
| OQ-1402 | What are `topology.graph` and `topology.subgraph`, and how is a large scope cut (M1-20)? | `graph` is the whole vhost, `subgraph` the breadth-first neighbourhood of one exchange or queue, depth 0 to 2. Both are held to 2,000 elements (nodes plus edges). Over the cap, nodes are ranked (focus, distance, degree, name), the largest prefix that fits is kept and the rest is folded into aggregate nodes (per kind and best-ranked kept neighbour) with a count, a sample and one edge. The marker is `aggregated: true` with `omitted` and exact `totals`. Dead-letter and alternate-exchange links are edges. The UI (M1-49) may want different ranking. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-49 is built against it. |
| OQ-1403 | Where does the verification publish of the issue go (M1-20)? | The issue says "routing.simulate with an optional verification publish (mandatory)" and the critique fix calls it a planned mutation behind the prod lock that "delivers a real message to N queues". The brief scopes this work package to reads (no `WritePort`, planning none). A real publish needs the publish port, the executor's plan, consent and prod-lock path and the mandatory/return handling. Working default: out of scope here; follow-up issue `routing.verify` (kind `command`, planning `required`, risk set by the environment class), which depends on the publish studio's port. Status: open - working default in use. | The follow-up issue is scheduled. |
| OQ-1404 | How big may a vhost be, and what happens past the bound (M1-20)? | Bounds: 50,000 queues, 10,000 exchanges, 200,000 bindings, 100,000 consumers, 1,000 policies, paged at 500; a snapshot lives 15 s (bounded map of 8). Rows past a bound are dropped and counted per kind and the gap `truncated` is raised. The bindings of a vhost arrive as one array and the client refuses a body over 16 MiB (about 40,000 bindings); that read then fails closed with the port's Problem, because a graph without its bindings would be wrong. The explorer reads the same endpoint as a stream with a cap; doing so here is the follow-up. Status: open - working default in use. | A vhost of 40,000+ bindings is a real case; then stream the bindings. |
| OQ-1405 | How is the policy layer of a queue decided (M1-20)? | Per layer (user, operator): the stat `policy` / `operator_policy` names the policy when the broker sent it (4.2.5+ or statistics on); a stat of `null` is "none"; an absent key is a stats gap and the list is matched with `matchPolicy`; an unreadable list, a stat naming a policy that is not listed, or an ambiguous match makes the layer `unknown`. An unknown layer gives `deadLetter: unknown` unless the argument decides it (the argument wins over every policy), and leaves the limits absent. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-05 records the stat fields per version and provider. |
| OQ-1406 | Which delivery limit does a source get (M1-20)? | Quorum sources: the stat `delivery_limit` when numeric (negative: unlimited), else the resolver's `delivery-limit` entry; an unreadable or odd value is `unknown`. Classic and stream sources get no limit (unknown, never unlimited): the critique leaves them open until the lab answers U-01(4), and the conservative reading raises `source-limit-unknown`. Status: open - working default in use. | U-01(4) is answered by the lab. |
| OQ-1407 | Where does `heldDeathSources` come from (M1-20)? | The brief says "from x-death". A topology read touches no message, so no x-death is visible to these actions. `routing.dlx-sources` takes `heldDeathSources` as input (at most 100 names, derived from the death-history normaliser M1-04 by the caller, the peek preflight M1-13) and echoes them as `heldDeathSourcesConsidered`, so a consumer can tell "x-death was looked at" from "it was not". Without it the held-death check is simply not applied, and no `complete` is claimed about it. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-13 wires the preflight; it must pass the names. |
| OQ-1408 | When is each gap raised (M1-20)? | `policy-unreadable`, `operator-policy-unreadable`: the list answered 403 (also the missing-tag 401 and a provider restriction), 404 or 405. `stats-fields-missing`: a queue row lacks `policy` or `operator_policy`, or a quorum row lacks `delivery_limit`. `other-clients-may-declare`: the profile identity is neither admin nor monitoring, so the listing may not show every queue; it is also true of every snapshot (a client can declare a queue a second later), which is why the answers carry `snapshot.takenAt`, and the gap is raised only for the first reason. `policy-match-undecided` and `truncated` are this module's. Lab cells for each cause (the CF cells of the critique) are M1-18's. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-18 and M1-05 add the CF cells; a maintainer may decide the second reason should always raise the gap. |
| OQ-1409 | Which version does the resolver get on a mixed cluster (M1-20)? | The lowest node version (`profile.versions.min`), as OQ-971 says; `null` gives `unknown`. The answer reports `mixedVersions`. Resolving per node and showing the disagreement waits for the UI (M1-50). Status: open - working default in use. | M1-50 asks for it. |
| OQ-1410 | Which alternate exchange does an exchange have (M1-20)? | The argument `alternate-exchange` if a string, else the `alternate-exchange` of the matching user policy (applies to `all` and `exchanges`). The registry has no exchange rows and no broker source was read for the exchange case; the documented general rule (arguments beat user policies) is the working default. An undecidable match leaves the AE absent and raises `policy-match-undecided`. Status: open - working default in use. | A registry row and a CF cell for the exchange case. |
| OQ-1411 | How is `rejectable` set (M1-20)? | A queue with at least one consumer that requires acknowledgement may nack, so `rejectable: true`; otherwise absent (OQ-1302: absent means "not known to" for loops). The consumer list is read best effort; if it fails or is cut the answer says `consumers: unreadable` or `truncated` and loops may be reported as drop loops that a consumer could turn into retry rings. A consumer that connects later is invisible. Status: open - working default in use. | A caller that knows the consumers passes them (an input is added then). |
| OQ-1412 | Size of M1-20 (M1-20)? | Landed as the spec and four code commits: about 3,850 lines of source and contracts and 4,200 of tests and fixtures (against 3,800 planned in all), plus 5,700 lines of generated OpenAPI. Six actions with a contract, a spec, an HTTP spec and a fixture each, the snapshot, the settings resolver and the graph. `oversize-approved` is not asked for, as in M1-02 and M1-03. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer objects. |
| OQ-1413 | Is the declare-time ("hypothetical") form of the resolver exposed (M1-20)? | `resolveHypothetical` ("what applies if I declare X") is in the library and M1-01 names M1-20 and M1-50 as consumers. The issue's acceptance criterion is per-key provenance of an existing queue. Working default: only the existing-queue form here; the hypothetical form is added with the UI that needs it (M1-50) as a second input shape of the same action. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-50 starts. |
| OQ-1414 | Why is the action `queues.effective-config` and not `effective-config.get` (M1-20)? | The issue names the file `effective-config.get.ts`. The kit (`ID_FORMAT` in `checkRegistry`) and the action generator accept `namespace.name` where the namespace is lowercase letters and digits only, so `effective-config` cannot be a namespace. Working default: `queues.effective-config` (the answer is about one queue), route `POST /connections/{cid}/effective-config/queries`, CLI `effective-config get`. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer prefers another id (a rename touches the contract, the route and the OpenAPI document). |
| OQ-1415 | Are parallel edges merged when a graph does not fit (M1-20)? | Found while building the cap: an exchange with many bindings to one queue (one per routing key) can exceed the cap by itself, so even the focus could not be shown. Working default: when the scope does not fit, parallel edges (same ends, same kind) are merged into one edge that counts them (`count`, label only when it is one). Everything that fits is drawn one edge per binding. `aggregated` then means "folded nodes or merged edges". Status: open - working default in use. | M1-49 says it wants the keys of every binding in the graph (then a drawer, not the graph). |
| OQ-1416 | Is the default exchange drawn (M1-20)? | Every queue is implicitly bound to the default exchange and the broker lists those bindings: one edge per queue would bury the graph. Working default: the default exchange has no node, its bindings are not edges, and links to a missing object or to the default exchange are counted in `totals.unrepresented`, so nothing is silently dropped. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-49 wants the default exchange drawn. |
| OQ-1417 | How deep may the headers of a simulated publish nest (M1-20)? | The OpenAPI emitter rejects recursive schemas, so `HeaderValueSchema` allows scalars, and lists and tables of scalars, nested one more level. The simulator reads only scalar header values (headers exchange arguments) and the `CC` and `BCC` lists. Status: open - working default in use. | The emitter learns references, or a publish needs deeper tables. |
| OQ-1418 | How is the queue list asked for, and what does that cost the broker (M1-20)? | `GET /api/queues/{vhost}?pagination=true&columns=name,type,arguments,policy,operator_policy,delivery_limit`, in the `interactive` lane and without `disable_stats`, because below 4.2.5 `disable_stats` removes the policy fields this module needs. Whether `columns` spares the broker the statistics, and whether the policy fields survive it on every version and provider, was not run against a broker. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-05 records the answer per version and provider; a probe-time check can replace the assumption. |
| OQ-1419 | Which gaps make `routing.simulate` inexact (M1-20)? | All of them. Some do not touch routing (`operator-policy-unreadable`, `stats-fields-missing`: operator policies and queue stats do not change a binding or an exchange alternate exchange), but a topology the principal cannot fully list (`other-clients-may-declare`) can. The conservative rule is one rule: any gap makes `exact` false and `unroutable` false, and the answer lists the gaps so a UI can word them. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-50 wants the routing-relevant gaps apart from the rest. |
| OQ-1420 | The library calls run on the event loop (M1-20)? | `simulate`, `deadLetterPath`, `findDlxLoops` and `dlxSources` are synchronous and bounded by their limits, not chunked. Measured on a development machine with 20,000 queues (10,000 dead-letter sources): `dlxSources` 26 ms, path plus loops 30 ms, `simulate` 6 ms; conversion and the graph yield every 500 items (longest slice about 3 ms). The p99 gate of I-5 (20 ms under load) is planned, not run; at 50,000 queues the three calls would be about 2.5 times longer. Status: open - working default in use. | The perf gate lands (I-5); then make the scans cooperative in the library or run them in the worker pool. |

## Spec questions: M1-12 (issue #97, docs/specs/M1-12.md)

Peek planner, pure. Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test. Ids start at OQ-1421 (M1-20 used OQ-1400..1420).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1421 | Does the planner wait for M0-62 (M1-12)? | The issue lists M0-62 (matrix generator, lane evidence) as a dependency. The planner reads the registry through `cell`, `isDoomed`, `peekCaps` and `warning`; all four exist on `main` with the b.2/b.8 data (M0-12, M1-01). M0-62 changes how far a cell is confirmed, not what it says. Working default: no wait. Every cell used is `code-derived`; the preview carries `semanticsVersion` and a `confidence` of `code-derived` or `unverified-version`, never `lab-verified`. When a cell flips, the planner needs no change; the receipt (M1-16) reads the status from the cell id. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer wants the planner gated on M0-62. |
| OQ-1422 | How does the planner know an era carries the delivery count into a dead-letter target (M1-12)? | The registry has no row for it: the `no_requeue` cells cannot carry it (OQ-390) and no hazard or capability states it. Working default: it is inferred from the doom rule, `carries = !isDoomed(era, {dc: 20}, finite 20) && isDoomed(era, {dc: 21}, finite 20)` (the strict `>` rule on the delivery count), which holds for the eras where fact sheet b.4 puts the carry-over (4.3, 4.4) and not for 3.13 and 4.0-4.2. The inference lives in one function (`limit.ts`) and a test pins a decision for every `Era`, so a new era fails the test until someone decides. The direction is safe for the known eras; a future era with a `>=` rule and a carry-over would be under-blocked. Follow-up: a registry row ("dead-letter carry-over") and one for peek-method availability per era. Status: open - working default in use. | The registry gets the rows. |
| OQ-1423 | Where exactly is the W-OVERLIMIT boundary, and what is an unlimited source (M1-12)? | The issue, DC-07 and blueprint 5.4 say a finite L "not above every source's L+1" blocks (`Lt <= Ls + 1`); fact sheet b.4 and b.8 say `L < max(Ls) + 1` blocks and `L >= Ls + 1` is safe. They differ at `Lt = Ls + 1`. The code reading (`DC > Lt`, arrival DC = Ls+1) says `Ls + 1` survives a nack peek but not a failed delivery: a close while holding adds one (W-CRASH) and the message is dropped. Working default: the issue's reading, derived from the registry and not typed in: worst arrival = `Ls + 1` plus the `deltaDC` of the `close_while_holding` cell (1 when the cell is a gap), blocked when `isDoomed` says so; minimum safe finite limit `max(Ls) + 2`; `-1` always safe. A source with no limit (`unlimited`) has an unbounded count (a message that failed many times and was then rejected), so any finite target limit is blocked and only `-1` is offered. Classic and stream sources have an `unknown` limit (OQ-1406 of M1-20), which gives the `unknown` verdict. Status: open - working default in use. | A lab cell (CF-43-QQ-DLX-CARRY-01) or a maintainer settles the boundary and the unlimited source. |
| OQ-1424 | Why is the rejection not exactly the one of core-interfaces.md (M1-12)? | The interface has `reason: string` and nine codes. "Warnings are codes with i18n keys, never prose", so the rejection carries `textKey`, `textVersion`, `reasons` (closed codes) and `warnings`, and it has `fix` and `offers`. Codes added: `INVALID_REQUEST`, `STREAM_QUEUE`, `UNVERIFIED_BROKER`, `METHOD_UNAVAILABLE`, `BUDGET_TOO_SMALL`. `RESCUE_PREFLIGHT_FAILED`, `SEMANTICS_GAP`, `GATE` are kept; `OVERLIMIT_DLQ`, `LIMIT_MINUS1_313`, `NO_SAFE_DISPOSITION`, `NO_QUORUM`, `SAC_ACTIVE`, `EXCLUSIVE` as named. `honestLabel` is `honestLabelKey`. M1-16 maps a rejection to a Problem. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-16 needs another shape. |
| OQ-1425 | Where do the four extra warnings live (M1-12)? | A-2 needs a human consent for "the dead-letter evidence is incomplete" and for "the limit is unknown", and the semantics rules need one for "unverified build" and for "conservative substitution". The registry has exactly the 16 warnings of b.8, W-OVERLIMIT has severity `block` and its text says the limit "is not higher than theirs", which is false when the evidence is incomplete (honest wording). Adding a 17th to the registry changes the b.8 set (RFC). Working default: `W-DLX-UNVERIFIED`, `W-LIMIT-UNKNOWN`, `W-BROKER-UNVERIFIED`, `W-SEMANTICS-GAP` are planner-owned (`textKey` `peekPlanner.<id>.v1`, English in `libs/shared/peek-planner/i18n/en/warnings.json`). Follow-up issue: move them into the registry. Status: open - working default in use. | The registry takes them. |
| OQ-1426 | Who is a "human" principal (M1-12)? | DC-09 forbids AI and unattended runs from letting the broker handle a doomed message; A-1 and A-2 block them elsewhere. The kernel has seven principal kinds and five surfaces. Working default: human interactive means the principal and everyone it acts for has kind `desktop-user`, `local-token`, `team-user` or `api-token`, surface `ui` or `cli`, and `unattended: false`. An MCP client, a job, the system or any unattended principal is non-human, also when it acts for a person. Status: open - working default in use. | Policy (M4, MCP) names other classes. |
| OQ-1427 | What does the planner need from the preflight (M1-12)? | `limitState` beside `deliveryLimit` (cells are keyed by it; 3.13 `-1` and unset differ and `DeliveryLimit` cannot say which), `singleActiveConsumer` and `hasActiveConsumer` (the interface's `sacActive` mixes them), `dlxSources` as `routing.dlx-sources` answers it with `complete` and `unknownReasons`, `heldDeathSources` (absent: not looked at; M1-20 OQ-1407), `rescue.queueName` (the product name is a placeholder: the planner may not invent `<product>.quarantine`), `limitFixedBy`, `visibleLimits`, `rttMs`, and `nowMs` in the facts (no clock in a pure library; the 60 s freshness of A-1 needs "now"). An absent field means unknown. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-13 is specified. |
| OQ-1428 | Which dispositions may be chosen, and what is the default (M1-12)? | DC-09 and blueprint 5.4. Working default: quarantine for everyone when its rescue preflight passes (the default when nothing is requested, A-1); raise-limit for broker administrators, never when the limit is unknown, capped by an argument or operator policy, or named `nsb.*` or owned by a provider; let-broker-handle for humans with the queue name as typed consent, never defaulted. A non-human principal needs the rescue (quarantine) or the preflight (raise-limit) at most 60 s old; a human's rescue is re-checked by the executor, not by age here. `raise-limit` uses `isAdmin` as the issue says, although writing a policy needs only the policymaker tag. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer accepts policymakers for raise-limit. |
| OQ-1429 | What does a plan say for a classic queue, which has no cells (M1-12)? | The registry cells are all quorum. A classic queue has no limit and no doom, so no decision needs a cell, but `expected` has nothing to show. Working default: `expected` is empty for classic queues, the hold uses both timeout formulas (no `consumer_timeout` cell to choose), and the preview says `effects: 'not-in-registry'`; no gap is raised for it (a gap would reject every AI peek of a classic queue). Follow-up: classic cells (b.3). Status: open - working default in use. | Classic cells land. |
| OQ-1430 | What happens on an era with no cells, such as 4.4 (M1-12)? | `semantics.md`: a gap makes the planner use the conservative 4.0-4.2 rules, extra consent, and reject unattended principals. Working default: the lookup order is the same era with limit `finite`, then `4.0-4.2` (a typed constant used as a key, as `caps.ts` does) with the limit state, then with `finite`; a substituted row adds `W-SEMANTICS-GAP` and lists the keys in `gaps`; a non-human principal gets `SEMANTICS_GAP`. The DLX hazard still follows the real era's doom rule, so a 4.4 queue is checked like 4.3. Status: open - working default in use. | 4.4 cells land (A-23). |
| OQ-1431 | What is the consumer timeout when the preflight cannot read it (M1-12)? | Fact sheet b.5: the default is 1,800,000 ms on every version; the global value is visible over HTTP only to administrators (`/api/config/effective`). Working default: the default is used when `consumerTimeoutMs` is absent. If an operator lowered it below 120 s without an argument or policy, the hold of 30 s can exceed `timeout - 90 s` on 4.0-4.2; the executor's watchdog (M1-14) is the backstop. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-13 reads the effective config. |
| OQ-1432 | Is a count above the cap clamped or refused (M1-12)? | The blueprint's formula `count <= min(cap, floor(target / rtt))` is a clamp. A request above the cap is clamped the same way, never silently: the preview has `requested`, the effective `count` and `limitedBy` (`cap`, `hold`, `bytes`, `request`). Only a budget that leaves no room for one message is refused (`BUDGET_TOO_SMALL`). Status: open - working default in use. | The UI wants a refusal. |
| OQ-1433 | How is A-3 read (M1-12)? | "Never offered for QQs with finite or unknown L on 3.13 or 4.0-4.2, nor on 4.3 queues failing the DLX check. Allowed only for classic, unlimited and provably non-DLX 4.3+ QQs." Working default: the second sentence is the exhaustive list: classic, or quorum that is unlimited and provably non-DLX (`complete`, no source, `heldDeathSources` present) in an era with the strict rule. An unlimited quorum queue on 3.13 or 4.0-4.2 is not offered the fallback (it is in neither list), and a finite limit never is. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer widens the list. |
| OQ-1434 | Why is the W-OVERLIMIT fix data and not a `Gate.fix` (M1-12)? | `Gate.fix` needs an action id; no policy-write action exists yet (A-4 is a later package). Working default: the rejection carries `fix: {kind: 'delivery-limit-policy', queue, limit: -1, minimumFinite}`; M1-16 maps it to the action when it exists. Status: open - working default in use. | The policy-write action lands. |
| OQ-1435 | Why is `gates` always empty (M1-12)? | Everything that blocks is a rejection with a code; everything that needs a person is a consent. A blocking gate in a draft would say the same twice. The field is kept because a `PlanDraft` has it. Status: open - working default in use. | The executor wants advisory gates. |
| OQ-1436 | Size of M1-12 (M1-12)? | Planned as six slices of about 800 lines each, tests included. `oversize-approved` is not asked for (the budget is per slice, as in M1-02 and M1-03). Landed at about 1,280, 1,000, 620, 730, 1,340 and 1,380 (slice F) lines including tests, against budgets of 600 to 800; roughly 4,000 lines are specs and builders, 2,000 source, 170 text. The slice budgets were exceeded, mostly by tests of a trust-critical rule set. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer objects. |
| OQ-1437 | Does the planner use the environment class (M1-12)? | `PeekFacts.env` is in the interface of `core-interfaces.md`. The guardrail policy (kernel) raises risk, approvals and extra consents by environment and forbids letting the broker handle a message there (`forbidLetBrokerHandle`); the planner decides nothing by it, so a production queue and a development queue get the same plan. The field is kept so a plan can be bound to it. Status: open - working default in use. | The guardrail policy wants the planner to refuse something by environment. |

## Spec questions: M1-19 (issue #104, docs/specs/M1-19.md)

Publish engine: the `messages.publish` action and the publish history. Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test. Ids start at OQ-1438 (M1-12 used up to OQ-1437).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1438 | Are `mandatory` and `confirm` inputs of the publish (M1-19)? | The issue's interface lists both as flags. `PublishLease.publish` accepts only `{ mandatory: true }` and the role has confirm channels only (DC-12). Working default: both fields exist in the contract as the literal `true` (omitted means `true`); `false` is `VALIDATION_FAILED` and no lease is taken. Turning either off needs a port change and a decision on DC-12. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants an opt-out, or confirms the fields can be dropped from the contract. |
| OQ-1439 | Which per-copy outcomes does the publish report (M1-19)? | The issue says confirmed, nacked, returned. `PublishOutcome` also has `blocked` and `timeout`, and a copy can be unsent (the loop stopped) or fail (a later copy threw). Working default: `confirmed`, `nacked`, `returned`, `blocked`, `timeout`, `failed`, `not-sent`; every copy appears once; `allConfirmed` is true only when all are `confirmed`; `timeout` reads "no confirm in time, the broker may still have routed it". Status: open - working default in use. | The studio design (M1-46) wants fewer or different states. |
| OQ-1440 | Does the publish read a semantics-registry row for `NO_ROUTE` (M1-19)? | The registry has no row for `NO_ROUTE`, `basic.return` or mandatory publish (searched `libs/shared/semantics/src`). Working default: none is read or added; the outcome is what the lease saw on that broker and the lab IT (slice E) shows it on 4.3.6; no behaviour is claimed for other versions. A conformance cell is a separate work package (`/new-semantics-cell`). Status: open - working default in use. | A cell for mandatory returns is wanted across versions. |
| OQ-1441 | Where does the scripted publish lease live (M1-19)? | A `confirmed` outcome carries a `ConfirmedCopyProof` that only a publish lease may mint (A-9). Working default: a test-only `fake-publish.ts` in `svc-messages`; if the architecture test refuses the import there, the fake moves to `libs/testkit`. Status: open - working default in use. | The architecture test is run on slice A. |
| OQ-1442 | Does the history keep the original or the masked publish (M1-19)? | "Returned for re-sending with secrets masked" can mean masking a sealed original on read, or never storing the secret. Working default: mask before sealing (credential keys at any depth, credentialed URIs, secret query parameters, JWTs and bearer values), so no secret the mask catches is ever stored; the cost is that a re-send cannot restore a masked value and a secret with no recognisable shape can pass the mask (the UI must say so). A body that is binary or over 64 KiB is not kept (size and sha256 are). No search over bodies. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants exact re-send or body search. |
| OQ-1443 | What is the retention of the publish history, and what purges it (M1-19)? | The issue says 30 days. Blueprint 6.3 gives no value for publish history and nothing for team mode; no recurring scheduler exists (`purgeExpired` of the capture store has no caller). Working default: a constant of 30 days for every mode; purge on every write and at module start, at most 200 rows a pass; expired rows are never returned even before they are purged. Status: open - working default in use. | A retention setting, a team value or a scheduled job is wanted. |
| OQ-1444 | Which ids, permission and MCP exposure do the three actions have (M1-19)? | The action generator takes `namespace.verb` ids and has no history permission to pick. Working default: `messages.publish` (command, risk medium, planning optional), `messages.publish-history.list` and `.get` (queries); the history reads use `messages.publish`; `--mcp=none` for all three; `--feature=none` (the UI stubs belong to M1-46). If the generator refuses three parts, the names are `messages.history-list` and `messages.history-get`. Status: open - working default in use. | The first generator run, or the MCP work package. |
| OQ-1445 | Is `svc-messages` registered in the engine by this work package (M1-19)? | The new contracts make `compose-services.spec.ts` AC-1 fail unless the ids are served or listed in `INTENTIONALLY_UNSERVED`. Working default: register the module in `coreModules` (additive edit of the composition root, the history repository joins `ServicePorts`); the history reads work, and `messages.publish` answers `CAPABILITY_MISSING` until the ConnectionManager is wired. The alternative is three `INTENTIONALLY_UNSERVED` entries with a reason. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer picks the alternative. |
| OQ-1446 | Who wires the ConnectionManager into the composed engine (M1-19)? | `apps/engine/src/unwired-writes.ts` refuses every lease (OQ-357, OQ-771). The publish, the peek action of M1-16 and the UI of M1-46 all need it. Working default: not in this work package; the action is proved over a scripted manager and, in slice E, over the real manager and adapter in the testkit. Status: open - working default in use. | A work package for the wiring is named or scheduled. |
| OQ-1447 | How does the publish turn an adapter failure into a Problem (M1-19)? | The adapter's errors carry a contract `ErrorCode` in `code` and a safe `detail`, but mapping is the caller's job (OQ-180, OQ-210 open) and a service cannot import the adapter. Working default: the action reads `code` and `detail` structurally; a known code becomes `AppError(code)`, anything else `INTERNAL` with a fixed text; a failure before any copy has an outcome is a Problem, a later one marks that copy `failed` and the rest `not-sent`. Status: open - working default in use. | OQ-210 is settled. |
| OQ-1448 | Who owns a history entry, and can it be deleted (M1-19)? | The capture store takes the owner from the principal. Working default: owner is `principal.id`; every read answers `NOT_FOUND` for another owner's, another connection's or an unknown id; no delete or clear action, and no cascade when a connection profile is deleted (the 30-day rule removes the rows). Status: open - working default in use. | A "clear history" action is wanted. |
| OQ-1449 | Which migration number and which OQ ids does M1-19 take (M1-19)? | `0011` is the next free migration on `5269954`; another work package in flight (M1-23 has a worktree) may take it first. OQ-1438 and up were free in every worktree checked on 2026-10-26. Working default: the one that lands second renumbers on rebase (file name and `id` together). Status: open - working default in use. | M1-19 slice B lands. |
| OQ-1450 | What are the limits of a publish (M1-19)? | The issue gives none. Working default: at most 100 copies, a body of at most 1 MiB, 64 headers nested at most 8 deep, a wait of 100 ms to 30 s per copy (5 s default); the lease's own maximum is 600 s. Bulk publish is PUB-04 (M4) and will need other limits. Status: open - working default in use. | The studio design (M1-46) or PUB-04 needs other values. |
| OQ-1481 | How does the publish history mask a long unbroken run of text (M1-19, slice C)? | The kernel's redaction expressions take 1.5 to 3.5 s on 64 KiB of one unbroken run (measured on this base), a stall of the event loop (I-5). Working default: a run of more than 1,024 non-space characters is replaced by `[long]` before the expressions see it and the body is listed as masked; every other character of a masked body is kept, so a re-send still works. Cost: a long base64 string inside a text or JSON body is not restored on re-send. Alternatives: omit such a body (`bodyOmitted`), or mask it in windows with a risk of splitting a secret. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer objects, or the kernel's expressions are made linear. |
| OQ-1502 | Should the two publish-history reads leave an audit row (M1-19, slice D)? | A query leaves no audit row and no receipt (the kit's rule). `messages.publish-history.get` returns a stored message body, masked. Working default: `audit: 'none'` for both reads, and the permission `messages.publish` (OQ-1444) over `messages.read-body`: history is per owner, so only the person who published, and holds that permission, has any entry, and `get` can only return what that person typed. Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer wants a read of stored bodies audited (`'outcome'`), or a separate history permission. |
| OQ-1503 | How large may the history's re-send answer be, and does it keep the publish limits (M1-19, slice D)? | The mask replaces a secret with a longer marker, so a value can come back longer than it went in (253 characters of `password=x` come back as more than 255). With the publish limits on the answer, such an entry could be recorded but never opened (`INTERNAL`: the result breaks its contract); a check found it. Working default: the answer's property and header strings are held to 1 MiB (the bound of the stored document), the publish input keeps its limits, so a re-send of a grown value must be shortened by the person before it is accepted as a publish. The cost is a second copy of the header-table schema in the API document (about 200 KB, the document grew from 581 KB to 782 KB). Status: open - working default in use. | A $ref-based emitter (the API document stops repeating the nested schema), or a decision to cut a grown value instead of widening the bound. |
| OQ-1504 | Should the history reads check that the connection exists (M1-19, slice D)? | `list` for a connection id that does not exist, or that the caller may not use, answers an empty window, and `get` answers `NOT_FOUND`: the history is scoped by owner and connection id, and the history module has no connection store (a service imports no service). Other reads answer `NOT_FOUND` for an unknown connection. Working default: no check; an unknown id has an empty history, nothing leaks. Status: open - working default in use. | The studio (M1-46) needs a 404 for an unknown connection, or the connection store becomes a port of this module. |

## Spec questions: M1-23 (issue #108, docs/specs/M1-23.md)

Credential providers and TokenManager, trust-critical. Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test. Ids start at OQ-1451 (M1-12 used OQ-1421..1437).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1451 | The issue says Keycloak; the lab has none (M1-23). | The broker-lab `oauth` variant is the broker's oauth2 backend with a per-run signing key and no identity provider; Keycloak 26 is M1-25 (#110, open). Working default: AC-24 and AC-25 run on that variant with tokens minted by the test (the broker's own `update-secret`, username check and expiry handling are real); the TokenManager is not offered to users until M1-25's cells are green (U-14). Status: open - working default in use. | M1-25 lands the Keycloak variant and the CF-AUTH cells. |
| OQ-1452 | Are the renewal and frame-size rules registry data (M1-23)? | The registry has no row for expiry, renewal or the pre-auth frame, and I-3 forbids version compares in `svc-identity`. Working default: three capability rules (`amqp091ClosesAtCredentialExpiry` from 4.2.6, `amqp10TokenRenewalEndpoint` from 4.1.0, `amqp091PreAuthFrame8192` from 4.1.0), code-derived, with citations to the research notes V09, V12, V13 and V41; a rule holds only while every node is in range, so a mixed or unknown cluster takes the conservative side.
