The 4.2.5/4.2.6 split is a source reading, not a lab result. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-25 records the lab evidence and the cells. |
| OQ-1453 | Which lab versions does `svc-identity:it` run (M1-23)? | The issue names 4.2.9 and 4.3.6, both at or above 4.2.6, so neither shows the "4.2.5 or earlier" rule. Working default: 4.1.8, 4.2.9 and 4.3.6. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer wants a different set. |
| OQ-1454 | Does M1-23 change `adapter-amqp091` (M1-23)? | The adapter has no `updateSecret` and fixes its credentials at connect. The issue's scope lists only `svc-identity`. Working default: a small additive change (`updateSecret`, credentials resolved at each connect, `OpenedConnection.renew`), counted in slice E. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer wants it as its own work package. |
| OQ-1455 | Where does provider configuration live (M1-23)? | Profiles keep one sealed secret per protocol and a strict versioned config with no provider fields. Working default: a new table `credential_providers` behind a `CredentialProviderStore` port; `StoredConfigSchema` unchanged; no action or UI writes it in this work package (a follow-up issue adds them). Alternative: a `StoredConfig` v2 migration. The table can be deferred, leaving only the in-memory fake. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer chooses the store or the follow-up lands. |
| OQ-1456 | How is the username claim read and what happens on a mismatch (M1-23)? | The broker derives the name from `preferred_username_claims`; the research notes (V21) give `sub` then `client_id` as the default, not read from source here. Working default: a per-provider `usernameClaims` list with that default; the first token fixes the name; a different or missing name is refused (fail closed); a token that is not a readable JWT is refused; the refusal reuses the `UNAUTHENTICATED` Problem (a new code changes the contracts). Status: open - working default in use. | M1-25 records the broker's default, or a maintainer wants a dedicated code. |
| OQ-1457 | How much of the pre-auth frame is not the PLAIN response (M1-23)? | The limit covers the whole `connection.start-ok` frame; client properties are not measured. Working default: a stated headroom estimate of 1,024 B (`PREAUTH_HEADROOM_BYTES`), warnings say "may exceed". Status: open - working default in use. | M1-25 records the real thresholds. |
| OQ-1458 | What are the refresh details beyond 75 % (M1-23)? | Working defaults: lifetime = `exp - iat`; provider `expires_in` only for a token without a readable `exp`; no `exp` means no schedule; a 5 s floor between refreshes and between forced-close reconnects; backoff 2 s doubling to 60 s, at most 8 attempts before `exp`; an already expired token is refused with `TOKEN_EXPIRED`. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer or the lab runs change a number. |
| OQ-1459 | Which requests are retried after a 401, and where is it wired (M1-23)? | DC-37 says idempotent only. Working default: GET, HEAD and OPTIONS (not PUT or DELETE, which change broker state); `refreshAfterUnauthorized` is delivered, the retry in `adapter-mgmt-http` (with the I-8 scheduler path) is a follow-up issue. Status: open - working default in use. | The follow-up issue is specified. |
| OQ-1460 | How is the token command run and where (M1-23)? | A configured command is code execution. Working default: argv array and no shell, 10 s timeout, 16 KiB stdout cap, minimal environment, stderr never returned or logged, behind `CommandRunnerPort` in `apps/engine`, registered only in the desktop and local modes (absent in the team server, so the provider fails closed). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the modes and limits. |
| OQ-1461 | x509/EXTERNAL cannot connect yet (M1-23). | `net-truststore` has no client certificate and `AmqpAuthKindSchema` has no `external`, though the AMQP adapter speaks EXTERNAL; the HTTP API has no certificate login. Working default: the provider and its contract ship; no action offers it until a follow-up issue adds the client certificate to `TlsTrust` and the profile schema. Status: open - working default in use. | The follow-up issue lands. |
| OQ-1462 | Who makes the token request, and which purpose (M1-23)? | A service cannot import `undici`; the purpose `oidc` is `planned`. Working default: a new adapter `adapter-token-endpoint` behind `TokenEndpointPort` (M1-24 reuses it), through the Dialer with purpose `oidc` made `active`, a hand-built form POST and no new dependency (`openid-client` is not in the catalog); plain `http` only for a loopback issuer. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer prefers `openid-client` (then the catalog, allow-list and ADR-027 lines are human edits). |
| OQ-1463 | Is timer-driven renewal an action or a job (I-2) (M1-23)? | `updateSecret`, `reconnect` and cancelling the engine's own consumers touch a connection, not a user's messages. Working default: `svc-identity` holds no `WritePort` or lease and calls `ConnectionAuthPort`; the architecture test stays green with no exception. There is no `svc-tap` yet, so "without dropping taps" is proved with a consumer on a lab queue and fake leases. Status: open - working default in use. | The `defineJob` check lands (M0-31/32) and the maintainer rules. |
| OQ-1464 | What does M1-23 do for AMQP 1.0 and streams (M1-23)? | `adapter-amqp10` (M1-07, #92) and a stream adapter do not exist. Working default: the renewal plan and the `putAuthToken` and `reconnect` seams are built and tested with fakes; with no sink the manager reconnects; nothing claims it works on a real broker. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-07 and the stream adapter land. |
| OQ-1465 | Size of M1-23 (M1-23)? | Estimated 3,400 to 4,200 changed lines with specs, in five slices (A providers, B token endpoint adapter and client credentials, C TokenManager, D size preflight and registry rules, E adapter, composition and lab) of about 600 to 900 lines each; `oversize-approved` is not asked for. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer objects. |

## Spec questions: M1-13 (issue #98, docs/specs/M1-13.md)

Peek preflight and rescue preflight, trust-critical. Agile mode: working defaults in use, none needs an answer before the first manual test. Ids start at OQ-1466 (M1-23 used OQ-1451..1465).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1466 | Where may the quarantine be declared, and over which protocol (M1-13)? | No lease declares a queue except an observe lease for tap queues. Working default: `ensureQuarantine` sends one `PUT /api/queues/{vhost}/{name}` and is called only from `execute()` of the peek action (M1-16) with the `send` of `ctx.writes.mgmt`; no file of the preflight names a write-capable symbol, so the I-2 rule needs no exemption. A lease method for declaring was rejected: it widens a role-typed lease. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-16 lands. |
| OQ-1467 | How is the quarantine "created once per vhost" when `plan()` may not write (M1-13)? | Working default: `queueReady` means "exists and conforms, or is creatable here"; the result says which, the plan preview (M1-16) shows that a durable queue will be created once, and a refused declare at the arm step stops the peek before any `get`. Alternative: an explicit "prepare the quarantine" action. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer rules on creating a queue from a peek on a production broker. |
| OQ-1468 | How does `svc-peek` get the DLX sources and the effective config (M1-13)? | They are answered by `svc-topology` and a service may not import a service. Working default: a `PeekEvidencePort` in `ports`, implemented in `apps/engine` (M1-16) over `ActionExecutor.run` of `routing.dlx-sources` and `queues.effective-config` with the caller's principal. Copying the snapshot and routing code into `svc-peek` was rejected. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-16 wires the composition root. |
| OQ-1469 | What when `classicEnabled` is `'unknown'` (M1-13)? | The real probe always writes `'unknown'` (`svc-capabilities/src/profile-builder.ts`). Working default: classic is assumed available and the result says `classicAssumed`; an existing conforming classic quarantine queue is proof; a refused first declare stops the peek. Treating unknown as disabled would make the quarantine unavailable on every real broker. A follow-up (probe fills the field, registry rule) is proposed in the spec, not filed. Status: open - working default in use. | The probe fills `classicEnabled`. |
| OQ-1470 | What is the quarantine name before ADR-029 (M1-13)? | Working default: `quarantineName(prefix)` gives `${prefix}.quarantine` with the prefix injected by the composition root (`product`, the placeholder the ConnectionManager uses); a prefix that is empty, over 40 characters, starts with `amq`, or contains "rabbitmq" or "rmq" fails at construction. Status: open - working default in use. | ADR-029 settles the name. |
| OQ-1471 | What makes the quarantine overflow-safe, and where is the "quarantine full" cell (M1-13, A-5)? | The registry has no quarantine row and the planner has no "full" reason. Working default: `overflowSafe` is false when headroom is short, and (an extension of A-5 added by this spec) when an effective `message-ttl` or `expires` would expire quarantined messages or the queue. Follow-ups proposed, not filed: a code-derived "quarantine full" cell with citations and CF ids, and a `quarantine-full` planner reason. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer confirms the TTL and expiry extension. |
| OQ-1472 | How much headroom must the quarantine have (M1-13)? | Acquisition stops at the first doomed message, so one message is rescued. Working default: one message and, when a byte limit is effective, `max(maxMessageBytes, 16 MiB)` where the limit is known, else 16 MiB. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer gives a number. |
| OQ-1473 | Where does `rttMs` come from in an HTTP-only preflight (M1-13)? | The planner means the time of one `basic.get`. Working default: the elapsed time of the queue read on the injected clock, floor 1 ms, reported as a proxy. The executor checks the target time before every `get` (M1-14), so a wrong proxy cannot extend the hold. Status: open - working default in use. | A throwaway-channel measurement at the arm step is built. |
| OQ-1474 | Where do `heldDeathSources` come from (M1-13; OQ-1407, OQ-1427)? | Reading `x-death` needs a message and a preflight touches none. Working default: always absent, so the planner says `not-checked` and the A-2 rule "a queue holding x-death entries is a DLX target" is not applied by this work package. Status: open - working default in use. | A saved capture of the queue can supply them. |
| OQ-1475 | Does an alarm or a blocked publish connection stop every peek (M1-13)? | The issue reads as unconditional; blueprint 5.5 scopes it to the rescue. Working default: the facts are filled whenever the rescue is checked, and they stop a plan that depends on the quarantine; an unreadable connection list is reported and not treated as blocked, because the pre-arm of the publish lease in `execute()` (M1-16) reads `PublishLease.blocked()`. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer rules. |
| OQ-1476 | Which name is the write permission tested against for a publish to the default exchange (M1-13)? | Not in the fact sheet or the registry. Working default: the user's `write` regex must match both the empty name and `amq.default`; `configure` must match the queue name only when the queue must be declared. The lab spec (AC-26) records the real answer. Status: open - working default in use. | The lab run shows it. |
| OQ-1477 | Do the follow-ups #286 to #289 block the preflight (M1-13)? | All four are open and none is used: the planner still infers carry-over and method availability, classic plans have no registry effects, the four warnings are planner-owned, and the W-OVERLIMIT fix is data. Status: open - working default in use. | They are closed. |
| OQ-1478 | Does M1-13 add executor states, and who owns `svc-peek:it` (M1-13)? | M1-14 says M1-13 adds `preflighted`. Working default: no state is added (the preflight is a plan input); `preflighted` and `rescuing` join with M1-15 and M1-16 if wanted. The `it` target and `libs/testkit/src/it/svc-peek.it.ts` are created by whichever of M1-13 slice D and M1-14 slice C lands first. Status: open - working default in use. | M1-14 slice C or M1-13 slice D lands. |
| OQ-1479 | Trust-critical work in progress and protected paths (M1-13)? | At most 2 trust-critical work packages in progress (AGENTS.md section 12). `CODEOWNERS` already covers `/libs/engine/svc-peek/`. No protected path and no dependency change is needed. Status: open - working default in use. | The count is checked before slice A. |
| OQ-1480 | Size of M1-13 (M1-13)? | Estimated 2,350 changed lines with specs, in four slices (A quarantine and the evidence port, B peek preflight, C rescue preflight, D lab) of about 550, 750, 800 and 250; `oversize-approved` is not asked for. The adversarial review runs on slice C and on the I-2 argument of slice A. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer objects. |

## Spec questions: M0-51 close-out (issue #47, docs/specs/M0-51.md)

The web shell landed in five slices (A to E). The spec's section "Status on 2026-10-26" proposes two closing slices (F: `layouts.put` and the prod lock, G: real-router proof, review, budget settings and close-out). Agile mode: working defaults in use. Ids start at OQ-1481.

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1481 | Are the landed slice sizes accepted, and is M0-51 closed after F and G (M0-51)? | The five slices changed 360, 1,217, 2,800, 1,710 and 1,404 lines (about 7,500 in all, 40 to 45 percent specs, fixtures and docs) against the 800-line limit and the spec's 2,550 to 3,000 estimate, and none carried `oversize-approved`. Same kind of question as OQ-498. Working default: accepted after the fact, `Closes #47` on the last close-out slice. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer labels #47 `oversize-approved` or says otherwise. |
| OQ-1482 | Is `layouts.put` made `scope: 'local'` so the first criterion of #47 holds on an unarmed connection (M0-51; extends OQ-529 and OQ-784)? | Today a layout save on an unarmed connection answers 422 `GATE_BLOCKED`, so a restart restores nothing there; the shell shows the Problem and keeps the layout in memory. Options are those of OQ-784: (a) drop the target and keep `cid` in the input (one contract line, the audit rows lose the connection id), (b) a second kind of local target (kernel, trust-critical), (c) leave it armed-only. Working default: no engine change until ruled; slice G proves the failure path (AC-16). Status: open - working default in use. | The maintainer rules at OQ-784. |
| OQ-1483 | What happens to the Angular raw-size budget of `web` (M0-51)? | `apps/web/project.json` warns at 500 kB and fails at 1 MB raw; the initial total is 893.37 kB raw (245,810 bytes gzip against the real limit of 350,000), so every production build prints a warning and a real one is easy to miss. The cause is mostly `zod` with every locale (OQ-690). Options: raise the limits, remove the raw budget and rely on `web:budgets`, or trim zod. Working default: warning 950 kB and error 1.25 MB, gzip gate unchanged. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer picks. |
| OQ-1484 | Is a fresh-session `/review-wp` of slices C to E required before M0-51 closes (M0-51)? | The spec asked for it for the status bar (fail-closed segments, AC-12); none is recorded. The WP is not trust-critical. Working default: one `/review-wp` over `5322aa7..80e8615`, no `security-review`. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer says otherwise, or the review is recorded. |
| OQ-1485 | Who owns the shell's browser flows: keyboard (AC-10), 1280x720 layout (AC-9) and axe (M0-51; extends OQ-528, OQ-342)? | `web-csp-check` already proves the shell frame is a styled grid with no horizontal scroll at 1280x720. The keyboard and axe parts need `apps/e2e-web` (M0-60, #52), whose interfaces list `@smoke`, `@security` and `@a11y` but no shell flow. Working default: a comment on #52 asking for a shell flow, no separate issue. Status: open - working default in use. | #52 names the shell flow or a follow-up issue is opened. |

## Spec questions: M0-64 (issue #57, docs/specs/M0-64.md)

Container image. Agile mode: working defaults in use. The machine that wrote the first slice had no Docker daemon, so the live and workflow criteria are unproved until a daemon or the repository runners run them. Ids start at OQ-1486 (the M0-51 close-out used OQ-1481 to OQ-1485).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1486 | Are the digest pins right, and does the distroless Node match `.nvmrc` (M0-64)? | Both base images are pinned by tag and multi-arch index digest, read from the registries on 2026-10-26: `node:24.22.0-trixie-slim` and `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13:nonroot`. The distroless tag does not show its Node patch version, so it may differ from 24.22.0; the contract is `engines >=24.15`. Status: open - working default in use. | The first image build reports `node --version`, or Renovate takes over the two `FROM` lines. |
| OQ-1487 | What is the image name and who may publish (M0-64)? | `image.yml` pushes only when the repository variable `IMAGE_NAME` is set (and on a `v*` tag or a manual `publish` run from the default branch), under `ghcr.io/<owner>/<IMAGE_NAME>`. No name is invented (ADR-029, A-22); the repository itself is public today (`gh repo view`) although A-22 says private until ADR-029, which is OQ-1492. Status: open - the workflow builds, tests and scans without it. | A maintainer sets `IMAGE_NAME` after ADR-029, or decides the codename. |
| OQ-1488 | Should Trivy ignore unfixed CRITICAL findings (M0-64)? | The issue says "no CRITICAL"; the workflow fails on any, fixed or not, without `ignore-unfixed` or a `.trivyignore`. A distribution package with no fix would block the image until the base image moves. Status: open - strict default in use. | A maintainer decides on `ignore-unfixed: true` or a reviewed ignore file with an expiry date. |
| OQ-1489 | How does a release tag start `image.yml` (M0-64)? | Tags made by the release-please action with `GITHUB_TOKEN` do not start workflows (OQ-01). Until M0-68 wires the release pipeline or a token or app is used, publish with `gh workflow run image.yml -f publish=true` from the default branch. Status: open - for M0-68. | M0-68 lands. |
| OQ-1490 | Do `docker diff` come back empty and does a `COPY --chown` directory give a writable `/data` (M0-64)? | Both are expected from Docker's behaviour and are asserted by the live test AC-7 only; they could not be run without a daemon. If either fails on the first run, fix the Dockerfile, not the test. Status: open - unverified. | The first run of `image.yml` on the repository. |
| OQ-1491 | Is `npm install --global pnpm@<version>` an acceptable way to get pnpm into the build stage (M0-64)? | The version comes from `packageManager`, so the image cannot drift from the repository; the package integrity is the registry's. A corepack pin with a hash is stronger but needs a `packageManager` hash (a protected-path-style change in practice, as `pnpm-workspace.yaml` supply-chain keys are human-only). Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer wants the hash pin. |

## Spec questions: M1-23 hardening after the slice B review (issue #108, docs/specs/M1-23.md)

Found by the independent review of slice B. Agile mode: working defaults in use. Ids start at OQ-1500 (M0-68 used OQ-1492 to OQ-1499).

| ID | Question | Context | Closes when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ-1500 | Should a malformed, too-large or non-bearer identity-provider answer have its own Problem code, `IDP_ERROR`, instead of `BROKER_ERROR` (M1-23)? | Slice B maps `malformed`, `too-large` and a token type other than `bearer` to `BROKER_ERROR`, because the spec named no code. "Broker" means RabbitMQ everywhere else in the Problem catalogue, so a person reading "broker error" would look at the wrong system. Working default: keep `BROKER_ERROR` with a fixed text that names the identity provider; add `IDP_ERROR` with the TokenManager slice or M1-24. Status: open - working default in use. | A maintainer decides, or slice C needs a distinct code to render the retry text. |
| OQ-1501 | How are an identity provider's 4xx answers (404, 403, `invalid_scope`) mapped to Problems (M1-23, issue #295)? | Every non-2xx answer that is not 408, 429, 5xx or a redirect is `refused`, and the provider turns `refused` into `UNAUTHENTICATED`. A wrong path (404), a missing scope (`invalid_scope`) and a policy refusal (403) therefore read as bad credentials. Working default: read only the RFC 6749 5.2 `error` value from a closed set (`invalid_request`, `invalid_client`, `invalid_grant`, `unauthorized_client`, `unsupported_grant_type`, `invalid_scope`) from a small JSON body and never echo `error_description` or any other text; `invalid_client`, `unauthorized_client` and `invalid_grant` become `UNAUTHENTICATED` with the text "the identity provider refused the client credentials", the others become `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming the setting, and a 404 says the endpoint is wrong. Status: open - working default in use. | #295 lands, or a maintainer chooses other codes. |
